# Backup image and repository encoding

## Complete memory image membership and addresses

The transaction owner admits a public memory capture before selecting its generation. A newly admitted operation can register the idle generation, while reentrant operations require an existing outer frame and select its originals. Authorization and failed-transaction admission precede registration. Temporary generation owners retire only after the statement gate and execution scope close; a retirement failure remains terminal on the selected cut. This boundary distinguishes the capture operation's own gate from an unfinished enclosing write without resetting transaction state or creating an implicit transaction.

The memory mapping uses image version 1, namespace bytes `UQAMEMORYIMAGE01` and envelope generation zero. Its five ordered families are header, catalogs, field analyzer bindings, named resources and tables. The Engine adapter selects retained native owners; Common Storage owns address encoding and the bounded source lifecycle. Selection visits one owner position at a time, and each codec completes its bounded preparation before producing bytes. An absent owner or excluded evaluated-index position consumes a position without scanning ahead. No live catalog guard survives a source call.

The fixed 33-byte `UQAMEM01` header contains the source's sixteen-byte database identity, a Boolean catalog-allocation presence tag and the original little-endian `u64` allocation position. Absent positions have a zero payload. Each Engine storage owner keeps one source identity across captures and shared adapters; a different memory Engine obtains an independent identity. Snapshot identities belong to the enclosing cut, so a no-op capture does not alter header contents. Restoring creates a new source identity while retaining logical object identities and historical mutation origins.

Catalog keys are big-endian `u16` slots. Slots 1–24 select domains, enums, composites, ranges, shells, schemas, views, SQL routines, roles, role memberships, database authority, builtin routine authority, system relation authority, foreign wrappers, foreign servers, triggers, rules, graph catalog OIDs, sequences, sequence authority, paired foreign tables and authority, secondary-index metadata, comments and scoring parameters. Existing native codecs preserve each component's complete representation. Field binding keys contain their original table and field names. Named resource keys contain a kind byte (graph, model, path index or analyzer, numbered 1–4) and the original name.

Table keys begin with separate schema and relation names, the complete sixteen-byte object identity and complete sixteen-byte storage generation. Their component suffix selects metadata (tag 0 and slot), document (tag 1 and big-endian `u64` document identity), evaluated text (tag 2 and document identity), vector index (tag 3 and field name), or evaluated named index (tag 4 and index name). An index suffix then distinguishes metadata (0) from an evaluated value (1 followed by its document identity). Table metadata slots 0–9 contain lifecycle properties, authority, columns, CHECK constraints, foreign keys, keys, hierarchy, dropped attributes, column statistics and maintenance state. Opportunistic column accelerators remain derived query caches outside named evaluated-index membership.

Every name in an address uses its original UTF-8 bytes, escaping a NUL byte as `00 ff` and terminating the name with `00 00`. At the first differing byte this preserves native byte ordering; an ended prefix sorts before any continuation, including an escaped NUL. Consecutive terminated names therefore preserve tuple ordering and have a unique inverse. Fixed-width object and storage identities follow complete names, so no truncation, hash collision or delimiter can alias distinct generations. Component tags, big-endian numeric identities and independent value-presence tags preserve the native enumeration order within each table. The mapping consequently emits strictly increasing unique addresses without collecting or sorting the database population.

Common Storage's `read_memory_image_address` reconstructs the structural address into independently admitted names and complete fixed-width identities. It checks the memory namespace and envelope generation, decodes only the declared structural families and component tags, requires canonical NUL escapes and valid UTF-8, and consumes the complete key. Catalog slots, resource kinds, metadata slots and cross-component membership remain the corresponding import owner's validation responsibility. For each encoded string, ordinary nonzero bytes decode identically, `00 ff` decodes to one NUL and `00 00` uniquely ends the field; no other escape is accepted. Concatenating these inverses with exact-width identity reads and disjoint component tags gives D(E(a)) = a for every structural address a. Conversely, complete consumption and the unique escape forms imply E(D(b)) = b for every accepted key b. The inverse therefore cannot merge distinct valid addresses or accept an alternate byte spelling. Admission precedes name allocation, and each completed string retains that same allocation lease without copying; failed UTF-8, suffix, memory or cancellation checks retire all partial fields. This structural inverse does not establish database closure or activate a restored Engine.

For preservation, let the selected committed state consist of the captured catalog roots, resource roots, durable table membership, each table's immutable metadata and evaluated physical owners, and the jointly captured catalog allocation position. These finite native collections enumerate each retained member exactly once under their existing ordering. The address argument above distinguishes every member and generation. Each existing component codec preserves its original native value; composition therefore preserves the complete selected tuple and its membership, including empty metadata and resources. The adapter neither evaluates SQL nor rebuilds stored expressions or indexes. Later publication changes a successor generation, while the cut retains the selected one; private and temporary state are absent by the original committed-source selection contract. Cancellation, encoding failure or unwind retires the cut through the common source state machine and cannot publish a completed repository root. Complete logical restore validation and Engine activation remain separate requirements.

The common Storage [backup module](../../crates/uqa-storage/src/backup.rs) owns versioned image identities, ordered inventory encoding, fixed backup roots and protected frames for the [online backup design](online-and-incremental-backup.md). The native local repository also owns immutable pack installation, full object verification, durable root publication, prepared-image recovery and private restore-directory publication. Provider snapshot capture, import and Engine backup support remain separate deliveries. Those deliveries remain tracked in [#1322](https://github.com/cognica-io/uqa-engine/issues/1322) and the [implementation plan](../plans/0031-online-and-incremental-backup.md).

Native routine construction carries separately retained original-syntax and bound-scalar payload sizes into each immutable owner's cached footprint. Scalar constructor frames add their own retained leaf sizes and their completed children's sizes with checked arithmetic. Image construction transfers only the increase in retained-literal bytes observed during that native tree; sequential definition and body trees do not charge previous owners again. Thus independent catalog capture reserves the sum of the native owner, retained literals and separately owned SQL body, including when that body is reused. The original payload leases remain owned by the literals; counting them does not allocate or duplicate their values.

## Memory image import admission

`MemoryImageImportReader` retains the original root lease and repository verifier while decoding memory mapping version 1. The fixed header must match the root's source identity and have no persistent commit sequence. Catalog slots 1–24 must appear exactly once in order; each selected relation generation starts with all ten metadata units. Resource kinds and component kinds match the mapping. A relation cannot introduce a second object or storage generation, and every named evaluated index must supply metadata before its values. Shared mapping constants drive both the Engine exporter and Common Storage's importer. Native codecs still validate each opaque payload, and the enclosing restore owner must establish cross-catalog closure and fresh activation.

For structural completeness, the reader retains only the previous and current decoded addresses. Inductively, an admitted catalog slot extends the unique required prefix; the first noncatalog unit is admitted only after slot 24. A first table unit must be metadata slot zero, and each incomplete metadata prefix admits only its next slot. Switching relation or reaching EOF requires the preceding prefix to be complete. Within a relation, every address keeps the same complete object and generation pair; a value entry requires the immediately preceding entry to belong to the same named index. The repository's independently checked strict address order prevents duplicate or reversed units. These predicates establish complete structural prefixes without retaining a population-sized set. They do not infer referential integrity from address order.

Every component must reach its verified payload EOF before advancing. After the last component, the same underlying reader finishes whole-object digest and overlap checks in bounded windows; only its complete certificate admits final image EOF. Thus a valid component prefix cannot bypass the remaining integrity checks. Failure discards the verifier, decoded names and lease and leaves the input terminal, including cancellation during the final object pass. Header metadata remains available after successful completion, while all admitted heap and verification workspace is released. No Engine is exposed by this input interface.

## Retained memory data selection

Engine's memory adapters select physical components from their original captured table sources. Document identities use Common Storage's admitted, ordered one-element document cursor. Text identities use the inverted index's own immutable document map, including indexed fields with no terms; this inventory is independent of current rows and current analyzer definitions. Its lower-bound seek performs logarithmic comparisons without allocation, treats zero and the maximum document identity as ordinary keys, and checks both retained and invoking controls. Vector membership selects the original field-to-source identity from the captured vector collection and resolves that child against the same cut.

Each selected document, text or vector reader retains only its physical source, original allowance and cancellation plus the existing fixed output continuation. Opening the native codec is deferred until preparation or nonempty reading. Missing physical payload for an advertised identity fails the component; no SQL, text analysis, vector construction or collecting fallback supplies replacement bytes. The existing `UQADOC01`, `UQATXT01` and `UQAVEC01` codecs and their input validation are unchanged. Engine composes these adapters with committed catalog/resource/table membership for complete public memory export. Native document and table reconstruction, logical closure, Engine activation and public restore remain unfinished.

For preservation, let D, T and V be the ordered immutable document, text-document and vector-field memberships of the selected table generation. Repeated strict successor selection enumerates each finite membership exactly once: the first result is its least key, and every later result is the least key greater than the previous result, so no selected key is repeated or omitted. The text membership includes a document exactly when its own retained text carrier exists, even when its term sequence is empty. Each key resolves through the same immutable source to its established native encoding. Existing codec inverse arguments therefore preserve tuple metadata and evaluated values, occurrences and field statistics, and physical vector state and scores. A later table replacement or Engine retirement cannot change these sources. Preparation and reads retain the original source controls; terminal failure releases the continuation and its retained owner. Selection adds no query operator, ordering rule or numeric approximation.

## Native field analyzer binding images

Common Storage's `UQAFAB01` image preserves a field binding's independent index/search names, ownership, last-assignment phase and both original `UQAANA01` revisions. The byte order is the eight-byte magic, one-byte owner (0 field or 1 GIN), one-byte phase (0 index, 1 search or 2 both), the index and search optional names, and the two complete analyzer images in canonical 4096-byte child framing. Each optional name uses a 0/1 presence tag and, when present, a little-endian u64 UTF-8 byte length followed by its exact bytes. The enclosing memory mapping supplies the retained table/field address. The two sides are never resolved against the current named registry.

Input reconstructs both revisions from their supplied descriptors and dictionary artifacts, then applies `FieldAnalyzerBinding`'s existing name, last-assignment and GIN ownership invariants before returning an unpublished owner. It rejects unknown tags, malformed names, inconsistent paired assignments, incomplete or noncanonical child frames and trailing data. Name buffers retain their input reservations; encoded dictionaries retain the analyzer image's existing admission, while descriptor preparation and decoded models use the destination Analysis resource limits. Source retirement, cancellation and failed input release the corresponding retained or private owners without publishing a partial binding.

For preservation, let B = (n_i, A_i, n_s, A_s, o, p) be a valid native binding, with independently optional names, original resolved analyzer revisions, owner and last-assignment phase. Tagged UTF-8 lengths are injective over each optional name, and the fixed owner/phase discriminants are bijective over their native variants. The established analyzer-image inverse preserves each revision's canonical descriptor, exact dictionary closure and token observations. Canonical child framing is invertible and keeps the two byte streams distinct. Thus decoding an encoded valid B yields the same names, flags and independently resolved analyzer observations, including equal names referring to different historical revisions when the last assignment permits them. Existing validation accepts that reconstructed native state. No name lookup, analysis of stored documents, SQL execution or index rebuild occurs, so all existing index/search compositions receive their original inputs.

Output retains only fixed header and child-frame state and borrows names and resource bytes. At most one child cursor is open at a time; successful EOF retires it before opening its sibling. Both original capture controls wrap the complete stream, so cancellation of either side invalidates metadata output as well as analyzer payload output. The independently controlled output owner and every failed input release their own reservations. Engine supplies retained membership selection and admitted table/field cursor keys; the codec and semantic binding validation remain in Common Storage.

## Native memory table metadata

Common Storage's `UQAMTM01` image preserves a table's 16-byte logical identity and 16-byte storage generation; optional relation, row-type, array-type and rule OIDs; persistence and on-commit discriminants; declared-column, integer-key, loaded-statistics and dirty-statistics flags; and the full 128-bit next-document position. The variable suffix retains the original ordered FTS field names, opaque analyzer bytes and optional row-array type name. SQL definitions, authority, constraints, evaluated data and Planner statistics remain companion images owned by their existing codecs. Temporary tables are excluded by committed membership and cannot be encoded with this metadata format.

The wire order is the eight-byte magic, both identities, a 0/1 catalog-identity tag, the present relation OID and separately tagged optional companion OIDs, six one-byte lifecycle/boolean fields, the 16-byte next-document position, a u64 FTS-name count and its length-prefixed UTF-8 names, length-prefixed analyzer bytes, and a 0/1 optional row-array-name tag followed by a length-prefixed name when present. Integers and lengths are little-endian. The header requires at most 90 bytes. Absent and present-empty names remain distinct. The independent [wire fixture](../../crates/uqa-storage/src/backup/memory/table/metadata.hex) includes omitted/present companion OIDs, a position above the u64 range, Unicode field names and opaque analyzer bytes.

Each fixed field's encoding has an exact inverse over its declared integer width or accepted discriminant. Every variable field carries its exact byte length; UTF-8 validation preserves the original string or rejects it, without normalization. Induction over the declared name count therefore recovers the same ordered names, followed by the same opaque analyzer bytes and optional name. Invalid tags, truncation, count admission failure and trailing data cannot yield a decoded owner. The decoder's admitted header, vector and individual payload reservations follow the returned native value; partial failure or unwinding drops them. Output borrows payloads, retains only its fixed cursor and emits at most 16 KiB per call. This representation introduces no expression evaluation or catalog rebinding; cross-catalog validity and destination publication remain restore requirements.

## Scheduled native component composition

Common Storage's `NativeBackupImage` consumes an ordered mapping of independently retained native codecs. A mapping selects at most one component or empty position per call; the shared stream separately schedules that codec's `ImageReader::prepare` allowance before exposing its unit. The current codec owns its original source and scratch, while its admitted opaque address remains available for ordering until the following unit. No complete component is encoded during selection. Engine composes this interface with catalog/resource/table adapters for complete full and incremental memory export. Native reconstruction, logical closure and public Engine restore remain unfinished.

For a committed cut, let the mapping supply the finite sequence `U = [(a_i, k_i, b_i)]`, where `a_i` is an opaque unit address, `k_i` its kind and `b_i` the owning codec's complete byte string. Assume the mapping selects only that cut, every codec preserves its established byte representation and each preparation step obeys its position bound. The stream rejects zero families and non-increasing addresses. It accepts a successor only after a nonempty read of the preceding codec reaches successful EOF. Therefore each exposed unit has exactly the selected address and kind, each emitted payload is a prefix of its unique `b_i`, and successful completion yields `U` once in order. Pending selection and preparation steps emit no payload and cannot change this sequence. Partitioning output into different positive windows changes only fragment boundaries; concatenation remains `b_i`, so each existing owner's inverse continues to recover the same native value.

An advance call either prepares an existing codec using at most its requested number of positions, or spends one position selecting the next component and gives preparation only the remainder. Thus each successful call accounts for at most its requested position bound. `visited_positions` counts these selection and codec-preparation positions; `copied_payload_bytes` counts encoded native component bytes returned to the caller, including each owner's wire metadata. The shared composition layer performs no separately attributed raw metadata reads or excluded-record scans; those counters remain zero. These counters do not estimate raw logical payload size, physical pages or elapsed time.

The stream takes its active owner out of its state before every fallible operation. Only a successful call reinstalls it; errors and unwinding leave the stream failed and retire the current codec and selected generation. The mapping's generation check runs at every selection, preparation and read boundary, including after output, so failure of another retained component invalidates the whole image. Empty output does not retire an active codec. Successful completion releases the selected generation and remains complete independently of later cancellation. Repository publication still requires complete source EOF and the existing inventory and root verification; no prefix can become a completed backup.

Core's immutable-map lower-bound operation follows the same existing AVL search used by a mutable root. At each node, an eligible key is the current least candidate and search continues into its left subtree; an ineligible key excludes that node and its left subtree, so search continues right. This invariant returns the least included or strictly greater key, depending on the bound, without visiting preceding entries. The selected immutable root keeps the same nodes across live mutation and retirement. Balanced height gives logarithmic comparisons; the search keeps only a node link and one candidate and allocates nothing. Resource and table adapters use this operation without copying a name inventory.


## Native index metadata and scoring parameter images

Execution owns `CapturedCatalogIndexes::image_reader` and `CatalogIndexes::read_image`. `UQAIDX01` and a little-endian `u64` count precede records in native schema/name order. Each record contains six length-prefixed UTF-8 strings: schema, index name, index type, owning table name, columns encoding and parameters encoding. A one-byte definition tag follows: zero represents absence; one precedes the length-prefixed original definition. Every string length is a little-endian `u64` byte count. Absence and an explicitly empty definition remain distinct. Capture selects only durable index rows; JSON encodings remain opaque and are neither parsed nor normalized during image transfer. Input rejects malformed framing, invalid UTF-8, invalid presence tags, empty or NUL-containing identifiers, duplicate or unordered keys and trailing bytes. Complete restore owns semantic definition and referenced-table validation before activation.

Common Storage owns `CapturedScoringParameters::image_reader` and `ScoringParameters::read_image`. `UQASCP01` and a little-endian `u64` count precede ordered key/value pairs of length-prefixed UTF-8 strings. This native carrier permits arbitrary string bytes, including empty strings and NUL; no JSON parser or scoring function runs during image transfer. Input rejects invalid UTF-8, truncated framing, duplicate or unordered keys and trailing bytes. The native entry's allowance funds both its key and encoded value and follows every immutable clone and captured generation. Decoding transfers its already admitted buffers into that entry without a second payload copy.

Both output cursors retain a fixed set of fields and a bounded native tree iterator, borrow captured text and check cancellation between copies of at most 4 KiB. Common Storage admits each cursor before boxing, limits each read to 16 KiB, and permanently retires failed or unwound cursors. Input constructs a private root, retains admitted payload buffers and tree nodes through success, and releases the whole partial root on denial, corruption, I/O failure, cancellation or unwind. Index reconstruction additionally admits its separate native lookup-key strings; catalog copies share immutable entries rather than duplicating payloads.

For preservation, let I be a finite ordered durable map from native index identities to complete native index rows, and let S be a finite ordered string-to-string scoring map. Let E_I and E_S emit the corresponding headers, counts and ordered fields, and D_I and D_S be the validating native decoders. Length framing is injective on UTF-8 byte strings, fixed tags distinguish optional absence from every present string, and strict key order makes all native insertions distinct. Field induction followed by induction on the record count yields D_I(E_I(I)) = I and D_S(E_S(S)) = S; native registry invariants make each index lookup key equal to its row identity. Canonical lengths, tags, ordering and exact EOF give the reverse identities for every accepted image. Therefore key lookups, enumeration order and every opaque encoding are preserved, and composing either restored registry with its existing observers gives the same result after complete restore validates cross-catalog references. The durable index projection excludes temporary rows before encoding. Scoring publication, replacement, rollback and independent capture continue to use the existing immutable root boundary; adding payload leases changes resource ownership, not the map operations or values. This component does not assemble or activate an Engine.

## Native durable comment images

Execution owns `CapturedComments::image_reader` and `CommentRegistry::read_image`. `UQACMT01` and a little-endian `u64` count precede records in native object/subobject/class order. Each record contains the original `u32` object OID, `i32` subobject number, `u32` class OID, a little-endian `u64` UTF-8 byte length and the exact description bytes. Durable capture excludes the session's temporary descriptions. Input rejects zero object/class OIDs, negative subobjects, duplicate or unordered addresses, empty descriptions, NUL, invalid UTF-8, truncated fields and trailing bytes. Object existence and cross-catalog address closure belong to complete restore validation.

The output cursor borrows the captured immutable root and description bytes, retaining fixed field storage and the existing bounded tree iterator. Opening it does not scan description payloads. Common Storage admits the cursor before boxing, limits reads to 16 KiB, and retires it on error, cancellation or unwind; the codec checks its supplied control between at most 4 KiB copies. Reconstruction admits each decoded text buffer before copying and moves that exact admitted string into the native retained map. A private candidate becomes available only after exact EOF, so malformed, denied, interrupted or unwound input releases its partial root without publication.

For preservation, let C be a finite durable map from valid native comment addresses to nonempty NUL-free UTF-8 descriptions, ordered by the native address comparison. Let E emit the header, count and ordered fixed-address/length/text records, and D validate those records into an unpublished native map. Fixed-width little-endian encodings preserve every address component; length framing and UTF-8 decoding preserve the exact text bytes. Strict key order makes each insertion distinct and reproduces the original order, so induction on the record count gives D(E(C)) = C. Conversely, canonical framing, strict order and exact EOF give E(D(b)) = b for each accepted image b. Thus object-address lookups, ordered description enumeration and exact text remain unchanged after enclosing restore establishes the corresponding original catalog objects. Applying E to a captured registry preserves its durable projection and does not introduce temporary descriptions.

Every output borrow remains tied to the captured generation, including after source replacement and Engine retirement. Every reconstructed text and map node retains its own decoding allowance; failure drops the complete partial ownership tree. Subsequent captures independently admit the retained footprint through the existing native registry. This component does not activate an Engine or complete whole-memory backup and restore.

## Native paired foreign catalog images

Execution owns `UQAFCT01`, the ordered durable foreign-table catalog image. Each relation records its original schema/name key, stored display name, permanent or unlogged persistence, object identity, optional relation OIDs and row-array name, server name and optional original server reference, ordered option map and original option sequence. The record then contains the existing SQL-owned column, dropped-attribute, table-CHECK and native table-authority images. Temporary definitions and their authority are excluded by the existing paired capture roots. Foreign rows, host callbacks and remote sources are not read by this codec.

SQL's direct `BoundTableSecurity` image interface uses the existing `UQATSA01` field order. Its reader borrows native column maps and independent ACL revisions without building a vector projection; its decoder admits map nodes and field payloads before allocation and transfers their reservations into native authority. Execution reconstructs definition and authority entries privately under the same relation key and persistence, transferring both key and payload reservations into their retained trees. No decoded catalog is returned before exact end-of-stream validation. Cross-catalog role, server, type and expression-reference validation belongs to complete restore before activation; optional legacy references are preserved without name rebinding.

SQL records the constructor-funded footprint of each independently retained literal while copying native declarations or decoding their images. Execution includes those child footprints in the immutable foreign entry alongside its direct payload reservation. The child leases remain with their native owners; independent captures reserve the complete cached entry footprint without scanning, copying or re-evaluating the definition. Thus a retained default or CHECK cannot bypass capture admission by storing its payload outside the enclosing column/check reservation.

Common Storage composes each complete child image using canonical frames: a little-endian u32 payload length followed by that payload, with every non-final nonempty frame exactly 4,096 bytes, an optional shorter final frame and a zero-length terminator. Output fills one admitted fixed-size frame and does not scan the child in advance. Input exposes exact child EOF at the terminator without consuming the following parent's bytes; a child decoder must still validate its own complete schema and EOF. Oversized frames, nonzero frames after a short frame, truncated headers or payloads and child trailing data are rejected. Interrupted I/O retains framing progress; other failures and unwinds terminate the cursor. Existing controlled owners check cancellation around bounded input attempts and output windows.

Let P select the durable paired records from a committed foreign catalog R, retaining order, persistence, identity references, native declarations and evaluated authority. Let E encode those records and D reconstruct a private catalog. For any child byte string b, canonical framing has the unique representation obtained by splitting b into consecutive 4,096-byte blocks and its possible shorter suffix, then appending the zero terminator. The length bounds and short-frame rule make the frame decoder an inverse that consumes exactly that representation and no following parent byte. The existing SQL column, dropped-attribute, CHECK and authority inverse arguments therefore apply unchanged to every framed child.

Length-prefixed UTF-8 fields and fixed-width identities preserve all remaining native fields; presence tags preserve absence independently of a present value. Strict relation and option order reject duplicate keys, and option-sequence validation requires a permutation of the recorded option keys. The single record supplies the same key and persistence to both restored trees. Applying these inverses to each record in order yields D(E(R)) = P(R), with identical original SQL syntax, selected identity references and authority observations. Native lookup and subsequent SQL consumers receive the same inputs; the codec introduces no query operator, re-execution, optimizer rewrite or approximation. Failure destroys the unpublished owner and all admitted partial payloads instead of publishing one half of a pair. This component does not complete whole-memory assembly or public Engine restore.

## Native rewrite-rule catalog images

SQL owns the captured rewrite-rule image through `CapturedEventCatalog<StoredRule>::image_reader` and `RuleCatalog::read_image`. `UQARUL01` and a little-endian `u64` relation count precede ordered schema/name pairs, each declaration count and ordered rule names. Empty durable relation membership remains representable; captured temporary roots are excluded and input rejects them.

Each entry contains the original `CreateRule` fields in native order: name, table, event, INSTEAD flag, optional condition, ordered original statements and replacement flag. It then records enabled state, the optional bound `ExpressionPlan`, optional OLD/NEW row identities with their original column order, optional relation/column/routine dependency sets, and optional catalog OID. Original statements and bound conditions remain separate; the decoder neither lowers the former nor rebinds the latter. Presence tags preserve absent legacy metadata separately from empty metadata. Dependency sets retain their strict native order and routine argument-type sequence.

Preparation borrows the selected roots without traversal or allocation. Output reuses admitted continuations, emits at most 16 KiB per read and checks cancellation between fields and at most 4 KiB copies. Input admits native nodes and metadata before allocation, rejects malformed framing, duplicate or unordered membership, inconsistent declaration keys and recorded OID collisions, and returns an unpublished native registry. Cross-catalog validity and replacement of process-local OLD/NEW identities belong to the existing restore/activation boundary.

Each reconstructed declaration retains its original-statement forest and bound-condition forest through the immutable event entry. Shared declaration roots can outlive the outer catalog. Full, partial, interrupted and unwinding destruction returns native fields to those forests, reusing admitted retirement capacity without another allocation. Ordinary event lookup, bidirectional iteration, capture and mutation keep the same native `StoredRule` interface.

For preservation, let R be a durable ordered rule catalog with valid local native references and distinct recorded OIDs. Each scalar, original statement and bound expression-plan field uses the established injective codec and its inverse. The exhaustive declaration schema, constructor/presence tags and ordered dependency sequences preserve every remaining field. Induction over each dependency set, declaration sequence and relation sequence therefore gives D(E(R)) = R under native stored observations, including absent legacy fields, original OLD/NEW addresses, action order and empty relation membership. No rule is rewritten or executed during encoding or decoding; existing rule selection and execution receive the same stored inputs. Shared immutable ownership preserves these observations through replacement and retirement, while failed reconstruction publishes nothing. The codec introduces no algebraic operator, approximation or optimizer transformation.


## Native routine catalog images

SQL's captured routine registry exposes a bounded `image_reader`; `RoutineRegistry::read_image` reconstructs an unpublished native registry. `UQAROU01` is followed by a little-endian `u64` name count. Each entry contains its framed durable name, overload count and original overload sequence. Each overload contains the complete stored declaration followed by its source/bound tag. Source bodies retain their original text without compilation. A bound SQL body contains its ordered query/mutation members in the existing native plan format without additional root headers. Session-compiled PL/pgSQL bodies are excluded from the durable carrier.

The declaration schema preserves optional object identity, catalog revision and OID; original name and creation flags; parameter order, mode, written and resolved type metadata, defaults and default types; result shape and original result-type metadata; language and source/statement body; SQL body form; creation search path; volatility, strictness, ownership, security and parallel safety; planner support, raw COST/ROWS bits, ordered configuration and complete EXECUTE ACL entries. Original statements and bound plans remain independent images of their respective native fields. Parser source origins are transient, while creation-time configuration actions and attribute clauses must already be consumed by publication; the producer rejects unpublished actions instead of silently discarding them.

Finite enum and option tags distinguish native constructors and absence. Counts and UTF-8 framing preserve sequence boundaries; fixed-width identity and numeric fields preserve original bits. Input requires strictly ordered durable catalog names, matching declaration names, valid native identities, unique recorded object identities and OIDs, and agreement between original source/statement form and the source/bound body tag. Existing expression, type, query and mutation readers retain their structural checks. Cross-catalog dependencies, overload legality and complete activation remain the enclosing restore's responsibility.

The routine definition and a bound body have separate immutable shared owners. Each shared owner exposes the same native value to ordinary SQL consumers and retains its own payload admission and destruction owner. A body reference can therefore outlive its function, catalog generation, capture and Engine. The definition keeps original statement/default construction in the shared syntax forest; the bound body keeps one existing native-plan forest per member. Native vectors, shared payloads and private image boxes are admitted before allocation. Decoded values move into their native immutable fields through infallible paired moves, and destruction returns their recursive roots to the retained forests before normal field cleanup. No execution variant or interpreter dispatch path is added.

For preservation, let R be an ordered durable routine catalog with consumed publication actions, locally valid native references and distinct recorded identities. Let P exclude only transient parser origins and session compilation caches. The existing original-statement, expression, type, binding and native-plan inverses preserve each nested field. The declaration's exhaustive ordered schema, independent presence tags and fixed-width representations preserve the remaining fields. Applying these inverses to each overload, then each strictly ordered catalog key, gives D(E(R)) = P(R). Original source bodies are never evaluated, and bound bodies keep their selected references and ordered operations. Native routine lookup, invocation and catalog observation therefore receive the same projected inputs; the codec adds no query operation, optimizer rewrite or approximation.

For ownership, each decoded allocation belongs to either the enclosing admitted catalog entry, the definition's forest or the independently shared body's vector/plan owners. The construction invariant assigns every unfinished subtree to exactly one forest; publication transfers each completed subtree with its owner without a fallible intervening operation. Shared handles preserve that owner until their own last reference is released. Before native destruction each recursive root returns to its original admitted retirement workspace, whose capacity already covers the construction path. Retirement therefore frees all private payloads without additional allocation or recursive syntax-stack growth, including denial, I/O failure, cancellation and unwinding. A new capture independently funds the full retained footprint of both definition and body; its lifetime does not substitute for either native owner. This component does not complete committed memory image assembly or public Engine restore.

## Original stored statement and bound body images

SQL owns `Statement::image_reader` and `Statement::read_image` for original stored query, mutation and NOTIFY bodies. `UQASTM01` is followed by a little-endian `u64` root count of one. Root tags 0 through 6 denote SELECT, VALUES, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE, MERGE and NOTIFY. Original syntax remains distinct from its executable lowering: SELECT retains its written CTEs, set-operation operands, sources, grouping, windows and locking; mutations retain target names and creation-bound flags, aliases, descendants, assignments, conflict clauses, original RETURNING aliases and MERGE target-column identities. A NOTIFY body retains the exact channel and payload. Other utility statements are outside the durable routine/rule body carrier and fail explicitly.

The explicit field schemas in SQL define sequence order and constructor tags independently of Rust layout. Counts, positions and UTF-8 byte lengths use little-endian `u64`; Booleans and option presence use independent zero/one bytes. Original scalar constructors 0 through 23 reuse the existing stored-expression encoding. Constructors 24 through 27 add scalar subqueries, EXISTS, IN-subqueries and window calls, retaining the complete original query child or specification. Window definitions preserve names, inherited indexes and original clauses; the compiler-only `raw_definition` equality key and parser-only reference-origin positions follow their existing durable-definition exclusions. Restored names do not retain an earlier client's SQL character position. Ordered MERGE column bindings preserve original sixteen-byte identities, optional signed attribute numbers and strictly ordered domain OIDs. Execution-scoped retained-row sources are invalid durable syntax.

`UnifiedPlan::image_reader` and `UnifiedPlan::read_image` independently encode a bound SQL body member as `UQAPLN01`, a root count of one and a query/command tag of zero/one. The following record reuses the native query or INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE/MERGE command schema. Original scalar/query/window addresses and private mutation arenas retain their existing validation. This owner does not substitute lowered plans for stored source syntax or parse a source string to manufacture an image.

Both reconstructed carriers use the same admitted forest traversal as native query input, specialized to their actual node type. Original syntax therefore does not enlarge executable-plan continuation frames. The owner admits vector capacity, names, payloads and required native boxes before constructing them, keeps every unfinished node in its forest and transfers completed children through infallible field attachment. Concrete constructors have independent stack frames; tag dispatch does not reserve every constructor's temporary storage simultaneously. Enum and struct schemas exhaustively select their native fields, including explicit exclusions, so adding a field requires revisiting its image projection.

Window references propagate only through fields in the same original SELECT. Nested SELECTs, set operands, CTEs and mutation bodies close their own scope. Completion rejects out-of-range window addresses, invalid inherited definitions and inconsistent multiple-assignment positions. Complete EOF validation rejects truncation and trailing bytes. These structural checks do not bind names, execute predicates or establish whole-catalog closure.

Successful and interrupted reconstruction retain the continuation capacity needed for iterative retirement. Retirement detaches recursive fields into that workspace before native field destruction; already flat fields need no additional traversal node. Existing boxes and vector allocations are reused or released without acquiring another reservation. The immutable public owners retain the complete allowance through cross-thread use and final release. The bound-body wrapper returns its outer query/command to that same owner before dropping the outer box. Vector growth may choose the exact required capacity when a preferred capacity does not fit; a successful smaller-budget reconstruction remains valid only while every actual allocation and replacement peak is admitted.

For preservation, let T be the finite original stored-statement carrier with valid local addresses, and let P be SQL's durable-definition projection, retaining original persisted fields while excluding transient compiler and parser-origin metadata. Let E encode the explicit constructor/field records and D decode them into the private immutable owner. At a leaf, the existing scalar, value, type and selected-binding inverses preserve the original stored observations. At each constructor, its tag selects one alternative; independent option tags, explicit lengths and the ordered field schema select the same child boundaries. Assuming the inverse for each child, infallible attachment reconstructs exactly that constructor and its projected fields. Structural induction over the finite syntax gives D(E(t)) = P(t), and the unchanged field ordering gives E(D(b)) = b for every accepted canonical image b. The same argument applies to a bound member through the existing native query/command inverse. Original source-versus-bound semantics, declaration order, identities and exact value representations therefore remain unchanged; any existing operation receiving those inputs observes the same carrier and composition. No query operator, evaluation, approximation or optimizer rewrite is introduced.

For ownership, each unfinished or completed child belongs to exactly one forest frame or one attached native field. Admission precedes allocation, and attachment transfers that child without duplicating its allocation or lease. A descent reserves continuation capacity before exposing another unfinished child. Retirement traverses no deeper than that admitted construction path, including the required empty child slot; flat placeholders introduce no new descent. Induction over construction and attachment preserves complete ownership, while induction over retirement removes every child without allocation or recursive query-stack growth. Failure, cancellation and reader unwinding therefore discard only the private candidate and release its complete admission. Routine/rule catalog assembly, complete memory backup, logical activation and public Engine restore remain separate requirements.

## Native query image output

SQL's `QueryPlan::image_reader` emits `UQAQRY01`, a little-endian `u64` root count of one, and the existing stored-syntax projection of the native query. `ExpressionPlan::image_reader` uses `UQAXPL01` with the same root count and emits its scalar followed by its complete ordered query arena. Embedded native scalars use the `UQASCL01` constructor records without another root header. Column types and selected function bindings reuse their existing image owners. The paired native reconstruction interface is described below; stored catalog integration and public restore remain unfinished.

Native struct fields follow their explicit order in SQL's query-image schema. A query retains `relations_bound`, CTEs and its relational root. Relational tags 0, 1 and 2 identify a query block, set operation and VALUES; set operations preserve both operands, all ordering and limit fields and their query arena. CTE bodies distinguish queries and commands, with command tags 0 through 3 for INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE and MERGE, exactly the command domain of `CteBody`. Mutation records retain assignments and indirection, conflict actions, original RETURNING aliases, selected role incarnations, rewritten view inputs/checks and all nested queries. Query blocks retain named/inherited windows, projections, privilege columns, sources, predicates, grouping, distinctness, ordering, access decisions and locks. Source tags 0 through 5 identify tables, joins, VALUES, functions, function groups and subqueries. All declared source metadata, original function binding and internal relation/column metadata remain present.

Every vector has a little-endian `u64` count and ordered elements. Options have an independent 0/1 presence tag. Booleans and enum constructors use explicit byte tags; integer fields keep their declared width and little-endian representation, while positions use `u64` addresses. Strings have a `u64` UTF-8 byte length followed by the original bytes. Ordered privilege-column and rewrite-position sets use native iterators, preserving each key exactly without copying or repeatedly seeking through the set. A column identity contains its optional qualifier and column separately. Bound roles retain original name, `u32` OID and sixteen-byte incarnation; named roles remain a distinct constructor.

The projection omits exactly the existing stored-copy exclusions: query analysis output/source/grouping, relation privilege subject, deferred outer rewrites, a table source's execution query and a subquery source's captured output. Execution-scoped retained-row sources and utility commands in a CTE are invalid stored query inputs. Their detection does not execute a source, resolve a name or reinterpret SQL. Every scalar keeps its original native query-arena address; the reconstruction owner validates addresses in their enclosing restored arena before publication.

Preparation retains only a borrowed root and performs no allocation or child traversal. Each continuation observes one native field, sequence element or set entry; the enclosing definition reader admits traversal frames and set cursors, returns at most 16 KiB and copies at most 4 KiB between cancellation checks. A large text payload stays borrowed across calls. Completion, denied admission, invalid sources, cancellation and unwinding retire every active continuation and its reservation; failure remains terminal. The original plan and its ordinary lifetime are unchanged.

For preservation, let P be SQL's established stored-copy projection on finite valid native queries, including the four data-modifying CTE constructors, and let E encode the records above. Constructor tags distinguish alternatives, option tags distinguish absence, fixed widths delimit inline metadata and sequence/text lengths delimit ordered children. Primitive metadata encodings and the existing scalar/type/binding encodings preserve their original observations. Structural induction over native query, source, CTE and mutation children therefore establishes injectivity of E on P's observable carrier: equal images imply identical projected constructors, field values and ordered children, including original arena addresses. Since E reads exactly P's retained fields and never observes its excluded fields, E(q) = E(P(q)). The reconstruction inverse below preserves these records and their original scope without rerunning SQL. This representation argument does not establish complete catalog reconstruction or Engine activation.

The traversal invariant is that the active field and admitted continuation stack encode precisely the unconsumed suffix of E(q). Advancing a struct selects its next field; advancing a sequence or native set selects its next original element; delegating a scalar/type/binding preserves that child's established image invariant. Each step consumes one field or emits the next bounded byte fragment, preserving order and making progress on finite syntax. On failure all continuations only borrow source nodes, so dropping them releases exactly the reader-owned scratch without retiring or mutating the source. This establishes bounded output scratch, constant native call stack through query depth and unchanged source observation under interruption.

## Native query reconstruction

SQL's `QueryPlan::read_image` and `ExpressionPlan::read_image` reconstruct these native records into immutable `CapturedQuery` and `CapturedExpressionPlan` owners. The input keeps the original query/source/CTE/mutation constructors, selected function and role bindings, scalar representations, field order and complete query arenas. It does not parse SQL, resolve a name, invoke a function, rebuild a view or execute a mutation. Transient analysis and execution fields start empty, matching the stored projection. This is a component interface; native table reconstruction, logical closure, fresh Engine activation and public restore remain unfinished.

The reconstruction owner retains every unfinished native node, its payload allowance and an admitted vector of traversal frames. Before descending it reserves space for the child and one additional retirement frame. Native child vectors and required boxes are admitted before population. A completed child moves infallibly into its parent; metadata containing scalar descendants remains in a temporary retiring owner until that transfer. Cancellation, truncated input, invalid constructors, denied memory and reader unwinding therefore leave no unowned partially reconstructed subtree. Bound role references retain their name and shared payload allowance inside the role binding, so cloning the reference cannot outlive its lease.

Scalar reconstruction records the largest query and window addresses without another recursive walk. Direct source references transfer to their enclosing query or mutation; derived-query bodies, CTE bodies and rule RETURNING inputs retain their separate namespaces. Before completing a node, input checks each observed address against that node's original arena. Canonical window definitions cannot refer to call slots, and inherited windows address earlier named definitions. Ordered sets reject repeated or reversed keys. Role incarnations and the shared native type/function/value records retain their existing validation. Cross-catalog logical validation remains a separate restore requirement.

Retirement uses the same already admitted frame vector. It first retires each node's scalar roots through the scalar owner's constant-stack operation, then detaches one native child at a time. A suspended parent contains only its remaining children; exhausted parents contain empty native placeholders and can use ordinary field destruction. If the deepest completed or partial input path has depth d, descent has already admitted at least d + 1 frame slots. Retirement visits only paths from that forest plus an optional empty required child, so it never exceeds that capacity. It performs no memory admission or allocation, including when the invoking allowance is exhausted. Each scalar and native edge is visited a bounded number of times, giving linear retirement work and constant native call-stack use. Ordinary public plan and interpreter lifetimes are unchanged.

For the inverse argument, restrict P's projected carrier to finite native plans with valid original arena addresses, role identities and shared scalar/type/binding records. Let D be the input above. Primitive readers invert the corresponding fixed-width, tagged and length-delimited fields. For each constructor, D creates the same native constructor and consumes exactly its ordered fields; the induction hypothesis applies to each scalar and native child. Distinct scopes are checked against their own completed arena without renumbering, pruning or rebinding. Consequently D(E(P(q))) = P(q), including private mutation rewrite state, while invalid framing or addresses produce no published owner. The existing observation-preserving projection and this inverse preserve every operation defined over the stored native carrier; they introduce no new query algebra or execution effect. The ownership invariant is independent of that structural induction: every fallible read leaves its current forest inside either the parent owner or a temporary retiring scalar owner until the next infallible transfer.

## Native stored view images

SQL owns `CapturedViewDefinitions::image_reader` and `ViewDefinitions::read_image`. `UQAVIW01` is followed by a little-endian `u64` durable-entry count. Each entry begins with its separately length-prefixed schema and relation name in strictly increasing native key order. The authority record contains the original owner incarnation, optional relation ACL, ordered column ACL map, optional relation revision and ordered column-revision map. Revision-only entries remain present. The definition follows with object identity, optional output names, persistence, ordered options, view kind, evaluated rows, optional column types, optional collations, population flag, optional relation OID quartet, optional array type name and the headerless native query record. Persistence tags 0/1 are permanent/unlogged; temporary entries are excluded by capture and rejected by input. Kind tags 0/1 distinguish ordinary and materialized views. Sequence, option, name, type, ACL and exact Core value encodings reuse their existing owners.

The producer borrows the immutable catalog and all definition payloads. Preparation performs no allocation or scan. Admitted continuations and ordered-map cursors stream one field at a time; reads retain the definition reader's 16 KiB output bound and cancellation polling. Materialized rows retain their original key order, value representations and multiplicity, including non-finite and signed-zero floating-point bits. No query, default, function or REFRESH is executed. Temporary names, analysis caches and execution-only query state remain outside the existing stored projection.

Input admits names, vectors, ordered-map nodes, native query frames and the concrete immutable definition owner before allocating them. It rejects malformed tags, non-increasing or duplicate keys, duplicate object/OID claims, invalid recorded OID quartets, truncation and trailing bytes. Original optional metadata absence remains distinct from an empty value; cross-catalog role, relation, routine, column and dependency validation belongs to the enclosing restore. Reconstructed catalogs are private inputs, not an activated Engine.

The immutable catalog entry owns the decoded view and its query retirement workspace together. Lookups expose only the existing borrowed `StoredView`; shared generations retain that same owner. Ordinary catalog insertion keeps its inline representation and existing copy semantics. Replacement creates a new definition rather than mutating a captured image. Before final field destruction, the image owner returns its query to the admitted iterative retirement workspace and leaves an empty query in the view. Thus complete and partial cleanup uses no new admission and no query-depth-dependent native stack. The catalog's cached footprint includes metadata, concrete owner allocation, query payloads, retirement frames and independently retained bound-role payloads. Every independent capture funds that complete footprint; shared role references retain their own original leases if they outlive the query.

Let V be a finite durable view in the existing stored projection, with authority S, native query Q, metadata M and evaluated rows R. The format has disjoint constructor/presence tags and explicit sequence/name lengths. The authority, type and Core value inverses preserve S, M and R field for field; the native query inverse preserves Q and its original local arenas. Structural induction over the ordered entries and each row's ordered fields therefore gives `decode(encode(V)) = V` under native stored observations. Ordinary view analysis receives the same Q and authority; materialized scans receive the same R without evaluating Q. Immutable root sharing preserves those observations across replacement and source retirement. During reconstruction, each admitted native payload has exactly one owner or a shared child with its own lease; map publication transfers that owner only after admission succeeds. Failure retires the unpublished owner forest. These facts establish preservation of existing relational and value operations without introducing an operator or approximation. Native table reconstruction, cross-catalog validation, Engine activation and public restore remain unfinished requirements.

## Native executable scalar images

SQL owns the `UQASCL01` executable-scalar image, `ScalarExpr::image_reader`, and admitted `ScalarExpr::read_image` reconstruction into an immutable `CapturedScalar`. The eight-byte header and little-endian `u64` root count of one precede the native constructor. Tags 0–23 retain the corresponding scalar-definition constructors; their executable records additionally retain a typed literal's resolved type and original parameter index and a composite row's resolved result type. Tags 24–29 encode physical row positions, fixed result collations, scalar query references, EXISTS, IN-subquery operands and window calls. Names retain their existing persistent identity contract: transient parser positions are not stored. Function selection, written call syntax, null ordering, original evaluated values and composite descriptors use the existing SQL and Core field encodings without resolving names or invoking SQL.

Optional fields use independent Boolean presence tags, except `ScalarOrder::nulls`, which uses one combined byte: 0 for absent, 1 for NULLS FIRST and 2 for NULLS LAST. Counts and query-arena addresses use little-endian `u64`, checked against the destination address space. A collation record contains its derivation tag (none, implicit, explicit or conflict), two `u32` identifiers and the original `u32` initial collation; unused identifiers must be zero. A window preserves its definition index, argument and partition order, ordered keys, frame mode, both frame-bound constructors and expressions, written BETWEEN flag, exclusion, filter and original call modifiers. Native query references remain addresses in the enclosing plan's arena. This component does not publish a query or resolve those references; the enclosing restored plan must validate its complete arena before catalog publication.

Preparation borrows one scalar root. Output reuses the admitted definition-image traversal, borrows payload bytes, emits at most 16 KiB per call and checks cancellation between at most 4 KiB fragments. External retained constants must already expose their captured resident value; image output never invokes their read callback. Input admits native strings, vectors, boxes, value payloads and explicit parent frames before construction. Its `CapturedScalar` owner retains those leases and owns retirement, including malformed input, denied admission, cancellation and input unwinding. Native IR retirement moves a suspended-parent link into its existing first-child slot, removes each remaining sibling once and empties all child slots before ordinary field destruction. It needs no new allocation and constant scalar call stack; the partial decoder frames use the same owner operation.

For the carrier of finite captured scalars with valid retained collations, define observation by constructor, ordered children, original field values and native value representation, excluding transient source positions. Distinct constructor tags and independent option tags separate alternatives; length-prefixed sequences and fixed-width scalar fields uniquely delimit the immediate record. The existing type, binding, descriptor and Core value inverses reconstruct the same immediate metadata. Induction on the scalar tree then gives `decode(encode(s)) = s` under this observation, including unchanged query addresses. Retained literals reconstruct a fresh immutable resource with identical value bytes and metadata; no evaluation participates in the correspondence. Consequently every enclosing operation that consumes these fields observes the same scalar input once its original query arena is restored. During decoding, each completed child moves from one admitted frame into exactly one parent slot; on failure, every remaining frame owns disjoint subtrees. The retirement link invariant preserves all unvisited children and visits each node once before releasing its lease. This establishes complete cleanup without a copied tree or depth-dependent native stack. Complete query/command images, stored view/routine/rule integration and public Engine restore remain separate unfinished requirements.

## Published analyzer configuration images

Analysis owns each unresolved analyzer definition and its independent encoded configuration image. Publication serializes the original settings once, without stage validation, compilation, resource resolution or file access. The immutable definition preserves the original configuration for ordinary execution, including deferred invalid regular expressions and reloadable source paths. An encoding error remains attached to that generation and is returned only if an image is requested; it does not invalidate the configuration or change ordinary execution admission. Replacing a definition publishes a new generation before the catalog cell makes it visible.

Let A be the original analyzer configuration, E(A) its existing JSON encoding when defined, and R its encoded vector capacity plus its shared native payload header. The published image owns exactly E(A) separately from A and from compiled resource owners. A successful independent capture reserves R before cloning the image handle. Thus every emitted byte is the existing configuration encoding, and every future configuration observation and deferred execution uses the same A as before. Capture performs a constant number of handle operations and one reservation, with no configuration traversal or encoding. Source replacement, transaction rollback and Engine retirement cannot modify the selected image; the image does not keep the original configuration or compiled resources alive. Encoding failures remain the original cached errors instead of being retried during preservation.

Engine registers this Analysis-owned image producer at the existing table source boundary. Original transaction snapshots retain the complete definition generation, and committed selection excludes private replacements. The common captured-value owner retains independent memory and cancellation through its existing lifetime. Table-properties images continue to contain the same configuration byte field; compiled field/default resources retain their separate image and restoration contracts. Statistics maintenance is already fixed-size inline state with allocation-free capture; no additional preservation mechanism is required for that record. Whole-Engine table assembly, logical closure and public restoration remain unfinished.

### Original configuration input

`AnalyzerDefinition::from_image` accepts the admitted configuration buffer from the table-properties reader. Analysis reconstructs its native stage values through Core's bounded JSON events, retaining the original byte buffer unchanged. It reads the publication schema rather than the resolved compiled descriptor: optional and empty settings, language profiles, filter order, invalid deferred parameters and reloadable file paths keep their original meaning. Feature-selected stages require their original language feature. Reconstruction never opens a path, resolves a dictionary, expands defaults, validates an executable stage or compiles a pipeline.

Let D decode this publication schema and let C be the set of configurations representable by the enabled native variants for which E is defined. The scalar rules recover the same boolean, integer or decoded string; each enum rule selects the original tag and recursively recovers its fields. Sequence rules preserve order and multiplicity, optional rules preserve omission versus presence, and map rules recover the producer's strictly ordered unique keys and corresponding values. Structural induction over these finite native values therefore gives D(E(A)) = A for every A in C. The original bytes are adopted without re-encoding, so independent image capture also remains E(A). These identities preserve configuration observations and every subsequent ordinary compilation or analysis composition, including its deferred errors and resource access. They do not identify unresolved configurations with resolved descriptors or replace the separate resource closure required by compiled analyzer images.

Input bytes keep their original allowance throughout reconstruction. Each decoded string, vector capacity and native map entry is admitted before allocation or publication; map node slack and allocator/reference-count bookkeeping follow Core's existing native map contract and are not claimed as exact allocator measurements. The completed configuration and encoded bytes become separate immutable shared owners, each retaining its corresponding payload and shared-header reservations. Cloned definitions retain both owners; captured images retain only the encoded owner. Partial construction drops native values before their aggregate reservations, and cancelled or underfunded construction cannot publish a definition. Final owner retirement releases exactly its retained leases, including when another thread holds the last reference. This changes ownership representation without introducing a query or ranking operator.

## Immutable statistics capture

Planner owns the immutable column-statistics generation and its cached retained footprint. Ordinary construction counts the native map header, live key/value layouts, string and vector capacities, and Core's existing native-value payloads. The standard B-tree's opaque node slack and allocator/reference-count bookkeeping remain outside this charge, matching the native map retention contract. Admitted generations additionally expose complete owned tree-node layouts and retain their original producer leases. Construction uses the invoking producer's traversal scratch and cancellation checks; independent capture reserves the cached footprint before cloning the immutable handle. Capturing never visits columns, histogram boundaries, common values or frequency buffers.

Let C map column names to their complete native distributions and let R(C) be the exposed retained layout defined above. Owner construction establishes R(C) before controlled publication; ordinary construction preserves a resource error if that count cannot be represented or its traversal allocation fails. Subsequent captures return that error instead of inventing a count or rescanning the population. An unshared ordinary replacement subtracts the old column payload and adds the new payload and any new inline entry; shared mutation first detaches the native map and recounts the resulting capacities. Thus each published generation keeps its corresponding R, including replacements that compact cloned buffers.

A successful capture of C reserves R(C) and shares C itself. Every lookup, histogram boundary, common value, frequency bit pattern and resulting selectivity therefore observes exactly the original object, so all existing statistics observations and planner compositions are preserved. A later mutation of a shared map publishes a distinct C' through native copy-on-write; it cannot change C or its charge. Admitted and image-decoded generations preserve the same argument because their public interface exposes no mutable payload. Capture performs a fixed number of handle operations and one reservation, independently of the population size. Engine registers the Planner capture interface at the existing committed-source boundary and preserves its original cancellation scope. Other table-metadata preservation, complete memory inventory and Engine restoration remain separate requirements.

## Native column statistics and maintenance images

Planner owns `ColumnStatistics::image_reader` and admitted `read_image` reconstruction. `UQACST01` is followed by the little-endian `u64` column count. Each strictly increasing, nonempty UTF-8 column name is length-prefixed with `u64`. Its 50-byte header contains distinct, null and row counts (`u64` each), minimum/maximum presence bytes (0 or 1), and histogram, most-common-value and frequency counts (`u64` each). Present extrema, histogram entries and most-common values follow as complete Core value images in their original order; frequency entries follow as raw little-endian IEEE-754 `f64` bits. No sorting, normalization, selectivity calculation or statistical consistency repair occurs. Core's native value format retains its existing nesting and controlled numerical-formatting limits; the enclosing relation restore checks column membership.

Preparation borrows the immutable generation and does not enumerate its columns. A fixed output cursor admits one active Core value reader and its numerical scratch, returns at most 16 KiB per read and checks cancellation between at most 4 KiB output fragments. Failed or unwinding readers/writers release that traversal and remain terminal. Input admits each string, vector, value and map node before construction, transfers complete payload leases through Planner's existing native builder, and returns the ordinary immutable `ColumnStatistics` carrier. Shared clones retain the decoded allowance until their last owner retires. A single pending column permits ordered-name checks without another copied name or corpus; native map comparisons retain the existing builder contract.

Storage owns the 88-byte `UQASMT01` maintenance image. The eight-byte header and little-endian `u64` count of one precede: optional 16-byte table incarnation; generation, pending changes and dirty-since clock (`u64` each); optional analyzed row count (`u64`); statistics format (`u32`); optional sampled commit (`u64`); and optional analysis generation (`u64`). Each optional field occupies one Boolean presence byte followed by its fixed payload; absent payload bytes must be zero. This retains absent versus present-zero legacy fields. Output and input use fixed inline storage and perform no payload allocation. Counters and clocks are copied without applying new changes or re-evaluating analysis eligibility. The enclosing relation restore validates the object identity and selected statistics format.

Let a statistics state be the ordered map of original names to the tuple `(distinct, null, rows, minimum, maximum, histogram, common, frequencies)`, with Values observed through their native image representation and frequencies through their bit representation. Let a maintenance state be its eight original fields, including independent option tags. Framed names and counts, fixed-width integer encodings, the Core value inverse, fixed vector order and raw frequency bits are injective on these carriers; the maintenance option tags distinguish absence from every present value. Fieldwise reconstruction therefore gives `decode(encode(S)) = S` on both carriers. Planner selectivity and maintenance decisions receive the same ordered inputs and preserved state, so their existing computations are unchanged. No new query operator, approximation, mutation or state transition is introduced. Allocation admission and private construction affect only whether reconstruction succeeds, and failure exposes no partial generation. The enclosing Engine capture and restore publication supply the existing transaction boundary; these codecs alone do not complete memory backup or restore.

## Native table authority images

SQL's captured table authority exposes bounded `image_reader` output and admitted `read_image` reconstruction. The `UQATSA01` header has a little-endian `u64` count of one. Fields retain the owner role's `i64` OID and 16-byte incarnation; optional relation ACL revision; column-ACL and column-revision counts; and optional relation ACL. Relation ACL entries precede ordered column ACL records, followed by ordered column revisions. Column ACL records contain the framed original name and the existing counted table-privilege ACL encoding; column revisions contain name and original nonzero 16-byte revision. Public grantees, explicit role incarnations, grantors, every privilege and grant-option bit, and absent versus present-empty relation ACLs remain distinct. The decoder enforces native role identities, Boolean framing, nonzero revisions and strictly increasing independent column vectors; the enclosing restore validates columns and the accompanying role catalog.

Let `A` be captured authority with valid native identities and ordered column vectors. Fixed-width identity encoding, framed names, independent presence bits, counted vectors and the existing ACL bit encoding are injective on their declared carriers. Decoding each field and vector position therefore gives `decode(encode(A)) = A`, including both the privilege paths consulted by authorization and the revisions consulted by concurrency. Substituting this reconstructed authority into those existing observations preserves their result; no grant, revoke, role lookup or query operator is executed by either codec. Preparation borrows the root in constant time. The fixed cursor emits at most 16 KiB per read and checks cancellation between at most 4 KiB copies without allocating payloads. Input admits names and vectors before construction and transfers every lease into the returned private owner; malformed data, I/O failure, cancellation and unwinding retire partial ownership. Output failure remains terminal. Complete Engine restore publication is separate work.

## Native table constraints and hierarchy images

SQL extends the shared bounded definition reader with ordered native table CHECKs (`UQATCK01`), keys (`UQATKY01`), foreign keys (`UQAFKY01`), one hierarchy (`UQATHR01`) and dropped physical attributes (`UQADAT01`). Each header is followed by a little-endian `u64` count; the hierarchy count must be one. Paired image readers and admitted input functions preserve native declaration order and absence, without resolving relations, creating indexes, evaluating constraints or publishing state. Cross-catalog identity uniqueness, live/dropped slot layout and partition compatibility remain with the enclosing relation restore owner.

CHECK records contain optional name, incarnation and `i64` OID; local, enforced, validated, NO INHERIT and detached-bound presence flags; the native scalar; and any detached partition specification and bound. Key records contain optional constraint and index identities, optional name, primary/unique tag zero/one, NULLS NOT DISTINCT and WITHOUT OVERLAPS flags, key/included-column counts and the corresponding ordered names. Foreign-key records contain optional referenced index, referenced key, name, enforcement incarnation and independent catalog identity; referenced table; the existing update/delete/match tags and five flags; counts for local, referenced, SET-action and referenced-partition vectors; then those vectors in order. Referenced-partition records share the existing column-reference encoding, preserving parent relationships and independent validation state.

A hierarchy header contains parent and parent-sequence counts, specification/bound presence, then local-column, identity-override, inherited-key and inherited-foreign-key counts. Payloads follow in that order. Specifications encode list/range/hash as zero/one/two, a key count and native scalars.
