Bounds encode default/list/range/hash as zero through three: lists contain a count and scalars, ranges contain lower/upper counts and ordered endpoints, and hashes contain original `i32` modulus/remainder. Range endpoints use minimum/value/maximum tags zero/one/two, with a native scalar only for value. Identity overrides retain their column and optional original sequence provenance. Inherited keys and foreign keys share their standalone record encodings. Dropped attributes retain `i16` number; `i64` length, modifier and dimension count; by-value flag; alignment/storage strings; `i64` collation; identity string; local flag; and `i64` inheritance count.

For an admitted native component `T`, every fixed-width integer encoding is injective, each enum tag identifies one constructor, and count/presence framing uniquely separates ordered and optional fields. Scalar and sequence fields reuse their existing inverses. Induction over each vector and scalar tree therefore gives `decode(encode(T)) = T`; fieldwise substitution into the original relation operations preserves their observations, including duplicate included columns, inherited provenance and bound identities. No new query operation or optimizer rewrite is introduced. Preparation borrows only the root, continuations visit each native field once, output is bounded to 16 KiB per read with checks between 4 KiB copies, and input admits every vector and payload before construction. Native scalar retirement remains constant-stack. Failed input retires all unpublished ownership; failed output remains terminal. Complete memory assembly and Engine restore acceptance are separate requirements.

## Native column definition images

SQL's `columns_image_reader` borrows an ordered slice of native `ColumnDef` values; `read_columns_image` reconstructs the same ordered vector under one retained memory allowance. The image starts with `UQACOL01` and a little-endian `u64` column count. Text uses a little-endian `u64` byte count followed by exact UTF-8 bytes; Boolean and presence tags are zero or one. Column order and optional legacy metadata remain unchanged. The component does not validate an entire relation, resolve types or routines, evaluate expressions, create a sequence or publish an Engine.

Each column starts with its name, optional `u32` collation, optional 16-byte incarnation and optional `i16` physical attribute number. Seventeen Boolean bytes retain, in order, primary-key, NOT NULL, explicit NOT NULL, validated NOT NULL, NO INHERIT NOT NULL, local NOT NULL, unique, enforced CHECK, validated CHECK, NO INHERIT CHECK, local CHECK, and presence of missing value, sequence provenance, default, generated declaration, CHECK expression and foreign reference. The existing native column-type encoding follows. Constraint metadata then contains optional NOT NULL name and catalog identity, optional CHECK name and incarnation, optional CHECK `i64` OID and optional default `i64` OID. A present catalog identity contains its original 16-byte incarnation and `i64` OID. Original absence remains distinct from an empty name or explicit zero collation.

Present payloads follow in the same order as their six presence flags. Missing values use Core's complete evaluated-value image. Sequence provenance records serial, identity-always, identity-by-default and legacy tags as zero through three, optional sequence name, optional table/column owner and optional original identity declaration. An identity declaration retains optional schema/name, optional persistence, every written sequence option, optional ownership-name vector and deferred diagnostic fields without interpreting them. Sequence options distinguish absence, an explicit absent value, an `i64` integer and original numeric text. Defaults and CHECKs use native scalar nodes; generated declarations retain virtual/stored tags zero/one, dependency count, original scalar expression and complete `UQAFNB01` dependency images. A foreign reference retains selected index, key/name, enforcement and independent catalog identities, referenced table/column, update/delete actions, match mode, all five enforcement/validation/deferral/period flags and ordered referenced-partition constraints. Action tags are no-action, restrict, cascade, set-null and set-default as zero through four; match tags are simple/full as zero/one.

Let `C` be an ordered native column vector with representable nonzero catalog identities, and let `E` and `D` be emission and successful reconstruction. The count preserves vector length and position. Explicit finite tags are injective, fixed-width integer encodings preserve exact bits and framed strings preserve every byte. Independent presence tags preserve both absent and present-empty states. The existing type, value, scalar and function-binding inverse arguments apply to each nested payload; original sequence text and deferred diagnostics are copied rather than interpreted. Fieldwise composition therefore gives `D(E(C)) = C`, including bound function incarnations, physical attribute positions, missing values and constraint provenance. Any later consumer receives the same fields and native scalar operations; the codec introduces no SQL operation or optimizer rewrite.

Preparation borrows only the column slice. Admitted continuations visit each column, string, dependency and partition record once, with at most 16 KiB emitted per call and cancellation checks between at most 4 KiB copies. Scratch depends on active metadata/scalar depth, not the byte size of a column name or expression literal. Input reserves native vectors, strings, boxes and scalar payloads before allocation and transfers the accumulated reservation with the reconstructed vector. Native scalar retirement remains constant-stack on failed partial construction as well as successful retirement. Errors, cancellation and unwinding release all private construction; output failure is terminal. Table-level constraints, hierarchy, remaining metadata, complete memory images and restore activation retain their own implementation and validation requirements.

## Native trigger catalog images

SQL's `CapturedEventCatalog<StoredTrigger>::image_reader` borrows the original durable relation and declaration roots; `TriggerCatalog::read_image` reconstructs an unpublished native catalog. `UQATRG01` and a little-endian `u64` relation count precede ordered relations. Each relation contains length-prefixed schema and local name, a declaration count and ordered trigger entries. Empty relation membership remains representable. Temporary roots are excluded by capture and rejected on input.

Each entry contains its key, optional 16-byte function incarnation, enabled tag, optional 16-byte trigger incarnation, optional constraint name, optional trigger `i64` catalog OID and optional constraint `i64` catalog OID. Enabled tags are 0 for origin, 1 for disabled, 2 for replica and 3 for always. Each optional field has its own Boolean presence tag; original legacy absence is preserved. Trigger and constraint OID uniqueness are checked independently because they address different catalogs; repeated function incarnations are valid references rather than duplicate declarations.

The declaration contains its original name, table and function strings, constraint flag, optional referenced relation, deferrability tag, row flag, timing tag, replacement flag, WHEN presence and four `u64` counts for arguments, events, UPDATE columns and transition relations. Deferrability tags are 0 for not deferrable, 1 for initially immediate and 2 for initially deferred; timing tags are 0 for before, 1 for after and 2 for instead of. Ordered argument strings precede event tags, UPDATE-column strings and transition entries. Event tags are 0 for insert, 1 for update, 2 for delete and 3 for truncate. Each transition entry preserves its name, new/old flag and table/row flag. A present WHEN condition is one complete `UQAEXP01` scalar image with count one. This retains exact native function bindings and evaluated literals without executing functions or reconstructing SQL.

Let a captured trigger catalog be the finite ordered map from durable relation identities to ordered maps of trigger names and native declarations. For a well-formed catalog, the field encodings are injective: text lengths preserve original bytes, fixed-width integers preserve their values, independent presence tags distinguish absent metadata and explicit enum tags have unique inverses. Sequence counts preserve both empty membership and declaration order. The stored scalar inverse applies to each WHEN condition. Induction first over fields, then declaration entries and finally relation entries gives `decode(encode(catalog)) = catalog` under native-field observation. In particular, the result preserves selected function incarnations, enabled state, deferral, transition names, argument values and bound conditions. The codec introduces no SQL operation, name resolution or optimizer rewrite. Definition-key and owning-relation checks reject contradictory membership; semantic validation against the relation/routine/type catalogs remains with the enclosing restore owner before activation.

Reader preparation retains only borrowed iterators and fixed field descriptors. Output visits each field once, emits at most 16 KiB per call and checks cancellation at the existing 4 KiB copy boundary. Strings and declaration sequences never become an encoded in-memory corpus. A WHEN reader reserves its own box and scalar continuation before construction; normal completion, error, cancellation and unwinding retire this scratch, and terminal readers cannot resume. Input reserves variable payloads, list storage, native map nodes and duplicate-identity scratch before allocation. Admitted insertion transfers the original payload reservations into the native event and relation trees without copying declarations; captures and clones retain those trees. Thus unsuccessful input has no published state, successful component input remains private, and source mutation or retirement cannot change a captured image. Complete Engine memory assembly and public restore activation remain separate requirements.

## Native function binding images

SQL's `FunctionBinding::image_reader` borrows the selected native function contract, and `read_image` reconstructs it under the supplied memory budget before returning one unpublished binding. Neither operation resolves a name, invokes a callback, evaluates an argument nor raises a retained overload-resolution failure. These images supply stored-expression reconstruction; domain, view, routine, trigger and rewrite-rule image assembly remains separate work.

The header is the eight bytes `UQAFNB01` followed by a little-endian `u64` count of one. The shared binding prefix contains optional 16-byte object identity, exact name, builtin flag, optional input collation, ordered argument declarations, optional invocation metadata and optional typed value metadata. Text has a little-endian `u64` byte length and exact UTF-8 bytes; Boolean and presence tags are exactly zero or one. Invocation metadata retains all argument positions, target/source declarations, parameter declarations, optional return declaration and variadic mode. Value metadata retains composite-field identity, native result/changed types and collation, or the ordered assignment path, slice masks and value-argument position. The existing foreign-callback prefix uses the same field codec and continues to omit the standalone extensions below, preserving `UQAFWR01` bytes.

The prefix is followed by an optional dispatch and a deferred-error tag. Dispatch presence precedes an explicit operation tag from zero through 44 and its typed payload. The [format tag tables](../../crates/uqa-sql/src/catalog/image/function_dispatch.rs) define every native operation independently of Rust enum layout. Numeric, pattern, Boolean, range, subtype and enum operations have their own explicit tags; identities use little-endian `u32`, optional identities retain their presence, and slice masks retain both original bytes. A present pattern OID must be nonzero because its native carrier is `NonZeroU32`; other carriers retain zero where representable. Error tags are zero for absence, one for an undefined-function signature, and two for the original operator SQLSTATE and message. Import rejects unknown tags, invalid Boolean values, truncation and trailing bytes.

For preservation, let `B` be a native binding whose fields are representable by their declared carriers, and let `E` and `D` be image emission and successful admitted reconstruction. Fixed-width integers and object identities are encoded injectively; length-delimited text preserves its exact bytes; presence tags distinguish absence from every present value, including empty text and zero identifiers. Explicit finite operation tables are bijections onto their admitted tags. The shared invocation/value codec preserves ordered vectors, optional boxes and native column-type trees. Composition of these disjointly framed field inverses gives `D(E(B)) = B`, including original names, identities, argument positions, variadic mode, selected dispatch, field/assignment metadata, collation and deferred errors. Hence any existing consumer observing those fields or later executing their selected operation observes the same binding; this codec introduces no new query operator, evaluation order or optimizer rewrite.

Export construction stores borrowed continuations without enumerating vectors or allocating output. Each read emits at most 16 KiB, with cancellation checks within the existing 4 KiB copy boundary. Native field, invocation and assignment positions resume without rescanning preceding entries; the foreign-callback path skips the two standalone extensions exactly. Import admits every payload and optional box before allocation and returns the aggregate reservation with the binding. Decode failure, cancellation and unwinding retire partial ownership; successful reconstruction remains private until the enclosing restore validates catalog closure. Thus partial output cannot publish an Engine or alter source state, and the existing terminal image-reader lifecycle applies unchanged.

## Native scalar expression retirement

SQL's native `Expr` owner retires its scalar child graph without recursive descent or new traversal allocation. This includes scalar children in function calls, row constructors, comparisons, CASE and window clauses. Query bodies retain their query ownership. Each suspended parent temporarily lends its first scalar slot to the preceding parent; the continuation removes remaining siblings from existing vectors or boxes before restoring that link. Native AST-to-IR projection transfers original vectors, names, declarations and evaluated values, and shares an immutable retained-literal handle. No replacement box or payload copy is needed for this transfer. Rust consumers that inspect an `Expr` variant borrow its fields because the native expression now implements `Drop`.

For preservation, consider the finite owned scalar forest reachable when retirement begins. Every original allocation has one owning edge, except immutable shared resources governed by their existing reference counts. At each iteration, the current subtree and the suspended-parent chain partition the unretired forest. Replacing a parent's first child with the preceding parent moves that single edge without cloning or losing it; removing a remaining sibling moves its sole edge into the current subtree. Restoring the preceding-parent link occurs only after the other scalar slots are empty. The retired parent therefore cannot recursively destroy another scalar subtree. Induction on these transitions shows that every original scalar node and payload retires exactly once, with no change to live values or scalar evaluation. Each node is suspended once and every sibling is removed once, so structural work is linear in nodes and edges. All continuation storage belongs to the original forest, and call-stack usage does not depend on scalar depth. If a retained resource unwinds during destruction, the still-owned pending forest remains acyclic and its ordinary `Expr` owner applies the same argument. Input errors and cancellation consequently need no caller-side cleanup traversal or extra memory admission to release partial native scalar trees.

## Native domain and stored scalar image output

SQL's `DefinitionImageReader` emits captured domain declarations and scalar syntax without reconstructing SQL text or resolving names. `CapturedTypeDefinitions<StoredDomain>::image_reader` emits `UQADOM01` followed by a little-endian `u64` durable-entry count; `Expr::image_reader` emits `UQAEXP01` and a count of one. The native input contract below reconstructs these component images. Logical cross-catalog closure and Engine activation remain unfinished; a component image is not a complete Engine backup or a public restore operation.

Each domain entry contains its original key, 16-byte object incarnation, `u32` OID, optional array OID, schema/name, bound owner identity, optional array name and optional ordered usage ACL. The declaration then contains its written name, optional collation name/OID, default and NOT NULL presence, CHECK count and native base type. NOT NULL metadata precedes the optional default expression, followed by CHECK entries in original order. Constraint names and catalog identities retain independent presence; identities contain their original 16-byte incarnation and `i64` address. Each CHECK retains its validation flag and expression. Temporary definitions are absent from the captured durable root before image preparation.

Scalar tags zero through 23 identify, respectively, star, qualified star, DEFAULT, column, qualified column, internal column, literal, typed literal, parameter, function, array, row, bound composite row, binary operation, unary minus, NOT, AND, OR, null test, BETWEEN, IN list, CASE, cast and retained literal. Every child sequence is length-delimited and keeps its original order. Function headers retain binding presence, DISTINCT, written call syntax, argument/order counts and filter presence; children follow as the optional `UQAFNB01` binding, arguments, ordered expressions and optional filter. Boolean, numeric and optional-field encodings reuse the catalog format. Query and window nodes violate the existing scalar-definition capture boundary and fail output instead of being approximated.

Literal payloads use Core's native `UQAVAL01` format, including original floating-point bits and retained datum representation. Typed literals preserve their declaration, selected collation and optional original composite source. Composite sources retain original value bytes and ordered descriptors, including live names/types/collations/attribute numbers and dropped-slot number/length/alignment. Bound row constructors retain written type, original attribute positions, optional physical layout and optional argument declarations. A retained literal preserves its captured resident value, native type, declaration and collation separately; export does not materialize an external source. Reference-name parser positions remain transient, as in the established persistent expression contract.

Preparation borrows one root or expression without scanning it or allocating traversal state. Reads retain admitted continuations and child encoders and emit at most 16 KiB. Ordinary field copying checks cancellation at most every 4 KiB; native value encoding retains its existing formatting and cancellation contract. A complete read releases traversal admission, while failed or unwound reads release all child encoders and become terminal. Input owners remain borrowed and unchanged. Catalog mutation and source closure after capture do not alter the captured generation's fields or image.

For preservation, define the persistent projection `P` to retain every native declaration field and evaluated literal carrier while omitting transient parser locations and runtime handle identity. Domain keys and entries use their captured order; expression children use their original structural order. Fixed-width fields, presence bits, text byte lengths, child counts and disjoint syntax tags make each emitted boundary unambiguous. Their field encodings are injective; function/type/value components retain their existing native encoding contracts. Induction on a finite scalar tree therefore shows that its image uniquely identifies `P(expression)`, and induction on domain membership and CHECK order extends this property to the captured domain root. No callback, function lookup, constraint evaluation or source mutation occurs during emission, so existing SQL operators and state transitions are unaffected. This argument covers the producer; executable native reconstruction and restore acceptance remain separate required evidence.

## Native domain and stored scalar image input

`Expr::read_image` decodes one complete `UQAEXP01` image into an admitted native expression. `TypeDefinitions<StoredDomain>::read_image` decodes `UQADOM01` into a private catalog, preserving original names, authority, array metadata and declaration order. Both reject wrong headers, tags, booleans, truncation and trailing data. Domain input also rejects unordered or repeated keys and repeated type or constraint identities. Original function bindings, evaluated values, composite descriptors and retained-literal metadata use their existing native codecs; input never resolves a name, invokes a callback, runs type input or evaluates a constraint. Schema/role existence, original domain semantic predicates, stored-expression dependencies and complete cross-catalog closure remain the enclosing logical restore owner's responsibility before activation.

Scalar reconstruction uses an admitted stack of unfinished native parents. Each header reserves its boxes, vectors and field payloads before allocation; the next child fills exactly one original destination. Function order flags are read immediately before their corresponding child. Domain defaults and CHECKs reuse the same scalar constructor without nested standalone headers. Native expressions and catalogs own their ordinary payload reservations, while shared retained literals retain independent value and metadata leases through later clones. Every reader error, cancellation or unwind drops the partial private state through the native owners. Scalar depth affects admitted traversal storage, not call-stack depth; value containers retain Core's existing depth and representation contract. This synchronous component decoder does not establish scheduled whole-Engine restore acceptance.

Let `P` be the persistent projection defined above, `E` its image encoder and `D` successful native decoding. For a leaf, its disjoint tag selects the original constructor and each field decoder is the inverse of the corresponding fixed-width, optional, text, type, binding or evaluated-value encoding. For a node with ordered children, assume `D(E(c_i)) = P(c_i)` for each child. The parent header fixes the constructor, field metadata, child count and destination sequence. The input cursor installs each decoded child in that sequence and advances once, so the completed parent equals its persistent projection. Structural induction proves `D(E(t)) = P(t)` for every finite admissible scalar tree. Applying the same argument to the ordered domain entries, defaults and CHECKs proves the corresponding catalog identity. Framing rejects a missing or additional field, and no partial result is published. Existing scalar observations therefore receive identical persistent syntax, selected dispatch and evaluated carriers; this introduces no algebraic operator or optimizer rewrite. Runtime allocation identity and transient parser positions remain outside `P`, as in the established stored-definition contract.

## Resumable domain catalog validation

SQL's `catalog::domain::validation::begin_validation` validates an immutable native domain view against the original role view. `Definitions` mode validates supplied legacy metadata without allocating missing identities; `Complete` mode subsequently requires every durable constraint name and identity. The existing synchronous domain restoration functions drain this same continuation. Ordinary declaration and restoration checks also share the constraint validator. Neither path executes domain expressions, resolves a role by its current name, migrates a catalog nor publishes an Engine.

Construction borrows both views without enumerating their entries or allocating scratch. Each advance checks at most one domain header, one role identity or one constraint. Header work is proportional to the selected retained names; ordered scratch operations are logarithmic in their current membership. Role-incarnation indexing visits one entry per step and stops as soon as the selected owner is present. Later owners reuse the visited prefix and continue the same iterator only when their identity is still absent. An empty domain catalog never scans roles. Every map node and rendered canonical name is admitted through the original `ProductionControl`; source and invoking cancellation remain independent. Failure or unwinding retires active scratch before returning control, and subsequent advances reject the retired continuation. Completion releases scratch and remains observable without repeating work. These checks are one logical-restore component; full catalog closure, artifacts and Engine activation remain separate requirements.

For preservation, let `D` be the original ordered domain map and `R` the immutable role definitions. For every visited role prefix `R_i`, the retained index is exactly `{r.identity() | r in R_i}`: initially it is empty, and each admitted insertion adds precisely the next original identity. An owner already in that set succeeds without another visit. Otherwise traversal resumes until its original identity is found or `R` is exhausted; exhaustion is the only missing-owner result. Thus membership of a valid owner is equivalent to the previous exact-incarnation search, including renamed roles, reused names and repeated equivalent references. Every original role is visited at most once, and no unneeded suffix is traversed. No name or OID-only substitution is possible.

For every processed domain prefix, the global object/OID sets contain exactly the identities accepted by the original validation loop. The current domain's local sets contain exactly the constraint names, object identities and OIDs preceding its current position. Each step applies the same predicates and short-circuit ordering, then inserts the same values; induction on the visited prefix therefore preserves both successful membership and the first invalid supplied field. Local constraint validation completes before that domain's global constraint-identity checks. Every domain completes those checks before `Complete` mode checks missing legacy fields, preserving the previous validation order. Pausing between steps changes no input or observable catalog. Both synchronous and scheduled consumers therefore accept the same immutable catalogs and retain the same metadata diagnostics; controlled consumers additionally report memory or cancellation failure without publishing a partial result. This changes no SQL carrier, operator, expression evaluation or optimizer rewrite.

## Native sequence images

Execution's sequence owner emits two images from one `CapturedSequences` generation: record metadata through `image_reader` and SQL-owned authority through `authority_image_reader`. The record iterator first selects the autonomous value by the original object and definition-generation pair. Neither image opens a live sequence, allocates a value, restores a session cache or resolves a role name. Both images are required by `SequenceCatalog::read_images`; final database-wide role, owner-column and OID closure remains the enclosing restore's responsibility.

The metadata header is the eight bytes `UQASEQ01` and a little-endian `u64` record count. Entries are strictly ordered by the original decoded `(schema, name)` pair. Each name is a `u64` byte count and exact UTF-8 bytes. The names precede a fixed 130-byte record with the following fields, in order. All integers use little-endian encoding.

| Field | Encoding |
| --- | --- |
| Object incarnation | 16 bytes |
| Recorded catalog OID | Boolean presence tag followed by `u32`; an absent value carries zero |
| Persistence and data type | One byte each: permanent/unlogged are 0/1; smallint/integer/bigint are 0/1/2 |
| Called and cycle | One Boolean byte each |
| Owning column dependency | One byte: absent/automatic/internal are 0/1/2, followed by the 16-byte table and column incarnations; absent ownership carries two zero identities |
| Definition generation | 16 bytes |
| Allocation and definition numbers | Seven `i64` values: start, increment, current, log count, minimum, maximum and cache size |

The companion authority header is `UQASQA01` and a `u64` count. Each ordered entry contains the same two length-prefixed names, the persistence byte, the bound owner's `i64` OID and 16-byte incarnation, and an optional ACL. An ACL has a Boolean presence tag and, when present, a `u64` entry count. Each entry encodes an optional grantee identity, a required grantor identity, a one-byte privileges mask and a one-byte grant-options mask, in that order; SELECT, UPDATE and USAGE occupy bits 0, 1 and 2 in each mask. `None`, an empty ACL, PUBLIC and an explicit role incarnation remain distinct. SQL's catalog image reader and admitted authority decoder own this representation.

Both output cursors have fixed state, perform no payload allocation and emit at most 16 KiB per read, checking cancellation between fields and at most 4 KiB between payload copies. Their borrowed source retains the original capture allowance. Import admits name bytes, ACL entries, persistent native nodes and OID-validation scratch before allocation. Validated names and metadata move directly into native sequence roots, retaining their original allowance; there is no uncharged copy of the names. Failed input, admission or cancellation retires the unpublished candidate. A failed output cursor is terminal, including writer unwinding.

Input rejects unknown headers or tags, truncation, trailing bytes, invalid names, temporary objects, zero required incarnations, repeated names or object identities, duplicate effective OIDs and mismatched authority membership or persistence. Recorded OIDs retain their presence; legacy absent OIDs keep the existing identity-derived interpretation. Definition validation reuses SQL's sequence definition owner, while nonnegative log counts and nonzero allocation generations retain the existing restoration contract. Legacy current positions outside present definition bounds remain unchanged, as they do through ordinary catalog restoration. Image input never repairs a sequence position or executes `setval`.

### Sequence image preservation argument

Let a captured sequence catalog be the ordered finite relation map `M`, its complete authority map `A`, and its selected immutable autonomous-value map `V`. Existing capture selects `V[(object, definition)]` only for the matching pair. Define `P(M,V)` to replace only a matching record's current, called and log-count fields; every other metadata field is unchanged. The metadata image enumerates exactly `P(M,V)`, and the companion image enumerates exactly `A` from the same capture generation. Temporary objects are absent from both prepublished durable roots.

For each admitted native record, fixed-width integer and byte-array encodings have exact inverses. Explicit tags distinguish every type, persistence, Boolean and optional value; length framing preserves decoded names, including embedded dots and quotes. Thus metadata decoding returns the same relation key and all fields of `P(M,V)`. The authority encoder preserves each original role incarnation and ordered ACL entry; its three-bit masks invert the three independent privilege Booleans, and its presence tags preserve PUBLIC, absent ACL and empty ACL distinctions. Therefore authority decoding returns `A`.

Strict ordering and equal counts, together with a matching authority lookup for every unique metadata key and matching persistence, prove that neither image has an unmatched relation. Unique incarnation and effective-OID checks preserve the native catalog's identity maps. Native insertion transfers the admitted names and record payloads without evaluating expressions, resolving roles or publishing the candidate. Consequently decoding the pair yields exactly `(P(M,V), A)` under the same surrounding catalog and runtime. Existing sequence allocation receives the identical definition and durable reservation position; unused session-local cached values are not revived. This introduces no new algebraic operator or optimizer rewrite. Full database restoration must additionally validate the referenced owner columns and roles and activate the selected fresh database history.

## Ownership and compatibility

Common Storage interprets archive structure and treats provider image bytes as opaque. The existing runtime dependency directions remain unchanged: Storage depends on Core and Analysis; no Engine, SQL, Execution or concrete provider dependency is introduced. The implementation uses the existing SHA-256, XChaCha20-Poly1305, random-source and Core memory interfaces without modifying external dependencies. No Cargo feature advertises a provider image implementation.

An image format is a provider tag and nonzero provider-owned version. Tags 1 through 4 identify memory, native SQLite, SQLite KV and redb respectively. Envelope decoding recognizes those mappings without asserting that an exporter or importer exists. Default provider capabilities are unsupported. Format migration and owner resource compatibility must be validated by the eventual importer; accepting an envelope is insufficient.

`BackupCut` carries the source incarnation, provider image format, optional committed record sequence and an opaque snapshot identity covering the jointly retained committed data and autonomous metadata. The snapshot owner must establish that joint cut; allocating a random descriptor does not create consistency. A root rejects a zero source incarnation. Repository, backup, snapshot, namespace, object and protection IDs are distinct nonzero 16-byte types. They are never interpreted as filesystem paths.

## Joint record and autonomous metadata snapshots

Common Storage's `CommittedBackupSnapshot` combines a retained record view with an independently typed `MetadataEpoch`, source identity and bounded identifier-watermark visitation. Unsupported persistence adapters return `None`. redb implements the joint boundary through the existing shared `Arc<Database>`. SQLite implements it through managed native and Key/Value record owners, including plain, encrypted, compressed and compressed-encrypted files. Neither retains a physical transaction between visits. Complete provider image encoding/import and the actual memory Engine remain separate deliveries.

redb record format 64 stores an unsigned current metadata epoch `C`, a creation/head-birth pair for each allocator namespace, and historical `(namespace, birth) -> watermark` entries. The format upgrade initializes existing namespaces at epoch zero in the same physical transaction as the format change. Existing record sequences, values, database identity, receipts and watermarks are preserved. Every supported identifier writer then publishes its watermark and metadata history together. Ordinary allocation leaves the record sequence and `C` unchanged.

SQLite record format 68 stores the same epoch, namespace births and watermark history in guarded `_uqa_mvcc_backup_metadata`, `_uqa_mvcc_identifier_epochs` and `_uqa_mvcc_identifier_history` tables. Its atomic upgrade streams existing allocations into epoch zero without collecting them in Rust. Native and Key/Value admission validate the shared record-table inventory; these internal tables do not enter catalog cache generations or native image families. A capture uses the ordinary FULL-synchronized physical writer and advances the existing sequence-durability certificate to the captured record sequence. That commit also covers earlier relaxed WAL observations and autonomous sequence publications without adding a record revision.

Capture takes record admission followed by metadata admission, then one physical writer. That transaction validates the source, reads the committed record sequence and metadata epoch `E = C`, and durably advances `C` to `E + 1`. It retains both logical leases before releasing admission; a failed capture returns no usable cut and retires all partially acquired leases. An unexposed sealed epoch after failed memory admission changes no record or allocation observation. Epoch exhaustion rejects capture without wrapping. A namespace updated in a later epoch archives its old head once and replaces its birth with `C`; repeated updates before the next capture coalesce in that head. No allocation collection is copied during capture.

For a captured epoch `E`, visitation yields the current watermark if its head birth is at most `E`, otherwise the latest historical watermark with birth at most `E`. A creation epoch greater than `E` yields no allocation. Missing required heads/anchors, invalid epoch ordering, an unsealed cut, changed source identity and malformed encodings fail. Each call visits at most its requested number of physical identifier positions in byte order; internal coordination positions and namespaces created after the cut still advance the cursor. The storage-internal callback borrows provider bytes within that read, so it must only encode or copy admitted data and must not write the repository, reenter the provider or invoke application callbacks. Cancellation, a false callback result or an error closes the physical read window.

Collection takes the same admission order and preserves the predecessor at the oldest metadata lease plus all newer revisions; with no metadata lease it discards superseded history. Record cleanup continues to use its independent committed horizon. Every adapter over the same live redb owner shares both registries. SQLite adapters over an in-memory pool share typed local registries; native file owners use distinct record and metadata lease transports. The `.uqa-backup-snapshots` sidecar uses `UQAMET01`, the database incarnation and OS byte locks. A live slot can hold metadata epoch zero; liveness comes from its lock, and process death releases it. Every joint snapshot also owns its original record lease, preserving existing restore exclusion. The common admission order excludes cross-process collection until both leases are installed. Lease destruction releases only its own allowance and does not wait for either admission gate or physical storage. Native database page-cache allocations remain provider-owned; retained snapshot owners use the caller's `StorageReadControl` allowance.

For preservation, let `R_s` be the existing committed record observation at sequence `s`, and let `A_E` be the finite partial map from allocator namespaces to watermarks at capture `E`. Assume the existing provider physical transaction, durable-commit and record-retention guarantees. The joint writer orders capture with every record and identifier publication, hence its returned pair `(R_s, A_E)` belongs to one physical state. Initially each namespace has exactly its current value at birth zero. Inductively, an update in the same unsealed epoch is invisible to all older cuts and changes no archived value; the first update in a later epoch saves the preceding value before atomically replacing it. A namespace created after a cut is excluded by its creation epoch. Therefore the greatest birth not exceeding `E` has exactly the value observed at capture. Missing data is an error, never a substituted current value.

Let `H` be the minimum retained metadata epoch. For any retained `E >= H`, its selected predecessor is either the predecessor selected at `H` or a newer birth. Collection removes only older entries, so it preserves every `A_E`. The record registry separately preserves every `R_s`. Admission excludes capture from collection until both leases exist, and direct source publication cannot violate retention because it preserves replaced heads in the same atomic transaction. Short read windows may observe later physical states, but this predecessor argument gives the same `A_E` and `R_s` on every visit. Concatenating increasing bounded windows therefore enumerates exactly the captured allocation map, even when later namespaces appear between windows. Identifier namespaces are monotone within an incarnation; internal reclamation markers are excluded from the image.

These operations neither evaluate SQL nor change any record carrier, score, graph value, sequence cache or catalog expression. Every existing deterministic observation over the selected record view and allocator map consequently has the same inputs and result; allocator reservations retain their original monotonicity and exhaustion behavior. Failure returns no completed snapshot, and uncertain native synchronization resolves through the existing storage recovery boundary rather than a caller-side state repair. This proves the joint input contract only; complete provider image coverage, import and fresh-history restoration still require their owning implementation and evidence.

## Chunked committed record reads

`CommittedRecordSnapshot::read_value_chunk` copies a caller-selected range and returns the selected revision, complete value length and copied length. Absent records, tombstones and live empty values remain distinct. A destination buffer may be empty; offsets beyond the selected value are rejected before copying. Callers own and account for destination storage, and discard its contents on error. Snapshot wrappers forward the provider method so a bounded implementation cannot accidentally fall back to full-value materialization.

SQLite selects the committed version's physical identity under its existing short read transaction, verifies its payload type and length, and uses the dependency's public [incremental BLOB reader](https://www.sqlite.org/c3ref/blob_read.html). The BLOB handle closes before the method returns. Compacted runs expand their existing at-most-128-byte template. redb borrows the retained version inside one read transaction and copies only the selected range; the reference memory version store retains its existing immutable value. Copying checks cancellation in at-most-16-KiB pieces. Source page caches remain provider-owned; SQLite binding workspace is proportional to the selected key, and no whole-value payload allocation is required. Repository writes occur only after return, when the physical read has closed. This is an image-input primitive; it does not by itself qualify complete provider images or the actual memory Engine.

For preservation, fix a committed key and its retained observation `(v, b)`, where `v` is the selected revision and `b` is either a tombstone or a finite byte string. For live length `L`, offset `o <= L` and destination capacity `c`, the result is exactly `(v, L, b[o..o+n])` with `n = min(c, L-o)`. The subtraction admits the range before any addition, so its end cannot exceed `L` or overflow. An offset beyond `L` is rejected; a tombstone permits only offset zero and keeps an absent length. Empty chunks are the concatenation identity, and concatenation is associative: any adjacent partition from zero through `L` reconstructs exactly `b`, independently of chunk sizes. No decoding, expression evaluation or normalization occurs.

Record retention preserves `(v, b)` across intervening publications and collection. SQLite resolves the version and reads its BLOB inside one physical view, while its run decoder expands the same existing bytes; redb's guard and the memory owner's immutable value provide the corresponding borrowed observation. The physical view ends between calls, but every call resolves the same retained logical version, so concatenation preserves both source bytes and all deterministic observations of them. Reading neither changes publication order nor adds source state. Failure returns no successful range, and cannot produce a complete image. These arguments establish byte preservation and finite per-call work; full-image inventory coverage and restore remain separate obligations.

## Bounded record-image input cursor

Common Storage's `RecordImageCursor` consumes a joint snapshot and emits its allocator watermarks followed by its live MVCC records, preserving byte order within each family. Watermarks use eight big-endian bytes; records retain their existing encoded bytes and exact committed revision. A provider image adapter must still assign qualified namespaces, account for its other state planes, define the image version and implement import. This raw stream alone does not assert complete Engine coverage or a restorable backup.

The retained source supplies physical record and allocator ceilings and bounded position visitors. A ceiling is the greatest current physical key, including possible post-capture keys. Every captured live key remains represented at or below it because the retained cut excludes its reclamation. SQLite merges ordinary heads with compacted-run keys; redb reads its existing head table. Each `advance(n)` visits at most `n` positions, including positions created after capture and tombstones, stopping at the first selected live unit or the ceiling. Empty byte keys are valid positions. Retained boundaries and continuation keys consume the invoking allowance. Later appends beyond the ceiling cannot extend the traversal; the position limit and cancellation bound each call even if writers continue inserting keys below that ceiling. No completion-time bound under indefinitely growing concurrent input is claimed.

After selecting a unit, the cursor requires its complete consumption before advancing. `read_current` copies at most the caller's remaining output capacity and validates the retained revision, total length and exact copied length. Every source read closes before either operation returns; repository writes therefore need no source transaction or callback. Scan progress includes excluded positions, and output-byte progress counts only successful copies. Neither counter is a page-I/O measurement. A failed read, failed advance or cancellation terminates the cursor and immediately releases its source and scan buffers. Typed cancellation and memory failures keep their resource error kinds across provider read wrappers. Completion releases the same resources and remains complete even if cancellation arrives later. No operation can reopen a failed or completed cursor.

For preservation, fix the jointly captured maps `(R_s, A_E)` above and their finite live domains. Retention preserves each captured key's representation, and each ceiling is at least its family's greatest retained live key. A strictly increasing exclusive continuation never repeats a key. Post-capture positions and captured tombstones produce no image unit, so filtering cannot add another state to the output. Every still-unvisited captured key lies after the continuation and at or below the ceiling; whenever traversal reaches its physical position, the retained snapshot yields exactly its captured value. Thus an exhausted traversal emits exactly `A_E` followed by the live part of `R_s`, with no omission or duplication. Finite additional source positions give finite completion; continuously inserted positions do not weaken each call's work bound or the identity of any emitted value.

For each selected value, the chunk identity established above shows that concatenating successive admitted ranges reconstructs its exact bytes. The cursor advances only after the declared length has been consumed, including length zero, so unit boundaries cannot merge or truncate values. Watermark encoding is injective over the existing unsigned allocation domain. The resulting stream therefore preserves the captured record and allocator observations without evaluating or changing an algebraic operation. Its dynamic workspace consists of a fixed number of bounded keys and the caller-owned output buffer, independent of record count and payload size; provider page caches remain outside that workspace. An error cannot certify a partial stream as complete. These conclusions establish the input adapter, not provider-image completeness, repository publication or restore activation.

## Wire formats

All integer fields are little-endian. Encoders write explicit fields, never Rust memory layouts. Unknown protocol versions, provider tags, unit kinds or required framing fail; reserved fields must be zero. The checked-in [fixtures](../../crates/uqa-storage/src/backup/tests/fixtures) were independently assembled from these layouts with Python `struct.pack` and `hashlib.sha256`, rather than emitted by the Rust codecs.

| Encoding | Fields in order |
| --- | --- |
| Object span, 80 bytes | Object ID (16), complete object SHA-256 (32), complete encoded object length (8), starting byte offset (8), encoded span length (8), frame count (8) |
| Inventory header, 16 bytes | `UQAINV01` (8), envelope version 1 (2), provider tag (1), reserved zero (1), provider image version (4) |
| Inventory unit | Tag 1 (1), namespace (16), generation (8), owner family (2), key length (4), key bytes, unit kind (1), decoded unit length (8), unit SHA-256 (32), object span (80) |
| Inventory footer, 49 bytes | Tag 0 (1), unit count (8), total decoded unit bytes (8), logical image digest (32) |
| Root, 240 bytes | `UQAROOT1` (8), envelope version 1 (2), reserved zero (6), repository ID (16), backup ID (16), parent ID or all-zero absence (16), source incarnation (16), snapshot ID (16), provider tag (1), provider image version (4), sequence-present tag (1), reserved zero (2), sequence or zero when absent (8), inventory object span (80), inventory summary without tag (48) |
| Frame header, 96 bytes | `UQAFRM01` (8), version 1 (2), protection mode (1), frame kind (1), repository ID (16), protection domain or zero for plaintext (16), object ID (16), byte offset within object (8), decoded payload length (4), nonce or zero for plaintext (24) |

Owner family zero is invalid. Unit kinds 1 through 5 denote metadata, records, tombstones, artifacts and resources. Inventory order is the lexicographic order of `(namespace, generation, family, key)` using unsigned integer order for generation/family and byte order for namespace/key. Duplicate or descending identities fail. Keys can contain arbitrary bytes, including empty keys; they are not names to create on disk. A unit spans one or more contiguous frames in one object, so its metadata has fixed size even for large payloads. An object may pack many small units.

Each span has a nonzero encoded length and frame count, a checked nonoverflowing end within its declared object length, and enough bytes for at least the minimum frame framing. A unit's declared decoded length must fit the remaining span bytes and the stated frame count under the decoded-frame limit. The local repository verifier compares each actual object's digest and length, walks exactly the stated frames and rejects overlapping references. Provider owners must separately validate decoded structures. The span parser does not claim those checks without accessing objects.

The logical image digest is SHA-256 over the domain bytes `UQA backup logical image`, a zero byte and byte 1, followed by the provider tag/version and each ordered unit's namespace, generation, family, key length/key, kind, decoded length and content digest. The unit content digest is SHA-256 over its exact encoded image bytes. Object locations, protection nonces, backup IDs and provenance are absent from the logical image digest, permitting verified repacking and re-encryption without changing image identity. They remain covered by the enclosing object/frame integrity.

A root's parent ID records comparison provenance. Incremental parent validation requires the same repository, source incarnation and provider image format and the exact recorded parent ID. Snapshot IDs and record sequences may differ. A root cannot name itself as parent. Complete inventory lookup never needs the parent descriptor; retaining and verifying the referenced objects is a separate repository obligation.

## Protection and bounded I/O

Plaintext frames append SHA-256 over the complete header and payload. This detects accidental corruption and is not authentication against an attacker who can rewrite the archive. Encrypted frames append a 16-byte XChaCha20-Poly1305 tag and use a fresh random 24-byte nonce. The complete header is associated data, binding the protocol, protection domain, repository, object, position, kind and length. Frames have no compression in this version, so encoded payload length equals decoded payload length. A future compressed encoding requires a distinct understood protocol.

Readers receive the expected repository, object, byte offset, frame kind and protection domain from their owning object reader. They compare that context before allocating a payload. The key is a separate caller-owned 32-byte repository credential; it is neither derived from a source SQLCipher credential nor serialized in the archive. Clones share a non-serializable owner, the final owner zeroizes the key bytes, and debug output redacts keys and unit key contents. Inventory and root frames use the same protection as unit frames.

Key and decoded frame limits are explicit and independent of the shared `StorageReadControl` heap allowance. Variable buffers reserve before allocation and retain the allowance until their owner drops. Inventory traversal keeps only its previous address and the current entry; it does not retain a directory of all units. Frame readers and protected writers keep at most one bounded payload buffer per invocation. Fixed stack scratch, including the 16 KiB I/O window, cryptographic state, allocator bookkeeping, caller-owned inputs/outputs and caller-owned keys are separate from the dynamic buffer allowance. A caller that retains many returned entries or frames continues to consume the shared allowance.

I/O and hashing are divided into windows of at most 16 KiB with cancellation checks. AEAD computation is bounded by the configured frame limit. Cancellation is checked again after an interrupted I/O result, so a stream returning repeated interruptions cannot prevent cancellation indefinitely. This is not an operating-system blocking-I/O deadline guarantee. No source lock or provider snapshot is owned by these codecs.

## Codec state transitions

An inventory writer admits entries in strict order and applies its own key and frame limits, including to entries constructed under a larger producer allowance. The reader uses the same admission boundary. Any append error, including invalid order, cancellation, memory exhaustion or partial I/O, makes that writer permanently failed; further appends and finalization return a lifecycle error. Finalization consumes an open writer and emits the counts and logical digest. Its returned stream still requires protected object finalization, synchronization and durable repository publication. Dropping an unfinished writer cannot produce a completed backup.

An inventory reader is open, complete or failed. It returns a completed summary only after the terminal tag, counts, digest and end of its bounded input are validated. A decoding error makes further reads fail; an early prefix never obtains a summary. Completing a reader verifies inventory structure, not payload integrity or restore compatibility. Frame operations consume one frame; partial output must be abandoned by the eventual object writer rather than appended to or published. No caller-side repair or state reset is exposed.

## Preservation argument

Let an owner image be a finite partial map from qualified unit addresses to `(kind, length, image bytes)`. Its domain is ordered by the address order above. An inventory `I` is the unique ordered sequence of descriptors containing each address, kind, length, image digest and immutable object reference; the referenced image bytes are not part of the descriptor encoding. The common codec operates on `I`. It neither evaluates SQL nor changes a carrier, score, posting annotation, graph entity, vector topology, sequence value or stored expression. Source snapshot completeness, object-content verification and provider decoding remain explicit premises for later integration proofs.

For fixed-width fields, little-endian encoding is injective on the specified unsigned range. For variable keys, the preceding exact length determines a unique boundary. Distinct tags and reserved/version checks separate every supported alternative. Induction over the ordered unit stream therefore gives `decode(encode(I)) = I` for every admissible inventory `I`: the header selects one format, each step consumes exactly one encoded unit and rejects duplicates or reversals, and the footer fixes the cardinality, total length and end of input. Root and span codecs have the same property by their fixed offsets and checked bounds. Truncated or appended inputs are not encodings of that completed inventory.

Assume SHA-256 collision resistance and the standard integrity/confidentiality guarantees of XChaCha20-Poly1305 with independent random nonces under each key. Unit digests certify byte equality subject to that assumption. The format-qualified logical digest commits to every ordered address, kind, length and unit digest, so substituting any semantic image component changes its committed input. Repacking changes only excluded repository references; it preserves the logical digest by equality of the included sequence, while the enclosing authenticated frame and object digest still commit to the new references. Expected-context comparison and associated data prevent an authenticated frame from being substituted across repository, domain, object, offset or kind. Plaintext checksums have only their stated accidental-corruption boundary.

Allocation ownership composes with the codec: each new variable buffer reserves while any old buffer remains charged, and failed admission publishes no replacement. Returned buffers retain their original allowance. Induction over inventory steps bounds live address workspace independently of inventory cardinality; the large-state test exercises 10,000 entries with a 64-byte dynamic allowance. A failed writer has no transition back to open or complete, and a failed reader has no transition to complete. Thus failure cannot convert a prefix or a mixed retry into a successful encoded inventory. These are codec properties, not a proof of filesystem durability, source consistency, object reachability, provider restoration or Engine carrier preservation; those owners must discharge their remaining obligations before public backup delivery.

## Verification

Owner tests compare independent wire fixtures, round-trip all four provider tags, validate parent incompatibilities, reject every truncated frame prefix and every one-byte frame corruption, exercise wrong keys and contexts, verify nonce separation, reject oversized advertised lengths before payload access, inject short/failed writes and interrupted reads, and verify terminal failure states. The automatic Linux/macOS regression inventory includes the 10,000-unit work/memory/output test and frame admission before payload allocation. No timing improvement is claimed.

## Key/Value mapping images

`KeyValueBackupImage` assigns image version 1 independently to `Redb` and `SQLiteKV`. It receives the provider's joint committed cut and retains it until completion, failure or drop. `RedbStorage::capture_backup_image` uses its already-open database owner. `SQLiteKeyValueStorage::capture_backup_image` uses the managed connection's existing record mapping and logical retention, including compressed rollback-journal modes. This exporter is not a native SQLite or actual memory Engine image codec; import, repository integration and Engine capability qualification remain separate requirements.

The ordered image address namespace is the fixed 16-byte mapping envelope `UQA-KV-IMAGE-001`, with envelope generation zero. Its families are 1 (header with empty key), 2 (autonomous allocator namespace) and 3 (committed record key). The envelope's fixed namespace identifies the mapping, not a transaction history. Original owner namespace, object incarnation and artifact generation identities remain in their complete opaque keys and values. The root's provider-qualified image format prevents conflating redb with SQLite KV. This address mapping is injective within a provider: the family distinguishes metadata planes, and the complete original key distinguishes units within each plane. Source history and snapshot identity remain in the root cut; comparing unrelated lineages is not authorized by an equal envelope namespace.

The 32-byte header contains `UQAKVI01`, the 16-byte source history identity and the eight-byte big-endian captured commit sequence. Allocator values contain eight big-endian bytes. Record values contain their eight-byte big-endian committed revision followed by the owner's unchanged encoded value, including empty values. The header contains no random snapshot identifier, timestamp or repository address, so equal cuts produce the same logical bytes. Physical receipt tables, SSI participants, process leases and old MVCC versions are outside these two logical input planes. Live semantic deletion records inside owner payloads remain opaque records; absent MVCC keys are omitted because the future import creates fresh history authority rather than reviving source transactions.

Let C be a valid committed Key/Value cut, R(C) its live record map, and A(C) its autonomous watermark map. Let D be the exact notification publication slot and G(k) identify a DiskANN generation payload or state key. The selected record domain is P(C) = {k in dom R(C) : k != D and (G(k) is undefined or state_C(G(k)) = Published)}. Every allocator in A(C) is retained, including generation reservations and stable data identity mappings that prevent reuse after import. Ordinary catalog, documents, evaluated expression keys, text postings, graphs, vector state, canonical changes, models and resources remain in R(C) under their existing owner encodings. The exporter does not execute SQL, user code, analyzers, or index rebuilds.

For valid source state, DiskANN publication installs the selected head and its Published state atomically. Replacing or dropping a head retires the previous generation in the same owning transaction. Therefore a generation is reachable by the committed selected head exactly when its state in C is Published; Writing, Frozen and Sealed builds are not selected, and Discarding and Retired generations are not current query inputs. Generation membership reads this same retained cut, not current metadata. Removing those unselected payloads preserves every query-visible selected generation, exact-side record and uncovered canonical change. The owner classifier validates each generation key and its fixed-width state; a missing, malformed or unknown state fails the image instead of silently including it. Keeping the original mapping identities and allocator maxima separately preserves future allocation safety. Excluding D prevents carrying pending source notification delivery into an imported database. These arguments assume a structurally valid source; archive authentication and complete import dependency validation remain necessary before target activation.

The raw cursor enumerates A(C), then R(C), in strict key order. Each mapping step either emits the next raw unit in P(C), explicitly skips one excluded unit, yields after a bounded raw scan, or completes. Skip changes the raw cursor from its selected unit to the next scan position without copying payload bytes. It does not change the pinned cut or revisit the excluded key. Induction on visited raw positions establishes that each selected unit appears exactly once, no excluded unit is emitted, and relative key order is preserved. Header-first and strictly increasing family numbers extend that order to the complete image. Byte-range reads concatenate the fixed revision prefix with the exact retained payload; splitting this concatenation into arbitrary caller buffers preserves the same byte sequence.

An advance examines at most the requested number of physical positions and at most one additional 18-byte generation state. An excluded unit returns Pending immediately, even if many later units are also excluded. No archive payload, whole metadata collection or complete artifact is copied under the source writer gate. Source reads close before each return. Memory retains only the cursor's charged boundary keys, notification key, fixed state and caller-owned output; provider caches remain outside the caller buffer allowance. Counters distinguish scanned positions, copied source bytes, generation metadata reads and excluded units; generated header/revision bytes and opaque page I/O are not source-read measurements. Completion or any error drops both references to the same captured cut and the charged scan keys. Terminal state rejects further reads, while completed advance remains complete even after late cancellation.

Owner tests compare actual redb and all four SQLite KV file modes against an independent full-read oracle outside the image allowance. Fixtures include published DiskANN topology, exact-side vectors and uncovered changes, private writes, an unpublished large build, delivery records, empty values and large ordinary records. A redb test replaces the selected generation after capture; both providers allow independent writes between chunks. Fixed lifecycle bytes cover every generation state and malformed input. The automatic regression inventory exercises exact output, bounded windows, 4 KiB retained allowances and immediate resource retirement. These export tests do not establish fresh-target import or complete public backup support.

## Native SQLite mapping images

`SQLiteStorageProvider::capture_backup_image` exports an initialized native provider through `SQLiteNativeBackupImage`. The connection owns admission to its bound record session, and `VersionedKeyValueStore::capture_backup_snapshot` forwards directly to that session's persistence owner. Capture does not reopen the mapping, scan all sequence definitions, migrate the catalog, complete an active transaction or include private writes. An unbound provider reports an unsupported capability; normal provider opening owns initialization. SQLite KV now forwards through its existing bound owner at the same boundary. The existing joint cut seals records and autonomous metadata durably before returning and releases every physical read between export calls in plain, encrypted, compressed and compressed-encrypted modes.

Native image version 1 uses address namespace `UQA-NATIVE-IMG01`, generation zero, and the same three ordered envelope families as the Key/Value image: header, allocator watermark and committed record. Its 56-byte header contains `UQANVI01`, the 16-byte source history identity, the eight-byte big-endian committed sequence, the 16-byte stable native data namespace, the four-byte native mapping version, the two-byte greatest assigned native family and the two-byte native physical catalog marker. The supported values are mapping 17, family 63 and physical catalog marker 49. The marker identifies the guarded native projection, independently of later high-level catalog declarations. Record units retain their original complete native key and an eight-byte big-endian revision followed by their unchanged encoded row. Allocator units retain the original namespace and eight-byte big-endian watermark. The transaction history and stable data namespace remain separate identities.

The existing native [family registry](../../crates/uqa-storage-sqlite/src/mvcc/native/family.rs), [row codec](../../crates/uqa-storage-sqlite/src/mvcc/native/row.rs), [format owner](../../crates/uqa-storage-sqlite/src/mvcc/native/format.rs) and [projection owner](../../crates/uqa-storage-sqlite/src/mvcc/native/projection.rs) define the source inventory. Every selected native record is retained, including families not exercised by a particular fixture; unknown family IDs, malformed typed keys and database-owned keys outside the captured namespace fail export.

| State plane | Existing native representation | Image treatment |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Definitions, schemas, relations, views, foreign metadata, analyzers and models | Families 1, 4, 6, 12, 13, 25, 26, 36–40, 41, 43 and 63 | Preserve evaluated records and encoded resources |
| Documents, BLOBs, table identities and physical index keys | Families 2, 3, 9, 10 and 44 | Preserve original rows, object identities and generations |
| Text postings, occurrences, statistics and accelerators | Families 7, 8, 11, 28–35 and 46–48 | Preserve decorated postings and all retained statistics |
| Graph entities, memberships, paths, lookups and standalone scopes | Families 14–18, 27, 45 and 50–56 | Preserve graph keys, payloads and original scope identities |
| Exact, IVF, HNSW, DiskANN and canonical reconciliation state | Families 19–24, 42, 49 and 57–61 | Preserve topology, vectors, coverage and uncovered changes; select only published DiskANN generation payloads |
| Sequences, cache revisions and autonomous reservations | Families 5, 39 and 62 plus the joint allocator plane | Preserve record values and the captured independent watermark map |
| Physical row projections and equality indexes | Existing projection of retained evaluated records | Do not archive duplicate physical SQLite pages; native import must reconstruct these projections without evaluating user expressions |
| Transaction receipts, SSI state, old MVCC revisions, process leases and notification delivery | Transient transaction authority and the exact native notification slot | Exclude from the image; restore must create new history authority |

`MappedRecordBackupImage` in Common Storage owns ordered cursor consumption, revision-prefix streaming, counters and terminal retirement for both mapping adapters. The native mapping owns its header, key validation and membership. The shared DiskANN state decoder receives one bounded read callback: native SQLite reads the original native binary-pair envelope, validates its key/row binding and decodes only its fixed 18-byte lifecycle state. Membership uses that same retained cut, so subsequent publication or retirement cannot change the exported generation set. Excluded payloads are never fetched. Native metadata-read counters include the bytes actually read from that envelope; they do not claim page I/O measurements.

For preservation, let C = (R_s, A_E, N) be a valid initialized native cut, where R_s maps complete native keys to committed revisions and encoded rows, A_E is the captured autonomous watermark map and N is the stable data namespace. Let D_N be its exact notification key and G(k) identify a DiskANN generation record. Define P_C(k) to mean k differs from D_N and either G(k) is undefined or its lifecycle state in R_s is Published. The image is the header, the ordered map A_E and the restriction of R_s to P_C, encoded under disjoint envelope families. Complete typed keys distinguish native family, owner, generation and tuple components, so this addressing is injective. Length-delimited rows and fixed-width revision/watermark prefixes are also injective. Thus decoding every emitted unit recovers exactly the selected map and its revisions, with no collision between metadata and record planes.

The shared cursor emits each original position once in increasing order, and the mapping either selects it or discards it without changing the cut. Induction over positions therefore establishes that emitted keys are exactly the domain selected by P_C, with their original values. Chunking changes only the partition of a byte concatenation, so it preserves every encoded row. The joint-cut argument above preserves R_s and A_E under later writes, and membership reads use R_s rather than live state. For a valid source, published DiskANN heads reference published generation state, while canonical vectors, exact-side records and uncovered changes remain selected. The exclusion therefore preserves the stored search state while dropping private, retired and delivery authority. No SQL expression, analyzer or callback is reevaluated. All existing operations over the selected carriers retain their original inputs; this export argument does not claim fresh-target query equivalence until native import and complete logical validation are implemented.

Capture retains no collection proportional to source size. Each advance visits at most its requested raw positions and performs at most one fixed-size generation-state lookup; payload reads use the caller's buffer. Variable native key and row workspace is charged to the original allowance. Completion, cancellation, invalid sequencing and failed memory admission release the mapping, cursor and both references to the cut; completion remains stable after later cancellation. Owner tests cover all four file modes, real DiskANN artifacts, large metadata under a 128 KiB allowance, exact raw-record and watermark comparison after source deletion, independent writer progress, private transaction preservation, captured lifecycle selection, malformed keys/state and immediate retirement under a 4 KiB allowance. These are export and repository-verification obligations; native import, actual memory Engine images and public backup jobs remain outstanding.

## Native record-image input admission

`SQLiteNativeImageImportReader` validates the native envelope through Common Storage's `MappedRecordImageImportReader`. The shared reader owns the three envelope families, root/provider compatibility, revision and allocator bounds, exact unit consumption and complete repository verification. The native adapter validates the 56-byte header against its root and current mapping, requires a nonzero stable data namespace, validates every complete typed record key and excludes the native notification slot. The data namespace is available only after header admission and retires with the active reader. The Key/Value reader retains its existing public interface and mapping rules through the same state machine.

For an admitted root, the header predicate binds source history, cut sequence, data namespace, physical mapping, assigned families and native catalog marker. Every later address must use the native envelope namespace with generation zero. Each selected record has a valid typed key in the admitted database namespace or its original nonzero object/generation namespace; its revision lies in the closed interval from one through the captured sequence. Allocator keys exclude transient reclamation authority. The complete repository reader separately establishes ordered uniqueness, exact unit lengths, payload digests, complete-object digests and disjoint referenced ranges. Therefore induction over admitted units preserves exactly the original revision/watermark prefixes and payload concatenations while rejecting an invalid envelope before exposing that unit. Forwarding Key/Value mapping checks preserves its previous acceptance predicate and output; both wrappers share the same terminal retirement and complete-verification boundary.

Header storage and mapping-owned notification keys use the original allowance. A record payload can exceed that allowance because reads forward into the caller's bounded buffer. Completion and failure release the input and mapping, so no admitted prefix or partially verified archive can be resumed as a completed image. Four native owner tests cover a literal header and a 512 KiB payload under 64 KiB, incompatible roots, 22 well-framed but semantically invalid envelopes, unread input, cancellation and memory exhaustion. Native row decoding, physical projection, artifact/dependency closure, target history publication and fresh-file restoration remain separate importer obligations; this reader alone does not activate a database.

## Local repository ownership and publication

`LocalRepository::create(path, protection, limits, control)` exclusively creates a native local directory; `open` authenticates its existing identity and protection profile. Repository access uses the public capability-directory interfaces of `cap-std` and `cap-fs-ext`. Only the selected parent path uses ambient authority. Internal directories remain pinned, final-component symlinks are rejected, and object IDs are formatted into fixed hexadecimal names without interpreting image keys as paths. Unix files and directories are created with modes 0600 and 0700 before the process umask. Regular-file admission uses nonblocking opens on Unix so a substituted pipe cannot wait for a writer. The filesystem must support file locks, hard links, atomic same-directory rename and successful file/directory synchronization; unsupported operations return errors.

Directory synchronization opens an I/O-capable handle relative to the pinned directory capability. Cloning a Linux `O_PATH` descriptor is insufficient because it cannot be synchronized. Renaming the selected directory or replacing its original pathname does not redirect synchronization, and an unsuccessful sync cannot acknowledge publication or cancellation.

The directory contains `repository`, `admission`, `generation`, `objects/`, `roots/` and `operations/`. Its 41-byte public prologue consists of `UQAREP01`, repository ID, protection-mode byte and protection-domain ID or zeros. A protected `Repository` frame at offset 41 repeats and authenticates that prologue. Frame kinds 4 and 5 identify repository and operation-intent metadata. The 24-byte generation record contains `UQAGEN01` and a random comparison identity; an exclusive mutation atomically replaces and synchronizes it before installing references. It coordinates future collection revalidation and does not claim protection against replay by an attacker who can rewrite the repository.

`begin_backup(cut, parent, maximum_encoded_bytes, control)` allocates a new operation ID and exclusive OS lease, pins a compatible parent when supplied, and writes a protected intent containing `UQACAP01`, backup/source/snapshot IDs, provider tag/version, optional sequence tag/value and optional parent ID. The intent has 86 decoded bytes. Independently opened lease descriptions coordinate readers and writers across processes. Each Rust lease owner explicitly unlocks its own description before closing it; a descriptor inherited during another thread's process creation cannot prolong an acknowledged cancellation or completed operation. The owner does not clone its lock into another Rust lease. Independently opened readers have separate descriptions, so retiring one cannot unlock another. Process termination also releases OS-owned resources without process-ID liveness guesses. A busy lease returns `Busy`. The source owner remains responsible for the consistency and completeness of the supplied cut.

`begin_pack` borrows capture exclusively.
