Each `append` streams one complete unit or inventory into frame-aligned ranges; many small units can share one pack. `finish` synchronizes the object and its staging directory, installs the immutable object by a no-clobber hard link under repository admission, and synchronizes the object directory. Repository admission waits on its separately opened file description and checks cancellation between attempts; ordinary contention preserves the synchronized pack and operation state. Operation leases remain nonblocking and report `Busy` when another live owner protects the same operation. A finished `PackObject` attaches its digest and length only to ranges created by that exact writer. Payload reads and writes do not hold repository admission. An unfinished, failed or cancelled pack makes capture terminally failed. No existing object is overwritten and no partial stream can be appended after failure.

`prepare` records the repository generation under admission, verifies every referenced object, frame and unit outside admission, then revalidates that generation and advances it under admission before writing and synchronizing its immutable prepared descriptor. A changed generation fails preparation before publishing new references, including through the manual inventory interface. Verification checks the complete inventory format, ordered identities, exact counts, decoded lengths, content digests, whole-object digest/length and globally disjoint frame ranges. Physical placement need not follow inventory order. Two existing encrypted spill maps retain verified object identities and ordered ranges under the invoking memory allowance, avoiding a database-sized resident directory or repeated hashing of the same pack. Shared temporary-file admission charges actual ciphertext extents, including both authenticated slots and framing, before file growth. Reopened handles retain one owner reservation, successful truncation releases it, and failed truncation retains it until reclamation. Compaction retains the same shared allowance while old and replacement files coexist.

The operation's encoded staging limit covers intent, new packs and the prepared descriptor; verification receives the remaining allowance. Standalone verification and publication recovery take an explicit temporary-workspace limit. Counts describe file byte extents, excluding filesystem allocation metadata, fixed repository coordination records and caller-owned inputs. Stack buffers, keys and fixed I/O owners retain the codec boundary described above. Retained source history, job duration and public progress belong to the later source/job adapters.

`publish` accepts only a prepared operation. It resolves an already installed matching root before consulting late cancellation. Otherwise, it links the exact prepared descriptor into `roots/` without replacing any destination and synchronizes that directory before returning completion. An uncertain install or synchronization result retains the prepared identity for retry. `open_backup` admits only a completed root, synchronizes its directory and holds a shared operation lease. `verify(maximum_workspace_bytes, control)` validates its object graph; this is archive verification, not a provider restore drill.

`resume_publication` requires an exclusive lease, an authenticated original intent, no durable cancellation marker and a matching complete prepared descriptor. It verifies the object graph and synchronizes the prepared file and operation directory again before allowing publication. An interrupted capture without that descriptor cannot bind a new snapshot to its old operation ID. `cancel` resolves an installed root as completed; before publication it durably links the immutable intent as `cancelled`, synchronizes the operation directory and releases its leases. Cancellation synchronization errors retain a cancelling state that rejects publication and permits cancellation retry. An acknowledged cancellation cannot be resumed.

Operation directories retain operation identities, cancellation fences and prepared descriptors for recovery. The repository retention owner below removes roots, reclaims inactive staging links and unreferenced objects, and supports standalone copies without changing image encodings. A successful low-level publication does not claim provider completeness, fresh-target activation or a public Engine backup API.

## Repository preservation argument

Let a repository state be a set of immutable objects, operation intents and completed roots. Let `V(r)` mean that root `r` has an authenticated, strictly ordered complete inventory whose referenced spans match existing objects, whose frames authenticate at their declared positions, whose unit bytes match their lengths/digests, and whose referenced ranges do not overlap. Assume the stated filesystem synchronization and atomic-link semantics, cooperating operations using repository admission/leases, and the cryptographic assumptions above. Waiting for admission changes none of these objects or operation states, so any finite sequence of contended attempts preserves the same pending transition. Acquiring admission executes that transition once; cancellation before acquisition terminates capture without publishing a root. Eventual acquisition requires the competing holder to release admission and OS scheduling to grant a waiting attempt; no fairness bound is claimed. Out-of-band deletion or corruption causes verification failure; no availability guarantee is made against a repository writer bypassing that protocol.

Pack finalization establishes durable object contents before any completed root can reference them. Preparation checks `V(r)` without mutating source or object bytes and synchronizes the descriptor only after that check. Publication adds precisely that descriptor after all referenced objects exist durably, and synchronizes its containing directory before acknowledging it. Therefore every acknowledged completed root satisfies `V(r)` at publication, subject to the stated corruption boundary. Existing completed roots and object bytes are unchanged by these operations. Their decoded images, and consequently every previously established algebraic observation of those images, are preserved by identity; no new SQL or ranked operator is introduced.

The publication linearization point is the no-clobber root installation, with durable acknowledgement after directory synchronization. A failure before installation exposes no completed root. A failure after installation is resolved by matching the same backup ID and complete descriptor and synchronizing its directory; it cannot publish a second cut under that ID. Exclusive operation ownership serializes preparation, publication and cancellation. Durable cancellation precedes lease release and is checked during recovery, so a cancelled intent cannot transition to prepared publication. Once a root exists, cancellation resolves completion rather than removing that root. These cases exhaust the state machine's transitions, including failure and process exit. Lock ownership follows those transitions: each admitted lease has one Rust retirement owner and one separately opened description. Retirement calls unlock on that description before descriptor closure, so a duplicate retained by an unrelated fork does not extend the lock. The kernel's independent shared descriptions preserve every other active reader. Deterministic tests retain duplicate descriptors through both shared-reader retirement and acknowledged cancellation; the latter returns `Busy` on the preceding implementation and `Finished` after this correction.

Verification's object table is keyed by all 128 identity bits; its range table uses a unique object ordinal plus the full 64-bit offset. After duplicate starts are rejected, sorted interval traversal rejects every overlap by comparison with the preceding end for that object. Thus arbitrary nonoverlapping physical placement is accepted, but no unit can silently alias another unit's frames. Each distinct object is hashed completely before verification finishes, and every unit is separately authenticated and digested. Spill encoding changes only the representation of this finite ledger, preserving its lookup and order; both resident and spilled paths have the same result.

For temporary storage, a shared reservation admits the whole next physical extent before any growth. A mutex serializes the sum across files; reopened handles share the same file owner. Successful physical shrink reduces that owner's reservation, failed shrink retains it, and final owner destruction releases it after file cleanup. Consequently admitted ciphertext extents do not exceed the shared limit, including simultaneous compaction files. Memory and staging failure cannot produce a completed root because capture becomes failed or remains in an explicitly recoverable prepared state. Provider completeness and source-to-restored-query equivalence remain obligations of the source/import owners.

## Bounded repository image verification

`BackupLease::read_image` retains the root lease through its borrow and returns a `BackupImageReader`. Each `advance` decodes one size-bounded inventory entry, hashes one whole-object window capped at 16 KiB, or checks one globally ordered frame range. `read_current` streams at most one decoded frame range into a nonempty caller buffer. The importing owner may consume these bytes only into an inaccessible target; they remain provisional until `verified` returns a `VerifiedBackupImage`, and that result establishes repository integrity rather than provider dependency validity or target activation. The ordinary full `verify` operation drains this same state machine instead of maintaining a second verification algorithm.

The reader admits the root's provider-qualified inventory and uses the existing bounded encrypted object/range ledger. It requires exact unit length, payload digest and frame exhaustion before advancing to another entry. Completing the inventory requires its exact root summary. It then visits each distinct object once, checking the declared length, whole-object digest and EOF in bounded windows, including bytes not referenced by inventory units. Finally it visits ranges in ordinal/offset order, rejecting any same-object overlap. Duplicate starts and inconsistent metadata for one object fail at ledger admission. A completed result is created only after all three stages finish; skipping an unread unit, cancellation or any failed check enters a terminal failure and drops every input, file and ledger. Completed advance remains stable after later cancellation.

Let I be the ordered inventory, O its referenced objects including the inventory object, and R its frame ranges. The inventory-stage invariant is that every consumed prefix of I has its exact authenticated decoded payload, and its metadata is represented in the ledger. The object-stage invariant is that the prefix of O already visited matches every declared byte length and complete-object digest; a partial hash retains only its file position and hash state, so partitioning reads into windows preserves the SHA-256 input concatenation. The range-stage invariant is that the ordered prefix of R has no duplicate start or overlap. These invariants and the final root-summary equality imply the same complete verification predicate as the previous full traversal, under the established authentication and digest assumptions. No partial stage can construct the completion result. Provider import validation remains an independent condition before any target becomes usable.

Object writers already encode an empty unit as exactly one empty frame and emit no empty frames inside nonempty units or inventories. Readers enforce that canonical shape. This prevents an unbounded run of zero-payload frames from making one inventory or unit read perform unbounded framing work. A sole empty unit remains valid. Inventory key and frame limits bound metadata decoding independently of the object-byte window. The object-byte counter measures only the explicit whole-object pass; frame decoding, payload copies and OS page/cache activity are not included in that counter.

The owner suite streams 512 literal payloads under a 128 KiB allowance with seven-byte object windows, rejects incomplete and cancelled reads with immediate retirement, detects corruption in an unreferenced part of an otherwise readable pack, and distinguishes canonical empty units from empty padding frames. Existing immutable-object, overlap, spill-quota, protection, publication and failure-recovery tests use the same verifier.

### Owned reader leases

`BackupLease::into_image_reader` transfers the root's existing OS lease into the reader. `BackupLeaseInput` lets the shared record decoder and all three persistent provider importers accept either this owned lease or the original borrowed form. Mapping and root admission still precede image traversal; no provider format, validation order, payload algorithm or history transition changes. A completed or failed owned reader releases its streams and verification ledger before its lease, even while the terminal reader handle remains alive. Construction failure and unwinding also retire the transferred lease. Independent readers retain independently opened lease descriptions; moving one description never unlocks another.

Let V be the original reader state and L its shared root lease. The owned extension has state (V, L) while V is active and (V, empty) after completion or failure. Its transition on an input operation applies exactly the original transition to V, then releases L only when V becomes terminal. Therefore projection onto V is a simulation preserving every emitted byte, digest, error and verification result, including the original prefix invariants above. While V is active, L excludes root retirement under the repository's existing exclusive-removal rule. After V is terminal no future operation accesses image objects, so releasing L preserves all subsequent observations. Field and explicit retirement order close inputs before unlocking L, establishing the same exclusion during ordinary drop and unwinding. The owning form transfers a fixed-size lease without duplicating image buffers, keys or verification workspace. Borrowed readers retain their previous lifetime and parent-lease behavior.

### Scheduled private restore

`BackupRestoreOperation::into_job` admits a `BackupRestoreFactory` through the same durable preparation boundary as the consuming importer. Only a new intent calls `import`; an already started intent calls `recover`. The factory owns the provider continuation and an independent root lease, while the operation retains its original root lease until durable preparation. `BackupRestoreJob::advance` performs one admitted provider work step and returns `Pending` or `Prepared`; publication remains an explicit call to the original `publish`. A prepared or completed operation needs neither a factory call nor its source archive. This is a storage-owner scheduling interface; the production logical-image validator and complete public Engine restore adapter remain separate requirements.

The factory contract is the existing importer contract divided into continuations: installed code admits bounded initial state, each work step respects its provider's declared record, byte and workspace limits, and completion certifies complete archive validation, owner-format and logical validation, durable data and the exact new-history transition. A work value closes all provider handles when dropped. The scheduler takes that value out of its active slot before every step. A pending result restores it; error or unwinding drops it and leaves the slot empty. Completion drops it before opening the prepared-marker boundary. Thus a failed or unwound work value cannot execute again through the job, and neither publication nor a second fresh import can bypass the durable started intent. Explicit cancellation closes active work before recording the existing cancellation transition. Dropping a job closes work before releasing operation admission, permitting recovery of its persisted request.

For the preservation argument, let the durable states be N (new), S (started), P (prepared), C (published) and X (cancelled), with the existing uncertain-record resolution rules. The extension adds only an optional work value W while the durable state is S. The admission invariant is that the operation's exclusive lease and selected target identity are retained, the final destination is absent, and a root lease survives until P; W may release its reader lease earlier without changing that invariant. Each pending work transition is a stuttering transition under projection (S, W) to S and cannot publish. By the factory contract, the transition finishing W establishes precisely the precondition for the original S-to-P transition; dropping W closes all provider handles before that transition. Errors leave S without W and hence require the existing recovery admission. No new transition from N or S to C exists. P-to-C, cancellation, lost-reply resolution and completed receipts use the original implementation unchanged. Induction on a finite step trace therefore gives the same prepared image and publication observations as a conforming consuming importer, while interrupted traces retain exactly the existing recoverable durable intent. Later target writes remain outside the meaning of a receipt and are never replayed away by resolving it.

`RestoreDirectory::begin_file_work` retains the private directory handle and charged native pathname around a filename-based provider continuation. It validates the installed basename and regular file before construction, verifies directory identity after construction and before and after each work step, and retires the provider before its directory/path ownership on completion, error or unwinding. A provider-owned regular file replacement remains permitted; a nonregular replacement or moved/replaced parent makes the work terminal. Recovery resolves the retained directory's current location. The argument assumes the same exclusively owned staging entries as synchronous pathname interoperation: these checks detect an observed location change, and do not authorize an external actor to move live provider files. No process working-directory change or dependency-private API is introduced.

The additional scheduler work is a fixed number of owner transitions, metadata checks and durable marker writes per call; provider step bounds remain the provider's declared bounds. Filesystem synchronization can wait independently of those bounds. Root and operation leases, continuation fields and directory handles have fixed cardinality; pathname storage shares the original allowance, and image buffering still belongs to the original verifier. A failed job can retain its admitted target name until cancellation or drop, but never a failed continuation's image streams, pathname workspace or physical connections. Owner tests exercise independent root leases, constructor failure, cancellation, unwinding, preparation/publication faults, pathname movement and stable terminal observations. Actual Engine fixture imports run this scheduler over native SQLite, SQLite KV and redb, preserving source-loss, incremental-root, protection-mode, allocation-history and subsequent-write checks; their finite fixture observer does not substitute for the unfinished production logical-image validator.

## Complete provider image capture

`BackupImageSource` owns a provider-qualified cut and yields strictly ordered unit addresses, kinds and exact lengths, with bounded source-position advances and caller-buffer payload reads. Every source call ends its physical read before returning. Common Storage's synchronous `BackupOperation::prepare_image` consumes that owner, requires the exact operation cut, and prepares a complete image through the existing publication state machine. It does not interpret provider keys or payload encodings. The implemented Key/Value adapter supplies redb and SQLite KV images; other mappings and the public Engine job scheduler remain separate responsibilities.

The capture groups at most 32 units per pack, seals after a soft 64 KiB decoded threshold, and also seals before pending metadata exceeds its allowance-derived target. A larger individual unit spans bounded frames in one object; it is never collected into a payload-sized buffer. Pending keys and entry capacity retain the job's memory allowance. Each unit's exact byte count and SHA-256 are checked while streaming, then its final object reference enters the ordered inventory only after its pack is installed. The complete inventory is written through an authenticated ephemeral file with a separate physical-ciphertext quota. The file's key never reaches disk. The source is released before inventory installation and full verification; the spool is released before preparation verification begins. Preparation verification shares the same workspace quota; the operation's encoded staging limit independently bounds its intent, immutable objects and prepared descriptor.

Let the source image be the ordered sequence I = ((a_i, k_i, n_i, b_i)), where each address a_i is unique and increasing and the byte string b_i has length n_i. After each consumed unit, the pending-pack invariant is that its selected frame range decodes to exactly b_i and its stored digest is SHA-256(b_i). Appending chunks preserves this invariant because both framing and hashing consume the same concatenation, and short or excess input fails before preparation. Sealing a pack fixes its object digest without changing any decoded unit. Appending its entries to the inventory preserves the processed prefix of I; the inventory writer rejects duplicate or decreasing addresses. Therefore induction over packs gives a complete inventory with exactly the source's address, kind, length and bytes. Pack boundaries and fresh encryption nonces do not enter the logical image digest, so changing packing leaves that image unchanged under the existing digest/authentication assumptions.

Successful source exhaustion, inventory completion and the existing full verifier are all necessary before the operation reaches its prepared state. Every earlier failure makes that operation terminal, drops the consumed source and transient workspace, and leaves no completed root. Publication and uncertain acknowledgement reuse the existing backup identity and durable transition. This preserves the source adapter's previously established observations without introducing SQL evaluation, callbacks or reconstruction. It establishes a complete repository image for an implemented source; provider import validation and fresh-target activation are still required to establish restored query equivalence.

The capture suite covers empty images and units, a 2 MiB unit with 512 small records under a 128 KiB allowance, exact output and owned-source counters, shared-pack counts, malformed source lengths/order, mismatched cuts, cancellation, I/O failure and workspace/staging exhaustion. Actual redb and all four SQLite KV file modes capture DiskANN and large record bytes, change the source after capture, publish protected images, remove the source and verify every original committed record and revision against an independent full-read oracle. These tests qualify repository capture and source independence; they do not claim a fresh database restore.

## Complete incremental inventories

When `LocalRepository::begin_backup` retains a compatible parent, `BackupOperation::prepare_image` merge-scans its authenticated complete inventory with the selected source. Only an exact unit address, kind, decoded length and SHA-256 digest can reuse a parent object range. The address includes namespace, generation, family and key; equal bytes under another address do not establish the same unit. Current-only or changed units receive new ranges, and parent-only units are absent from the child. A tombstone remains an explicit current unit when the owning image emits one. The child's complete inventory records all its references directly; its parent ID is provenance, not a restore dependency.

The current `BackupImageSource` contract does not supply certified fingerprints or replayable payloads. For a possible parent match, capture hashes the current unit once while writing an encrypted temporary file. A matching digest discards that file and retains the parent range. A differing digest streams the file into a new pack without rereading the source. Units without a candidate stream directly into packs. At most one candidate file is retained, sharing the physical ciphertext quota with the inventory spool; thus a large possible match can exhaust workspace even when it would eventually be reused. No new pack is created for an entirely unchanged image. Memory retains one parent entry, one selected entry, bounded frame buffers and the existing bounded pending-pack metadata, independently of inventory cardinality. Comparison visits each parent entry at most once and each current entry once, plus its payload bytes.

Let P and C be the parent's and current source's finite mappings from unique ordered addresses to `(kind, length, bytes)`. Assume the existing authenticated-frame and SHA-256 collision-resistance contract, exact source lengths, and a retained immutable parent. Define R(C, P) at each address a in C: reuse P's range precisely when its kind, length and digest equal C(a); otherwise encode C(a) in a fresh range. In the reuse case, full preparation verifies that the referenced range decodes to the authenticated parent digest. Digest equality then establishes the same bytes under the stated assumption. In the fresh case, the same streaming byte-count and digest invariant as full capture establishes C(a). Therefore every emitted entry decodes to C(a), regardless of its physical origin.

Induct on the consumed current prefix. The next current address has exactly one emitted entry, and no parent-only address is emitted. The inventory owner rejects duplicate or decreasing current addresses. The parent cursor advances monotonically, so no eligible entry can be skipped or substituted; draining its suffix checks its complete footer and summary even for an empty child. The induction gives `decode(R(C, P)) = C`. Consequently a full capture of C and an incremental capture of C have equal logical image summaries, although their packs differ. Applying the same argument to children or branches proves that restore needs only that child's inventory and reachable objects. Removing an ancestor manifest cannot change this mapping; safe object deletion still requires the separately pending retention/GC implementation.

The parent lease remains owned by the operation through durable child publication or cancellation. Source exhaustion releases the source before final inventory installation. Existing preparation fully verifies all referenced objects and ranges, including reused payloads, before permitting publication; candidate-spool, inventory, source, cancellation or verification failure leaves no completed root. This does not add SQL evaluation, callback execution or a new query operator. Given an owning image's preservation contract, identical decoded image bytes preserve its previous observations and compositions. Complete Engine image validation and activation remain separate obligations.

`BackupCaptureReport.image` separates parent entries visited, current payload bytes hashed, reused units/bytes, newly encoded units/bytes and newly installed object bytes. `source` retains the source owner's actual position, metadata-read and payload-copy counters. Object bytes include new data packs and the child inventory, excluding publication descriptors; reused payload bytes are decoded lengths, not a claim about avoided repository verification reads. This baseline reads and hashes every current payload. Certified fingerprint acceleration, payload-free unchanged-source reads, GC, standalone copying and key rotation are not implemented by this comparison.

Owning tests compare incremental and full logical summaries and exact decoded units under both repository protection modes, including deletion, tombstone transitions, namespace/generation replacement, branching, empty images and ancestor-manifest removal. A 257-unit fixture with a payload larger than two MiB exercises equal and changed comparisons under a 128 KiB allowance, asserts actual source/hash/write counts and verifies that an unchanged child writes only its inventory. Corrupt parent metadata or reused objects, workspace denial, cancellation and truncated current input cannot produce a published root and release partial ownership.

Provider import tests capture parent and child images from actual redb, SQLite Key/Value and native SQLite owners. Each child combines inherited DiskANN artifacts with changed, inserted and deleted records and a new allocator watermark; writes after the cut cannot replace the selected bytes. After deleting the original source files, fresh import preserves every original record/revision, the watermark and artifact observations, establishes a new history identity and accepts later writes. SQLite exercises all four file modes in each mapping, with source and destination using the same mode. Persistent source counters exclude generated mapping headers and revision prefixes, while hash counters cover the complete image bytes; the tests check that difference explicitly. Complete memory Engine selection and public Engine acceptance remain outside these provider-image results.

## Standalone repository copies

`BackupOperation::prepare_copy(source_lease, maximum_workspace_bytes, control)` copies one completed root into another repository. The destination operation must have no parent and must name exactly the source root's cut; its repository identity must differ. The source lease holds the original root throughout copying. The method streams through the existing complete image verifier and image encoder, then compares the destination inventory's complete logical summary with the source root before creating any prepared descriptor. It does not need the original live database, an ancestor manifest or a provider-specific exporter. Provider logical validation and fresh Engine activation remain the importing owner's responsibility.

Every destination unit is encoded into newly owned packs, so no source object reference survives. The destination repository determines plaintext or authenticated encrypted protection, including metadata; keys and protection domains are independent of the source. Encryption uses the existing random nonce owner. This permits key rotation by creating and verifying a copy under a new key; it does not alter the old repository, remove its key or certify that other old roots have been retired. An explicitly selected plaintext destination produces plaintext output.

Source verification includes whole pack digests and all range checks after unit streaming, so corrupt bytes in an unselected part of an inherited pack still reject the copy. Only successful complete source verification, matching logical summaries and full destination verification admit preparation. Failed copies enter the existing terminal capture state, retain no source or workspace buffers and cannot publish. Installed unreachable destination objects remain subject to repository collection. Once preparation succeeds, ordinary publication, cancellation and lost-reply recovery use the destination's existing operation identity; neither the source repository nor its key is needed again.

One shared physical-ciphertext allowance bounds source verification ledgers, the inventory spool and final destination verification. Memory admission remains under the original control, and payload reads stay bounded by frame and caller windows, including units larger than memory. `BackupCopyReport` records copied payload bytes, bytes hashed in the separate source whole-object pass, newly written object bytes and peak physical workspace. Object bytes include packs and the complete inventory, excluding the publication descriptor and filesystem allocation metadata. These counters make no physical page-I/O or controlled-timing claim.

Let a source completed root R denote the ordered finite image I = ((a_i, k_i, n_i, b_i)) under the established authenticated-frame and SHA-256 assumptions. The source verifier accepts unit i only after its length and hash match the authenticated inventory; it accepts the whole image only after the inventory summary, complete object digests and non-overlapping ranges match R. Copying preserves the address, kind and declared length of each unit and streams its exact byte sequence to the existing encoder. By the complete-capture prefix invariant, after i units the destination decodes to exactly I's first i entries. The source reader cannot skip a unit or reach completion with an unfinished verification stage. Induction and final summary equality therefore give `decode(copy(R)) = I`, including an empty I and units inherited from earlier backups.

The destination inventory contains only spans returned by its own newly installed packs. Hence its object closure is disjoint from the source repository, and removing the source, its ancestors or their keys cannot change any destination read. Encoding and decoding with different protection keys are identity on each accepted byte string under the authenticated encryption contract. Consequently a provider importer applied to the copy receives the same image as it would from R. Any query, graph, ranked retrieval or composed observation preserved by that image/import contract remains equal, including payloads and evaluated artifacts; equality of document support alone is not used. The copy introduces no algebraic operator, source mutation or evaluation of stored code.

Publication preserves this equality because the operation reaches Prepared only after both verification boundaries and summary comparison. Errors before preparation expose no completed root, and retries after uncertain publication resolve the exact already prepared root. Shared source leases prevent cooperating repository operations from retiring the input while it is consumed. Repository root removal and GC still require their separate ownership protocol; this copy does not implement that protocol or authorize deletion of a source.

## Repository root retention and collection

`LocalRepository::list_backups(maximum_workspace_bytes, control)` authenticates completed descriptors into a memory-admitted, encrypted spill map and returns `BackupListing`. `next_backup` yields descriptors in backup-ID order. Discovery records and revalidates one repository generation under admission; a concurrent mutation returns `Busy` before any listing is exposed. The resulting metadata snapshot does not retain roots against later deletion. Consumers acquire `open_backup` before reading image data. Preparation is absent from the completed listing, and terminal completion, cancellation or failure releases its storage.

`remove_backup(id, control)` exclusively admits the operation's OS lease, verifies its original intent and any completed root, durably links that exact intent as the existing `cancelled` marker, then unlinks the completed root and synchronizes its directory. It can also retire an abandoned or prepared operation that no live owner holds. Active capture, publication, parent, verification, restore and copy leases prevent retirement with `Busy`. A later call for the same ID resolves interrupted removal; an unknown or corrupt identity is an error. Once the retirement fence begins, cancellation cannot split its synchronization/unlink boundary. An I/O error can leave either the old discoverable root or a completed removal, so the same method resolves the outcome. Operation records remain for durable identity resolution. Historical completion receipts never reinstall a removed root.

`collect_garbage(maximum_files, maximum_workspace_bytes, control)` first discovers completed roots, recoverable prepared images and active operation object sets outside repository admission. It authenticates every selected complete inventory and its containing object, checks its exact format and logical summary, and validates referenced object presence and length. It does not read unit payloads or replace full image verification. Complete references retain their entire immutable objects. Active captures additionally retain all private pack names, including installed and unfinished packs. Source-independent recovery retains prepared inventories even after every parent manifest disappears. A cancelled operation retains no unpublished image, but a completed root still present after interrupted retirement remains live until its own unlink succeeds.

The collector uses charged, encrypted spill maps for object identity and referenced ranges and a memory-admitted candidate vector bounded by `maximum_files`. Before deleting anything it reacquires repository admission and compares the generation with discovery. New operation creation, immutable object installation, prepared-reference publication, root publication/removal and collection advance that generation inside the same admission boundary. A mismatch returns `Busy`; invalid or incomplete discovery returns its error with no deletion. A successful mutation advances the generation and deletes at most the requested number of files while holding admission. It checks cancellation between entries and synchronizes each containing directory after unlink. An I/O failure or cancellation can follow earlier safe deletions; repeating collection discovers the remaining work without restoring deleted garbage.

Inactive operation directories may retain private pack hard links or interrupted capture files. They are collected before unreferenced installed objects, with a shared operation lease acquired again before each staged unlink. A resumed owner keeps its private entries for a later pass. Neither `intent`, `lease`, `prepared` nor `cancelled` is removed, and collection never adopts another operation's source cut. An empty directory left before operation-lease creation has no object ownership and is harmless; missing ownership on a nonempty operation is an error. All directory and file access remains relative to pinned capabilities and rejects substituted symlinks and nonregular object files.

`BackupCollectionReport` separates completed/prepared roots, active writers, the union of referenced encoded frame extents, retained whole-object bytes, initially unreferenced object bytes, removed object and staging entries, remaining candidate files and peak encrypted workspace. Shared ranges contribute once. A pack containing one required frame remains whole, so retained object bytes can exceed referenced frame bytes. Removed byte counts are directory-entry lengths, not reclaimed physical blocks or page I/O; staging hard links, external hard links and filesystem allocation affect physical space. Fixed I/O owners and OS directory buffers retain the existing repository accounting boundary. No root, inventory, object, intent or protection format changes.

### Retention preservation argument

Let `C` be the completed roots, `P` the noncancelled durable prepared roots, and `A` the private object names of active capture operations. For each root `r`, let `R(r)` contain its inventory object and every object named by that complete inventory. Reachability is `L = union(R(r), r in C union P) union A`. Union is associative, commutative and idempotent, so root enumeration order, branching and repeated inherited references cannot alter `L`. Parent IDs record provenance and are not edges in `R`: deleting an ancestor manifest therefore does not remove any descendant's required references. Marking all names in `A` can conservatively retain unfinished or unused files, but cannot exclude an active capture's already installed object.

Assume the existing cryptographic and filesystem contract, nonreused operation/object identities, and cooperating owners using the generation and lease protocol. Admission serializes generation replacement with every addition of externally usable references. During discovery, an old completed/prepared root either remains represented in `L`, or a concurrent mutation changes the generation. An active writer's preexisting installed objects have private names and are marked; an installation missed by enumeration must advance the generation before installation. Incremental capture also holds its parent lease until preparation/publication or retirement. A resumed prepared image already has a complete immutable inventory, including inherited objects whose parents no longer exist. Thus successful generation revalidation implies that no admissible live or recoverable owner can require an installed object outside `L` at the deletion boundary.

Manual preparation can introduce references whose originating root is already retired. Its full verification establishes the object condition only for the observed generation. Revalidation under admission prevents a collector from deleting such an object between verification and prepared-descriptor publication: if collection wins, preparation observes a changed generation and fails; if preparation wins, it advances the generation and publishes `P` before collection can enter deletion. A collector with an earlier discovery then fails, and later discovery marks the new prepared root. This closes the verification/publication interval through the owning state transition without requiring caller-side retention repair.

An active reader holds the same operation's shared OS lease; root removal requires an exclusive lease. Consequently no successful removal can hide a root still needed by a live parent/verify/restore/copy reader. The durable cancellation marker precedes root unlink and is already rejected by publication recovery. If interruption leaves the root discoverable, `C` still retains it regardless of that marker. If interruption follows unlink, the marker prevents later resurrection. A completed in-memory receipt returns only its historical result and performs no new installation. These cases preserve both read ownership and one-cut-per-operation identity across retries and process exit.

For a stable generation, deletion selects only installed objects outside `L`. Removing any subset of those objects leaves each `R(r)` for retained roots unchanged. By induction over the bounded deletion sequence, every retained image has exactly the same inventory, framed bytes and previously established query observations. Private links of an inactive operation are separately redundant: recovery reads the installed objects and its prepared descriptor, while an unprepared capture cannot resume with another source. Reacquiring its lease prevents deletion under a newly resumed owner. Removing a private hard link does not change the installed immutable object's bytes. The same induction therefore covers staging cleanup, interrupted batches and repeated collection. Explicit retirement changes the set of retained backup roots, but introduces no SQL or retrieval operator and preserves the established algebra of every image that remains retained.

For accounting, assign each marked object a unique ordinal and order ranges by `(ordinal, offset)`. For one object, add only the positive portion beyond the preceding covered endpoint, taking the maximum endpoint after each interval. Induction over this ordered sequence gives the exact union length, including duplicate and overlapping references across roots. Whole-object retention then follows from membership in `L`, independent of that union length. Spill encoding preserves exact keys and values; shared ciphertext admission and the bounded candidate vector constrain the representation without changing reachability or authorizing partial discovery.

## Fresh redb record image import

Common Storage's `KeyValueImageImportReader` validates the mapping envelope while consuming the same complete repository verifier. An image must name the destination provider and image version 1, have a committed sequence, and begin with exactly one `UQAKVI01` header whose source and sequence equal the root. All units have the fixed mapping namespace and generation zero. Allocator units contain one big-endian watermark and exclude reclamation bookkeeping; record units contain a nonzero committed revision no newer than the cut, followed by opaque owner bytes. Notification delivery state, foreign namespaces, unsupported families and incorrect kind/length combinations are rejected. An importer cannot skip a record's verified end or obtain completion before whole-object and global range verification. Decoder failures release all input and charged workspace; completed progress remains terminal after cancellation or an invalid subsequent read.

`RedbBackupImport` receives an empty private regular file and an already retained restore request. Its physical owner installs record format zero, the exact image root and target identity, the normal table set, and the existing typed legacy-writer fence in one initial transaction. Every subsequent advance writes at most one allocator or record in its own durable transaction. Allocators start a new metadata-history baseline at epoch zero with their original maxima; records have one live version with the exact captured revision. The full reserved redb value, including its live-value tag, and copied key use the original memory allowance. The public redb `insert_reserve` API supplies the writable destination, and repository reads fill it in at-most-16-KiB windows. A configured per-value limit fails before allocation, rather than buffering an oversized unit outside the allowance. Native page-cache and B-tree physical workspace remain provider-owned; no process-RSS or bounded native-I/O claim follows from this decoded-value bound.

Let a verified mapping image at committed cut s be the ordered set of live records R = {(k, r, b)} with 0 < r ≤ s and the allocator map A = {n ↦ w}. The import invariant after each committed unit is that every processed allocator equals its entry in A and every processed record has `HEADS[k] = r` and `VERSIONS[(k,r)] = 1 || b`, with no other imported record version. The initial database satisfies the invariant for the empty prefix. A single-unit transaction adds exactly its previously absent key; ordered inventory validation and duplicate admission prevent replacement. A failed or cancelled transaction leaves the preceding prefix, so induction establishes the invariant for every committed prefix. The database remains format zero throughout, and the ordinary record owner rejects it. The complete verifier and decoder are necessary before the final transition, so a verified complete input establishes the invariant for all of R and A, including empty images and empty live values.

The final physical transaction changes format zero to the current record format and invokes the existing exclusive history-replacement owner with the retained source/target identities. It clears receipt and SSI authority and changes the database identity atomically, while leaving R, A, s and historical data-origin bytes unchanged. Therefore every current committed raw lookup and ordered live-record observation at s returns its original revision and byte string. Identifier requests start from the same maxima, and subsequent commits receive the new history identity. No source transaction receipt is relabeled as a target transaction. A failed final synchronization can recover only the unavailable original prefix or the complete new history; it cannot expose the source history as an ordinary completed database. On a completed target, the existing request resolver recognizes the target identity and performs no history replacement, preserving all later writes. Import itself refuses nonempty files and cannot reapply the image over those writes.

For every deterministic owner operation whose behavior depends only on these raw records and allocator values, this equality supplies the same persisted inputs. Existing logical decoders, artifact dependency checks and catalog restoration remain responsible for the validity of those inputs and their higher-level observations. This argument does not certify arbitrary image contents as valid SQL/catalog/vector state or publish a user-visible destination. The repository restore owner below persists external intent and publishes a completely prepared private directory; its provider adapter must validate logical dependency closure and close every owner before returning success. Public Engine authority, complete logical validators, other provider importers and destination protection conversion remain separate requirements.

The owner tests restore actual DiskANN artifacts, committed revisions, an empty key/value, a large value and allocator watermarks after deleting the source file, then execute the shared artifact restore/write fixture and retry the same history request after new target writes. They bound each import advance to one unit and verify immediate reservation retirement. Further tests reject 22 independently constructed invalid mapping envelopes in both Key/Value formats, absent sequences and unsupported versions, dropped/cancelled/over-limit imports, populated targets, corruption after all unit payloads have been copied, and injected final synchronization failure. The existing closed-backup history tests continue to exercise the shared transition.

## Fresh SQLite Key/Value image import

`SQLiteKeyValueBackupImport` consumes the same Common Storage mapping decoder for the SQLite KV provider. Its connection owner acquires exclusive restoration admission on an existing empty regular file, configures the selected plain, SQLCipher, compressed or encrypted-compressed destination through existing interfaces, and retains physical source identity until closure. The schema owner installs the current table definitions and write guards with metadata format zero, the exact root, source/target identities, captured sequence and allocator epoch zero. The pending restore target rejects ordinary connection opens; both ordinary record initialization and history restoration reject format zero. Other opens wait at the existing physical ownership boundary while an import owns exclusive admission. No external SQLite source or private dependency API changes are required.

For the same verified mapping R = {(k, r, b)} and A = {n ↦ w}, let P be the committed input prefix. The physical invariant is `heads[k] = r`, `versions[k,r] = b`, and the version metadata's payload length equals |b| for every record in P; each processed allocator has watermark A[n] and birth/head metadata epoch zero. Initially P is empty. An allocator transaction inserts its previously absent watermark and epoch together. A record transaction inserts a zero-filled BLOB of exactly |b| bytes; the existing version trigger records its row identity and length. The importer fills that BLOB by concatenating verified chunks in order, consumes the verified end of the unit, inserts its head and commits. Incremental BLOB writes do not alter the row identity or length. Thus a successful transaction extends P by exactly the next decoded unit. On any chunk, cancellation, bound or commit failure, the ordinary transaction owner rolls back or retains an uncertain committed prefix; both alternatives still satisfy the prefix invariant and remain unavailable. Ordered inventory and duplicate-key admission prohibit replacement of an earlier imported record. Induction establishes the invariant for every admitted prefix.

Only complete inventory, payload, whole-object and frame-range verification permits the schema owner to replace format zero with the canonical current metadata definition in one transaction. That transaction retains the source identity and pending target; it cannot expose source-history sessions. Under the same exclusive physical admission, the existing history-restoration state machine updates the auxiliary coordinator and main identity to the exact externally retained target and retires original receipt/SSI authority. Its durable pending state excludes ordinary open between those publications. `SQLiteKeyValueBackupImport::recover` first opens the existing private file under exclusive restore admission and compares its immutable 240-byte root and target identity with the requested image. A mismatch cannot enter the history transition. The existing history owner rejects format zero, completes a matching verified transition or recognizes the existing target without changing later work; recovery never reads or imports archive records again. The importer closes all physical handles before returning completion and refuses every populated import destination, so retry cannot reapply the image over target writes.

Consequently, activation preserves every current raw lookup, ordered live-record observation and identifier maximum at the captured sequence. Empty values, empty mappings with a nonzero cut and original data-namespace bytes retain their meaning. Destination encryption and compression only transform the physical representation beneath these observations; changing them introduces no logical mapping conversion. The existing owner decoders therefore receive identical inputs. As with redb, this is a raw mapping preservation result, not proof of arbitrary catalog or artifact dependency validity. The enclosing restore adapter must validate those dependencies before publishing the private directory. Native SQLite, memory Engine images and complete logical validation remain separate work.

The configured maximum value size is additionally capped by the native incremental BLOB API's signed length range. The importer charges its copied key, SQL parameter bytes and 16 KiB buffer to the original allowance before use; it does not allocate a value-sized UQA buffer. Repository frames and the encrypted global verification ledger retain their existing memory and physical workspace limits. SQLite's native page cache, filesystem allocation and I/O latency remain outside the encoded-buffer bound. Every failed advance drops its reader and physical owner; completed progress resolves before later cancellation. Tests exercise a value larger than the full UQA allowance, actual DiskANN records after source deletion, all sixteen source/destination protection combinations, original revisions and watermarks, fresh transaction identity, later writes, lost history replies, object corruption after copying all units, process exit without destructors, cancellation, exhausted memory/workspace, populated files and invalid constructor options.

## Private restore publication

`LocalRepository::begin_restore(backup, destination, control)` retains a completed root and persists a fresh `RestoreId` and `DatabaseRestore` request outside the target before provider files can be created. The destination is a new directory under a caller-selected, pinned parent; even an existing empty directory is a collision. `BackupRestoreOperation::prepare(importer)` consumes an installed provider adapter through `BackupRestoreImporter`. That adapter owns complete archive/format validation, physical import, logical dependency closure and the provider's exclusive history transition. It must synchronize its data and close every provider handle before success. `RestoreDirectory` only exposes regular files with single-component installed names; archive unit keys cannot become paths. This is a synchronous lower-level interface, not a completed Engine job API or a general logical-image validator.

The authenticated 160-byte intent binds the restore ID, backup ID, complete root digest, source and new target history identities, parent directory identity and digest of the native destination component. The 208-byte initialized descriptor additionally binds the physical private-holder and image directories. Both fit the repository's existing minimum frame allowance. Distinct frame kinds separate intent, initialization, import-start, preparation, cancellation and completion. Each immutable record is written and synchronized under a unique temporary name before a no-replacement hard link installs its final name. Recovery validates an existing record's contents and retries synchronization; an interrupted temporary write cannot occupy a phase's final name. Protection and repository/operation identity are checked before the payload is admitted.

The owner creates a private `.uqa-restore-<id>/image` directory under the destination parent and synchronizes initialization before entering the provider. It persists `started` before invoking fresh import. After interruption, the same operation can only call the provider's recovery method for that exact request; it cannot run fresh import over a populated file. An incomplete provider format can therefore remain unavailable and require a separate attempt. Provider success is followed by a protected preparation marker inside the image and a repository preparation record. A durable image preparation marker lets recovery finish the repository preparation record and publish without reopening the original source or its archive. Cancellation before publication installs a durable terminal record, releases admission and prevents later import/publication; private abandoned-directory reclamation remains a retention requirement.

Publication closes the image handle and moves `image` from its pinned holder to the pinned destination parent in one exclusive rename. Linux uses [`renameat2` with `RENAME_NOREPLACE`](https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man2/rename.2.html), Apple platforms use [`renameatx_np` with `RENAME_EXCL`](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/main/bsd/man/man2/rename.2), and Windows uses the public [`SetFileInformationByHandle`/`FILE_RENAME_INFO`](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/winbase/ns-winbase-file_rename_info) operation with a pinned root handle and replacement disabled. Existing files, directories and symlinks cannot be overwritten, including a collision after the initial existence check. Unsupported operations fail instead of falling back to replacement, copying or an unchecked rename. Source-holder and destination-parent synchronization precede the completion receipt. The local filesystem must support the required exclusive rename, file/directory synchronization and stable directory identity; preparation storage is on the destination filesystem even when the archive is elsewhere.

`resume_restore(id, destination, control)`, publication retry and cancellation first resolve an already installed matching target from its physical directory identity and protected preparation marker. Completed resolution does not invoke an importer, reread the archive, change history or rewrite database contents, and takes precedence over late cancellation. A terminal receipt records the original publication, not the future health of its database. A new recovery attempt rejects a missing or replaced completed destination rather than recreating it. Renaming the selected parent while an operation is live does not redirect publication; a reopened path must resolve to the originally recorded parent and component. Private staging remains exclusively owned by the operation; arbitrary concurrent modification by an actor with its filesystem permissions is outside this protocol, as it is for an open database file.

For preservation, let B identify a verified archive, R its fresh history request, D the exclusively owned image directory and P the initially absent destination entry. Assume the importing owner establishes its exact-image and logical-validation contract before certifying D, and the filesystem implements the operations above. Before `started`, external intent fixes B and R. Every recovery uses those same values, because all later certificates contain that intent and cannot be substituted across frame kinds or directories. Before preparation, no transition can install P. Provider failure leaves D private and cannot affect a preexisting destination. On preparation, the provider contract establishes the query-observable image and new history in D; directory certificates preserve that same D. The exclusive rename has only two outcomes: P was absent and now names D, or an existing P is unchanged. This proves that successful activation exposes precisely the certified image and never replaces an independent database.

After activation, every operation with this restore ID resolves the same physical D and returns its receipt without calling import. For any sequence W of later committed target operations, recovery therefore leaves W(D) unchanged; it cannot reset the image to B or replace its history again. Before activation, durable cancellation is terminal under the exclusive restore lease, so no future admitted owner can publish that cancelled attempt. A lost rename or synchronization reply is resolved from D before cancellation is considered, preserving the completed side of the transition. These invariants prove failure/retry preservation by induction over admitted transitions. Restore introduces no algebraic operator: the importing owner's preserved data supplies the same operation inputs at activation, while subsequent operations retain their ordinary semantics. The argument does not replace the remaining owners' logical dependency proofs.

Repository lifecycle workspace consists of fixed-size descriptors and frames plus one bounded, charged destination component, independent of image cardinality. Record decoding and encryption retain the invoking allowance; provider data, native page caches and physical filesystem I/O retain their separate contracts. Terminal completion/cancellation releases the archive and restore leases and the destination allocation. Owner tests exercise direct kernel no-replacement collisions, pinned-parent movement, wrong staging identity, malformed and phase-substituted markers, process exit, interrupted import, prepared archive loss, cancellation synchronization failure, missing completed targets and lost publication replies. A redb integration fixture imports actual DiskANN state after deleting its source, recovers a closed import, publishes it, mutates it through ordinary storage and resolves the same operation without reimporting. This fixture qualifies the composition for those actual artifacts; complete catalog/resource validation and public Engine support remain outstanding.

## Native pathname interoperation for private SQLite images

`RestoreDirectory::with_file_path` connects a pinned private directory to installed providers that require a filename. It admits only an existing regular file under a validated single-component name, excluding the restore marker. Common Storage owns native path lookup and directory identity; SQLite retains connection modes, physical source identity, exclusive import/recovery admission and history transitions. The callback must close every provider connection before returning and must not retain the path. The operation changes no process-wide working directory and opens no ordinary database file beside SQLite, so the bridge cannot release SQLite's POSIX record locks by closing another descriptor. A provider may replace its own closed regular file, as compressed-container compaction requires; the retained directory, rather than the data file's original inode, is the publication authority.

For retained directory D and installed name n, let P(D) be the absolute path returned by the live native directory handle. Linux/Android use the existing [`/proc/self/fd` interface](https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man5/proc_pid_fd.5.html), Apple uses [`F_GETPATH`](https://developer.apple.com/library/archive/documentation/System/Conceptual/ManPages_iPhoneOS/man2/fcntl.2.html), and Windows uses [`GetFinalPathNameByHandleW`](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/fileapi/nf-fileapi-getfinalpathnamebyhandlew); unavailable lookup or an oversized result is an error. Fixed platform scratch space and the resulting native pathname are charged to the invoking allowance before use and retired on every result. The bridge checks that the parent of P(D)/n resolves to D before calling the provider and again after successful provider closure, and requires n still to denote a regular file relative to D. A parent moved before lookup is resolved at its current location. Movement detected after the callback cannot certify preparation; the durable started state routes retry to provider recovery, which resolves D anew. Lookup requires exclusive ownership of the private staging entries during provider operations. It is pathname interoperation, not a sandbox for arbitrary callbacks or a guarantee against concurrent file replacement by another actor with access to private staging.

Let I be the stored complete image root, T its retained target history and S the repository restore state. Import writes I/T before record transfer and admits the history transition only after complete repository verification. Recovery compares I/T before invoking that same history owner. Therefore successful recovery of a started operation can only complete the original verified transition or observe it already complete; a different image or target cannot alter either coordinator. The existing format-zero check prevents unfinished input from becoming valid through recovery. The fixture adapter then reads the actual restored DiskANN state, closes every connection and returns success. Common Storage writes its protected preparation marker only after callback success and directory identity verification. Its exclusive directory rename therefore publishes this validated fixture exactly once, while later completed-operation resolution performs no image import and preserves subsequent writes. Complete catalog and artifact-dependency validation remains necessary for an arbitrary Engine image; the fixture adapter does not assert it.

Owner tests cover invalid names and nonregular entries, cancellation and exhausted path memory, callback failures, regular-file replacement, parent movement before and during the callback, and complete memory retirement. SQLite composition tests cover all four destination protection modes, source deletion, lost import replies, existing-target collisions, actual DiskANN reads/writes and repeated completed publication after late cancellation. History-boundary tests reject a different root and target before accepting the original request at each existing transition boundary, and partial imports reject image recovery in every file mode.

## Fresh native SQLite image import

`SQLiteNativeBackupImport` owns `Reading`, `Projecting`, `Publishing`, `Complete` and terminal `Failed` states under the same exclusive physical file admission as the Key/Value importer. It accepts an already created empty private regular file and the retained `DatabaseRestore` request. The shared raw-record importer streams original revisions, encoded rows and identifier watermarks into format zero through public incremental BLOB operations. Complete repository verification precedes physical projection. Plain, SQLCipher, compressed and encrypted-compressed destinations reuse existing connection owners and require no dependency modification. The private file stays unavailable to ordinary open and history recovery until its raw and physical contents have been verified.

The native format owner constructs the current schema only for an exact fresh format-zero native import. It reuses catalog schema construction, removes that construction's fixed bootstrap rows before materializing any captured input, and preserves the header's original data namespace. Cache-generation triggers are installed after projection so captured cache records are restored exactly; evaluated equality and coverage projection retain their existing owners. The projection owner reuses its ordinary parent-before-child family order and row publication, visiting at most one staged row per advance. No source expressions, SQL defaults, user functions or analyzers are reevaluated. Ordinary foreign-key enforcement remains enabled throughout physical insertion.

Let R be the finite decoded mapping of distinct record keys to captured revisions and encoded rows, N its stable data namespace, and P_f the final physical rows of native family f. Each staged row is decoded against its complete typed key before insertion; the original raw history is never rewritten by projection. Let c_f be the number of staged rows admitted for f. The verification pass visits every physical primary key exactly once, derives its native key from N and its current owner, validates the owner binding, and requires byte equality with the corresponding staged row. Thus every row in P_f is the image of a distinct member of R: physical primary keys are unique and the validated family/owner/key encoding is injective on these rows. Verification also requires |P_f| = c_f. Since each such staged member belongs to family f and there are exactly c_f staged members, injection plus equal finite cardinality proves bijection. Therefore no missing, extra, overwritten or aliased physical row can pass, and all lookup and ordered-row observations over the native mapping agree with R. Every raw key, revision, autonomous watermark and N is preserved independently of the new transaction-history identity.

Native dependency owners additionally require sequence definitions and value records to be paired, reject repeated sequence object identities in the ordered definition stream, validate every stored graph selector's source and every source's at-most-three derived selectors, and require every DiskANN generation payload to have its bounded published-state metadata. These checks use existing codecs and projection definitions. They establish native physical coherence; complete logical catalog, analyzer/model/resource and artifact-dependency closure remains the enclosing restore adapter's responsibility and is not implied by this mapping proof.

Only after both passes succeed does the format owner install and validate the canonical guards and mapping, and the image schema owner atomically replace format zero with the current record definition while retaining the exact root and pending target. The history owner checks that immutable image binding before touching either coordinator. A current pending image has already passed its physical validation and needs no baseline conversion or whole-image rescan; closed-database restoration retains its ordinary migration and source-validation path. Both admissions use the same durable main/auxiliary transition and uncertainty recovery. Matching recovery rejects format zero, finishes a verified pending transition or recognizes a completed target without importing archive rows again. Completion closes all handles and wins over later cancellation; every failure retires the reader, charged row cursor and exclusive file owner. The optional retained image-binding table and its three write guards are checked as part of native format admission.

Raw transfer uses the configured value bound, a copied key and at-most-16-KiB chunks. Projection and comparison charge one complete native row, its decoded columns, physical/native keys and SQL bindings to the original allowance; a row larger than that allowance fails explicitly. Reader and projection heap owners are also reserved before allocation. One progress call handles one input unit, one physical row or a fixed format/repository boundary. Family counts occupy a fixed array, and ordered cursors avoid corpus-sized sets and collections. Native SQLite caches, schema-metadata lookup work, filesystem allocation and I/O latency retain their existing separate accounting boundary; the admitted-row limit is not a bound on SQLite virtual-machine instructions or wall time. Five automatic work/memory/output gates cover all sixteen protection conversions, source loss with actual DiskANN and graph state, exact raw rows/revisions and sequence state, drop/cancellation and exhausted input/projection bounds, ten well-framed but physically invalid images, all three uncertain history boundaries in each destination mode, and later-write preservation. A repository fixture composes the native importer with pinned private paths, lost import replies, exclusive target publication and completed-operation recovery in all four destination modes. That fixture validates its actual restored artifacts and retains the separate requirement for arbitrary logical-image validation.

## Evaluated memory text documents

Common Storage owns the versioned `UQATXT01` document image for actual `MemoryInvertedIndex` state. The enclosing unit address supplies document identity. An image contains a nonzero little-endian u64 field count followed by strictly ordered UTF-8 field names, each prefixed with its u64 byte length. Every field has the existing 84-byte `IndexedFieldMetadata` encoding and a u64 term count. Each strictly ordered canonical `TokenTermKey` has a u64 byte length, its original tagged bytes, a nonzero u64 occurrence count and its ordered occurrences. An occurrence stores little-endian u32 position and position length, a zero/one offset flag, and, when present, four little-endian u64 original UTF-8/UTF-16 offsets. Equal occurrences remain separate. Empty analyzed fields remain present even with no terms; a document absent from the text corpus produces no unit.

`InvertedIndex::write_image_document` is an explicit provider capability. The memory implementation borrows reverse keys and posting buffers from the selected immutable generation; it does not collect a second field map, vocabulary or occurrence list. Read-only wrappers retain the original cancellation alongside the caller's output allowance. I/O attempts contain at most 4 KiB and check cancellation between partial or interrupted calls. The return count describes emitted bytes, including fixed metadata; it is not a timing or page-I/O estimate.

`RetainedInvertedIndexBuilder::add_image_document` decodes under its original allowance and requires exact standalone input consumption before publication. It rejects invalid versions, encodings, ordering, duplicated fields/terms, empty posting lists, invalid graph edges, decreasing positions, invalid source offsets and inconsistent normalization lengths. Emitted-token length equals the number of occurrences; overlap-discounted length equals the number of distinct start positions across all terms of that field. The distinct-position workspace has admitted ordered nodes. Buffer capacities, reverse names/keys and all corpus nodes are reserved before allocation/publication; failed input or admission releases temporary ownership without changing earlier documents. Existing counter/footprint publication reconstructs the immutable corpus without invoking any analyzer, SQL expression or resource callback.

Analyzer bindings, descriptors and external-resource closure are separate required image components. A field can reference a historical revision independently of the currently selected search configuration. This codec preserves that fingerprint and metadata; it does not establish that the referenced resource exists. The enclosing validated database image must supply those immutable owners before Engine activation. This document-level primitive, including retained reconstruction, does not expose complete memory Engine backup or fresh writable Engine activation.

### Preservation argument for evaluated text documents

Let a valid text document be the finite ordered mapping D from field names f to (m_f, T_f), where m_f is the complete existing field metadata and T_f maps each canonical term key to its nonempty sequence of occurrences in emission order among equal positions. The document identity belongs to its enclosing address. Validity requires the existing occurrence/revision formats, ordered unique fields and terms, valid graph edges and offsets, and the normalization equation above. Descriptor/resource closure is an independent premise supplied by the enclosing database image. Let E be the specified encoder and R its decoder on fully admitted, valid images.

Every scalar in E has a fixed width or an explicit length followed by exactly that many bytes. The fixed tag, strict zero/one flags, existing metadata validator and canonical term-key validator distinguish all encoded alternatives. Therefore decoding the fixed prefix determines exactly the number and boundaries of fields. Induction over fields determines each original UTF-8 name and exact metadata and term count; induction over those terms determines its original tagged key and occurrence count; induction over occurrences recovers every integer and optional offset without deduplication or reordering. Exact input exhaustion excludes an unobserved suffix. Consequently R(E(D)) = D for every admitted valid D. Conversely, every accepted image defines precisely one such D; validation and admission failure produce no new published document.

For a finite corpus C of uniquely addressed documents, reconstruction uses the existing replacement owner only after validating a complete previously absent document. Its field counters add each m_f.length and one field-document membership; the document counter adds one per nonempty field mapping. Induction over imported document identities yields exactly C's postings, reverse memberships, integer field lengths and counts. Corpus totals do not depend on import order, since checked nonnegative integer addition is associative wherever the complete valid total fits; overflowing inputs are rejected before that document is published. The existing legacy position projection, exact occurrence frequency, graph/phrase observations and BM25 inputs are therefore unchanged. Query compositions consume the same carriers and statistics, preserving their existing laws without adding a new algebraic operator or numeric approximation.

Under the lazy capture owner's committed-cut and source-adapter premises, the frozen source supplied to E is the original source at that cut. Substitution into R(E(D)) = D preserves that cut after subsequent source writes and source closure. This establishes the document codec and retained reconstruction only; it does not prove joint Engine capture or completeness of unimplemented resource images. Independent byte fixtures exercise raw UTF-16 keys, equal graph edges and historical metadata; actual memory captures exercise synonym overlaps, original Unicode offsets, empty fields and corpus statistics. Malformed/truncated units, deferred invalid analyzers, exact retained capacities, cancellation and small allowances exercise the publication and resource premises. A 32,768-occurrence export requires no heap scratch and bounds each output call to 4 KiB.

### Writable text image ownership

`RetainedInvertedIndexBuilder::finish_writable` adopts the completely evaluated corpus as a native `MemoryInvertedIndex`. It transfers the original corpus reservation into the shared state and reserves the destination header before publication. The retained default and compiled field bindings stay unchanged; existing native binding mutations prepare an independently writable candidate before validation and replacement. Adoption neither runs analysis nor resolves a deferred default or external resource. The immutable `finish` interface keeps its existing read-only and cancellation semantics.
