Let C be an accepted corpus under the preceding text-image contract, B its supplied analyzer bindings, and W(C, B) the returned native owner. Publication transfers the existing maps, occurrence vectors, counters and B without rewriting them, so every text observation O satisfies O(W(C, B)) = O(C, B). A subsequent native operation a uses the same staging, revision validation, checked counter update and publication as an ordinary memory index. Therefore a(W(C, B)) has the ordinary operation's result and error boundary; induction over any admitted sequence of operations preserves those observations and failure atomicity. A deferred invalid analyzer can still reject a later analyzed write without invalidating the already evaluated corpus. This argument assumes the enclosing image supplies the correct bindings and resources; it does not establish complete Engine logical closure or activation.

The corpus reservation is a field of the shared state and is destroyed after its payload. Shared read and writable snapshots therefore retain the original reservation until the last original root disappears. Native copy-on-write clones allocate independent payloads and do not retain the original reservation, while the old snapshots keep it. Independent deep clones behave the same way. Controlled captures retain their own read allowance and cancellation while sharing the source root; source retirement cannot release that root's input reservation prematurely. The header reservation belongs only to its original index object. Ordinary later writes keep their existing allocation contract; the input allowance does not prepay future writes. Admission or cancellation failure before publication drops the private destination and its retained bindings, leaving no published owner. The independent wire fixture, real compiled revisions, native mutations, shared and deep snapshots, unread source retirement, denied admission and unwinding exercise these ownership and preservation premises.

## Lazy memory source capture

Common Storage's `MemoryCaptureRegistry` registers a process-local cut without enumerating its sources. `MemoryCaptureCell<T>` owns a source's read/write admission and uses `MemoryCaptureSource::freeze_at` to retain an independent immutable value with the cut's opaque identity; independent sources use its default `freeze` implementation. The first read through a cut freezes an unchanged source; the first later write freezes it before returning any mutable guard, and final source retirement preserves it before releasing its live data. Later reads reuse that same retained value. Document and text adapters call their existing controlled snapshot interfaces, preserving evaluated tuples, postings, occurrence metadata, analyzer bindings and original resource ownership without rerunning analysis. Other owners require their own adapters. This primitive does not establish the actual Engine's committed catalog/data/resource boundary or complete its image codecs.

The registry separates capture admission from its live registration index. A write guard retains shared admission through its last mutation; capture registration takes exclusive admission and checks cancellation while waiting. Mapped guards retain the same admission. Nested cell guards use recursive shared admission so a waiting capture cannot deadlock an already admitted operation. An enclosing transaction owner must register cuts at committed boundaries and exclude registration during its multi-cell publication or rollback. Separate nonoverlapping cell guards alone do not make a transaction atomic. Cells registered after a cut are outside that cut, and a cell rejects a capture from another registry even if their local numeric identities match. An unregistered `MemoryCaptureCell::new` allocates no private registry and uses the same state boundary; registration must precede publication in a shared source. Repeated registration in the same domain preserves its identity and birth, while transfer to another domain is rejected. Controlled source reads and writes poll cancellation while waiting for state admission.

Define the eligible cells of a cut q as those registered before q, and let v(x, q) be cell x's value at q's registration. Let F_x be its source-owned freeze operation using q's original control and identity and A_q the cut's initially empty map of frozen values. Assume all logical source mutations pass through the cell's write admission, F_x returns an independent immutable owner preserving the source's existing observations, and registration occurs at the required committed boundary. For an eligible x, the first event after q that could change or observe it is a captured read, an admitted mutation or final source retirement. If it is a read, no preceding mutation has changed v(x, q), so the read installs F_x(v(x, q)). If it is a mutation, write admission installs the same frozen value before exposing mutable state. Retirement installs that value before destroying the source, while exclusive ownership rules exclude concurrent source guards. These are the only insertion paths. Both preserve an existing entry, hence induction over later reads and writes gives A_q[x] = F_x(v(x, q)) for every admitted captured observation.

Registration's exclusive admission orders q after every already live write guard and before every subsequently admitted guard. If a reader observes no archived value immediately before a writer preserves it, the reader checks the archive again after a failed live-source lock attempt. It can therefore select the newly archived value while that writer still owns its mutable guard; it never has to substitute the new live value or wait for a writer that needs the retained read to finish. The archive mutex serializes competing first reads and writer preservation, so one cut installs at most one frozen value for each cell identity. Capture identity and cell-birth admission prevent a later cell from being mistaken for a value that existed at q.

For every existing observation O supported by the frozen owner, the source contract gives O(F_x(v)) = O(v). Substitution in the invariant above yields O(A_q[x]) = O(v(x, q)). Compositions over several selected cells receive their original inputs at q when the enclosing committed-boundary assumption holds. Thus source capture adds no new query operator or numeric approximation and preserves the laws and observations already proved by its document, text and subsequent owner adapters. Neither this result nor the scalar and actual-storage fixtures proves that all Engine state is registered; that remains a distinct integration obligation.

A cut transitions only from active to failed or to destruction. Cancellation or a failed archive/node/source reservation makes it failed, releases its archived owners and removes its live registration before source write admission continues. Every later capture check/read reports the first failure, including after cancellation-token reset; no path repopulates a failed archive. Failure changes no source value and consumes no ordinary mutation, so the source retains its existing success, error and rollback semantics. An archive job must observe this terminal cut state through its source adapter before it can certify completion. Independently retained frozen values remain immutable and keep their original leases; their existence cannot reactivate a failed cut.

Registration admits its fixed capture object and one exact-layout `OwnedMap` node under the invoking allowance before either escapes. A first source observation admits its archive node and complete frozen wrapper before calling the source's controlled freeze. Source payload and generation retention belong to that freeze's original allowance, while allocator bookkeeping and diagnostics retain their existing contracts. Last-handle destruction unregisters the cut and releases its archive; an escaped frozen value retains its own wrapper and payload charges until its final owner drops. The registration index holds weak handles, avoiding a retention cycle. Source writes select one live registration at a time without building an uncharged list of active captures, and release the index lock before invoking or destroying a capture owner.

For c live cuts and m values already retained in a selected cut, registration performs one O(log c) index insertion and no source visits. Archive lookup is O(log m). A write considers the active eligible cuts, but invokes each source freeze at most once per cut; its cost includes the source owner's declared capture/detachment cost. This is not a claim of constant-time writes or complete bounded Engine capture. Tests compare one-source and 8,192-source registration allocation/work, exercise independent cuts, mapped and nested guard admission, exact memory exhaustion, cancellation during admission, last-handle destruction during writer preservation, and deterministic read/write interleavings. Actual document and text fixtures retain the original values and term frequencies after replacement and source closure, including original cancellation and final lease retirement.


## Source keys and independent membership reads

A registered `MemoryCaptureCell<T>` owns a stable shared allocation containing its identity and source lock. `MemoryCaptureKey<F>` retains a weak reference to that allocation, the domain identity and the source birth. It never owns the mutable source value. Unregistered cells keep their inline lock and allocate nothing. `CapturedVectorIndexes` admits each key's fixed source allocation together with its copied field name before publishing its membership entry; the original source may still own its live payload, which is outside the key's retention. Reference-count bookkeeping follows the existing Core allowance contract. Future payload growth does not change this fixed retention or keep future payloads alive after source retirement.

Let k identify eligible source x and let q select its generation. If k upgrades its weak source, `captured(q)` enters x's ordinary selected-generation reader, which either returns A_q[x] or freezes v(x, q) through the same lock and archive boundary. If the weak upgrade fails, the owning cell has completed retirement: its destructor preserves q while it still owns a strong reference, before dropping the source. Therefore the reader finds A_q[x] or observes q's terminal failure. A concurrent reader may temporarily retain the registered source allocation, but source retirement preserves its archive before the owning cell returns. If unwinding destroys a source without an archived value, a subsequent key read invalidates the incomplete cut instead of substituting current membership. The first terminal failure is preserved even if later cancellation or another source failure races that observation.

These cases exhaust key observation and preserve A_q[x] = F_x(v(x, q)) under the assumptions of the preceding source-capture argument. Domain and birth checks reject foreign cuts and newly created sources; the immutable source identity prevents same-name replacements from supplying old images. Vector membership now reads each key directly and never requires the current collection or a retained mutable table. Its exact, IVF, HNSW and DiskANN images keep their previously proved evaluated bytes and controls. No query operation or score law changes. The tests exercise unchanged live children while their collection is exclusively borrowed, replacement, unread retirement races, later payload growth, independent admission, cancellation and final lease release. This source identity boundary is a prerequisite for admitted Engine membership; committed multi-source selection, temporary-object projection and bounded catalog publication remain separate requirements in #1346.

## Immutable source selection

Common Storage's `MemoryCaptureSources` represents a finite immutable set S of numeric source identities from one registry. `with_source` and `without_source` use Core's persistent map under the membership owner's allowance; a domain mismatch or denied construction publishes no successor. The set stores neither mutable source handles nor source payloads. The publication owner retains its selected source generation until registration. `capture_selected` admits the complete map footprint before sharing its root and then uses the same exclusive capture admission as unrestricted `capture`. The root belongs to the active capture state, so terminal failure releases both membership and archived values.

Let E_q be the sources eligible by domain and birth at cut q, and define its selected carrier as E_q intersected with S. The original capture argument establishes A_q[x] = F_x(v(x, q)) for each eligible source that reaches preservation. Selection tests immutable membership under the same archive mutex before either the first read or the first write/retirement insertion. For x in S, the insertion and failure transitions are unchanged, so the existing induction proves the same equality. For x outside S, preservation returns without invoking F_x or allocating an archive entry, and reads reject the request without changing an otherwise active cut. Thus the selected archive is exactly the restriction of the original source observations to S; it cannot contain private or unrelated source values absent from S. Domain/birth checks still reject foreign or later incarnations, and numeric identity never follows a reused object name.

Adding a key implements set union with its singleton and removing it implements set difference. Core's immutable map argument proves these operations preserve all predecessor roots. Consequently repeated addition/removal is idempotent, updates of distinct keys commute, and every cut's S remains unchanged by later membership publication. Unrestricted capture retains its original E_q carrier. Query and image observations of every selected source therefore preserve their existing operations and laws; this selection adds no query operator or numeric approximation. Engine must still choose the matching committed generation, eligible catalogs and autonomous overlays before this source-level theorem applies to a complete backup.

A root with n identities reaches n fixed node and entry layouts and no separately allocated key/value payloads. Its cached cardinality admits that exact structural footprint under each cut's original allowance in constant work with respect to n; cloning its root allocates no heap storage or source data. The registry's existing registration-index work depends on active cuts rather than source count. Each later source check uses logarithmic membership lookup, and unselected preservation allocates nothing. Independent cuts reserve independently and retain no later mutable source payload. Registration failures release the selection lease before returning; cancellation or source failure releases active membership and archive ownership without preventing ordinary writes or invalidating another cut. Detached frozen values retain their existing independent leases and controls.

Fixtures exercise selected and excluded live/retired sources, reads while an excluded writer is held, successor roots, empty selections, foreign domains, later births, independent failure/cancellation, every insufficient registration allowance, exact admission and final lease release. Registering one versus 8,192 identities allocates identical heap bytes and visits no source; membership admission still grows with its retained footprint. These finite cases exercise the general selection and retention obligations above.

## Immutable table membership and deletion

Core's `BudgetedSharedMap` owns immutable ordered-map deletion and snapshot iteration; Engine's `TableRegistry` owns the joint live-table and durable source-membership publication. A membership entry contains its exact relation name, immutable object/OID/persistence attributes and weak identities for all 22 table sources. It contains no `Arc<TableState>`. Temporary tables are excluded before their source keys or payloads are copied. Insertion and removal prepare a replacement membership root before publishing the corresponding live-table change. Rename checks destination/source admission inside this owner and rebinds the existing source identities to the destination name; it does not resolve a replacement table's sources. Memory undo retains only the membership root, using the existing undo-owned table handles when restoring the live lookup map. It does not retain another live table-map owner during the transaction.

For the map proof, let an AVL tree represent finite partial function M over a totally ordered key domain, with unique keys, accurate subtree heights and height differences at most one. Define deletion D_k(M) as M restricted to keys other than k. A missing-key search returns the original root. At a selected leaf or single-child node, replacing the node with its child removes exactly k. At a two-child node, the least right-subtree entry s is greater than every left-subtree key and less than every other right-subtree key; replacing k by s and recursively deleting that first right entry therefore represents exactly D_k(M). Reconstructing the search ancestors changes only that selected child. Structural induction proves the resulting ordered sequence and every surviving key/value association equal D_k(M).

Deletion decreases a child height by at most one, so a reconstructed node's imbalance is at most two. If it is left-heavy and its left child has nonnegative balance, one right rotation restores balance; a zero-balanced child leaves the rotated subtree's original height, and a positive-balanced child decreases it by one. A negative-balanced left child requires left-then-right rotations, after which all three local balances are within one and the subtree height decreases by one. The mirror cases establish the same result for a right-heavy node. Every rotation preserves its in-order sequence, since it only reassociates the same strictly ordered subtrees and entries. Induction back to the root therefore restores the AVL invariant while preserving D_k(M). Lookup and ordered iteration consequently agree with the finite-map interpretation. In particular, D_k(D_k(M)) = D_k(M), D_a(D_b(M)) = D_b(D_a(M)), and deletion from the empty map is the identity; existing insertion/lookup composition follows pointwise from D_k's definition.

Construction copies nodes along the search and successor paths and detaches any off-path rotation pivots before mutating them. Existing entries and unaffected subtrees are shared without copying their keys or values. Hence no node reachable from the predecessor is mutated, and induction over arbitrary insertion/deletion/root-retirement sequences preserves every retained snapshot's observations. At each reconstructed level, one new node and at most two off-path pivots suffice. Reserving three node layouts per such level before construction bounds admission by O(log |M|). Comparisons finish before construction; failed admission returns without changing the predecessor. Missing keys and removing a root with at most one child need no node allocation. Snapshot iteration uses the existing bounded AVL traversal stack without heap allocation. A root with n entries reaches exactly n node layouts and n entry layouts, so its structural payload charge is n times their summed sizes; separately owned key/value buffers and reference-count bookkeeping retain their existing contract.

For Engine composition, write L for the live table map and P(L) for its non-temporary projection with each table replaced by its immutable attributes and original weak source identities. Initially both maps are empty. Successful insertion and removal perform the same name operation on L and P(L), while temporary entries affect only L. A successful rename transports the same attributes/identities between the same two keys in both maps. Failure publishes neither candidate. Restoring a memory transaction reinstalls its saved membership root and the matching table handles already owned by its undo frame. Induction over these owner operations establishes membership = P(L) at completed operation boundaries, including savepoint rollback and same-name replacement. The source-key proof then supplies every selected table component from its original incarnation after later mutations or Engine closure; later mutable table payloads are not retained by membership.

Capture retains one immutable root, its cardinality and its checked aggregate name/source-allocation footprint. It admits the root's structural bytes plus that footprint under the invoking allowance, without traversing the table corpus, and uses the common controlled captured-value owner with the original cut identity. Source cells independently admit the actual table data and metadata when read or displaced. An underfunded membership cut becomes terminal without blocking ordinary publication or another funded cut; final root/source retirement releases their respective allowances. These claims cover the table-membership component, not a joint committed Engine cut: shared-catalog temporary filtering, private/committed selection, autonomous overlays and bounded metadata preservation remain required in #1346.

Core fixtures compare retained roots with an independent `BTreeMap`, exercise deletion rotations in both insertion/removal orders, deny intermediate admission, use borrowed keys and verify a 32,768-entry removal under an 8,192-byte allocation ceiling with at most 64 comparisons. Engine fixtures exercise real DDL, rename, same-name replacement, unread source closure, original object/OID identities, all 22 source-wrapper allowances, transaction/savepoint restoration, temporary payload exclusion and independent failed/funded cuts. Capturing one versus 128 tables allocates the same fixed bytes, and transaction admission retains no additional live table-map owner. An additional persistent-registry fixture retains 127 of 128 table identities in place with zero allocation and exactly one predicate call per entry. These tests exercise the proof obligations; the argument above establishes their general semantics.

## IVF mutation admission and retained model catalogs

[`IVFIndex`](../../crates/uqa-storage/src/ivf_index/owner.rs) owns one `MemoryCaptureCell` containing plain evaluated IVF state. Its canonical vectors, normalized coordinates, centroids, assignments, lists, counters and probe setting have no independent interior-mutable aliases. Replacement, deletion, clearing, explicit training, `set_nprobe` and stale-query training all acquire this owner's write admission. Pure queries use shared read admission. A query validates its input and zero-k case before considering training; after acquiring exclusive admission it trains only if the state remains stale. Controlled queries retain their own training workspace and cancellation. The existing numerical algorithms, operation order and scored-posting construction are unchanged.

`register_memory_capture` attaches an index before publication in a memory domain. `captured` returns an export-only `VectorImageSnapshot` at the selected cut. Its evaluated data and original allowance remain together after source mutation or closure; it exposes no query that could retrain a saved stale generation. A first observation retains a controlled detached copy using the existing IVF workspace bound, so registration does not scan vectors and preservation can still cost O(vectors + coordinates + centroid-list entries). An archive retention failure terminally invalidates that cut and leaves the ordinary source operation available. Joint Engine transaction registration, membership capture and complete image activation remain separate integration requirements.

Let S be the complete IVF state tuple encoded by `UQAVEC01`, and let Q be an existing read operation and T an existing state transition. The owning read guard exposes one unchanged S through Q, and the owning write guard excludes other state observations through T. Every mutating entry point reaches that write guard, including training invoked by a shared query API; private candidate construction is not externally observable. Consequently each completed concurrent observation is ordered before or after a complete transition, never between assignments and centroid/list publication. The numerical functions receive the same ordered vectors and parameters as before, so each such sequential observation preserves its existing scores, ordering and training result. Applying the lazy-capture invariant to this owner yields F(S at q) for every eligible cut q. Export-only access cannot apply T to F(S), and the existing vector-image round-trip proof therefore applies to the original canonical and derived fields at q. No algebraic operator, optimizer rewrite or new numeric approximation is introduced.

Engine's model catalog retains `Arc<DeepModel>` values inside its existing immutable catalog root. For a catalog mapping M from names to model values, a snapshot shares M; a subsequent publication creates a distinct map root while retaining unchanged immutable model owners. Inserting or removing a name changes only the new mapping. Model replacement installs a new owner, and rollback restores the previous root. Induction over publication, replacement, removal and rollback preserves the complete layer/parameter value at each name in every earlier root. Public owned model results and incoming save arguments keep their existing copy semantics, so application mutation cannot modify a retained catalog value. This changes catalog retention cost, not inference, serialization or model validation. A two-MiB model fixture verifies zero allocation for root capture and less than 64 KiB of total allocation for publishing a separate small model, with retained pointer identity and original values after source closure; transaction rollback and independently mutable public results are checked separately.

## Memory vector membership and Engine source admission

`MemoryCaptureKey<F>` retains only domain, source identity and birth, independently of the mutable source. An unmodified live source may have no frozen value yet. Its final `MemoryCaptureCell` destruction preserves every eligible cut before destroying the payload. Keys can therefore select an admitted image after the live owner and registry disappear; reservation failure or cancellation instead invalidates the affected cut. `try_write` uses the same preservation boundary and does nothing when source-lock admission fails.

A registered vector collection freezes only an ordered, budgeted map from field names to source keys. It never captures a child while holding the parent archive lock. Each key names an export-only `VectorImageSnapshot` produced by that concrete owner. Exact, HNSW and memory DiskANN mutations enter an exclusive adapter; IVF uses its own state boundary, including query-triggered training. The mutable collection adapter registers new indexes before publication and retires previous indexes before replacement, removal or whole-map replacement. It never exposes mutable boxes or the raw map that could replace a registration without admission. Failed incoming registration leaves current membership unchanged. A captured collection carries the exact cut identity and rejects another cut even when both use the same memory allowance.

Let M(q) map each field present at cut q to its immutable source identity. Collection preservation gives exactly M(q) before the first later membership mutation or retirement. For every x in its range, the source preservation theorem gives A_q[x] = F_x(v(x,q)) after its first read, mutation or retirement. An exporter resolves x from A_q before consulting live membership, and consults a live index only when its identity equals x. Unpublication preserves x before it can disappear from that lookup. Thus replacement under the same field, renaming, DROP and continued mutation of a detached index cannot substitute a later source. Binding the collection to q prevents combining M(q) with A_r for another r. A stale IVF query preserves its own source before training even when the collection has never frozen. Consequently composition over captured fields receives the exact original evaluated vector inputs; no search, training, topology construction or normalization is rerun to reconstruct the image.

Actual memory Engine table creation registers non-temporary document, text and vector owners in one domain before publishing the table. Persistent providers, temporary tables and read-only portal adapters use unregistered cells. Ordinary DML, TRUNCATE, index publication and transaction rollback reach these owning boundaries. Table membership now uses the existing immutable catalog-root owner: taking its root shares names and table handles; a later insertion or removal changes a different root. This establishes constant-allocation membership retention; the [table metadata owner](#memory-table-metadata-generations) separately preserves each mutable table component. Selecting committed state while a transaction is private, pinning the complete durable catalog and autonomous state, resource admission and complete image activation remain separate integration obligations.

Frozen collection nodes, names, keys and headers use the original capture allowance. Each physical image retains its own existing source reservation and cancellation contract. Source closure cannot retain unadmitted payloads through weak keys; terminal capture failure releases its owned archive while normal mutation and destruction continue. Native vector membership now retains an immutable ordered field-to-key root and a matching exact identity set at publication. First preservation shares that field root and independently admits its cached complete footprint; it does not visit fields or copy names. Per-owner physical snapshot costs remain unchanged. Tests cover all four memory vector methods, same-name replacement, a detached live owner, mismatched cuts, stale IVF training before collection preservation, registration failure, independent limits, final source loss, and actual Engine DML/DDL/rollback. Actual one-table and 128-table engines with eight-KiB rows allocate less than four KiB when registering a cut and retaining membership; this does not claim that complete Engine backup has been delivered.

### Native vector membership preservation

Let a native generation be `(F, S, B)`, where `F` is the ordered map from a field to its immutable source key, `S` is exactly the set of those source identities, and `B` is the cached sum of name capacities and fixed source allocations retained by weak keys. Each map entry separately owns its construction reservation. Insertion first registers the incoming physical owner, constructs the admitted successor field/key and identity paths, and computes the successor footprint. Removal and whole-map replacement prepare the corresponding complete successor before retiring or replacing any current physical owner. A failed preparation therefore leaves `(F, S, B)` and every current index unchanged. Successful publication establishes `S = range(F)`; the preceding source-preservation argument applies to exactly those incarnations, including names reused for different physical owners.

The immutable root representation preserves ordered-map lookup and iteration by induction on insertion and removal: untouched entries retain the same keys and values, the selected insertion replaces or adds exactly its supplied field, and removal deletes exactly that field. Structural path sharing changes neither the map nor vector image observations. At capture, independently reserving `structural_bytes(F) + B` before retaining the root covers all reachable nodes, entry storage, names and weak source allocations under that cut's allowance; reference-count bookkeeping retains the existing memory contract. Root sharing does not retain a mutable index or permit a later incarnation to replace an earlier one. Because both cardinality and `B` are maintained during publication, capture needs constant work and no field traversal. Each physical image still receives its own controlled admission when resolved. Cancellation and final release follow the original captured owner even after source closure. No vector evaluation, scoring, training, topology, wire encoding or optimizer rewrite changes.

### Published exact source membership

`MemoryCaptureMembership` owns disjoint published members `G_i`, each an immutable set of source identities from one registry. Its selected root is `U = union_i G_i`. Publication of a fresh member requires the same registry and disjointness from the surviving members; replacement prepares `U' = (U - G_old) union G_new`. A child update prepares the corresponding difference and union in both its member and `U`, rejecting missing predecessors and overlap before either root changes. Removal prepares `U - G_i`. All fallible path construction and member-entry admission precede publication, so induction from the empty owner establishes both exact union membership and failure preservation after every operation. A snapshot shares the already-published root; registering its cut visits neither groups nor sources.

Update addresses contain a weak publication-owner reference and a monotonically allocated member identity. Replacement and removal retire that identity permanently; later updates through it return without inserting sources. Reattaching the same physical collection publishes a new member. Consequently mutations through a retained handle to a removed table or collection cannot reintroduce it into later selections, while earlier selected cuts keep their original source obligations. The vector publication adapter changes the member's selected source identities before publishing its matching prepared native field root; a rejected global update leaves both live and native membership unchanged. The enclosing Engine transaction/publication boundary must still keep selection and cut registration consistent with complete table and catalog publication. These Storage interfaces do not themselves establish that Engine boundary or expose complete backup jobs.

### Engine live source publication

Let `T` be the live table registry, `D` its non-temporary relations, `M` its immutable name-to-source metadata root and `U` its published exact source set. For each `t` in `D`, the corresponding member contains exactly its table component identities and its current native vector child identities. Table creation prepares `M`, the complete source group and its vector update address before installing the live handle. Removal prepares the successor metadata and removes the published member before unpublishing the handle. A rename changes the metadata name and update-address lookup while keeping the same physical source identities. Temporary tables have no member. Induction on these operations and the disjoint-member proof establishes that `dom(M) = D` and `U` contains exactly the component and child identities named by the current durable tables.

Whole-registry replacement prepares every disjoint table group before taking vector guards. This detects aliased table owners before the same vector lock could be acquired twice. It then admits all child identities while retaining those guards and binds the new addresses only after every preparation succeeds. No fallible operation follows binding. The registry owner publishes the prepared live map, metadata root and publication owner within its existing operation boundary. Replacing the owner retires all previous update addresses; a retained detached table cannot mutate the successor selection. Any preparation failure discards only the private candidate and leaves the preceding handles, metadata, groups and child bindings unchanged.

Memory rollback belongs to the transaction state machine: it restores the original physical store values and metadata first, then builds the published groups from those restored source incarnations and installs the original immutable membership root with its restored handles. In particular, writable vector restoration can create fresh child keys; publication selects those keys rather than keys from the discarded private generation. Failure remains a rollback failure handled by the transaction owner. Persistent catalog reload instead retains a typed restoration token for its original registry; the token restores logical provider handles without allocating a memory source generation. Memory catalog epoch observation follows the same reload owner without a provider restoration token, because it does not replace memory table membership. This preserves the existing refresh and failure behavior while preventing logical reload from substituting for physical memory undo.

The idle cut adapter holds transaction admission and the idle statement gate while retaining `U`, `M` and the durable catalog roots. It constructs a fixed number of catalog source wrappers and registers the union of the table source root and that fixed catalog root under the matching catalog identifier cut. Union registration independently admits both roots and retains them without merging their nodes; membership tests use set union, and a repeated identity is preserved once in the cut's identity-keyed archive. Thus registration work and allocation are independent of table and vector-field counts, while the admitted retained footprint still accounts for every reachable root node. Source retirement after registration occurs outside operation admission. An active transaction continues to select its outer committed source generation through the existing boundary, preserving autonomous identifier and sequence observations. These adapters establish bounded internal idle selection and native publication; complete Engine image encoding, activation and public backup jobs remain separate requirements.

The capture registry owns its weak registration entry, while each cut and detached value own their respective admitted retention. Dropping the last live registry releases exactly that registration entry without releasing the cut archive or changing any captured value. Engine lifecycle tests retain the registry separately when comparing unchanged payload admission, then release it before observing the detached values. The Storage lifetime test checks the exact registration charge separately from the final value charge. Neither observation requires retaining a mutable source owner, and cancellation and final release continue to use the original capture allowance.

## Immutable transaction generations

Engine stores each transaction-begin and savepoint undo owner in an `Arc<EngineDataSnapshot>`. That owner separates the exact mutable table handles needed for rollback from an `Arc<EngineSnapshotData>` containing the selected document/text/vector views, evaluated value-index caches, per-table metadata/scalars, weak table membership and immutable durable catalog roots. Foreign memory source registrations remain in the undo owner. Per-table catalog fields share their existing `CatalogCell` roots, as the durable catalog already does. Retaining only the data generation does not retain a mutable `TableState`, an Engine or a session. This internal source generation is not a public or independently admitted backup image.

Let D_t denote the generation selected at transaction or savepoint boundary t, and let R_i denote each immutable metadata root in D_t. Sharing D_t changes only its ownership count. A live metadata mutation uses `Arc::make_mut`: if R_i has a retained observer it constructs another root before mutation, and otherwise no saved observer can see the mutation. A rollback installs R_i itself instead of a cloned equivalent. Consequently, by induction over ordinary writes, DDL, error recovery, savepoint restoration and object replacement, every retained D_t keeps its selected metadata values and physical snapshots. Replacing a relation under the same name cannot change the retained identity; restoration uses the original handle. Later source closure releases live ownership without changing D_t. The existing physical owners supply their separate data-preservation obligations.

Statement failure selects the last savepoint generation when one exists and the frame's initial generation otherwise. Sharing that selected owner preserves the prior restoration choice, session-state restoration, failed-transaction admission and transaction completion semantics. Nested failure and failed completion may drop the live frame after retaining its generation; the reference keeps that generation alive through recovery. Autonomous sequence positions remain restored by the existing generation-qualified history after catalog restoration, rather than being overwritten by a claimed complete committed snapshot. No SQL operation or algebraic rewrite is added, and no query, expression, analyzer or vector training is executed to share a generation.

For each included relation key k, construction inserts its original handle into H_t and its captured values into D_t in the same iteration. Both maps apply the same provider/temporary-table predicate, and neither is published until construction succeeds; hence their domains are equal. Restoration selects H_t[k] and writes exactly D_t[k], including the same physical snapshot reconstruction and metadata root installation as before the ownership split. Thus separating the pair preserves restoration by every key, including same-name replacement. The data generation has no edge back to H_t, so dropping the undo and live Engine owners releases the mutable table even while D_t remains observable. This ownership claim does not imply that the internal generation has already filtered all temporary catalog objects or admitted their retained payloads to a backup allowance.

The outer frame's data generation is separately readable while an application callback holds the statement gate. A deterministic fixture stops that callback after private writes and a savepoint, acquires the transaction-state mutex without waiting, and retains the outer data Arc with zero allocation. The selected documents still show the state before the outer transaction, while subsequent callback completion and later writes do not change them. A separate fixture preserves outer and savepoint values through replacement, rollback, commit and source closure and verifies that a weak reference to the original live table cannot be upgraded. An actual backup still needs an admitted selection boundary, matching catalog filtering and autonomous sequence/identifier overlays; merely cloning this internal Arc is not complete capture.

Retaining an already-created data generation has constant metadata allocation and does not clone table definitions or the table map. Creating the initial generation still enumerates relations and obtains the existing writable physical snapshots and evaluated-key/foreign-memory state; it is not a constant-time complete backup cut. A large immutable column definition is shared without copying its payload. Backup retention limits, committed/private selection and the remaining resource owners are independent requirements. Regression fixtures exercise one and 64 tables under a 64-KiB recovery-allocation bound, a 512-KiB column default under a 64-KiB initial snapshot-allocation bound, and failed SQL, savepoints, DDL, same-name replacement, rollback, commit and final Engine closure while preserving distinct generations.

## Transaction table source generations

Each outer memory transaction owns a capture registry for its original committed data generation. Private nested frames and savepoints retain unregistered undo generations. Its non-temporary tables register the immutable physical views already required by undo, the evaluated named value-index caches and the 18 original table metadata roots with their existing producers. Each table entry retains its original object identity, catalog OIDs, persistence and ON COMMIT behavior. The immutable selection contains all 22 table-source identities, the exact vector child identities and the generation's table-membership source. Temporary tables remain part of undo where required but enter neither this registry nor its selection. Persistent undo remains unregistered. The integration fixtures register cuts against the outer frame's generation; Engine adapts that committed cut through its public memory backup jobs.

Let D_t be an immutable original committed data generation and let P(D_t) restrict its table map to non-temporary relations. For every component i of relation k in that projection, construction assigns a fresh identity s(t,k,i) whose source value is D_t[k,i]. Registration domains are distinct for distinct generations and for the live Engine. The selected set S_t contains exactly these identities, the selected vector children and its membership identity. The existing selected-source theorem therefore yields observation(F(s(t,k,i))) = observation(D_t[k,i]) for each successful captured component. Neither a private live successor nor an unregistered savepoint generation can enter S_t. Names only index this immutable membership: rename, replacement or removal after t cannot substitute another object for its original identity.

Physical sources reuse the original writable snapshots once, and metadata sources share the original immutable Arc roots with undo. The new registry supplies identity and retention admission, not another evaluation or interpretation of stored data. Restoration still installs the selected original metadata roots and reconstructs writable physical views through the same storage interfaces. The integer-key mapping flag is retained with its matching table values. Named value-index caches preserve their already evaluated keys; text and vector images use the original owning codecs. Thus the change preserves the existing document support, values, postings, rankings, identifiers and rollback semantics without introducing a query operator, numeric approximation or optimizer rewrite.

An accepted cut retains the admitted selection and membership roots, not the raw transaction data Arc or its live table handles. On first read, the native producer admits its selected source under that cut's original allowance. On transaction-generation retirement, membership is preserved before component owners disappear; each registered owner then preserves its unread selected value before releasing its last strong reference. Every child vector source is selected explicitly, so collection retirement cannot leave an unread image without its original source. Induction over read, transaction/savepoint completion, source retirement and final Engine closure preserves the selected observations. A detached returned component retains its own leases and original cancellation even after the cut handle is dropped. Insufficient admission or cancellation invalidates only that cut and cannot consume COMMIT, ROLLBACK or another cut's allowance.

Cut registration shares two previously constructed immutable roots and uses their cached structural and payload footprints. It performs no table or vector-child traversal and invokes no table producer. Its fixed allocation is independent of the table count; admission still accounts for the full retained membership. Constructing a transaction generation still visits its tables and vector membership, and final retirement may preserve unread selected payloads through their native producers. This component consequently establishes fixed-work cut admission, not bounded transaction publication or retirement. Shared durable catalogs, autonomous sequence/identifier overlays, the no-active-transaction publication boundary and the complete Engine cut remain separate requirements.

Fixtures exercise a private callback with a one-MiB current value and temporary payload beside a 64-KiB capture, outer-message private definitions, rename/replacement identities, savepoint rollback and transaction completion, every table wrapper's original allowance, independent failed/cancelled/funded cuts and final lease release. Exact, IVF, HNSW and DiskANN cases compare original text/vector image bytes and evaluated expression-index values after unread source closure; retained vector images still obey the original cancellation after their cut is dropped. One-versus-128-table registration has identical allocation and leaves all 22 table sources unread. These cases exercise the stated integration obligations without claiming the unfinished complete backup boundary.

## Committed source ownership within transaction frames

Let a transaction stack contain frames F_0 through F_n, with immutable undo generations D_0 through D_n and any saved intermediate generations U_i. Only D_0 describes the committed state before the active transaction. The transaction coordinator chooses `CommittedOriginal` when admitting the outer frame and `PrivateUndo` when admitting a nested frame or savepoint. The former constructs the existing source selection S(D_0); the latter constructs the same undo values without a capture registry, component keys or composite catalog sources. Idle internal selection chooses a committed original while holding the existing operation and transaction admission. Persistent undo remains unregistered under either boundary.

The committed selector reads only F_0's source owner while the stack is nonempty. Replacing source registrations for every D_i with i greater than zero, and every U_i, by the empty set therefore cannot change its selected domain, source values or independent admission: none of those identities was eligible for S(D_0). The original registered owner continues through exactly the same source-preservation lifecycle. Private undo still retains each original table handle, physical view, metadata root and durable registry used by restoration; its interpretation and the selected rollback target are unchanged. Thus nested completion, savepoint rollback and failure recovery preserve their original state transitions without constructing an unused backup domain.

Autonomous sequence history is recorded in every live frame independently of capture registration. An outer source owner joins that history to the original sequence definitions; an unregistered nested frame uses the existing history-only owner. Both receive identical value records and preserve their original object and definition incarnations. Removing a nested source domain neither removes its rollback history nor changes the outer frame's record. Induction over nextval/setval, nested completion and rollback therefore preserves both the native nontransactional position and each already selected committed sequence image.

Private undo now performs no backup-source registration, family projection or capture-preservation work. Its ordinary physical undo construction and catalog retention remain, including work proportional to the number of undo tables; no constant-time transaction-snapshot claim follows. Fixtures verify both nested COMMIT and ROLLBACK, savepoint restoration, original table metadata and row values, autonomous sequence positions, source closure, cancellation and final admission release. Other native catalogs and the production idle publication boundary retain their separate unfinished requirements.

## Transaction definition catalog generations

A transaction's source domain registers its original domain, enum, composite, range, shell, schema, view and routine roots alongside the original tables and sequence records. Engine supplies these immutable roots and their existing SQL producers; it performs no type, namespace, routine or view interpretation. SQL selects durable namespace members before reserving result capacity or copying names, authority, syntax and evaluated view rows. Individual type and routine capture still preserves a complete supplied definition, while catalog capture excludes temporary namespace members. Routine selection borrows the canonical registry key, decoding quoted identifiers without allocating an intermediate name. A temporary-looking local name in a permanent namespace remains selected; malformed registry names fail under the original production allowance.

For each original catalog C at boundary t, define the durable projection P(C) as its ordered entries whose type identity, decoded schema name or routine registry-key namespace is not a temporary namespace. For views, use the existing persistence predicate instead. This definition assumes the ordinary catalog invariants: a type's recorded identity and a routine's canonical key identify their registered namespace. The owning publication and restoration code already enforces those invariants. Let F be the existing admitted definition constructor. Capture emits exactly [(k, F(v)) | (k, v) in P(C)], preserving key order. Selection examines only the original borrowed identity; the first pass counts that domain, and the second copies exactly its members. Therefore adding excluded entries changes neither retained result capacity nor copied payload admission, including for an excluded entry whose payload alone exceeds the entire allowance.

Projection is idempotent, P(P(C)) = P(C), and preserves the relative order and complete values of surviving entries. Each existing constructor preserves object identities, OIDs, role incarnations, ACL distinctions, type definitions, bound query structure, source bodies and materialized value representations. Their prior preservation arguments therefore imply equality of every observation over P(C) before and after capture. Selection neither interprets source routine text nor invokes a routine or query. No new algebraic operator, optimizer rewrite or numeric approximation is introduced; ordinary SQL execution, undo and namespace publication retain their existing inputs and behavior.

Each registered definition cell owns an immutable original Arc root and has a fresh identity in the same domain as the transaction tables. Private writes detach from those roots through existing catalog publication; later savepoints retain unregistered undo values. The selected-source theorem consequently preserves the joint original table and definition observations. Reading, COMMIT, ROLLBACK, private replacement, schema deletion, name reuse and final Engine closure cannot substitute a live successor for a selected definition. Generation retirement preserves each unread selected root through its native producer before releasing the source. Captured results retain neither the raw undo generation nor live Engine handles.

Registration admits the eight weak source keys and the fixed source allocations they pin before exposing the internal cut. Payloads receive independent admission at first preservation. A failed or cancelled constructor publishes no partial image, and dropping the final result releases its leases. Returned definitions continue to obey the original capture cancellation after their cut and Engine are gone. The number of catalog families is fixed, so registration traverses neither definition bodies nor catalog entries. First preservation and retirement may still visit selected catalogs; this does not establish bounded publication or retirement.

Owner fixtures compare retained allocation with and without one-MiB temporary names or routine bodies and large excluded ACLs; type fixtures try every insufficient allowance and the exact admitted footprint. Engine fixtures select unread definitions beside private writes and savepoints, complete both COMMIT and ROLLBACK, replace the schema and close the Engine before comparing original identities and definitions; range records are published and mutated through their native catalog owner. A long callback fixture captures original definitions under 64 KiB beside a one-MiB temporary default. These obligations concern the retained transaction boundary; the remaining shared catalog families, autonomous allocation overlays, no-active-transaction publication, complete Engine image and public jobs remain separate requirements.

## Transaction authority and resource generations

The transaction source domain extends its original roots to roles, memberships, database/builtin/system authority, foreign wrappers and servers, trigger/rule definitions, graph identities, and graph/model/path/analyzer resources and scoring settings. Execution owns the foreign-table membership/authority join. SQL owns trigger/rule namespace selection and every existing catalog constructor. Engine registers retained original roots and owning producers; it performs no authorization, syntax analysis, graph traversal, model evaluation or resource reconstruction.

Let D_t be the immutable transaction generation, F_t its ordered foreign-table definition map and A_t its matching authority map. Define K_t = {k in dom(F_t) | F_t[k].persistence is not Temporary}. The capture is the ordered relation J_t = [(k, F(F_t[k]), A(A_t[k])) | k in K_t], where F and A are the existing independently admitted definition and authority constructors. The join rejects any k in K_t absent from A_t, publishing no partial result. This uses the ordinary publication invariant that both maps in one transaction snapshot describe the same relation incarnation. It never joins an original definition to current live authority. The captured definition retains object/OID/server identities and the authority retains bound roles, absent versus empty ACLs, column grant paths and complete tuple revisions. Consequently every admitted row in J_t has the original definition and privileges of that original object, independently of later name reuse.

For trigger and rule maps E_t, the projection retains exactly entries whose original relation-key namespace is not a temporary namespace. SQL's existing temporary-namespace predicate operates on the decoded namespace component, so an ordinary relation whose local name resembles a temporary namespace remains included. Both foreign and event selection count eligible entries before reserving result capacity and skip excluded entries before copying names, authority or bodies. Therefore adding an excluded entry changes neither retained output capacity nor payload admission, even if its payload exceeds the entire budget or its excluded foreign authority is absent. These projections are idempotent, preserve order and retain complete selected definitions; the raw event constructors continue to capture all supplied entries for existing callers.

Each catalog and resource source is a fresh identity in the same registry as the generation's tables and sequences. The source cells retain D_t's original Arc roots. Graph publication clones a write candidate and publishes a new graph handle; memory path indexes retain immutable evaluated path maps, model definitions and compiled analyzers are immutable behind their handles, and catalog updates detach the root before mutation. Thus a later private successor cannot alter a resource selected through D_t. The native resource constructors preserve exact graph image bytes, model parameters, evaluated path pairs, compiled analyzer images and scoring strings, while graph catalog identities come from the same original generation. Existing resource proofs therefore apply without reanalysis, retraining, evaluation or numeric approximation. Table, catalog and resource observations compose as the product of their original observations; no query operation or optimizer rewrite changes.

Reading and retirement use the selected-source preservation theorem. An unread source is frozen before its final source owner is released; transaction completion, savepoint changes, same-name replacement and Engine closure cannot substitute another generation. Weak keys admit their pinned fixed source storage, while every producer separately admits its payload under the original cut's memory and cancellation controls. Foreign-join failure, resource denial and cancellation leave ordinary transaction completion and independent captures unchanged. Image children retain their own leases and original cancellation after the aggregate cut disappears. Registration visits a fixed number of family keys, but preservation can still visit all selected catalog/resource payloads; no bound on transaction publication or retirement follows from this construction.

Owner fixtures cover excluded one-MiB names/bodies, permanent and unlogged foreign definitions, temporary-looking local names, incomplete selected authority, absent/empty ACLs and removed-column revisions, exact admission and independent cancellation. Engine fixtures preserve original role/authority/foreign/event generations and table data through private changes, savepoints, COMMIT/ROLLBACK, replacement and unread source closure. Resource fixtures compare original native image bytes and evaluated path pairs, graph identities and scoring settings, including an independently underfunded capture and child cancellation after Engine loss. This connects 30 of the 33 durable roots to their original transaction source domain. Comments, index metadata and field-analyzer bindings still need eligibility derived from original object membership. Autonomous sequence/identifier overlays, the no-active-transaction publication boundary, bounded retirement, complete Engine image validation/activation and public backup jobs remain separate requirements.

## Transaction table metadata selection

Execution's `TableMetadataCaptureSource` receives original table identities and persistence together with the original index and field-analyzer roots. Engine only adapts its retained transaction data into those owning inputs. Lookup decodes canonical or legacy-public relation references without allocating names and performs an ordered lookup in the original membership. A malformed reference or missing original table fails capture. Temporary membership is excluded before output capacity, index names, encoded definitions, field names or analyzer resources are admitted; Permanent and Unlogged members remain selected. Neither the current table map nor a later named-analyzer revision participates in selection.

Let T be the original table membership at boundary t, I its index records and A its field bindings. Define P_T(R) to retain exactly the records r whose owning relation exists in T and whose persistence is not Temporary; an unresolved owner makes P_T undefined and capture fails atomically. The source freezes T, I and A together. Both Storage producers count P_T(R) before reserving capacity, then copy precisely that same ordered domain through the immutable predicate. Thus their output is P_T(I) and P_T(A), respectively. The projection is idempotent and preserves the relative order and every surviving registry key. Original relation-name uniqueness makes the join unambiguous; later deletion, same-name creation and private changes cannot alter T or substitute a different relation incarnation.

Each selected index retains its exact relation identity, method and original encoded table, column, parameter and semantic-definition strings. Each field binding retains independent index/search revisions, recorded names, ownership and last phase. Existing analyzer image preservation gives equality of the resolved descriptor and dictionary bytes on both sides, even when their common recorded name now resolves to another revision. Together with the selected original physical index and table images, this preserves their matching lookup, text analysis and ranking inputs. The projection adds no query, optimizer rewrite, algebraic operation or numerical approximation.

The shared transaction source domain selects this composite source beside the matching table data. The registry admits its fixed weak identity before exposing a cut, and the owning producers admit every selected output and resource before publication. A failed second producer drops the already admitted first result; source retirement freezes unread metadata before releasing its original roots. Retained outputs and analyzer children obey the original cancellation after Engine and cut closure. Source construction still walks table membership, and catalog preservation still walks metadata; this establishes neither bounded publication/retirement nor a complete public Engine backup boundary.

The owner fixtures compare exact native analyzer images and every encoded index field, test quoted Unicode names and original persistence, and measure identical allocations and payload admission with and without one-MiB excluded metadata. Exact and insufficient allowances, missing or malformed joins and independent failed/cancelled captures exercise atomic cleanup. Engine fixtures retain unread metadata across private DDL, analyzer reassignment, savepoints, COMMIT/ROLLBACK, table-name reuse and closure, checking that original data and metadata survive together while raw source roots retire. The comment selection below completes original durable-root eligibility; autonomous allocation overlays, no-active-transaction publication and complete image/restore jobs remain separate requirements.

## Transaction comment eligibility

Execution retains comment eligibility in the native description generation. The description publisher supplies the addressed object's actual persistence with its complete class/object/subobject address. SQL resolves type lifetime from the bound declaration; relation row types use actual relation persistence and generated arrays follow the same element identity and owner. The comment registry stores disjoint durable and temporary roots. `CommentCaptureSource` selects the durable root without inspecting definitions, table descriptors, namespaces or comment entries. Engine registers that selected owner beside the transaction's other original sources.

Let G be the original generation, C its ordered map from addresses to exact descriptions, and P its publication-time persistence map on the same domain. Define D = {a in dom(C) | P(a) is not Temporary} and T = dom(C) minus D. The native invariant is C = C_D union C_T with disjoint domains D and T. Empty roots establish it; a successful insertion removes the selected address from the other partition and installs its exact text in the selected partition before publishing both roots. A failed candidate changes neither root. Deletion removes the address from its existing partition. Induction proves that every ordinary observation sees C and every source selection sees exactly C_D. Ordered merging preserves object/subobject/class ordering, while an unrelated catalog class sharing an OID remains a different address. Opaque and dangling addresses retain the lifetime recorded by their owning publisher; capture never guesses lifetime from a missing current definition.

SQL type publication derives temporary lifetime from its native namespace identity. Relation row types and their arrays derive it from the owning table, view or foreign table's persistence rather than its name. Existing type lifecycle rules reject moves into or out of temporary namespaces, so rename, shell completion, owner changes and ordinary namespace relocation preserve that lifetime. Replacements receive new object identities. Registry callers publishing descriptions for other catalog classes supply the same native lifetime alongside their addressed object; the catalog-class fixtures retain permanent, unlogged, opaque and dangling entries and exclude temporary subobjects. Original selected roots remain immutable through private changes, savepoints, COMMIT, ROLLBACK and source closure.

Selection clones only the durable root and performs no heap allocation, regardless of durable or temporary cardinality. Freeze independently admits its cached complete footprint and shares immutable descriptions. Unselected temporary text is neither retained nor charged; a four-MiB excluded value can retire before the source is frozen. Failed admission publishes no result, independent cuts retain independent allowances and cancellation, and an escaping captured owner preserves its original description bytes after Engine closure. Actual PostgreSQL 18.4 observations cover type, domain, composite, shell, table/view row and array descriptions, owner changes, rename, savepoint and full rollback, and fresh-session lifetime. Joint idle publication, complete Engine images and public backup/restore jobs remain separate requirements.

## Transaction sequence catalog generations

Execution's native `SequenceCatalog` owns complete sequence records and matching SQL authority. A record contains its canonical relation name, immutable object identity, optional recorded catalog OID, actual persistence and complete `SequenceState`, including allocation-generation identity, owner identity, options and physical position. Records and bound authority each occupy disjoint durable and temporary immutable maps. Additional native indexes associate object identities with definition generations, while ordinary OID metadata retains its existing lookup and restoration behavior. The catalog exposes no independent mutation of a record's identity, persistence or authority membership.

Let the preceding observation be G = (D, I, P, A, O), where D, I, P, A and O denote definitions, object identities, persistence, bound authority and recorded OIDs. Its durable domain is K = {k in dom(D) | P(k) is not Temporary}; the complete observation is R_G(k) = (k, I(k), O(I(k)), P(k), D(k), A(k)). The native representation stores exactly this tuple under k, with authority in its matching SQL-owned entry. Successful insertion admits both entries and their object index before replacing any installed root; removal and rename similarly prepare complete successors. Therefore induction over successful mutations preserves equal record/authority domains and disjoint durable/temporary membership. Rejected or abandoned preparation leaves the installed roots unchanged. Incomplete selected metadata cannot be manufactured through this interface; durable loading still rejects corrupt input before publishing a generation.

Native admission preserves every inline record field and copies the exact qualified-name bytes. SQL's exhaustive authority producer preserves bound role identities, each ACL path and the distinction between absent and explicitly empty ACLs. Object-keyed OID replacement updates the cached selected OID with its metadata root in the same candidate; unrelated or retired OID metadata does not become a captured record. Thus the durable record and authority roots present exactly the ordered graph of R_G. The two ordered native partitions merge by canonical key for ordinary observations, preserving their preceding order and lookup behavior. No serialization, validation, role binding or numerical interpretation changes.

Each immutable entry keeps its complete payload lease; Core retains tree nodes and caches the reachable footprint. Capturing the durable record and authority roots separately admits their cached footprints and retains the roots, with constant work, no entry enumeration and no payload copying. Temporary payloads contribute neither work nor capture admission. Borrowed observation joins authority through the selected key without consulting live state. A failed second admission drops the first reservation and publishes no partial result. Independent captures retain independent allowances and original cancellation, while the native payload leases survive until their last retained reference retires.

Engine retains this one complete catalog generation beside the transaction's immutable table values and registers its sequence source in the same capture domain. Snapshot, rollback, private selection and initial restoration retain complete native records instead of independently mutable metadata maps. Execution prepares catalog and authority successors before their corresponding persistent writes and publishes after successful storage operations; the existing definition transaction owns rollback of preceding dependent changes. Native position preparation admits the selected key, immutable path and record before a value reservation or assignment has an external effect. Prepared publication changes only the selected physical position and performs no further fallible admission or key comparison. System allocator failure keeps Rust's existing process-level behavior; this is not a claim that publication performs no allocation.

Sequence snapshot reuse compares actual immutable record, authority, OID and membership roots, together with existing role roots, committed version and private-storage revision. A copied outer catalog owner or abandoned preparation retains the same roots and therefore does not cause a new catalog read. Every published content change replaces an affected root. Identity equality establishes identical retained entries without scanning them; distinct roots conservatively require a fresh read. Thus reuse cannot substitute stale temporary definitions or authority, and successful shared-position allocations preserve the existing bounded catalog-read behavior.

Owner fixtures cover extreme positions, owner/definition identities, permanent/unlogged membership, optional ACLs, OIDs, excluded temporary payloads, rejected complete publication, exact independent admission and cancellation. Native captures at 1, 64 and 4096 entries exercise constant allocation work and shared payload pointers. Engine fixtures retain complete table/sequence observations through private DDL, rename, name reuse, savepoints, completion and unread closure. These checks exercise the representation and ownership obligations above; they do not establish complete Engine backup activation.

## Transaction sequence value history

Execution owns the history used by transaction, savepoint and exception rollback. Its immutable maps contain `(object identity, definition generation) -> physical value state`, with `current`, `called`, `log_count` and the existing autonomous-persistence flag. Session `currval` observations and the last-value object identity remain separate native fields. A memory transaction shares one registered `SequenceCaptureSource` between native recovery and the original catalog source; other frames retain ordinary unregistered history without definition roots. There is no separate backup value accumulator.

For original catalog generation G, partition native history H into H_G, containing exactly the durable object/definition pairs present in G, and H_other. Original object indexes decide this membership when preparing each update, so capture need not scan unrelated history. For k in K, define J(G,H_G)(k) as R_G(k) with only `(current, called, log_count)` replaced by H_G(I(k), D(k).definition_generation), if present. A captured value retains G's durable record/authority roots and H_G's immutable root; its borrowed iterator performs exactly this lookup. Hence observation equals the ordered graph of J, with unchanged names, identities, definitions, persistence, OIDs and authority. Name reuse changes object identity and ALTER RESTART changes definition generation, so neither can overwrite the selected original position. Decreasing values and `called = false` remain exact; no maximum or synthesized preceding value is used.

Every value operation prepares both the value-history and session-observation successors in all active frame owners before consuming a cached value or mutating physical sequence state. Core's prepared insertion determines the immutable path and complete allowance while holding exclusive access; publication follows that path without additional comparisons or fallible admission. Failed preparation and external errors before publication release the prepared owners without changing their original roots. Successful publication replaces exactly the selected object/generation value and its session observation, updating the last-value identity only for the existing nextval rule. DISCARD releases only observation roots and last-value identity. Induction over these transitions gives the preceding native rollback inputs, now retained without copying the complete history.

Recovery selects only matching surviving object and definition identities, excludes autonomous or temporary values from durable restoration, and preserves the preceding deterministic object order. Execution admits all selected native position successors before publishing any recovered native record or session observation. The transaction owner handles a recovery failure through its existing cleanup outcome; Engine supplies session, frame and physical-storage adapters. The original definition-generation rules still distinguish allocation progress that survives rollback from progress belonging only to a discarded replacement.

Common Storage orders capture registration, source reads and source writes through the existing capture cell. A cut before a history update retains its prior H_G root before publication; a later cut retains the successor. Immutable G and H_G therefore preserve exactly J for the selected cut through subsequent value operations, private DDL, DISCARD and source retirement. Capturing these fixed roots performs no cardinality-dependent work and admits only their cached durable footprint. H_other, temporary definitions and session observations contribute no captured payloads. Denied or cancelled capture invalidates that capture without consuming or rewriting native history.

A physical position can be ahead of the last value returned because a cache block has been reserved. J preserves that endpoint and excludes session caches, so interpreting the captured definition and value tuple as a fresh sequence retains the same native allocation inputs. The representation introduces no SQL operator, optimizer rewrite or numerical approximation, and leaves query, document, posting and ranked-result composition unchanged. Joint idle publication, remaining native metadata ownership, complete Engine image encoding, restore activation and public backup jobs retain their separate requirements.

Owner fixtures exercise rejected and abandoned preparation, successful publication after exhausting the remaining allowance, original unread cuts, decreasing values, excluded definitions and objects, independent cancellation and final lease release. Engine fixtures exercise actual cached and uncached nextval, setval, private ALTER/rename/name reuse, rollback, committed catalog-read bounds and failure isolation across memory, native SQLite, SQLite KV and redb. Their finite results supplement the state-transition and representation arguments above.

## Memory catalog identifier reservations

Execution's `CatalogOidCounter` owns the memory database's nontransactional catalog OID position. `capture_with` admits one fixed-size optional position and holds the counter's allocation mutex while its callback registers the matching source-domain cut. The callback performs bounded cut registration and does not allocate OIDs, serialize resources or reenter that counter. Engine supplies this owner beside its original transaction sources, so the returned table/catalog/sequence cut and counter position belong to the same registration boundary. Persistent providers retain their existing durable allocator namespace and joint physical snapshot instead.

Let m = 16,384 and N = 2^32 - m. A valid memory counter state P is either uninitialized or an absolute position p in [m, 2^64 - 1]. For an initialized counter, allocation checks p + 1 for overflow, stores the resulting position q and returns m + ((q - m) mod N).
