An uninitialized counter first seeds that position from the greatest existing catalog OID under the original rule. Capture copies P without initializing, advancing, wrapping or narrowing it. The fresh constructor accepts exactly this domain and installs an independent mutex containing the same P. Therefore the next native transition receives the same absolute input, and induction gives the same position/returned-OID sequence for the same selected catalog occupancy. In particular, positions beyond the 32-bit range preserve their wrap history and the largest 64-bit position remains exhausted. The constructor does not reset an existing counter.

Every memory OID reservation and the capture callback hold the same owning mutex. Consequently there is a point during source registration at which the retained position equals the live position; allocations completed before that point are included, and later allocations cannot alter the captured scalar. Original table and catalog sources are immutable while the sequence history obeys the registry's write/capture order. Thus the pair combines the selected committed definitions/data with exactly the autonomous OID and matching sequence observations at that boundary. OIDs consumed by private or subsequently rolled-back DDL remain consumed even though those definitions are absent from the selected original generation. A name or numeric OID lookup in the later live catalog cannot reconstruct this reservation history and is never used for that purpose.

Memory document identity floors have a different native contract: without a durable allocator, they roll back with the original table snapshot. Their existing captured next-ID value is paired with that table's immutable object and storage-generation identities. This component neither overlays later private document counters nor changes their rollback behavior. Durable document reservations continue to be captured by their provider-owned namespaces. The distinction preserves the existing owning allocator semantics rather than manufacturing an autonomous counter where none exists.

The optional OID position has fixed retained size, independent of the number of catalog objects or reservations. Admission checks the original cancellation while waiting for the allocation mutex, so cancellation can finish while another operation still holds that mutex. A failed callback, memory admission or cancellation returns no completed pair and releases partial reservations and the counter lock; capture itself never consumes an OID. Detached results retain their original cancellation after the Engine and counter are gone, and fresh counter construction does not retain a source alias. These operations introduce no query operator, optimizer rewrite or numeric approximation. This establishes the selected counter and data inputs; capture outside an active transaction, bounded source publication/retirement, complete image encoding/validation and fresh Engine activation remain separate requirements.

## Committed memory selection admission

The internal source-cut consumer selects its generation inside the transaction owner. An outer frame supplies its original table/catalog sources and shared native sequence-value history; private live state and nested savepoints are never candidates. With no frame, selection must acquire the statement gate while retaining transaction admission before constructing the idle generation. A busy unframed operation releases transaction admission before waiting, then reconsiders the frame stack: a newly started frame can supply its original generation while the statement and its application callback remain busy. Both kinds of wait observe the capture's original cancellation. An unframed operation cannot capture its own unfinished writes through a reentrant or delegated gate.

Let S be the statement gate, T the transaction-stack mutex, D the current memory data and O the original generation of the outer frame. Native transaction creation, commit and rollback hold S and T while changing the existence or ownership of O. A successful selection either holds T and chooses O, or holds T, observes an empty stack and acquires S without waiting while T is held. In the first case no completion can remove or replace O during registration, and private writes cannot change its immutable source identities. In the second case no statement can mutate D and no transaction transition can occur before registration. If S is unavailable, releasing T permits the operation that holds S to publish its new frame. Thus every successful selection refers to one original committed data generation; the established sequence-history and OID admission arguments attach their native autonomous observations at the same registration point. A capture racing completion can select the preceding or following committed generation, but cannot mix their table definitions and rows.

Idle selection releases S and T before retiring its temporary source owner. Retirement preserves the registered values through the original producer and admission boundaries, after which the cut is checked again for terminal failure. No undo table handle escapes in the result. A denied or cancelled capture changes neither frame status nor transaction depth, and a failed frame remains failed until its native rollback resolves it. Successful native writers and completion do not depend on capture success. These ownership rules preserve the existing query inputs without introducing an operator, rewrite or numerical approximation.

This consumer remains internal to the source-cut tests pending the complete production image consumer. Its idle constructor still enumerates table/catalog metadata, and source retirement may preserve whole metadata collections. The selection argument does not establish bounded publication or retirement, and this constructor is not the final public backup implementation. Tests exercise actual idle captures and temporary payload exclusion, verified admission contention, a frame appearing while its statement remains held, reentrant refusal, failed frames, independent cancellation, source closure and COMMIT/ROLLBACK races. Bounded owning publication/retirement, complete image encoding/validation, fresh activation and public jobs remain required.

## Memory table metadata generations

Each non-temporary memory table registers its columns, table checks, foreign keys, key constraints, hierarchy, full-text field names, row-array name, dropped attributes, declared-column flag, table authority, column statistics, analyzer configuration, physical storage generation, document-ID watermark, integer-key mapping, statistics-loaded/dirty flags and statistics-maintenance state before publication. Persistent and temporary tables keep unregistered owners. Immutable object identity, persistence, ON COMMIT behavior and catalog OIDs remain properties of the table incarnation; derived document-count caches and process-local lifecycle counters are not image metadata. Complete table membership, durable catalogs and committed transaction selection remain separate integration requirements.

`CatalogCell<T, F>` retains its existing immutable `Arc<T>` root inside the Storage mutation boundary and adapts an owning producer from T to an admitted detached F. Reads and transaction snapshots share the original root. A write preserves selected cuts before `Arc::make_mut` exposes a successor; restoration likewise preserves the displaced value before replacing its root. Final source destruction preserves unread selected values. A failed producer invalidates only that cut, releases its archive and still allows the ordinary source operation. `CapturedMemoryValue<F>` rejects a payload reservation from another allowance and checks the original capture cancellation on access, including after the source and registry are gone.

SQL copies definitions with exhaustive syntax constructors, preserving identities, physical attribute positions, constraints, partition metadata, nested evaluated values and expression structure without parsing, binding or evaluation. Retained literal payloads are copied into independent owners with their exact type, declaration and collation; sharing an unrelated statement's old payload lease would not satisfy capture admission. This path checks the capture's supplied controls and does not inherit the ambient SQL statement's cancellation. Query and window expressions violate the stored-column definition domain and are rejected before a candidate escapes. Complete authority includes owner and privilege paths together with relation and column ACL tuple revisions, including a revision whose ACL entry is absent. Ordered revision and ACL vectors admit each buffer and name explicitly.

Planner's existing controlled statistics producer preserves counts, evaluated min/max/histogram/MCV values and frequency bits. Analysis streams its existing configuration representation into an admitted buffer without compiling deferred expressions or reopening resource paths; immutable compiled descriptors and resource closure have their separate image owner. Storage copies every inline statistics-maintenance field without payload allocation. Engine supplies state handles and producer adapters, and does not implement syntax traversal, analyzer interpretation or statistics algorithms.

Let M be the tuple of these table components at a selected source-domain cut c. For a component i, let P_i be its owning producer and let O_i denote all existing observations of that component, including exact values and identities. Exhaustive constructor copying proves O_i(P_i(x)) = O_i(x) by structural induction: inline fields are identical, ordered containers preserve every key and position, strings preserve their bytes, Core's evaluated-value copy preserves the complete carrier, and retained constants detach the already evaluated payload without changing its type metadata. Authority preserves each privilege and revision pair; statistics use their existing exact producer. Analyzer configuration encoding is an inverse representation of the stored configuration vocabulary, including invalid deferred patterns and unresolved paths, and performs no semantic transformation. Thus component capture introduces no new query operator, comparison rule or numeric approximation.

Before the first post-c mutation of component i, its live root still denotes M_i. The owning guard invokes P_i while that root is immutable, records the result once for c, and retains admission through the subsequent mutation. If no mutation occurs, the first captured read or final source retirement performs the same preservation. Later replacement and rollback cannot replace the recorded value. Induction over any finite sequence of writes, restorations and retirements therefore keeps O_i(capture_i(c)) = O_i(M_i), provided that capture succeeds. This argument is component-wise: only the enclosing transaction owner can establish that all selected M_i and table/catalog membership belong to one committed database state.

All copied buffers, container nodes and retained literal owners are admitted before construction under the original capture allowance; nested payload leases remain attached to the returned value. Failed or cancelled construction releases partial output, and dropping the final retained owner releases its reservation. Registration does not traverse these payloads. First preservation can perform work proportional to the selected metadata and its evaluated payload bytes; it is not a constant-time write guarantee. Owner tests cover large denied payloads, independent capture/SQL cancellation, complete authority and statistics, actual DDL and TRUNCATE, savepoint/transaction rollback, same-name replacement and unread Engine closure. They exercise these boundaries without substituting for the unfinished joint-cut and fresh-activation requirements.

## Evaluated value-index capture

Execution's `ValueIndexes` owns the live name-to-cache collection and implements Common Storage's `MemoryCaptureSource`. Its frozen result contains only named SQL index keys; opportunistic column accelerators remain derived query caches. Each `CapturedValueIndex` contains its original field, carried/search-key role, temporal/row/comparison eligibility and the ordered map from full-width document identity to evaluated `Value`. Absence and present SQL NULL remain distinct. Eligibility is copied from its owner, including conservative observations of removed values; it is not recomputed from the current population. The snapshot borrows resident values and decodes individual spilled values with retained allowance, without any expression, routine or SQL comparison callback. This carrier does not itself encode a complete archive or establish catalog coverage for an unbuilt index.

Let E_i be the map from document identities to evaluated stored values for index i, and let P_i be its existing eligibility state. Resident evaluated values live in Core's immutable retained map. Each insertion records the complete physical payload footprint without invoking SQL comparison or interpreting type metadata; replacement and removal update the cached sum in the same root publication. Capturing that root retains the original entries and admits the complete reachable footprint in constant work, without copying or visiting the values. Shared path updates leave every selected root unchanged. Temporary retained keys share their existing immutable native record roots and encrypted runs; capture neither spills the resident tier nor decodes or visits its values. Both representations preserve every distinct identity and the exact evaluated carrier, including identities whose values compare equal and identities holding NULL. Capture copies P_i directly. Therefore the frozen pair is exactly (E_i, P_i), independently of the current definition of the expression that originally produced E_i. Later routine replacement cannot change its inputs by reevaluation. The named child's `MemoryCaptureCell` preserves this pair before its first later write or destruction; the collection separately preserves the original name-to-source selection described below. Replacement, clearing, DML, rollback and source closure cannot substitute another population under the same name. This composition introduces no query operator or optimizer rewrite.

Temporary retained value indexes encode each payload with `UQAVAL01`, split into consecutive chunks of at most 4 KiB, with the exact total byte length in the existing node record. For a payload v, concatenating those chunks gives Core's image E(v); its decoder gives D(E(v)) = v by the value-image preservation result. The reader checks chunk identities, lengths, total consumption and trailing data, skips only tombstones excluded by its selected private snapshot, and rejects malformed or incomplete input. In particular, an ordinary map resembling a JSON type tag is never reinterpreted as another carrier. Float bits, positional record fields and opaque tuple metadata keep their exact representations. Balanced-tree keys, node ranks, publication roots and comparison rules remain unchanged. Replacement writes a private candidate and publishes its root only after complete value and node construction; failed encoding or decoding publishes no partial replacement. These runs are process-local scratch, not a persistent database format requiring migration.

Resident root retention admits the complete reachable node, entry and payload footprint from maintained cardinality and payload sums before publishing the captured owner. Native temporary-record publication similarly maintains the sum of exposed key/value capacities and native layouts; a successful replacement subtracts its prior contribution and adds its successor in the same immutable-root publication. Rollback restores both the root and its cached sum. Induction over publication and rollback therefore preserves the complete reachable resident footprint. Sealing each immutable run counts its native metadata, filter capacities, block boundaries and exposed temporary-file ownership once; run-set publication retains their sum. Capture reads these cached totals and shares the two roots in constant work. Each independent capture admits the full retained project-owned footprint, including field/name buffers and snapshot headers, even when physical allocations are shared. Reference-count and allocator bookkeeping, kernel state and dependency-private allocations are excluded from this native footprint model. Decoded run caches exist only while active readers retain them and remain separately admitted by their original owner.

For a captured live identity, its node fixes the original encoded byte count. The image reader first visits native chunk metadata in order, including deleted tail positions, within each caller-selected preparation limit. Every live chunk must have the next ordinal and exactly the smaller of 4 KiB or the remaining declared length. EOF is a final position and requires zero remaining bytes. By induction on live chunk ordinals, successful preparation establishes exactly the finite ordered partition of E(v), with neither gaps nor extra live chunks. The immutable source cannot change between preparation and output, so subsequent exact chunk reads emit E(v) using a single 4-KiB buffer without scanning tombstone tails or constructing v. A requested decoded observation instead applies Core's existing D and transfers only that value's admitted payload; insufficient decode memory does not invalidate the retained source. Original capture and invoking cancellation are checked during preparation and output. The existing terminal ImageReader boundary releases cursor workspace on failure, completion or unwind; retained source leases last until their final owner. Native source tests verify zero capture reads/writes, exact independent admission, undo footprints, source loss, byte-exact wide output, deleted positions, malformed chunks and both cancellation signals. Native name-collection preservation is described below; table assembly and Engine restore remain separate requirements.


## Evaluated-index component images

Execution's `UQAEVI01` metadata image preserves the native field, carried-key flag, temporal and row eligibility, comparison policy and live value count. Its wire order is the eight-byte magic, three 0/1 flags, comparison tag (0 ordered, 1 typed-field, 2 fallible), little-endian u64 live count, and a length-prefixed UTF-8 field. Input admits the field before allocation and rejects unknown tags, malformed UTF-8, truncation and trailing data. Historical eligibility is not derived again from surviving values. This metadata decoder does not publish an index; the enclosing restore owner must validate the complete identity/value inventory against its declared count.

Each evaluated value uses Core's unchanged `UQAVAL01` carrier, addressed by its original full-width document identity. Resident values borrow the immutable root and the existing Core continuation; spilled values use Common Storage's original chunk reader and bounded preparation. Selection exposes one physical identity position, including deleted spilled entries, so the mapping can account for tombstones without hiding a scan inside one live output step. Selected readers keep both the original capture control and the invoking output allowance after source retirement.

Let I be the immutable map of live document identities to evaluated values and P the preserved native policy. The metadata fields have explicit injective integer, flag, tag and UTF-8 encodings, so successful decoding of their complete image recovers P and |I| exactly. Strict successor selection visits each physical identity once; removing positions marked deleted yields exactly dom(I). For every remaining identity d, resident output applies Core's E to I(d), while spilled output concatenates the native chunks already established to equal E(I(d)). The Core inverse D therefore recovers I(d) exactly, including SQL NULL, float bits and nested carriers. These facts compose to preserve (I,P) when the enclosing importer validates the complete inventory and count. No SQL expression, analyzer, comparison callback or source routine executes during output. Failed or cancelled cursors retire their scratch through the existing terminal reader boundary. Complete memory inventory and fresh Engine restoration remain separate requirements.

## Native evaluated-index membership

Execution owns immutable name-to-source membership separately from each named evaluated index. The native retained map stores the original ValueIndexKey and an admitted weak MemoryCaptureKey; its cached payload footprint includes the name capacity and registered source layout. Opportunistic column accelerators and persistent-session caches remain unregistered query state. The enclosing source cell preserves the name root before membership mutation, while each named child preserves its original evaluated value root and eligibility through its own ordinary write or retirement boundary. Capture retains and admits the name root in constant work without visiting children, copying keys or decoding values. Ordered successor lookup visits the immutable tree path only.

Let N_c map each durable index name to the unique source identity selected at cut c, and let E_c(k), P_c(k) denote that child's evaluated values and eligibility. New named indexes register their identity before joining membership; source-group publication and live name replacement share the enclosing write admission. Preparation constructs the complete successor name/source roots before publication. A denied registration, payload reservation or group update therefore leaves the live collection and its published selection unchanged. Replacement, deletion and clearing retire displaced child owners, whose existing admission boundary preserves every previously selected child before releasing its mutable source. Registry identities are never reused for a replacement with the same name.

For a captured name n, lookup requires the same cut identity and resolves precisely N_c(n). The child preservation invariant gives the original pair (E_c(N_c(n)), P_c(N_c(n))); the resident and spilled root arguments above give unchanged evaluated observations for that pair. Membership sharing leaves N_c unchanged through later updates. Induction over insertion, replacement, removal, complete replacement and rollback proves that selecting the name and selecting its values cannot mix generations. The source group is installed in both live table publication and original transaction snapshots; rollback builds and publishes the restored child set through the same owner. A child captured from one cut can outlive the live collection and cut with its own retained budget, while an unresolved name root requires its original cut to select a child.

The existing value-index preparation still builds every row update before publishing any field. Read and write guards expose the original native cache without changing stored-key comparison, null handling or eligibility. Failed backup retention invalidates that capture through MemoryCaptureCell and does not consume the ordinary SQL write. Fallible membership operations report their failure through the existing schema, maintenance and transaction cleanup boundaries. The extension changes no query operator, ordering law or optimizer rewrite; it preserves the original field observations and their selection under source retirement. Complete table metadata, memory-image assembly and Engine restore retain their separate requirements.

## Evaluated memory graph images

Graph owns `MemoryGraphStore::write_image` and `read_image` in its [image module](../../crates/uqa-graph/src/memory_store/image.rs). They encode a borrowed immutable memory graph and construct a fresh unpublished graph, respectively. They do not invoke SQL, Cypher, analyzer or host callbacks. Committed Engine capture, surrounding catalog identity and source-retention admission remain the enclosing backup owner's obligations. A completed graph codec call does not activate an Engine or publish a repository root.

The `UQAGRA01` image begins with little-endian u64 next-vertex and next-edge counters. Four counted sections follow, each prefixed with its u64 entry count: global vertices, global edges, named graph memberships and label registries. Vertices contain their u64 identity, a UTF-8 label prefixed with its u64 byte length and one complete Core `UQAVAL01` Map image of evaluated properties. Edges contain u64 identity, source and target, then the same label and properties encodings. A graph contains its sized UTF-8 name and two counted, strictly increasing u64 sets for vertex and edge membership. Both global maps and both name maps are strictly ordered; every membership must reference a global entity of its own kind. Global allocation counters must exceed existing identities, preserving unused reservations and exhaustion. Empty graphs and unattached global entities remain present.

Each label registry contains its original 16-byte allocation identity, little-endian u32 next-label counter and four counted ordered maps/sets: sized UTF-8 label name to u32 identity, sized name to one-byte kind (0 vertex, 1 edge), u32 identity to u64 sequence watermark, and u32 dropped-label identities. Registry names need not name an existing graph: the existing import owner permits metadata before graph creation. Registry values are preserved without migration, including missing historical kinds, aliases created by registry merges, overlapping merged tombstones and unused reservations. Decoding rejects unknown kind tags and duplicate or decreasing keys; it does not reinterpret these retained fields by replaying entity insertion or registry observation.

Endpoint identities are evaluated edge payloads. Existing graph difference and label removal can retain edges whose endpoints are outside that partition or absent globally. The image preserves those existing carriers and their query diagnostics; it does not add an endpoint foreign key. Source validation instead checks exact entity identity, primary membership, reverse membership, label indexing and both adjacency directions. Missing primary entities and inconsistent nonempty derived entries fail before output. Empty derived index buckets are omitted on reconstruction, because all public label, membership and adjacency observations treat an absent bucket as empty.

Common Storage's [image I/O](../../crates/uqa-storage/src/image_io.rs) supplies bounded length/scalar I/O to both graph and text codecs, reusing Core's exact value codec for properties. Metadata I/O uses at most 4 KiB per call; property payloads use Core's at-most-16-KiB windows. Every partial or interrupted attempt checks the original cancellation before another attempt. Encoding borrows graph state without collecting a second entity, membership or property map. Core's numeric-format workspace remains controlled separately. Blocking operating-system I/O retains its existing cancellation boundary.

Import admits each native immutable node and each decoded or copied name/property buffer before its allocation or publication. Reconstructed adjacency and reverse membership retain their own node/name leases. Primary map and set layouts use Core's exact native-node accounting; evaluated property buffers keep Core's existing retained-value model, including exposed capacities. The returned `MemoryGraphStore` owns these leases directly, and cloned generations retain them until the last reachable node is released. Later mutations admit their new nodes and buffers under the same native allowance. Decode, reconstruction, representable cardinalities, exact standalone EOF and source-invariant validation all finish before a candidate escapes; any error drops partial state and returns its complete allowance. Import requires both its retained footprint and the peak workspace needed to construct immutable successors.

### Preservation argument for evaluated graphs

Let the primary carrier be S = (V, E, P, L, n_v, n_e), where V and E are finite identity-to-entity maps with exact evaluated properties, P maps graph names to separate finite vertex/edge identity sets, L is the complete ordered registry mapping and n_v/n_e are the original global allocation counters. Membership identities reference their respective global maps; edge endpoints are payloads rather than additional membership constraints. Let D(S) be the derived reverse membership, vertex/edge label indexes and incoming/outgoing adjacency obtained by inserting each primary membership once with its entity's unchanged label/endpoints. Source validity means its nonempty derived entries equal D(S). Empty buckets are identified with absent buckets for public observations.

Every integer has its fixed-width inverse, every UTF-8 name has a length-delimited byte inverse, and every properties Map has Core's carrier-preserving image inverse. Distinct kind tags encode the existing enum. Induction over each strictly ordered map and set therefore reconstructs exactly V, E, P and L; equality includes floating-point bits, opaque tuple bytes, historical registry fields and unused watermarks. The two fixed counters are recovered unchanged. Exact EOF rules out a valid prefix with an ignored suffix. Thus read(write(S)) = S for every source-valid, representable S under sufficient allowance. This is byte/carrier identity, not merely graph connectivity or equality under a weaker numeric comparator.

For each (graph, vertex) in P, reconstruction inserts that membership once in the corresponding reverse set and that vertex's label set. For each (graph, edge), it inserts the reverse membership, edge label, outgoing source and incoming target entries once. Strict input membership ordering excludes duplicate insertion. These are exactly the defining comprehensions of D(S), so reconstructed nonempty derived state equals source-valid derived state. Consequently graph enumeration, label/degree observations, traversal inputs, evaluated properties and graph-algebra inputs are identical, including loops, parallel edges, shared entities and defined dangling-edge behavior. Union, intersection and difference retain their existing set laws because their two membership carriers are equal before and after reconstruction; this codec introduces no new graph operator, optimizer rewrite or numeric approximation.

Future deterministic allocation and mutation consume the same counters, label registries and primary/derived observations. They therefore produce the same success, identifier, exhaustion or existing diagnostic and the same successor primary state. Induction extends this correspondence to finite compositions of defined existing operations under the same external catalog/runtime conditions. Destruction of the source cannot affect the reconstructed graph, which owns its decoded properties and names. All failures precede candidate return, so a malformed or underfunded prefix never becomes a published graph. Repository integrity, joint committed Engine capture and complete surrounding resource validation remain separate prerequisites for database backup correctness.

Owner tests compare independently assembled bytes and literal graph observations, preserve exact property bits and lazy datums, exercise graph algebra, dropped-label edges, registry merges and later allocation, reject every truncated fixture prefix and corrupted primary/derived state, sweep all insufficient import allowances, and check cancellation and partial-I/O failure. A two-MiB property export uses no admitted dynamic scratch and calls output in at most 16-KiB windows. These finite checks exercise the boundaries above; they do not replace the preservation argument or claim complete Engine backup qualification.


## Evaluated memory vector images

Common Storage owns `VectorIndex::write_image` and `vector_index::image::read_vector_image` for exact memory, IVF, HNSW and memory DiskANN indexes. The writer exports the selected physical owner without constructing a full canonical export map. The reader returns a private writable `Box<dyn VectorIndex>` only after complete decoding, owner validation and standalone EOF. Each native owner retains its decoded allocation leases; callers do not carry a separate reservation alongside the index. Unsupported owners reject this capability before output. The [DiskANN extension](#evaluated-memory-diskann-images) retains sealed physical generations and canonical origins. Catalog bindings, committed source capture and fresh Engine activation require their own integration; these codecs alone do not expose database backup.

The `UQAVEC01` envelope contains a one-byte method (0 exact, 1 IVF, 2 HNSW, 3 memory DiskANN) and little-endian u32 dimensions. Counts and identities are little-endian u64 unless stated otherwise. A vector is its counted sequence of little-endian f32 bit patterns, with finite components and an exact dimension match. Zero dimensions retain the existing exact/IVF/HNSW Rust owners' carrier; DiskANN retains its own dimension validation. All metadata and coordinate I/O uses at most 4 KiB per call, checking cancellation between partial or interrupted attempts. Unknown methods, invalid tags, unordered identities, truncated input and trailing bytes fail. Declared counts are checked for host-size conversion and admitted allocation before construction.

Exact images contain document and total-vector counts, followed by strictly increasing document IDs and nonempty counted tensors. Tensor order and coordinate bits, including signed zero, are retained. The decoder installs validated tensors directly into its private `MemoryVectorState`, using that owner's existing count and footprint maintenance. It does not call normalization, index training or a query. The resulting immutable root has no legacy full-map export cache.

IVF images contain nlist, nprobe, training threshold, a one-byte Untrained/Trained/Stale state, trained-size and deletion counters, counted centroids and counted vectors ordered by (u64 document, u32 ordinal). Each vector retains both raw and normalized coordinates, the exact original f32 norm and a strict optional centroid assignment. The source owner keeps centroid lists in sorted key order; reconstruction derives those identical lists from the canonical assignments. Import does not normalize, reassign or train vectors, including Stale indexes. Future queries retain the existing owner's training transition. Norms may be positive infinity after finite-input arithmetic overflow; they cannot be NaN or negative. Untrained state has no centroid or assignment and zero training/deletion counters; trained and stale states retain their original nonempty centroids and valid assignments.

IVF export borrows the native state owner through one read guard, checking cancellation while waiting. It validates one coherent training generation and retains that guard through output. The committed capture supplies a detached selected owner before repository output. `VectorIndex::capture_image_with_control` returns a `VectorImageSnapshot` that exposes image output without query access, so an exported generation cannot retrain itself. Live shared-reference queries, training and configuration changes enter the same native state write boundary, preserving any applicable selected cut before mutation. Engine supplies source registration and committed transaction admission; the codec and lazy capture registry do not independently establish that database-wide boundary.

HNSW images retain m, construction/search ef, rebuild threshold, seed, optional entry ID, maximum level, next-node ID, deleted count, full-rewrite flag and every node in identity order. A node contains its ID, document, u32 ordinal, level, deletion flag, original raw norm and coordinates, original normalized norm and coordinates, and each layer's counted adjacency in its existing order. The final ordered dirty-node map retains both identities and values. The decoder reconstructs active (document, ordinal) membership from nondeleted nodes and preserves dirty/full-rewrite state for later persistence deltas. It never reruns normalization or graph construction. Structural validation checks identity counters, dimensions, finite coordinates, contiguous active ordinals, layer bounds, unique reciprocal edges, entry/max-level consistency and layer-zero reachability. Normalized-vector norms retain the owner's exact 1.0 representation; original raw norms preserve defined overflow.

HNSW's existing spillable map owner supplies private ordered builders, payload admission and temporary-file lifetime. Export reads spilled records and performs topology-validation workspace under the invoking backup allowance, independently of retained source admission. Ordinary invariant validation without an explicit control retains its original allowance. Import can spill evaluated nodes and vectors when their corpus exceeds memory; it does not substitute another storage backend or claim a bound on temporary-file bytes. Decoded transfer buffers and the builder's own copies coexist under the same allowance until ownership transfers, after which temporary reservations release.

Exact/IVF imports admit concrete headers, exact owned-map nodes or standard B-tree entry payloads, outer tensor/list buffers and all coordinate capacities. Standard B-tree slack and allocator bookkeeping retain their existing native-owner accounting boundary. HNSW maps retain their own controlled resident/spilled representation, and its native index retains the admitted header across candidate replacement. Every failed candidate drops its buffers, map owners and reservations. The initial image reservation does not prepay later ordinary mutations or IVF query-triggered training. Retained-reader output observes both its original cancellation and the invoking workspace control, including original cancellation between output attempts; cancellation does not interrupt a blocking operating-system call.

### Writable vector image ownership

Exact import divides admission between the index header and its immutable tensor root. The header lease follows the original native index; the root lease follows its shared allocation, including ordinary snapshots, writable forks and retained backup images. A native deep copy allocates independent tensors and does not retain the original root lease. IVF keeps its original reconstruction reservation inside the native data owner after its payload fields, so promoted source readers retain the lease and payloads retire before that reservation; detached IVF snapshots already construct independent payloads through their existing snapshot contracts. HNSW maps keep their existing per-root admission, while its admitted header lease is shared across native candidate replacements. DiskANN already owns its header, canonical roots, sealed pages, temporary allowance and future lineage. None of these moves replays vector insertion, normalization, training or graph construction.

For each decoded allocation a, let L(a) be the native owner of its original reservation. Exact root sharing preserves the same pair (root, L(root)); replacement either mutates the unique root while retaining its lease or constructs a distinct native root and leaves the selected old pair unchanged. IVF detached copying shares no imported payload, while HNSW and DiskANN immutable maps already carry their own leases with every shared node. Native destruction drops payload fields before their reservations. Thus moving a decoded index into live registration, capturing it, retiring the source or failing registration cannot leave a shared imported allocation without its original lease. Header leases may remain conservatively retained by surviving native candidates. Existing ordinary mutation and controlled snapshot admission still apply to newly allocated data; imported admission is neither released at activation nor reused as payment for a second payload population.

These ownership transfers leave every field of the decoded vector carrier unchanged. The image inverse and subsequent-operation argument below therefore still apply after native adoption. Failed decoding, trailing input, denied publication or unwinding publishes no destination and retires the private owner. Complete table assembly, cross-catalog validation and fresh Engine activation remain separate restore requirements.

### Preservation argument for evaluated vectors

The exact carrier is a finite ordered map from document IDs to nonempty ordered tensors of finite f32 coordinates, together with its fixed dimensions. IVF extends the canonical (document, ordinal) map with evaluated raw/normalized coordinates, norms, assignments, ordered centroids, configuration and training counters/state. HNSW extends canonical values with evaluated node identities, normalized values, ordered layered adjacency, tombstones, entry/max-level/allocation metadata and pending persistence state. All claims assume source-valid states, representable counts, successful I/O, sufficient admitted workspace and the same surrounding runtime/catalog semantics. They do not assume that a logical canonical vector map uniquely determines an approximate index.

Fixed-width integer and f32-bit encodings are injective. Length-delimited vectors retain both arity and order; distinct method/state/option tags retain their alternatives. Induction over each strictly ordered identity sequence therefore recovers every explicitly encoded field exactly. For exact indexes, the existing replacement owner derives count and footprint from those tensors, so its restored logical state equals the source. For IVF, define list j as the increasing sequence of keys whose original assignment is j. Existing training and insertion maintain exactly that sequence; ordered reconstruction therefore restores the same list and iteration order without computing a new assignment. For HNSW, active membership is exactly the injective (document, ordinal) projection of nondeleted nodes, so reconstructing that map preserves the existing identity relation. Both remaining derived structures are thus uniquely determined by their retained primary state.

Consequently decode(encode(S)) equals S in every operation-observable field, modulo allocation layout, empty caches and resident versus spill representation. Exact and threshold scans consume the same tensors in the same order. IVF candidate selection consumes the same centroids, assignments, normalized values and probe parameter; its existing stale-training transition starts from identical inputs. HNSW traversal consumes the same entry, adjacency order, normalized values and search parameters. Final scoring uses the same raw values and norms in the same numeric order. On the same floating-point execution model this preserves result support, score bits, tie decisions and diagnostics, including the owner's defined overflow behavior; it adds no approximation and does not assert associative floating-point arithmetic or a cross-platform bitwise floating-point guarantee.

For subsequent deterministic operations, identical input state and configuration give the same successor state: exact replacement changes the same ordered tensor; IVF insertion/deletion/training consumes the same counters, lists and numerical input; HNSW allocation and graph changes consume the same next ID, seed, topology and tombstones. Dirty maps and full-rewrite flags give identical persistence deltas. Induction extends the correspondence to finite compositions of defined operations under equivalent runtime and resource conditions. Existing query operators and their algebraic laws are unchanged because every supplied carrier and observation is unchanged; no optimizer rewrite or new ranking law is introduced.

Candidate privacy separates parsing from publication. A prefix, invalid reference, failed I/O or failed allowance cannot escape as an index, because return follows complete validation and EOF. Source export mutates no selected physical state. These facts preserve failure isolation but do not prove a joint Engine cut, repository authenticity or completeness of surrounding catalog/resources. Those remain prerequisites for complete backup correctness. Independent wire fixtures, exact-score comparisons, stale-state transitions, future deltas, corruption/truncation tests, source destruction and spill/allowance tests exercise these obligations separately from this argument.

## Evaluated memory DiskANN images

Common Storage's [memory image adapter](../../crates/uqa-storage/src/diskann_index/memory/image.rs) extends method 3 of the vector envelope. `DiskANNMemoryIndex` retains the sealed physical owner beside its canonical index; both references share the existing immutable, admitted records. Construction and explicit rebuilding publish the pair only after complete sealing. Image capture clones these immutable roots and the selected options into an export-only owner, with constant-size capture admission and no traversal or training. Later canonical writes replace ordered paths; rebuilding replaces the complete physical root. Captured output remains unchanged after either transition or source closure.

After the envelope, the image contains the physical generation's 16-byte database identity and u64 table, index and generation IDs, followed by a counted existing `DiskANNManifest` encoding. Memory table/index IDs are both 1. The manifest retains its existing parameters, complete build provenance, origin summary and artifact digests. Five u64 values retain resident, cache, in-flight page, query-record and construction-record byte ceilings in that order. The existing provenance supplies partition, merge, training and batch options without another independent configuration encoding. The source temporary-file allowance is host policy rather than image data: `read_vector_image_with_temporary` attaches the destination's shared allowance, while standalone `read_vector_image` attaches the existing default allowance for future builds. Neither path performs a build or creates build files during import.

The physical section contains a u64 record count and strictly ordered records. Each has a one-byte kind (0 codebook, 1 codes, 2 exact-side, 3 origins), u64 first identity and counted raw bytes; codebook first identity is zero. The manifest is carried only in its earlier field. A u64 graph-page count follows, then each consecutive u64 page ID and its 4,096 raw bytes. Input admits record lengths against the construction ceiling before allocating, checks page count/order against the manifest, and passes every physical artifact through the existing complete memory-source sealer. Query-record limits remain independent: a valid source can retain a larger side record whose later query requires more read allowance. Import does not silently relax that limit.

The canonical section has a strict optional revision tag; a present revision uses the existing `DiskANNChangeIdentity` encoding with document zero. It then contains current-vector and changed-vector counts, a document count, and strictly increasing documents. Each document carries its u64 ID, existing 56-byte canonical origin and exactly the origin's count of dimension-checked vectors in original ordinal order. Empty tensors, including deletion origins, remain represented. A final counted, strictly increasing change stream uses the existing change-identity encoding. Canonical decoding uses the controlled persistent ordered-map owner directly without invoking mutation or allocating replacement revisions.

Validation recomputes populations, checks the revision against current origins and requires every change to name its current canonical version. A merge walk over the sealed physical origin stream and current canonical documents requires every base document to remain represented. A changed origin must appear in the change map, an unchanged origin must not, and each document absent from the base must appear as an uncovered change. Thus a self-consistent reduced population cannot hide a deleted change entry. Complete physical verification and prepared-query validation precede standalone EOF and return of the private candidate. This validates owner structure and coverage; enclosing repository authentication is still responsible for detecting an externally rewritten, otherwise valid payload.

The physical generation, old canonical origins and selected revision retain their exact identities. The mutable revision allocator is intentionally fresh: restored writers use a new nonzero incarnation and checked monotone counter, so divergent source and restored mutations cannot reuse one origin identity under the existing unique-incarnation assumption. A later explicit rebuild likewise uses the new lineage while retained older readers keep their original generation. These internal identities are separate from SQL sequence watermarks, which remain part of complete Engine capture and restore. Allocator exhaustion retains its normal error; fresh restoration does not reproduce the source writer's remaining counter capacity.

Original source, capture and invoking cancellation remain independently effective. Source records retain their original allowance; capture owns its fixed wrapper; output buffers and decoded candidate state use the invoking allowance. Metadata, vector and page I/O checks cancellation between at-most-4-KiB attempts. The source and restored owners keep their existing native allocator/reference-count bookkeeping boundaries. Physical sealing, canonical validation and prepared query opening may fail for invalid input or insufficient memory; all temporary and candidate reservations then drop without publishing an index. Cache contents and runtime cache statistics are not archive state. Engine still needs joint catalog/data/resource selection, live mutation admission and atomic activation before these owner images constitute a database backup.

Image reconstruction uses the input control for parsing, physical sealing, canonical validation, prepared-reader creation and final EOF. The returned writable owner has a fresh runtime cancellation signal shared by its canonical source, retained query and origin reader. All input allocations and future native work retain the original memory allowance; separating cancellation neither releases existing leases nor enlarges that allowance. Ordinary non-image DiskANN construction continues to use its supplied control for both preparation and retained execution.

### Preservation argument for memory DiskANN images

Let the selected carrier be `(G, P, C, D, r, n, O)`: physical generation `G`, complete sealed artifacts `P`, ordered canonical document tensors with origins `C`, uncovered change map `D`, current revision `r`, population pair `n`, and build/query options `O`. Assume valid source state, the existing complete-artifact and origin codecs, successful admitted I/O, unique writer incarnations and the same floating-point execution model. Archive authenticity and the surrounding Engine catalog/resource cut are independent preconditions. The image introduces no retrieval operator or new approximation.

The integer, origin, length and f32-bit encodings are injective over this carrier. Induction over ordered records and pages recovers `P` byte for byte; the existing manifest decoder and complete sealer recover the same generation, topology, quantization, exact-side values, digests and provenance. Induction over ordered document tensors and changes recovers `C`, `D`, `r` and `n`. The coverage merge proves that `D` is precisely the current-origin difference from the base, including additions and empty deletion tensors. Configuration comes from the same original fields. Therefore decoding an exported valid source yields the identical selected carrier, modulo memory placement and cache contents, without deriving a different graph from its canonical vectors.

Existing navigation consumes the same codebook, codes, page adjacency, parameters and canonical visibility. Exact-side handling and final tensor reduction consume the same raw coordinates, origins, ordinal order and changed-document set. For the same floating-point execution model, defined queries therefore yield the same document support, score bits, threshold decisions, tie ordering and selected-generation metadata. Numeric overflow and approximation remain those of the existing owner; this establishes no new recall bound or cross-platform floating-point identity. Unchanged query carriers preserve their existing compositions and algebraic laws, with no optimizer rewrite.

For future writes, compare states modulo a one-to-one renaming of newly allocated origin and generation tokens. Existing tokens are fixed. Fresh source and destination tokens cannot equal existing tokens or each other under the incarnation and checked-counter assumptions; equality-based coverage checks consequently make the same decisions. Identical admitted mutations replace the same tensors and change membership, and identical explicit rebuild inputs/options pass through the same existing construction owner. Induction preserves subsequent logical observations under equivalent resources and before either allocator exhausts, while deliberately preventing divergent writers from impersonating each other's future identities. Captured immutable roots remain fixed throughout this induction because each mutation or rebuild publishes replacement ownership.

No candidate is returned until all sections, coverage checks, physical sealing, prepared-query validation and EOF succeed. Failure therefore exposes no partial index. The export-only handle cannot invoke a query that changes its captured evaluated state. These arguments establish memory DiskANN image fidelity and ownership; they do not establish Engine-wide transaction capture, repository publication or restore activation. Independent outer-wire fields, physical and canonical round trips, exact score comparisons before and after source destruction, fresh-lineage mutations/rebuilds, removed-change corruption, bounded capture and unsuccessful-import retirement exercise the corresponding boundaries.


Let I be the image-input cancellation signal and O the fresh runtime signal. Every fallible construction operation still observes I and uses the original admission budget. The private canonical/query/origin constructors store O without changing any encoded field or reservation; publication still follows complete validation and EOF. A failure before return retires the entire candidate. After return, cancelling I cannot change its logical state or operation admission, while invocation cancellation and O retain their existing runtime checks. All ordinary DiskANN constructors identify I and O, preserving their previous cancellation behavior. Under equivalent runtime controls, the unchanged values, maps, prepared readers and leases give the same query and mutation transitions. This extends the image inverse through subsequent writes, rebuilds, retained captures and source retirement without resetting a cancellation signal or admitting an uncharged replacement population.

## Analyzer descriptor and dictionary images

Analysis owns immutable compiled revisions and their exact dictionary dependencies; Common Storage owns streaming `UQAANA01` framing. `CompiledAnalyzer::dictionary_resources` borrows verified artifacts in `(language, SHA-256)` order without accessing a resolver, file or cache. The existing resolution boundary retains one handle per distinct required artifact. Tokenizers, dictionary-backed Unicode profiles and runtime filters contribute dependencies. Inline synonyms, user dictionaries, expanded stop sets and effective Japanese N-best settings already reside in the canonical descriptor. A dictionary used only to expand a stop set is not a runtime dependency.

The stream consists of the eight-byte magic, a counted canonical descriptor, a resource count, and ordered resources. Each resource contains a one-byte language tag (`0` for Nori, `1` for Kuromoji), its 32-byte artifact hash, and counted original dictionary bytes. Counts are little-endian unsigned 64-bit values. Existing descriptor fingerprints, dictionary formats, normalization profiles and dictionary contents are unchanged. This is an image of one compiled revision; catalog names, field bindings, historical revision selection and the enclosing committed Engine cut are separate owners.

`write_analyzer_image` borrows immutable descriptor and artifact bytes and returns the written byte count. Its I/O windows are at most 4 KiB and poll the original cancellation owner between partial or interrupted attempts. It never reopens a source path. `read_analyzer_image` validates descriptor length, language availability, resource count, strict ordering, dictionary length and complete input before private reconstruction. Resource count is bounded by the destination stage limit plus the tokenizer and normalization dependencies. Oversized payloads fail before allocation. Truncated, trailing, duplicate, unordered, unknown or missing resources cannot produce a compiled result.

`AnalyzerResources::restore_image` creates an isolated resource owner with the destination's original validation limits and resolvers that accept only supplied exact hashes. Neither an installed bundle, an existing compiled cache nor a host resolver can fill a missing image artifact. Existing descriptor and complete dictionary validators run before the candidate is returned; the reconstructed dependency list must equal the supplied list, so unused artifacts also fail. No candidate or partial cache is published into the destination owner.

Encoded input buffers and the resource-input vector use `StorageReadControl` admission. Imported dictionary buffers transfer into shared retained dictionary artifacts without copying their payloads; their reservations survive until the last compiled or resolved owner releases them. The temporary input-vector reservation covers reconstruction and retires when that vector is destroyed. Descriptor parsing/preparation, executable stage objects and decoded native models retain the existing Analysis-owned descriptor, stage and dictionary limits; they are not represented as allocations charged to the Storage allowance. Cancellation is checked during framed I/O and around the existing bounded Analysis preparation call, not inside native model validation. These are explicit owner boundaries, not a claim that all compiled model memory is measured by the archive reader.

### Preservation argument for analyzer images

Let a compiled revision be `A = (D, R)`, where `D` is its validated canonical descriptor and `R` maps every required `(language, hash)` identity to its verified original bytes. Its observable function `T_A(x)` returns the complete analyzed stream, including ordered terms, raw UTF-16 forms, positions, lengths, source/terminal offsets, morphology, and errors; its normalization function is `N_A(x)`. Compiled revisions and their dictionaries are immutable. The argument assumes the same supported descriptor/profile implementations, sufficient destination validation and execution limits, and the existing content-identity collision assumption. Unsupported features or insufficient limits reject restoration instead of substituting another revision.

The encoder is injective on admitted `(D, R)`: fixed tags and counted fields determine every boundary, strict identity order determines each map entry, and the stream retains all descriptor and resource bytes. The decoder accepts no trailing bytes and applies the existing descriptor and dictionary validators, including requested-hash verification. Exact-only lookup and closure equality establish `D' = D` and `R' = R` for every successful decode. Retaining dependencies at the original resolution boundary is essential: later name changes or cache eviction cannot change the encoded map. Excluding a dictionary used only during default expansion is sound because the resulting explicit set is already a component of `D`, and the restored stage has no dictionary lookup.

Preparation selects each tokenizer, character filter, token filter and normalizer from the same descriptor and exact resource inputs. Therefore each restored stage is the same deterministic function on its defined input. Induction over the ordered stage sequence gives equality after every stage, including source mappings and terminal state, and hence `T_decode(encode(A))(x) = T_A(x)` and `N_decode(encode(A))(x) = N_A(x)` for every admitted input. Resolved user rules and effective N-best costs are preserved rather than reread or re-estimated. Typed malformed-image, feature and resource failures have no published candidate; they are outside this successful-image equality.

For any existing downstream operation `F` whose inputs are complete analyzer observations, substitution gives `F(T_decode(encode(A))(x)) = F(T_A(x))`. This includes term/phrase construction and normalization; it does not assert semiring laws for decorated postings or prove a joint database snapshot from an analyzer alone. Existing retrieval composition laws remain applicable under their original preconditions because this extension changes neither the carrier nor an operator. Equality for a full restored Engine additionally requires the separately owned data, catalog, binding and index cuts. Failure, cancellation and destruction release only candidate-owned reservations; an immutable source remains usable and unrelated retained handles keep their original leases.

Owning tests compare complete token observations and exact image bytes, preserve resolved synonyms after file deletion, restore Korean/Japanese artifacts after source and cache loss, reject host/cache fallback, validate hash and closure mismatches, enforce feature/length/order admission, and observe reservation retirement at the last handle. These finite checks exercise the implementation boundaries of the argument; they do not replace it.


## Evaluated stored-model images

ML owns `UQAMOD01` for the complete stored `DeepModel` carrier: ordered `DeepLayerSpec` values, fusion alpha and stored gating. Image output borrows the captured catalog model; input returns `Budgeted<DeepModel>` only after complete framing and ordinary model validation. It neither trains a replacement nor serializes through decimal JSON. The enclosing Engine catalog remains responsible for model names, versions, provenance, authorization, joint capture and activation.

The header is the eight-byte magic, the little-endian 64-bit IEEE representation of alpha, one gating byte (`0` none, `1` ReLU, `2` Swish), and a little-endian 64-bit layer count. Layer tags `0` through `15` select Input, Embed, Dense, Flatten, GlobalPool, Softmax, BatchNorm, Dropout, CNN1D, CNN2D, Propagate, Conv, Pool, Attention, RNN and LSTM respectively. Fields follow the stored variant's declaration order. Dimensions/counts are unsigned 64-bit values with checked native-size conversion; scalar weights and parameters retain their IEEE bits. Vectors and UTF-8 labels have counted payloads. Boolean values admit only `0` and `1`; global pooling uses `0` Avg, `1` Max and `2` AvgMax; graph pooling uses `0` Avg and `1` Max; aggregation uses `0` Mean, `1` Sum and `2` Max; direction uses `0` Out, `1` In and `2` Both. Unknown tags, invalid UTF-8, invalid booleans/enums, truncation and trailing input fail privately.

Layer arrays, weight/bias vectors and label buffers reserve before allocation and retain the original `StorageReadControl` allowance through the final decoded owner. Fixed stack windows transfer at most 4 KiB per I/O attempt. Input rejects excessive allocations through the original allowance; failure or cancellation drops the private model and all its leases. Shared decoded handles retain that reservation until the last owner closes. Ordinary shape/numeric validation runs between cancellation checks; framed payload I/O polls throughout. Validation borrows weight slices instead of constructing another complete runtime model, so neither export nor the final import check duplicates the corpus of weights. Allocator bookkeeping and short validation diagnostics are separate from retained model buffers.

Runtime layers and stored model specs now project into one private borrowed validation representation. The same validator keeps first-layer admission, alpha validation, layer order, dimension products, tensor lengths and finite-value checks in their original order. Only fields already observed by those checks are projected; graph labels, enum choices and other evaluated parameters remain present in the image carrier. Ordinary runtime construction and `DeepModel::validate` continue to use those rules. This change adds no model layer or numerical backend and does not modify JSON persistence.

### Preservation argument for stored-model images

Let an admitted stored model be `M = (L, a, g)` with finite alpha `a`, stored gating `g` and an ordered finite list `L` of validated model specs. Each spec consists of a variant tag, integral dimensions/flags, exact strings and finite IEEE-754 binary64 parameters. The domain is stored `DeepModel`, not a runtime `Signal` operator containing executable host references. Compatibility requires support for this image version and every stored layer, native dimensions able to represent the declared integers, and sufficient destination resources.

Encoding is injective on this domain: distinct variant/enum tags remain distinct; counted payloads have unique boundaries; UTF-8 encodes each retained label exactly; and `to_bits`/`from_bits` are inverse maps on every stored float, including signed zero and finite subnormals. By induction over the layer list, a successful complete decode recovers each variant and field in its original position. Thus `decode(encode(M)) = M` in bitwise stored representation. The ordinary validator rejects invalid shapes, nonfinite parameters and incompatible counts before any decoded candidate is exposed.

For each original runtime layer or model spec `l`, let `V(l)` be its borrowed validation view. `V` retains every field read by the preexisting checks with the same value or slice contents; ignored fields had no validation effect. The common validator performs the same first-layer/alpha checks followed by the same ordered layer checks, so its acceptance and semantic diagnostic order are unchanged. Runtime allocation for `to_layers` remains an execution-construction concern, while validation no longer allocates copies of those weights. Failure does not publish a model or alter an existing catalog handle.

Let `P(M, X, C)` be the existing prediction operation with feature/signal input `X`, context `C` and a fixed supported backend. Equal stored bits and order select equal layer operations, tensors, graph labels and gating. Induction over execution steps yields equal intermediate states and the same final scores, class probabilities or defined runtime errors. No arithmetic is performed by image conversion, so it introduces zero additional numerical approximation; backend arithmetic keeps its existing assumptions. Substitution into any downstream fusion or ranking composition preserves that operation's inputs and existing laws. This argument does not infer a joint Engine catalog/data snapshot or interchangeability of different numerical backends.

Owning tests include an independent literal tag/count/IEEE fixture, every stored layer and enum, full CPU score/probability comparison after source loss, semantic-validation correspondence, malformed input, cancellation and last-handle retirement. A two-MiB weight fixture counts actual allocations: export uses bounded scratch, import allocates one retained payload, and a 64-KiB allowance rejects before allocating that payload. These deterministic bounds do not claim a latency improvement or MLX runtime qualification.

## Immutable memory graph and model resources

Graph owns `GraphStoreHandle::capture_image_with_control` and its export-only `GraphImageSnapshot`. ML owns `DeepModel::capture_image_with_control` and `ModelImageSnapshot`. Each image retains the existing immutable `Arc` after admitting its complete exposed payload footprint. Graph includes global entities, all partition memberships and derived indexes, reverse memberships, label registries and allocation state; ML includes every layer buffer and string. Capacities, rather than lengths alone, determine retained string and vector charges. B-tree live entries are charged, while unavailable node slack and allocator/reference-count bookkeeping remain outside the payload contract. Persistent graphs belong to their provider and cannot enter this memory owner.

Let R be a valid immutable graph or model, E its existing image encoder, and S(R, c) a successful captured owner under control c. Capture changes no field of R and retains that exact allocation. Therefore E(S(R, c)) = E(R), including graph identifiers, memberships, derived state, model parameters and floating-point bits. Engine publishes graph and model changes by replacing immutable resource references. An external mutable `Arc` access likewise detaches before mutation while S holds a reference. Thus, by induction over replacements, rollback restoration and destruction of live owners, every successful image observation of S remains E(R). No new algebraic operator, approximation, graph reconstruction, model training or query evaluation is introduced.

Memory Engine construction registers both resource catalogs in its capture domain. On first preservation, the catalog adapter copies each original name into an admitted ordered map and requests an image from its resource owner. The mapping preserves exactly the selected names and resource references; later names cannot enter it. The catalog cell invokes that producer before replacement, rollback restoration or final source destruction, so the existing component-capture argument applies. Engine owns only registration and retained-resource adaptation. Committed/private selection, complete durable catalogs and joint Engine activation remain separate requirements.

Source retention and image-output working memory use separate controls. A retained resource keeps its original cancellation even after it is cloned out of the catalog and that catalog, capture registry and Engine are destroyed. Common Storage's retained-output adapter checks this original control before each output attempt and after successful completion, preserving typed cancellation through codec I/O errors; the existing codec also checks the invoking control. A fresh invocation cannot revive an originally cancelled image. Failed admission drops partial names, resource images and reservations, invalidates only that cut and allows ordinary publication. Each independent capture admits its own resource footprint; clones within one captured resource share its lease, which retires only after the final clone.

Registration does not visit graph entities or model buffers. First preservation counts graph entries and nested properties and visits model layer headers without copying entities or weights; it is not a constant-time source-write guarantee. Owner tests cover denied payloads, unused retained capacity, exact images after mutation and source loss, independent allowances and both cancellation scopes. Engine tests cover rollback, savepoint restoration, same-name replacement, post-cut names, unread closure and resource children surviving their parent catalog. These component obligations do not establish a complete committed Engine backup cut.

## Captured database, schema and sequence authority

SQL's `catalog::security::capture` owns independent authority production. A database or sequence value contains its bound owner identity and optional ordered ACL. Each ACL entry contains the exact optional grantee, grantor, privilege mask and grant-option mask. A schema additionally contains its optional namespace OID, object incarnation and tuple revision. Exhaustive constructors preserve every field; `None`, an empty ACL and a nonempty ACL remain distinct. Native registry admission retains exact names and both qualified-name components without parsing, normalization or resolution against current role names. Sequence authority belongs to the matching complete [native sequence generation](#transaction-sequence-catalog-generations), including its actual persistence partition.

Let A be this authority carrier, C its capture operation and O an existing privilege or identity observation with a fixed role catalog and evaluation context. C leaves every scalar and mask equal, preserves each ACL position and optional state, and copies names byte-for-byte. Structural induction over ACLs and ordered registry entries gives O(C(A)) = O(A). No rebinding or privilege inference occurs during capture, and no authorization or query operator changes. This component result requires the final Engine image to select the corresponding role catalog at the same committed cut; capturing authority alone cannot establish that condition.

The authority producer admits each destination vector before allocation and each name through its owning production control. Immutable native entries retain these leases; catalog capture admits their cached footprint independently and shares the selected roots. Engine's catalog-cell boundary preserves selected generations before grant/revoke, rollback restoration or source retirement; sequence records and authority share one source. An underfunded or cancelled cut fails and releases partial output without changing ordinary authorization. Tests cover ACL absence and grant paths, namespace tuple identity, denied large ACLs, exact qualified names, real SQL grants/revokes, savepoint/transaction rollback, dropped objects, unread Engine closure and independent captures.

## Captured role and membership generations

SQL's `catalog::roles::capture` owns detached role definitions and membership rows. A role definition contains its OID, immutable incarnation, tuple revision, exact name, attribute set and connection limit. Captured attributes use an ordered, admitted vector with exactly the source set's elements. A membership retains its original map key, row OID, role/member/grantor bindings and independent admin/inherit/set flags. Each binding preserves its name and immutable OID/incarnation pair; capture does not resolve a name against a newer catalog or combine separate grant paths.

Let R map exact role names to definitions and M map the three bound endpoint identities to membership rows. The role capture C_R copies every scalar and string and maps the attribute set to its ordered element sequence, preserving membership in that set. C_M copies every map key, endpoint and flag and preserves the source order. Thus every original identity lookup, attribute predicate and membership edge is unchanged under the inverse ordered-map interpretation. Induction over a role-membership traversal preserves its reachable identities and each edge's option checks; equal visible names or OIDs with different incarnations remain distinct. No privilege, SQL execution or optimizer rule changes. This equivalence assumes R and M belong to the same selected generation; Engine-wide committed selection remains an independent obligation.

The producers admit destination vectors and strings before allocation and retain their reservations through the resulting owned value. Bindings are copied without retaining the source session's allocation or cancellation. Exhaustive field construction prevents a newly added logical field from being silently omitted. On a denied allocation or cancellation, the producer drops all partial output. The existing capture-cell state machine preserves the selected catalog before mutation, rollback restoration and final source destruction, and isolates a failed cut from ordinary role publication. Tests cover all attributes, tuple versions, multiple grantors, recycled OIDs with distinct incarnations, source cancellation, denied large names, real SQL role and membership changes, savepoint rollback, same-name replacement, unread closure and independent underfunded/funded cuts.

## Captured system, routine and foreign authority

SQL's `catalog::security::capture::relations` preserves three additional stored authority registries. A built-in routine entry retains its OID, tuple revision and ordered EXECUTE ACL with original grantee/grantor incarnations and grant-option flag. A system relation retains its exact qualified name, optional table ACL and every column ACL, including empty entries with independent tuple revisions. A foreign table uses the complete existing table-security producer, including the bound owner, optional relation ACL, column ACLs and all relation/column revisions. Capturing stored authority never projects through effective privilege views that discard empty ACLs or revision-only entries.

Let S be any of these registries and C its capture. Exhaustive constructors preserve each scalar, mask, option and identity; string production preserves each name's bytes, while ordered vectors preserve map iteration and every original ACL position. Structural induction over entries and nested ACLs therefore gives an inverse ordered-map interpretation with exactly S's tuple contents, presence states and keys. Consequently both authorization observations and tuple-version comparisons are unchanged for the corresponding fixed role catalog and invocation context. System-table write masks and routine execution checks continue to use their existing owners. No query operator, privilege rule or optimizer rewrite changes; the final backup must still select the matching role and object catalogs at one committed cut.

The producer admits every vector and copied name before allocation and transfers all leases to its retained result. Original cancellation is checked during construction, and failed production drops partial values and reservations. Engine registers these sources before memory publication; permission mutation, rollback restoration and final destruction preserve selected generations through the existing capture-cell state machine. Independent denied and funded cuts cannot change ordinary GRANT/REVOKE publication. SQL tests cover absent/empty/nonempty ACLs, column tombstones, exact qualified names, routine and foreign tuple identities, large denied payloads and cancellation; Engine tests cover real grants, savepoints, rollback, same-name foreign-table replacement and unread closure.

## Captured type definitions

SQL captures the complete stored domain, enum, composite, range and shell type registries as ordered admitted entries. Each definition keeps its exact registry key, immutable object identity, public OIDs, names, bound owner and optional ACL. Domains additionally preserve every bound declaration, default and constraint identity; enums preserve every label OID, immutable order-key byte and float4 sort-position bit; composites preserve all attribute numbers, including dropped slots and selected collation identities; ranges preserve the independently named multirange and its identities, selected operator class, collation and support routines. Shell capture does not complete or reinterpret an undefined type.

For a registry R, let C(R) replace its ordered map representation with the ordered entry sequence and apply the exhaustive syntax constructor to each definition. Scalar fields and fixed identities are copied exactly; owned strings and enum keys preserve every byte, and vectors preserve length and order.
