The existing detached syntax producer preserves expression structure and the representation of every captured constant, without evaluating, parsing or rebinding. Structural induction over each exhaustive definition therefore gives I(C(R)) = R, where I interprets the ordered entries as the original map. For a matching selected catalog, type lookup, attribute-number projection, enum comparison, bound constraint evaluation and range support selection have identical inputs and results. Dropped slots, independently moved multiranges and absent versus empty ACLs cannot be conflated. No SQL type rule or optimizer rewrite changes; complete backup must still select the corresponding routine, role and data generations at one committed cut.

The producer reserves destination vectors, names, boxed enum keys and nested syntax before allocation. Constant values receive independent capture ownership instead of retaining a source session's allowance. Failure drops all partial destinations and reservations. Engine registers the five producers before publishing memory state and uses the existing catalog mutation/rollback/destruction boundary, so an unread cut retains the selected generation while a failed cut leaves ordinary DDL usable. Allocation-counter tests compare actual live bytes with admitted output; representation tests cover identities, ACL states, enum keys, support objects, dropped slots and noncanonical float bits in detached constants. Engine tests exercise domain/enum/composite/shell SQL DDL and the actual stored-range registry boundary, including rollback, same-name replacement, closure and independent denied/funded captures. Native range declaration/publication remains in the existing type implementation plan; this capture unit neither implements nor qualifies that DDL.

## Captured catalog metadata and identities

Execution owns complete sequence, comment and index generations. A sequence retains all inline state fields: start, increment, current value, called flag, log count, declared data type, limits, cycle/cache settings, immutable definition generation and bound owner/dependency identities. Its native record also retains the exact name, immutable object ID, original public OID claim and persistence, paired with SQL-owned authority. A comment retains its complete class/object/subobject address and exact text; an index retains its registry key and complete row, including original encoded columns, parameters and optional definition. SQL retains graph namespace/label-sequence OIDs and all label relation/type/default/constraint/TOAST/index/trigger identities. Common Storage retains stored scoring parameter strings. Encoded strings remain byte-exact, with no JSON decoding, normalization or recalibration.

For each selected registry R, capture C preserves every map key through an admitted immutable root or an ordered admitted entry vector, as specified by its owning producer. Copy scalars retain every bit; exact string constructors retain every byte; nested label vectors retain every label and constraint key with its original value. Exhaustive index and graph-label constructors prevent omission of new owned fields, while sequence state and comment addresses implement `Copy`, requiring every field to remain inline. Induction over each registry and nested label map gives I(C(R)) = R under the inverse ordered-map interpretation. Consequently address lookup, OID occupancy, stored-index decoding, scoring-parameter retrieval and a sequence's next transition receive identical inputs. In particular, `called = false` at an integer boundary is never replaced by a synthesized previous value, and identity/definition generations are never derived again from current names. There is no new query operator, arithmetic approximation or optimizer rule. This argument assumes the corresponding registries and data resources belong to one selected cut; the Engine transaction owner must still select committed state and reconcile autonomous sequence positions before full backup is exposed.

Native admission retains complete entry and payload leases; root capture admits their cached footprint independently. Copying producers admit destination entry buffers and owned strings before allocation and retain their leases with the resulting values. Graph companion maps preserve all originally stored entries; index metadata retains absent versus empty definition strings. Capture failure releases partial allocations and invalidates only that cut through the existing source owner. SQL and Storage allocation-counter fixtures compare actual live bytes with admitted output; owning tests cover exact identity/persistence, extreme sequence positions, address distinctions, opaque encodings, large late payloads and original cancellation. Engine tests use real SQL and graph/scoring APIs, verify rollback and same-name replacement, retain unread catalogs through closure, and isolate denied scoring captures from ordinary publication and independent funded cuts.

Native sequence and index producers select their durable partitions before capture admission. Comment selection, other companion metadata and complete Engine image membership still require the owning eligibility projection at the committed cut. Temporary table payload owners remain unregistered; the Engine regression compares independent captures before and after adding temporary rows to the same catalog, so variable encoded object identities cannot distort the retention comparison. Component capture does not establish final image eligibility.

## Captured foreign definitions

SQL owns foreign-wrapper and server capture. A wrapper retains its exact registry key, name, immutable identity, bound owner, handler variant, optional validator reference and ordered option vector. Native handler enums remain descriptors, while function-backed handlers and validators retain the original routine OID and complete bound call definition. A server retains its exact name, wrapper name, options and all metadata: OID/incarnation, owner, optional type/version, optional original option order and optional wrapper incarnation. Legacy absence is never repaired by resolving a current name during capture.

Execution owns the foreign-table carrier and copies every stored field: exact registry and declaration names, persistence, immutable identity, public relation/type/array OIDs, optional array name, original server name and bound reference, columns, dropped physical slots, constraints, options and option order. Existing SQL producers copy complete bound column and constraint syntax; retained constants are detached into the capture allowance, including after source cancellation. Foreign runtime rows, remote credentials held outside catalog options and host function pointers are not part of these descriptors, and capture invokes no handler, validator or remote operation.

Let W, S and T be the selected wrapper, server and foreign-table registries. Their captures preserve every scalar, identity, optional state and owned string; ordered map entries become ordered admitted vectors, while declaration-order vectors retain their original ordering. Exhaustive constructors enumerate every source field. The inverse ordered-map interpretation therefore yields I(C(W)) = W, I(C(S)) = S and I(C(T)) = T by structural induction over entries and nested syntax. Default and constraint constants preserve their evaluated representation through the existing detached producer. Lookup by immutable identity, option projection, attribute numbering and bound declaration evaluation consequently receive identical inputs for matching selected role/routine/type catalogs and external bindings. Reusing an object name cannot change a saved identity reference. This does not assert equivalence of external data at two different times; no external data is copied or queried.

Every entry buffer, string and syntax node is admitted before allocation, and the completed value retains its independent leases. Failure releases partial definitions through the existing capture state machine without changing ordinary DDL or another cut. Owning tests compare actual live allocations with admission, cover every handler variant and legacy optional state, verify exact slots and noncanonical float bits, and retire cancelled source-default ownership independently. Engine tests exercise real DDL, dropped columns, savepoint/transaction rollback, same-name replacement, unread closure and denied/funded captures. Complete image membership, joint committed selection and restore-time compatibility validation retain their separate obligations.

## Captured materialized path indexes

Graph owns `PathIndex::capture_memory_with_control`. It copies the selected memory index's exact label-sequence map and already evaluated `(start, end)` sets into ordered admitted vectors. Sequence boundaries remain explicit, including empty sequences, empty result sets and labels containing separators. No graph traversal, definition parsing or path evaluation occurs during capture. Persistent indexes and retained query views are rejected by this memory-only producer; their selected provider snapshots retain their separate image obligations.

Let a materialized index P be a finite ordered map from finite byte-exact label sequences to finite ordered sets of vertex-ID pairs. Capture C enumerates every map entry once in key order, copies each label without transformation and enumerates each pair set once in its original order. Define I to interpret the resulting entry vector as that map and each pair vector as its set. Key uniqueness and pair uniqueness follow from the source map/set, and byte/ID copies are identities, so I(C(P)) = P. Consequently membership, absence, pair lookup and all compositions consuming these evaluated pairs have identical inputs and outputs. Labels `a/b` and the sequence `a`, `b` cannot be conflated. Capture introduces no reachability operator or rewrite and does not claim that re-evaluating paths against a later graph would return P.

Every entry buffer, label-vector capacity, string and pair-vector capacity is reserved before allocation. Copy loops check original cancellation, and partial failures drop all constructed entries and reservations. Completed values own these leases independently of the source map and other capture allowances. Ordered vectors avoid retaining opaque source B-tree nodes or unadmitted graph owners. Empty vectors retain their ordinary zero-allocation representation.

Engine registers the path registry before memory publication and delegates native root and element retention to Graph as described below. The existing capture-cell owner preserves the selected registry before graph mutation invalidates its indexes, before explicit drop/replacement, during rollback restoration and on unread closure. Thus subsequent operations can replace the live index while the captured P remains unchanged. A denied cut becomes terminal without preventing an ordinary update or invalidating a separately funded cut. Retained catalog children continue to check their original cancellation after the Engine and cut handles are gone.

Graph tests compare capture with actual built index results, verify independent source retirement, distinct sequence boundaries, large labels and 20,000 pairs, independent allowances and query-view rejection. Engine tests cover actual graph mutation, index invalidation, savepoint/transaction rollback, same-name replacement, unread closure, original cancellation and denied/funded cuts. These copied-producer results establish the materialized resource boundary. Native catalog membership is described below; one committed Engine cut, image encoding and fresh Engine restore remain separate requirements.

### Immutable native path-index generations

The primary memory `PathIndex` stores its evaluated relation as ordered immutable vectors. Construction consumes the existing ordered map and pair sets, preserving their exact order and unique entries, then caches the complete reachable payload footprint from the owning header and actual vector/string capacities. Ordinary lookup uses binary search over exact label sequences and returns the same ordered pair set. The copied capture API above remains available; `capture_native_memory_with_control` independently admits this cached footprint and shares the immutable materialization without traversal or copying. Neither representation holds a graph handle, a mutable registry or a callback that can reevaluate paths.

For the materialized finite map P defined above, let V(P) be its ordered vector representation. Each sequence and pair moves without value transformation, so interpreting V(P) gives P. Native capture N shares V(P), hence I(N(V(P))) = P. Binary search selects exactly the same unique sequence that map lookup selected, including the empty sequence and labels containing separators; reconstructing its ordered pair set is the inverse of the pair-vector representation. Thus lookup, absence, path enumeration and every composition consuming these evaluated pairs retain their existing observations. Duplicate sequence declarations retain the same final map entry. No arithmetic, reachability approximation or optimizer law changes.

Graph's `PathIndexCatalog` owns immutable ordered registration roots. Each entry records its copied key capacity and the cached footprint of its memory materialization; Core adds complete immutable tree node storage. A separately maintained non-memory count changes only with a successfully published root. The empty catalog satisfies this invariant. Insertion computes the successor count and footprint, admits its key and changed tree path, and publishes both together; removal computes its successor only after admitted tree deletion; clear installs the empty root and zero count. Private multi-removal and session-rebinding candidates publish only after every selected operation succeeds. Induction therefore preserves exact membership, capability count and cached footprint for each generation.

A memory capture first checks the capability count, rejects persistent/query-view entries without evaluating a graph, and independently admits the complete cached root footprint. It visits no registrations and allocates no nodes or payload. Separate captures each fund their complete retained closure, including repeated references to a shared materialization. Selected child creation admits its cache node, copied name and wrapper before transferring that materialization's already admitted bytes from the parent's remaining lease; repeated child selection shares the existing wrapper. Before parent retirement, each transferred lease remains reachable through the parent's child cache. After retirement, an escaping child retains its original materialization and cancellation scope while unrelated root nodes, names and materializations are released. Thus transfer conserves admission and no reachable native payload loses its original allowance. Reference-count bookkeeping follows the existing shared-owner convention.

Engine keeps the original catalog-cell and graph transaction boundaries. Memory registration prepares its complete native successor before publication. Persistent construction admits the complete registration successor before entering Graph's existing physical checkpoint; storage failure restores the old definition and evaluated pairs and drops the unpublished root. Explicit deletion and graph invalidation prepare removal roots before their dependent persistent deletions, then publish after success. Restoration and physical-session rebinding prepare complete private registries before replacing the current root. Existing transaction rollback restores the original registry and graph state together. A failed independent backup capture remains isolated from these ordinary operations. Joint committed Engine publication, native graph entity ownership, complete image encoding and fresh Engine activation remain separate obligations.

### Materialized path-index images

Let P be the finite map from exact finite UTF-8 label sequences to finite sets of ordered pairs of unsigned 64-bit vertex identities. Its native representation V(P) above has strictly increasing sequences and strictly increasing pairs within each sequence. The versioned encoding E(P) starts with `UQAPTH01` and a little-endian unsigned 64-bit path count. Each path contains a label count, each label's byte count and UTF-8 bytes, a pair count and the two little-endian unsigned 64-bit identities of each pair. Empty sequences, empty labels and empty relations have distinct count boundaries. Separators inside labels are bytes, not delimiters, and identifiers retain all bits. The codec does not need a graph or infer the relation from graph contents.

Every count determines exactly the following field boundary. Fixed-width identity decoding inverts identity encoding, and length-delimited UTF-8 decoding inverts the original valid label bytes. Induction over labels gives the original sequence; induction over pairs gives its original ordered set; induction over paths gives V(P). Complete EOF validation excludes extensions and truncation, and strict sequence/pair validation excludes duplicate or reordered representations. Therefore D(E(P)) = V(P), and E(D(b)) = b for every accepted image b. The decoder neither sorts nor deduplicates hostile input. The ordinary native lookup and enumeration interpret V(P) identically before and after this inverse. Any existing composition consuming those exact sequences or relations consequently receives the same values and presence/absence observations. No new query operator, numeric approximation, path evaluation or optimizer rewrite is introduced. Relationship validation against a complete restored graph belongs to logical Engine image validation, not to this graph-independent carrier codec.

The native owner is private and immutable. Its map/set constructor establishes strict order, and image reconstruction publishes it only after the checks above. Borrowed and owned output therefore need no corpus validation pass: preparation is immediately ready with zero visited payload positions. The encoder retains three native iterators, a fixed header and an unfinished borrowed label slice. Each transition emits a count or pair, except a bounded transition between pair and path sections; empty labels and relations still emit their count fields. Each read copies at most 16 KiB and polls cancellation between headers and label windows of at most 4 KiB. Concatenating any partition of successful nonempty reads yields E(P), independently of output window sizes or suspension. Synchronous output drains that same encoder. Failed output remains an unpublished partial image.

Import reserves the owner header and every vector/string capacity before allocation, transfers those reservations without copying into the resulting immutable native generation and validates long common label prefixes in cancellation-aware windows. The cached footprint equals the owner size plus actual reachable capacities; reference-count bookkeeping follows the existing convention. Native clones share the original admission; independently captured images also retain their own required allowance. Field order frees payloads before retiring their reservation. Input errors, trailing data, admission failure and unwinding drop the private candidate without publishing it. Common Storage's independently owned continuation retains the captured source and both cancellation owners until successful EOF, failure, unwind or drop; a failed reader cannot resume and complete EOF remains complete. Replacement, catalog removal and Engine closure cannot alter the selected immutable relation.

Owning Graph tests use literal reachability results and an independent format fixture, including empty sequences/labels, separators, non-ASCII text, NUL and extreme identities. They exercise malformed ordering, duplicates, huge counts, partial/interrupted I/O, constant-state output over large labels/pair sets, exact retained capacities, both cancellation owners, source loss, thread transfer and terminal retirement. Protected full/incremental repository tests preserve selected pairs during source replacement, verify exact reuse and changed images and restore children after source and ancestor removal. An Engine resource-capture test streams the selected original after graph mutation, same-name replacement and Engine closure. These are component obligations; complete committed memory image assembly, catalog/graph closure validation, fresh activation and public restore jobs remain open under #1322.

## Captured analyzer revisions and field bindings

Analysis owns detached capture of a compiled analyzer's canonical descriptor and exact encoded dictionary closure. The producer copies descriptor bytes and every ordered `(kind, SHA-256, encoded bytes)` resource through Core's admitted constructors. It neither resolves a name/path nor retains the compiled pipeline, decoded dictionary models or a source import allowance. Resolved synonym data, expanded stop sets, inline user rules and component revisions remain in the canonical descriptor. The captured carrier exposes only borrowed descriptor and dictionary views.

Common Storage owns `AnalyzerImageSnapshot`, its original control and the existing `UQAANA01` framing. Live and captured revisions share one framing implementation. Snapshot output borrows the detached buffers, polls both original and invoking cancellation and requires no payload-sized scratch allocation. Each snapshot clone shares the same source allowance; separately created snapshots admit independent copies. Dropping the final snapshot releases its descriptor, artifacts and retained header. Encoded dictionaries imported under a source allowance can retire before the new snapshot because capture copies their bytes into its own allowance.

Common Storage also captures each field binding's exact table/field key, independent index and search names and images, owner and last-assignment phase. These sides are captured from their retained compiled revisions, not resolved against the current named registry. Equal names therefore do not imply equal revisions. Unassigned sides, independent historical bindings and GIN ownership remain distinguishable. Engine registers the named and field registries in the existing memory capture domain before publication and supplies only the retained-resource adapters.

Let an analyzer image A be `(D, R)`, where D is the canonical descriptor byte string and R is its ordered exact resource closure. Analysis capture C copies each scalar/hash and each byte string without transformation, so C(A) = A by identity of the copied fields and ordered enumeration. The shared framing function E receives the same D, count and resource entries, hence E(C(A)) = E(A). The existing image reconstruction argument then yields identical complete token observations for the same component revisions, limits and input, including graph edges, offsets, increments and normalization metadata; equality of token terms alone is insufficient. Capture itself never analyzes text.

For a field binding B, the constructor exhaustively preserves its key, optional names, owner and phase and applies C independently to both selected sides. The inverse entry-map interpretation therefore restores the same index/search revision relation, even when a current named definition has been replaced. Every subsequent composition receives the same selected analyzer inputs under the existing binding rules. This component result assumes the matching evaluated postings and catalog generation; the final Engine owner must still select one committed joint cut and validate image membership before activation. No tokenizer rule, ranking algebra or optimizer rewrite changes.

Admission precedes every destination vector, copied descriptor, name and dictionary buffer. Original cancellation is checked between bounded copy/output windows, and partial failure drops preceding entries and nested image reservations. The capture-cell boundary preserves unread selected registries before publication, rollback or destruction; a failed cut cannot redirect ordinary analyzer updates or consume another cut's resources. Owner fixtures cover exact byte output, resolved-file removal, independent source/clone retirement, late allocation failure, all enabled dictionary languages, both cancellation scopes and historical field sides. Engine fixtures exercise real named registration and field assignment, rollback, replacement, unread closure and independently failed/funded captures. Complete committed image membership and Engine restore retain their separate obligations.

## Captured trigger definitions

SQL captures the complete stored trigger registry as ordered relation and trigger entries, including empty per-relation maps. Each entry preserves its declaration, bound function incarnation, enable mode, trigger incarnation, optional constraint name and public trigger/constraint OIDs. The existing exhaustive syntax producer retains timing, event order, update columns, transition names, arguments, deferrability, referenced relation and the original WHEN syntax. Literal payloads receive independent capture ownership; OLD/NEW references and bound routine identities remain expressions rather than substituted runtime values. Capture neither invokes a routine nor creates a trigger.

For a valid stored registry T, capture C enumerates each ordered map exactly once and constructs every stored field without deriving a new name, identity or OID. The inverse map interpretation I therefore gives I(C(T)) = T, using the existing detached-expression identity for WHEN literals and structural identity for its remaining syntax. Enable-mode selection, event matching, constraint scheduling, transition naming and bound function lookup consequently receive identical inputs for matching relation, routine and transaction contexts. Optional legacy identities remain absent rather than being resolved against a newer catalog. No trigger rule, deferred-event queue, SQL operator or rewrite is introduced or changed. Capturing the declaration does not capture private queued trigger events.

Entry buffers, copied names, argument vectors and condition payloads are admitted before construction. Failure or original cancellation releases every partial definition. Engine registers the source before memory publication; its existing catalog owner preserves selected definitions before DDL, rollback restoration or unread closure. Independent denied and funded cuts keep separate outcomes while ordinary DDL remains usable. SQL fixtures cover all enable modes, constraint and transition declarations, legacy absence, exact allocation, detached retained literals and late argument failure. Engine fixtures use actual trigger DDL, bound WHEN routines, constraint identities, enable changes, rollback, same-name replacement and unread closure. Matching routine definitions use the routine capture owner below; complete committed membership, logical restore validation and activation remain separate requirements.

## Captured views and materialized results

A stored view consists of its immutable relation identity, bound query syntax, fixed output names, persistence/options/kind, evaluated materialized rows and their type/collation descriptors, population state, public catalog identities, row-array name and complete relation authority. Individual definition capture enumerates every stored field, using ordered admitted entries for materialized row maps. Durable catalog capture selects non-temporary definitions before copying their names or payloads. It copies evaluated values through Core's representation-preserving producer and admits query constants independently of their original retained owner. It neither executes the query nor changes whether the materialized view is populated.

Let P denote the existing persisted-query projection: it removes analysis output/source/grouping descriptors, invoking-session relation authorization, deferred runtime substitutions, executable table-query caches and transient subquery output identities. These fields are explicitly excluded by the existing SQL serialization contract and must be rebuilt in the next analysis scope. An execution-scoped retained-row source has no persisted interpretation and is rejected. For valid stored query syntax Q, capture recursively constructs the same relational, source, CTE and scalar nodes as P(Q), preserving names, bound identities, query-arena positions, type metadata, window structure and literal representations. The scalar constructor uses admitted iterative frames and detaches retained literals at their leaf owner; it does not substitute OLD/NEW references, rebind names or evaluate expressions. Ordinary executable copies retain their existing shared-input semantics. Controlled and uncontrolled captures both detach the stored constants.

For a valid view V, interpret each captured ordered map as its original map and each captured literal by its exact value representation. Exhaustive field construction gives the same view metadata and authority and a query equal to P(Q) under that interpretation. Ordinary views therefore undergo the same subsequent catalog analysis and query evaluation with the same inputs. Materialized views expose the same stored row sequence, field keys, multiplicity, types, collations and floating-point bits independently of the current query result; their population state and later refresh definition also remain unchanged. Consequently relational, retrieval and scalar compositions receive the same observations, assuming matching captured catalog dependencies. Capture introduces no algebraic operation or optimizer rewrite.

Every destination vector, field name, query node and evaluated payload is admitted before construction. Shared literal payloads keep Core's established boundary that excludes reference-count bookkeeping from the payload allowance. Failure or cancellation releases completed siblings and suspended scalar constructors, and a source read error retains its original SQL diagnostic. Independent captured literals no longer pin their original allowance or compiled input owner. Engine's catalog boundary preserves an unread selected registry before mutation, rollback or destruction; a failed cut cannot prevent ordinary DDL or affect another funded cut. Owner fixtures cover complete query structures, exact allocation, materialized non-finite and signed-zero bits, ACL identities, transient-owner exclusion, execution-only source rejection, nested retained constants, source retirement and late failures. Engine fixtures cover actual ordinary/materialized DDL, volatile evaluated rows, rollback, replacement, unread closure and independent capture outcomes. The durable view projection below excludes temporary definitions at this owner boundary; filtering of other shared registries, committed selection, image encoding and fresh Engine activation remain separate requirements.

### Durable view membership

For an immutable view registry M, define D(M) as the ordered pairs (k, v) in M for which v.persistence is Permanent or Unlogged. SQL's durable catalog producer first counts precisely these entries, checking the original cancellation for every visited definition. It reserves output capacity for |D(M)|, then visits the same immutable map and applies the same predicate before copying any key, authority, syntax or evaluated row. Therefore its output keys, order and multiplicity equal D(M), and each included value preserves the complete view interpretation proved above. Temporary names and payloads are never passed to a copying or validation constructor. The predicate reads the selected object's persistence rather than inferring ownership from a namespace spelling, so a same-name object in another namespace cannot alter eligibility.

Projection D is idempotent and preserves disjoint registry union: D(D(M)) = D(M), and D(A union B) = D(A) union D(B) for disjoint keys. For selected views, the existing capture interpretation and all downstream relational operations remain unchanged when their dependencies belong to the same selected catalog. Excluded session objects have no durable observation to reproduce. This changes only the backup catalog projection; ordinary view registration, query execution and individual definition capture retain their existing behavior.

Both passes use constant traversal workspace and perform O(|M|) metadata visits; this component makes no constant-time publication claim. Destination allocation depends on the selected definitions only, including their exact vector capacity. A temporary-only registry produces an empty vector under a zero payload allowance without visiting session-owned query nodes; empty output still observes cancellation. Immutable catalog generation retention fixes the predicate and payload to the same source version through mutation, rollback, replacement and closure. Tests compare actual allocations before and after adding three MiB of temporary keys/payloads, preserve Permanent and Unlogged definitions, reject cancelled empty selections, and exercise a permanent view beside a one-MiB same-name temporary view in an actual Engine under a 32 KiB capture allowance. Filtering of other shared registries, bounded catalog publication, committed selection and complete Engine backup remain separate requirements.

## Captured routines and rewrite rules

SQL owns detached routine and rewrite-rule images. A routine image enumerates its entire declaration, including object and tuple identities, OID, signature/defaults, written type references, return form, language, attributes, owner, EXECUTE ACL, configuration and creation search path. Its body is either original source or an ordered creation-bound SQL plan sequence. Empty bound sequences remain distinct from source bodies. SQL's declaration owner already rejects SQL-standard PL/pgSQL bodies; a session-compiled PL/pgSQL body cannot enter a valid durable routine image. Capture does not call a parser, compiler, routine resolver or executable body. The derived definition-version cache is not durable state and can be recomputed from the identical stored declaration.

Let P denote the existing persisted-syntax projection. Besides the query exclusions described above, P omits routine client source mappings, raw window parser identities and unbound EXPLAIN parameter carriers. Execution-scoped retained rows have no persisted representation and are rejected. Captured syntax preserves every other constructor, binding, statement order and literal representation; retained constants receive independent value/type/collation ownership. The ordinary executable-copy path continues to retain its original source mapping, window identity and parameter semantics. Thus the captured routine declaration and bound body equal P of their originals, and a source body remains byte-identical and unexamined. With matching catalog dependencies, defaults and invocation compilation receive the same inputs, including source bodies that fail only when called.

A captured rule enumerates its declaration/action statements, enable mode, bound condition plan, optional OLD/NEW binding, optional dependency sets and public OID. Ordered admitted vectors represent map and set entries without changing membership, sort order or optional absence. The binding owner copies its original internal relation identities and positional column names together with the condition plan; no new OLD/NEW identity is allocated during capture. Later process-local restoration retains its separate remapping boundary. Under the inverse ordered-map/set interpretation, each captured rule equals its original persisted projection. Event selection, bound condition evaluation, rewrite action composition and dependency checks therefore receive identical inputs for matching captured catalogs; no relational rewrite or new execution operation is introduced.

Every destination vector, string, syntax node and constant is admitted before publication. A denied late overload, dependency or action releases the complete partial image; original cancellation and independently funded cuts retain separate outcomes. Engine registers both catalog sources before publication and uses the existing selected-generation owner for DDL, rollback restoration, same-name replacement and unread closure. Owning tests cover attributes, overload order, all enable modes, optional dependencies, allocation accounting, transient owners, cancelled-source constant detachment and late failure. Engine tests exercise actual routine/rule DDL, unexamined invalid source bodies, bound dependencies, rollback and closure. Joint committed membership, complete image encoding, logical restore validation and fresh Engine activation retain their separate requirements.

## Immutable scoring parameter generations

Common Storage owns `ScoringParameters`, an ordered finite map from signal names to opaque encoded strings. Engine's existing catalog cell, transaction snapshots, rollback and persistent-cache adapters retain that map's immutable root. Let I(R) be the ordered name/value interpretation of root R. Insertion of (k, v) produces I(R)[k := v], removal produces I(R) without k, and a missing removal is the identity. The shared tree's existing insertion/deletion laws preserve all other entries and their ordering. Replacement constructs the complete successor before assigning it; failed path admission leaves R and its payload total unchanged. Undo restores the original root, hence restores I(R) exactly, including duplicate JSON keys, whitespace and numeric spelling. No scoring operation, JSON decoder, cache invalidation rule or persistent catalog representation changes.

Core's `RetainedMap` stores each entry's owner-supplied footprint and the sum P of those footprints. Common Storage supplies the sum of the scoring key and encoded-string capacities. For insertion, P' = P - old(k) + capacity(k) + capacity(v); for removal, P' = P - old(k); the empty root has P = 0. Checked arithmetic precedes tree mutation and assignment follows successful mutation, so induction over successful updates establishes P for every published root. Replacement of an equal key replaces its key capacity as well as its payload. Failed updates preserve the induction invariant.

A capture reserves B(R) + P under its original independent allowance, where B(R) is the shared tree's constant-work structural byte count, then retains the immutable root. Reference-count bookkeeping follows the existing shared-map accounting convention. Admission visits no entries, allocates no payload and copies no string. Two captures each reserve their complete retained footprint even when their roots share physical allocations. Later writes copy a logarithmic tree path and retain unrelated entries by immutable reference; neither later payload growth nor a private replacement can become reachable from the captured root. The capture's containing memory-value owner preserves cancellation after source closure. Failed capture admission does not change normal catalog publication.

For every captured key k, lookup and ordered iteration return exactly I(R), so all existing calibration and learning observations composed with the same parameter decoder are preserved. The storage fixtures exercise constant capture allocation across 1, 64 and 4,096 entries, independently funded retention, significant string capacities, replacement/removal/clear, denied mutation, cancellation and original-byte fidelity. The Engine fixture exercises the actual committed-cut consumer, later native writes, savepoint rollback, closure and unrelated four-MiB payloads. This establishes bounded preservation for the scoring catalog; complete committed publication, image encoding/restore and public backup jobs retain their separate requirements.

## Immutable model catalog generations

ML owns `ModelCatalog`, mapping ordered names to immutable `Arc<DeepModel>` values. At insertion, ML computes the model footprint W from its allocation, layer-vector capacity and every separately allocated layer buffer or label. The same exhaustive layer visitor supplies direct model-image capture; the catalog caches W instead of traversing layers during later capture. Core's `RetainedMap` supplies immutable path updates and the cached sum of name capacities plus W. Its ordered-map and footprint induction above applies unchanged. Model replacement installs a distinct immutable owner, removal changes only the successor root, and rollback restores the original mapping. Public save/load copies, JSON persistence, validation and inference keep their original meaning; no model is retrained or reconstructed during capture.

For a selected root R, capture independently reserves B(R) + sum(K(k) + W(k)) and shares R in constant work, without traversing names, layers or weights. Here K is the stored name capacity and B follows the shared map's structural accounting convention. Let C be the set of model names already selected as detached image handles. The captured state retains B(R) + sum(K(k)) + sum(W(k) for k outside C); each cached image retains W(k) plus its newly admitted handle. Thus the sum of root and image payload leases remains exactly the original model footprint, with only new cache keys, nodes and handles added. Selecting an unseen name first admits all new metadata, then transfers W(k) from the root reservation without releasing or re-reserving it. Selection is serialized by the captured catalog's private cache lock, so each name transfers once. Repeated or concurrent selection shares the same image. Every fallible admission and cancellation check precedes the transfer; failure leaves C and the original payload lease unchanged. The private cache lock uses the existing workspace parking_lot mutex, whose state is inline; a denied first read with no remaining allowance allocates nothing, including on macOS, where the standard mutex would otherwise allocate a retained OS lock before payload admission.

The captured root is destroyed before its cache and remaining reservation. While the root exists, its cached images retain every transferred model lease even if a returned child is dropped. After root destruction, an escaping image retains only its own immutable model and original cancellation control; unrelated models, tree nodes and names are released. Induction over selection, repeated selection, parent destruction and child destruction proves that every reachable captured model remains funded until its last handle is destroyed. Independent captures reserve and partition independent allowances, including when they share physical model allocations.

For every selected name k, the detached image retains exactly R(k). The existing model encoder therefore produces E(R(k)) before and after native replacement, rollback or Engine closure, preserving layer order, labels, dimensions, gating and floating-point bits. The extension adds no query operator or numerical approximation; composing the selected model with existing serialization or inference preserves the same observations. Owner fixtures exercise every existing layer-image case, spare weight capacity, 1/64/4,096-entry catalogs, denied root and child admission, failed native updates, concurrent child selection and release of unselected models. The Engine fixture exercises the internal committed-cut consumer, native publication, savepoint rollback, later growth and closure. Other catalog producers, bounded joint publication, complete image validation, activation and public backup jobs remain separate unfinished requirements.

## Immutable native type catalog generations

SQL owns `TypeDefinitions<T>` for stored domains, enums, composites, ranges and shells. Its canonical representation consists of disjoint ordered roots D and T for durable and temporary definitions. Every native lookup and ordered iteration observes the map I(D) union I(T). The existing temporary-namespace predicate classifies each definition by its stored identity before insertion; an insertion removes the same key from the opposite root and publishes both successor roots only after all operations succeed. Starting from empty roots, induction over insertion, removal and clearing establishes disjointness and one definition per key. Merged iteration selects the smaller next key from the two roots, so its sequence is exactly the original ordered-map traversal. Native serialization uses that sequence as a map and retains the existing persistent encoding.

Each new entry uses SQL's exhaustive definition-copy producer before publication. The producer's structural preservation argument above applies to every field, expression and opaque constant. It does not evaluate a default, rebind a name or reconstruct enum positions. Its retained lease supplies the entry's cached payload size W; the stored key contributes its actual capacity K. Core's immutable retained tree maintains the sum P = sum(K + W) under path copying. Existing entries are immutable and shared. Consequently changing one entry cannot alter another entry's definition or cached footprint, and retaining a native generation requires only retaining its two roots. A rejected copy, footprint overflow or tree admission leaves both original roots unchanged. The native definition lease remains attached to its immutable entry through rollback, source replacement and retirement.

Capturing a generation selects D alone and independently reserves B(D) + P(D), with B following the existing retained-tree structural accounting convention. Neither the temporary root nor its payloads are reachable from the captured value. Capture checks the original control, reserves its complete selected footprint and retains one immutable root without enumerating names or definitions. A second capture reserves the full footprint under its own allowance even if the physical root is shared. Failed capture admission does not publish a partial value or alter native DDL. The enclosing Storage captured-value owner retains the original cancellation and independent lease after source closure. Subsequent insertion, deletion, rename or temporary classification changes install successor roots and cannot affect an earlier captured root.

The captured serializer emits the same ordered name/definition pairs as the existing durable definition producer, hence interpreting its output recovers exactly I(D). Combined with exhaustive definition copying, this proves preservation of type lookup, enum order, dropped attribute positions, bound domain evaluation and range support selection for the same selected catalog inputs. Execution retains validation, conflict checks, OID claims, persistent writes and restoration; Engine retains catalog cells and transaction publication. An immutable entry shared by two generations has identical serialized content, so publication may skip its serialization without changing the set or ordering of changed definitions. Distinct entries continue through the original serialized comparison and conflict rules. This unit changes no SQL operation or optimizer rewrite and does not establish joint bounded publication for the remaining catalog families.

## Immutable schema authority generations

SQL's `catalog::named_definitions` supplies the immutable ordered owner shared by type registries and `security::schema_registry`. A schema generation represents a finite map S from the exact stored namespace name to `BoundSchemaSecurity`. The schema producer preserves its optional namespace tuple, including OID, object incarnation and revision, its bound owner incarnation, and the exact optional ordered ACL, including grantees, grantors, privileges and grant options. Copying neither resolves a role name nor computes effective privileges. Structural equality of the copied fields therefore preserves every authority observation of the original value, including the distinction between an absent ACL and an explicitly empty ACL.

Let p(n) be the existing temporary-namespace predicate used by the legacy schema capture producer. Native schema insertion places a name n in the temporary root exactly when p(n), and in the durable root otherwise. The common owner's disjointness, ordered-iteration and atomic-successor arguments above apply unchanged. The combined native map is S, while the durable root denotes the restriction of S to names for which p(n) is false. Consequently iterating a captured schema root yields exactly the ordered name/authority pairs produced by the existing schema capture function. Temporary ACL payloads are not reachable from that root and cannot increase its independently admitted footprint.

Each native entry owns the admitted exhaustive authority copy and its original key capacity. Immutable path updates maintain the same cached structural and payload totals as the type owner. Schema capture independently admits the complete durable root using these totals, without visiting namespace names or ACL entries; failed admission leaves native authority unchanged. Native replacement shares every unrelated immutable entry. A rejected replacement or path update publishes no partial successor. Schema rename stages its existing transition identity and member relocations through Execution, then publishes the final old-key removal and new-key replacement together only after both successor operations succeed. The existing statement transaction retains responsibility for persistent writes, rollback, tuple coordination and error handling.

The bootstrap constructor contains the same four fixed namespace definitions as the previous map constructor; its finite names and ACLs fit the native construction allowance. Restoration retains its existing role-incarnation validation, tuple and OID uniqueness checks, one-time built-in migration and persistent row encoding. It publishes each accepted row through the same native owner as ordinary schema creation and authority changes. The map representation changes neither the membership nor ordering consumed by namespace lookup, role dependency analysis, grants, rename or removal. Engine only retains these native roots and supplies its existing state and transaction adapters.

An independently funded capture retains its original durable values through private grants, savepoints, COMMIT/ROLLBACK, schema rename, same-name replacement and source closure. Storage's captured-value boundary continues to enforce the original cancellation and lifetime allowance. No new SQL operation, authority rule or optimizer rewrite is introduced. Owner fixtures compare complete ordered authority with the legacy producer, exclude large temporary ACLs, bound capture and insertion work across growing namespace counts, and exercise exact/insufficient admission and final release. Actual Engine fixtures select committed authority through the existing transaction owner while private grants exist. Other catalog families, joint idle publication, complete image validation and public backup jobs remain separate unfinished requirements.

## Immutable role and membership generations

Let R be the ordered finite map from exact role names to role definitions, and let M be the ordered finite map from the target/member/grantor incarnation triple to a membership tuple. SQL's shared definition registry interprets its native roots as these same maps. Every role and membership is durable; namespace-shaped role names do not change eligibility. The existing disjoint-root and atomic-successor arguments apply with an empty temporary root. Native iteration therefore yields exactly the original ordered keys and values, while retaining a generation requires only retaining its immutable roots.

The exhaustive role producer preserves OID, incarnation, tuple revision, name, connection limit and the ordered attribute set. Attributes use Core's existing owned set, whose nodes have explicit allocation sizes and the same unique sorted iteration and sequence serialization as the previous ordered set. Thus membership in the attribute set and its serialized representation are unchanged. The exhaustive membership producer preserves OID, all three bound role endpoints and the ADMIN, INHERIT and SET options. A bound endpoint preserves its original incarnation and fallback name; copying never resolves it against a later role or infers membership from a reused name or OID. Componentwise equality establishes equality of every original role and membership observation.

Each published value retains its producer's admitted payload lease. String keys contribute their actual capacities, while a membership key consists entirely of inline identities whose storage is already counted by the retained tree. The generic key interface exposes only these separately allocated payloads. Immutable path updates maintain the original cached structural and payload sums. Replacing one definition copies only the selected payload and the tree path; every unrelated definition remains shared. Any producer, footprint or path-admission failure publishes no successor. Independent capture reserves the full cached selected footprint, including every role attribute node and membership endpoint name, without enumerating either catalog. Two captures each admit that complete footprint even when their physical nodes are shared.

The native role map serializes in its original key order with unchanged definition fields. Captured roles and memberships retain their original ordered key/value pair representation, and membership pair serialization is unchanged. Execution retains role-record format 3, membership-record format 1, OID claims, private-record overlays and initial-open migration. Validation still checks stored names, identities and revisions in its original order. A missing bootstrap entry can be inserted during restoration, but an existing malformed entry remains subject to validation and cannot be overwritten by bootstrap construction. Native commands retain tuple and shared-object locks, writer admission, persistence before publication and transaction-owned rollback.

SQL revocation and grant-cycle analysis retain immutable candidate roots rather than copying the selected graph into a mutable tree. Every changed key remains restricted to the original target incarnation, and ADMIN traversal and dependent-grant removal retain their existing target filters. Unchanged keys contribute no publication delta. Therefore the resulting changed tuples, duplicate-recipient notices, cascade outcomes and original conflict checks remain identical. Execution installs the candidate only after the same validation and persistence steps succeed; retaining a private candidate does not expose partial authority to the live catalog.

A committed capture selects the original role and membership roots through the transaction owner's existing source boundary. Later attribute changes, revocations, savepoint rollback, COMMIT, rename, same-name replacement and source closure cannot mutate those roots. Storage's captured-value owner retains independent admission and checks the original cancellation token. The owner tests compare complete legacy values and encodings, bound native insertion work, verify allocation-free root capture and deny each complete capture by one byte. The Engine fixture exercises the actual original-generation adapters during private role and membership changes. No new SQL authority rule or optimizer rewrite is introduced; other catalog families, joint idle publication, complete image validation and public backup jobs remain unfinished.

## Immutable graph catalog identity generations

Let G be the finite ordered map from exact graph names to stored graph identity records. Each record contains its namespace OID, label-sequence OID and ordered map from label IDs to complete label records. A label record contains its sequence, relation/row/array/rule identities, defaults, ordered NOT NULL names and identities, TOAST objects, optional primary/endpoint indexes and trigger. Graph names never imply temporary eligibility. SQL's native definition registry represents exactly G in its durable root; a graph without a recorded value remains absent and keeps its existing name-derived legacy behavior.

Define I as ordered lookup and iteration over a native or retained root. Exhaustive constructors preserve every scalar and optional field. Each nested owned map inserts exactly the source entries in key order and preserves the corresponding names and values. Induction over the ordered entries gives I(copy(G)) = I(G), including absent records and empty maps. Owned-map serialization visits the same keys and values as the previous ordered maps, so existing per-graph JSON records are unchanged. The detached ordered-pair producer and native capture have the same ordered observations; the detached graph representation did not define a serialized format. No SQL operator, identity derivation or optimizer rewrite changes.

Each definition copy admits every nested map node and owned name before allocation. Its completed payload lease contributes to the native root's cached footprint together with each graph-name capacity and retained-tree node. Capture independently reserves that complete cached footprint and retains the immutable root without enumerating records or labels. A selected graph update copies that graph's existing record and its tree path; unrelated graph records remain shared. SQL registry publication work is bounded by the selected per-graph payload and tree height, not by the total payload of the graph catalog. The graph mutation owner retains its existing graph/label discovery, and changing a graph containing many labels still copies that selected record. Failed production or path admission leaves the original root unchanged.

Execution stages all graph identity changes before writing metadata. New graphs receive their namespace, label sequence and default labels in the existing AGE order; missing labels follow the graph store's existing label order. Existing records and labels keep their identities, while unchanged graphs produce no record delta. Rename deletes the old key before recording the new key, including an identical old/new name. Label removal deletes only the selected label identities; graph removal deletes only its selected record. The enclosing graph transaction retains responsibility for storage rollback, and Engine installs the completed root only after all record writes succeed. Thus a successful successor has exactly the previous operation's map interpretation, and a failed candidate cannot publish a partial native root.

The committed source boundary retains the original root independently of subsequent private label changes, savepoint rollback, COMMIT, same-name replacement, rename and Engine closure. Since no operation mutates retained nodes or definition payloads, every observation through that root remains I(G). The captured owner checks its original cancellation scope and releases its independent admission on retirement. Owner tests compare the complete legacy observations and original JSON record, measure exact copy allocation and allocation-free capture, deny admission, bound selected publication and preserve unrelated payload sharing. The Engine fixture exercises original capture through actual graph transactions; the existing AGE allocation-order fixture also covers reopening and renaming with native SQLite, SQLite Key/Value and redb. Engine composes joint idle publication and these source roots for public memory export; native reconstruction and public restore remain unfinished.

## Immutable foreign-wrapper generations

Let W be the finite ordered map from exact wrapper names to definitions. A definition contains its name, OID and immutable incarnation, bound owner incarnation, handler variant, optional validator and declaration-order option pairs. A function handler or validator retains its original routine OID and complete function binding, including the bound incarnation and argument types. Every wrapper is durable; its name does not imply a temporary namespace. SQL's shared native definition owner therefore represents exactly W in its durable root and an empty temporary root.

The existing definition-purpose syntax producer exhaustively copies each definition, handler variant, function reference and option pair under its destination allowance. It does not resolve callbacks, execute validators or derive a native adapter from the wrapper name. Structural induction over the handler variants, optional bindings and ordered option sequence gives equality with the complete source definition; ordered root iteration then gives I(copy(W)) = I(W), where I is the original ordered-map interpretation. Consequently native map JSON and captured ordered-pair JSON retain their original values and order. An absent handler, a native handler and a bound function remain distinct. A retained reference to a deleted routine or wrapper cannot silently bind to a later object with the same name.

Completed definitions retain their admitted payload leases, including nested callback names, argument types and ordered option strings. The native root adds each exact name capacity and immutable tree node to its cached structural/payload footprint. An independent capture reserves that complete footprint and retains the durable root without traversing definitions. Separate captures each fund the full retained value even when they share physical nodes. Replacing a selected definition copies that payload and its tree path; unrelated options and callback payloads remain shared. Failed producer or tree admission publishes no successor, and the final native and captured owners release their independent leases.

Execution retains existing creation validation, callback invocation, owner and name locks, writer admission and revalidation. Only after those operations does it construct an admitted successor; persistence succeeds before Engine receives that root. Removal checks the original wrapper incarnation in its selected generation, constructs the complete successor and then removes the stored record before publishing the root. The enclosing transaction owns rollback. Thus allocation failure cannot leave a newly persisted definition without its matching native root, and publication never replaces concurrent catalog state with an earlier pre-callback view. Restoration keeps format 2, reserved native identities, duplicate-record errors, validation order and initial-only server-reference migration. The registered native definitions remain independent of whether a particular handler was enabled in the build.

Committed capture retains the original wrapper root during private same-name replacement, savepoint rollback, COMMIT, subsequent replacement and source closure. Immutable payloads preserve I(W) throughout that sequence; captured readers retain their original cancellation scope and admission. SQL tests compare complete legacy values and both encodings, require allocation-free capture for increasing catalog sizes, bound selected publication and reject insufficient admission. The Engine fixture exercises original incarnations and options through real wrapper transactions. Other native catalog families, composite foreign/table projections, joint idle publication and complete Engine backup remain separate requirements; this component introduces no new SQL authority rule, handler capability or optimizer transformation.

## Immutable foreign-server generations

Let S map exact server names to their original definitions. Each value contains the name, wrapper name, sorted connection options and metadata: OID, object and owner incarnations, optional type/version, optional written option order and optional bound wrapper identity. The native SQL owner stores precisely S in its durable immutable root. Its exhaustive definition-purpose producer copies every field; an owned ordered option map admits exact nodes and string payloads before allocation. Induction over the ordered option entries and metadata fields gives equality of each produced definition with its source. Native option JSON equals the preceding sorted-map JSON, metadata serialization is unchanged, and observing captured options in order equals the preceding detached pair sequence. Missing legacy metadata remains distinct from an empty string or present reference. No whole-server capture encoding existed before this change.

Each immutable definition retains its produced payload allowance. Tree nodes and name capacities join the cached root footprint, so capture independently admits and retains that root without visiting definitions or options. Selected mutation copies only the changed definition and tree path; unrelated option and metadata payloads remain shared. Insufficient admission publishes no successor, and final source/capture retirement releases every associated lease. SQL tests compare complete independently constructed values, legacy captures and option encodings, measure exact copied allocation, require allocation-free root capture and bound selected publication beside unrelated large payloads.

Execution keeps existing name validation, callbacks, object/owner locks and writer refresh in their original order. Creation admits its complete successor after those operations and before writing the existing server row; removal verifies the original incarnation and admits its successor before deleting the row. The native root publishes only after persistence succeeds, with rollback retained by the transaction owner. Restoration preserves duplicate checks, catalog validation, format-2 markers, metadata envelope version 1 and initial-only identity migration. Wrapper migration replaces only selected legacy server definitions and persists all migrations after the existing validation boundary. Native adapter construction still supplies an ordinary option map to the same FDW interfaces. No transport or handler implementation changes.

The committed source retains the original S through private replacement, savepoint rollback, COMMIT, another same-name incarnation and Engine closure. Immutable definitions make every subsequent captured observation equal to its original value; cancellation and admission remain independently owned. The Engine fixture exercises that sequence through actual SQL. Composite foreign-table/authority projections, joint idle publication and complete Engine image activation retain their separate requirements.

## Immutable built-in and system authority generations

Let B map built-in routine OIDs to ACL tuples, and let R map exact system relation identities to their relation and column ACL tuples. A routine tuple contains its revision and complete ordered EXECUTE ACL. A system tuple contains an optional relation ACL and a sorted map of column ACLs; each ACL keeps its revision, bound grantee and grantor incarnations, complete privilege bits and grant-option bits. Missing tuples, present empty ACLs and empty column replacements remain distinct. These catalogs represent fixed system objects and have no temporary partition.

SQL's native definition owners represent exactly B and R in immutable roots. The existing routine authority producer copies each tuple and ordered ACL entry. The exhaustive system authority producer copies every field and uses owned ordered column maps to admit exact nodes, names and ACL buffers. Field-wise equality and induction over the ordered maps and vectors establish equality with the preceding catalog interpretation. Routine map JSON and captured ordered-pair JSON preserve their previous encodings; system column-map JSON and ordered captured observations also remain unchanged. No lookup resolves a saved role name to a different incarnation, and no copy recomputes a tuple revision or default privilege.

Each native definition retains its complete admitted payload. Inline OIDs belong to their tree nodes; relation keys additionally retain both original name capacities. The immutable root caches those structural and payload footprints. Capture admits the complete root under an independent allowance and shares it without traversing ACL entries or columns. A selected replacement copies only that definition and tree path; unrelated ACL payloads stay shared. Failed payload or node admission preserves the original root, and final native/captured retirement releases the corresponding leases. Independent captures fund their full retained roots even when the physical tuples are shared.

Execution prepares complete routine and system authority successors while retaining their existing registry and role-dependency ownership. All fallible native publication precedes durable ACL writes. After every existing persistence operation succeeds, the owning execution boundary installs each admitted root without further allocation; rollback remains in the transaction state machine. A discarded role-dependency candidate releases registry guards before any lock wait or writer refresh. Stored format markers, tuple revisions, private per-record overlays and validation remain unchanged. Initial restoration validates the complete admitted successor before publishing it or persisting allowed migrations. Ordinary GRANT, REVOKE, default privilege lookup and column ACL interpretation therefore observe the same tuples and ordering.

Committed capture keeps the original B and R through private privilege replacement, savepoint rollback, COMMIT and source closure. Immutable payloads preserve every original ACL and revision until the captured owner is retired; cancellation and memory admission remain tied to that owner. This representation introduces no new query operator, permission or grant path. Database authority, user routine/view/event catalogs, composite projections and joint idle publication retain their separate requirements.

## Immutable database authority generations

Let D = (o, A) denote database authority, where o is the exact bound owner incarnation and A is either absent or an ordered finite sequence of complete bound ACL entries. Every entry retains its optional grantee incarnation, grantor incarnation and CREATE, CONNECT and TEMPORARY privilege and grant-option bits. An absent ACL and a present empty ACL remain distinct. SQL's `DatabaseAuthority` owns one immutable admitted value of this carrier; it exposes borrowed `BoundDatabaseSecurity` observations and no mutable reference to its payload.

Construction uses the existing exhaustive database authority producer. It copies o unchanged and preserves the optional ACL tag. For a present ACL, its admitted vector has exactly the original length and copies each entry's four fields without name resolution, grant combination or reordering. Induction over that sequence establishes equality of every stored tuple. Native and captured serialization delegates to the original bound value, so both JSON bytes and the version-1 stored envelope retain their previous meaning. Existing privilege and dependency functions consume exactly that borrowed value; consequently all established grant-path and role-incarnation observations are preserved, with no new SQL operator or optimizer rule.

A native generation owns the ACL buffer lease and the admitted inline `Produced<BoundDatabaseSecurity>` payload in one shared immutable allocation. Reference-count bookkeeping remains outside the payload allowance, following Core's existing shared-production contract. The completed lease caches the full retained byte count. A capture first checks both cancellation owners, reserves that cached amount under its independent allowance and retains the shared value; successful capture has cost independent of ACL length and allocates no heap object. Every independent capture funds the complete value, even when physical storage is shared. Insufficient admission or cancellation cannot mutate the native value; dropping partial and completed owners releases their corresponding reservations after their values.

A semantic no-op reuses the original native generation without additional allocation. A changed successor admits and constructs its complete new value while the original remains alive. The owning Execution boundary completes that fallible step before serializing and persisting an ordinary GRANT or REVOKE, then installs the admitted value only after persistence succeeds. Initial restoration keeps the original decoding, version and bound-role validation order; valid legacy metadata is converted to an admitted successor before any migration write. Native admission failure therefore changes neither existing authority, stored metadata nor the catalog epoch, and ordinary rollback remains owned by the transaction state machine. Since this database has one ACL value, changing its grants may copy that selected ACL; capture and retention never do.

Engine's catalog cells and committed transaction sources retain these SQL-owned generations through the existing state adapters. The original committed D remains unchanged by private revocation, nested savepoints, COMMIT or ROLLBACK, subsequent grants or Engine closure. Capture observations remain D until cancellation or retirement. Owner fixtures compare absent/empty and large ordered ACLs, original encodings, exact and insufficient allowances, no-op sharing, denied native publication before persistence, and migration failure before metadata changes. The committed Engine fixture measures capture independently of ACL size and observes the original bound grants through both completion paths. Joint idle publication, remaining composite catalogs and complete Engine backup retain their separate requirements.

## Immutable native view generations

Let a stored view be V = (S, Q, M, R), where S is complete bound relation authority including independent ACL tuple revisions, Q is stored relational syntax, M contains object/catalog identities, public columns, persistence, options, kind, population state and row-type array name, and R is the ordered sequence of evaluated materialized rows with declared types and collations. Let P be the persisted-syntax projection defined in [captured views and materialized results](#captured-views-and-materialized-results). SQL's native `ViewDefinitions` owns two disjoint immutable ordered roots D and T, partitioned by the definition's persistence. Their native interpretation is the ordered union D ∪ T; durable capture observes D alone.

Native admission exhaustively constructs every field of V. It applies P to Q, detaching retained constant values from statement allowances and excluding only the existing transient analysis descriptors, execution substitutions and session authorization overrides. Bound relation/routine/type identities, collations and declared syntax remain stored. The earlier projection argument establishes equality of subsequent analysis and evaluation under the same catalog inputs; an execution-scoped row source remains invalid stored syntax. S and M are copied field for field. Each materialized row uses an admitted ordered owned map with the same keys and exact value representations as its former ordered map. Induction over rows and fields gives equal multiplicity, field lookup, original floating-point bits and composite datums. The owned map serializes the same ordered JSON object; security still occupies its separate catalog row, so stored view definitions and ACL formats do not change.

These equalities imply that native readers see the same view definitions, authority decisions and materialized values. Ordinary view expansion continues to analyze the stored query in its current scope; materialized scans return R without evaluating Q. Refresh creates a successor with newly evaluated rows while an earlier root continues to expose its original R. No relational, retrieval, scalar or ranking operator is introduced or rewritten by this representation change.

The generic immutable catalog owner admits the complete copied payload and the selected ordered-tree path before publishing either root. Unchanged entries retain their existing payload owners. Cached subtree footprints are sums of their complete retained keys, nodes and definition payloads. Consequently capture under allowance b requires the durable cached footprint to fit b, checks the original cancellation controls and retains D in constant work without traversing its entries or allocating payload storage. Each independent capture funds that complete footprint even when physical storage is shared. T contributes neither names nor payloads to that admission. The projection is idempotent and distributes over disjoint key unions because membership depends only on each definition's persistence.

Execution stages fallible successors before creation, alteration, rename, ACL or dependency persistence and publishes their roots only after the corresponding storage operation succeeds. ACL preparation retains an admitted immutable candidate without a view write guard; the existing definition transaction and relation locks serialize its publication after persistence succeeds, so persistence observers can still read the previous native authority. Role-dependent preflight candidates release their guards before waiting, and ordinary and materialized creation retain their distinct existing guard boundaries. Restoration preserves decoding and semantic validation order, admits the complete candidate under the native registry allowance before provisional migration publication, and retains the existing rollback owner for migration failures. Dependency selection borrows query structure before copying an affected definition. Relation selection follows sequential non-recursive CTE visibility and joint recursive CTE visibility; sequence and routine selection visit the same scalar nodes as the owning rewrites. Unchanged materialized payloads are not copied during selection.

Engine retains these SQL-owned generations through its existing catalog cells, session snapshots and transaction source adapters. Private replacement, ACL changes, savepoints, completion, later refresh and source closure cannot mutate the original selected D. Until cancellation or retirement, every captured V therefore has the same interpretation established above. Admission failure leaves the previous native generation intact, and completed owners release their native and independently captured reservations on retirement. Whole Engine image encoding, remaining catalog families, joint idle committed publication and destination activation retain their separate requirements.

## Immutable native routine generations

Let a routine catalog map each canonical stored name to an ordered vector of overloads. An overload is the pair (D, B), where D is the complete creation definition, including object and catalog identities, tuple revision, owner, ACL, signature, defaults, attributes, configuration and original source form, and B is either Source or the ordered creation-bound SQL plan sequence. The persisted-syntax projection P is the existing SQL-owned projection used by individual routine capture: it preserves semantic syntax and retained constant representations while detaching statement-owned payloads and excluding transient parser, parameter and analysis owners. Source bodies remain unexamined text; session-compiled PL/pgSQL is not a stored bound body.

The native registry owns disjoint ordered durable and temporary roots. The original borrowed canonical-name predicate selects temporary namespaces; validation occurs before insertion. Native lookup observes their ordered union, while durable capture observes only the durable root. Admission copies D and every bound plan through P and preserves overload order.
