Sharing an already admitted immutable overload or bound body in the same allowance preserves the same interpretation without a second copy. Thus each lookup has the same overload multiplicity, signature selection, identity, authority and source-versus-bound behavior as its original ordered map. The existing definition-version hash depends on the same serialized D; discarding a cached hash does not change its value.

Each immutable routine handle owns its definition lease, and each independently shareable bound-body handle owns its own plan lease. Dropping a catalog root cannot retire either lease while an invocation or analysis retains that handle. A definition may retire before its independently retained body; the body lease remains live until that last reference retires. Cross-allowance publication constructs a new admitted owner rather than retaining the source allowance. Every overload vector has its own admitted container, and its cached footprint includes the definition and bound-body footprints. The persistent map adds key and node storage to that footprint. Capture consequently reserves the complete cached durable footprint in constant work without visiting names, overloads or plans. Failure and either cancellation leave the original roots usable and release the attempted capture allowance.

For an update, let R be the original immutable registry and R' the fully admitted successor. SQL constructs selected replacements and preserves other handles; multi-target removal completes all fallible changes in a private root before replacing its output. Execution retains original name, role and object locks, validation order and transaction admission, constructs R' before durable persistence, and publishes R' only after persistence succeeds. A failed admitted copy or persistent write therefore leaves native readers at R; the existing transaction owns durable rollback. Restoration first admits the complete placeholder namespace, compiles stored bodies through the existing owner, and admits the complete final root. Compilation, admission and migration failure restore the original namespace; no source body is compiled merely to retain or capture it.

The captured encoding projects the same ordered name/overload pairs with the original definition and optional bound-body fields. Durable routine metadata retains its existing format and definition encoding. Since these representation changes preserve definitions, ordered resolution, authority, creation-bound references and all subsequent execution inputs, they introduce no new relational, scalar or ranking operator and preserve existing query compositions under the same catalog snapshot. Whole Engine image validation, fresh activation and public backup jobs retain their separate requirements.

## Immutable native event generations

Let an event catalog map each canonical relation identity to an ordered map of declaration names and complete trigger or rule definitions. A trigger retains its creation syntax, bound function incarnation, enable mode, object and catalog identities, constraint name and constraint OID. A rule retains its creation syntax, enable mode, optional bound condition plan and OLD/NEW binding, ordered dependency sets and catalog OID. SQL owns these values and their exhaustive admitted copying. The native owner separates durable and temporary relation roots using the same namespace predicate as durable capture; ordinary reads merge their ordered names.

Admission applies the existing stored-syntax projection to trigger conditions, rule actions and bound condition plans. It preserves original OLD/NEW relation and column identities through the binding capture owner instead of reconstructing them from current column names. Dependency sets use exact owned ordered nodes with the same equality, order and serialized sequence as the preceding sets. Each declaration keeps its complete payload lease, including its map key. A relation root's cached footprint includes its declaration nodes and payloads; the outer registry adds the complete relation key and its own immutable nodes. Therefore the cached durable footprint is the sum of every retained allocation needed by the selected original definitions.

Unchanged declarations share their immutable nodes when the destination uses the same allowance. Cross-allowance publication constructs independent admitted payloads and releases the source allowance when its final owner retires. Native declaration serialization preserves the original ordered map. Durable trigger capture preserves the prior ordered relation/name pairs, including empty relations, and the exact trigger fields. Native rule encoding preserves the same definition, optional bound condition and ordered dependencies; this does not introduce a new durable metadata version. The existing persistent metadata owner retains its original flattening, validation and ordering rules.

For each immutable ordered declaration map, Core's bidirectional cursor retains only the root, two borrowed boundary keys and the number of remaining entries. A forward step selects the least key greater than its previous front; a reverse step selects the greatest key less than its previous back. Induction over the consumed prefix and suffix shows that every step returns the corresponding entry of the original ordered map exactly once, and that exhaustion remains terminal. Each seek follows one balanced-tree path, giving O(log n) comparisons, constant cursor space and no heap allocation. This separate cursor leaves the existing forward iterator unchanged. Rule selection uses it to preserve original ascending and descending action order without copying declaration trees or retaining a large traversal stack in recursive execution frames.

A selected update first admits its changed declaration and immutable inner and outer paths. Other declarations retain their original addresses and payloads. Multi-relation rewrites operate on a private complete successor; callback or admission failure retires only that candidate. Execution prepares every changed trigger and rule root before the first corresponding persistent write and installs those roots only after the writes succeed. The existing transaction boundary retains persistent rollback. Restoration decodes and validates through the existing owner, preserves temporary membership, admits the complete restored candidate, completes any initial-open migration write and then publishes it. Memory failure therefore preserves the typed 53200 cause and both original catalogs; migration failure leaves original native authority installed while the caller retains durable rollback responsibility.

Capture checks both cancellation owners, independently admits the cached durable footprint and retains its immutable root in constant work without walking or copying declarations. Temporary payloads contribute neither names nor bytes. Private DDL, savepoints, transaction completion, same-name replacement and source closure cannot mutate a previously selected root. Engine supplies only catalog-cell, transaction-source and publication adapters. Subsequent trigger firing, rule ordering, condition evaluation and dependency analysis read the same complete definitions as before, so this representation change introduces no new relational, scalar or ranking operator. Joint idle publication, remaining composite catalog projections, complete Engine image validation, fresh activation and public backup jobs retain their separate requirements.

## Immutable native comment generations

Let C be a finite map from complete object addresses (object OID, subobject number, catalog class OID) to exact description strings, with the persistence partition established above. Execution's `CommentRegistry` stores durable and temporary descriptions in disjoint Core immutable ordered trees; each text payload retains its native production lease. Ordinary lookup checks both roots, and ordered iteration merges their next keys. Each address occurs in exactly one root, so this merge and each inclusive interval yield exactly C in its original lexicographic order. Both `obj_description` forms, catalog projection and class-specific deletion preserve their address and text semantics.

Insertion admits the selected text, removes any opposite-partition entry on a private candidate and admits the complete changed tree path before publishing both roots. Deletion removes only the selected address. All other entries remain shared. Consequently successful mutations preserve the ordered-map interpretation and exact UTF-8 bytes; failed text or node admission preserves the original roots and eligibility. The allocation leases follow their original native partition even when physical refresh combines durable and session-owned generations. No mutable description reference or independently mutable backup catalog escapes this owner.

For each root R, let F(R) be its reachable node and entry storage plus separately allocated text capacities. Every immutable mutation updates that sum with its root, establishing the cached footprint by induction. A captured source retains only the durable root, checks cancellation and independently reserves F(R) without walking entries. Each capture pays for its complete retained closure; temporary source retirement cannot invalidate it. Final native ownership releases the construction leases and final captured ownership releases its independent allowance.

Execution completes all native preparation before durable metadata writes or deletions and publishes only after those writes succeed. Temporary descriptions never enter durable metadata; a lifetime transition deletes or installs the corresponding persistent key in the same existing definition transaction. Restoration validates all canonical persistent keys and text constraints before constructing a successor. It retains the original durable root, admits only added, changed or removed paths under that root's allowance, and combines the complete result with the session's existing temporary root. Unchanged restoration shares all original payloads and requires no native admission, including failure cleanup under an exhausted allowance. Private refresh preserves that temporary root and selects durable changes through the existing storage transaction interface. Failure leaves the published registry and stored rows unchanged; Engine retains only the adapter and transaction boundary. Durable encodings, query operators, optimizer rules and stored description bytes remain unchanged.

## Immutable native foreign generations

Let T map exact relation identities to complete stored foreign definitions and let A map those identities to bound authority, including owner incarnations, absent or empty ACLs, column grants and ACL tuple revisions. A valid native foreign catalog satisfies dom(T) = dom(A). For each relation k, its durable eligibility is determined by T(k).persistence, independently of the spelling of k. Execution's ForeignCatalog owns both immutable roots privately. Its only membership-changing operations construct both successors and publish them together. SQL owns the exhaustive authority copy; Execution owns the foreign definition, FDW conversion and persistent row. No independent mutable definition or authority projection escapes this owner.

An insertion at k copies the complete original stored definition and authority through admitted constructors and replaces the same key in both roots. Its persistence partitions are chosen from the same input definition. A deletion removes k from both roots; an authority-only change requires an existing definition and preserves its membership and persistence. All fallible construction occurs on private successor roots. Therefore a failed operation preserves T and A, while a successful operation preserves equal membership and matching eligibility. This establishes the invariant by induction from the empty catalog. Restoration validates decoded pairs before publishing a native generation, and session retention and private-record merging use the same paired operations. Restoration declarations retain their preceding generation and restore it when their binding scope exits.

For each root let F be the sum of reachable tree-node storage, key payloads and admitted complete definition or authority payloads. Owned ordered option and ACL maps expose their actual node storage; the stored syntax copier preserves compiled bindings and retained scalar representations rather than serializing and rebinding them. Each mutation installs its payload and cached F together, and immutable readers cannot invalidate that total. Capturing a native catalog reserves the independently cached footprints of its two durable roots and retains those roots in constant work without allocating or traversing declarations. Failure or either cancellation owner exposes no partial pair and releases every capture reservation acquired by the call. A successful capture retains both original roots after native changes and closure; its final retirement releases its own admission independently of other captures. Temporary definitions and authority are absent from both captured roots.

Native table readers merge the ordered durable and temporary partitions without allocating; Core's compact bidirectional cursors bound iterator state while each step uses logarithmic comparisons. Since the partitions are disjoint and each cursor is ordered, taking the smaller next key yields exactly the preceding ordered-map sequence. Thus name and OID lookup, role dependencies, privilege checks, schema analysis, expression binding and catalog projection observe the same complete entries. Option conversion at the existing FDW interface produces the same ordered key/value pairs. Persistent schema and authority encodings remain unchanged, including dropped slots, generated array names, server identities and written option order. No remote rows, callbacks, new SQL operator or optimizer rewrite enter the capture.

Execution admits each foreign-record or ACL successor before its corresponding persistent write and publishes it after those writes succeed. Existing definition transactions continue to own rollback across preceding dependent-object changes. A rename selects its successor from the current generation after dependent rewrites so publication cannot overwrite those changes. Engine retains one catalog cell and maps read guards onto its immutable projections, preserving the lifetime of the original read boundary. Snapshot and rollback retain that paired generation together. This change establishes native foreign membership and capture ownership; it does not complete joint idle publication, sequence/table-metadata ownership, Engine backup image encoding, restore jobs or repository retention.

## Immutable native index generations

Let I map structural index identities to complete stored index rows, and let p(k) be the persistence of the owning table when row k is bound. Define D = {k in dom(I) | p(k) is not Temporary} and T = dom(I) minus D. Execution's `catalog::index::registry::CatalogIndexes` privately owns two immutable ordered maps for I restricted to D and T. Membership is selected from actual table persistence during native publication and restoration, not from a schema-name prefix. A replacement or rename preserves its existing partition; explicit rebinding selects the owning table's current persistence. Each successful mutation constructs complete successor roots before replacing either installed root, so their domains remain disjoint and their union is exactly the ordinary native index catalog.

The existing Storage producer copies every row field without decoding its strings: relation identity, index type, table name, encoded keys, parameters and optional semantic definition. Native construction also admits the separate structural key. Each immutable entry retains those complete payload leases, while Core admits its ordered tree nodes. Reference-count bookkeeping keeps its existing Core accounting boundary. Thus the cached reachable footprint is the sum of all durable structural allocations and complete key/row payload capacities. Capturing D independently reserves exactly that cached sum and retains its root; no entry is enumerated and no payload is copied. Temporary names and payloads contribute no capture work or admission. Every captured row remains owned by the retained immutable root and its original construction leases until the last reference retires, independently of the capturing operation's separate reservation.

Core's `CatalogIndexRegistry` observes structural keys without depending on Execution. Its iterator records a front key, a back key and remaining cardinality. A forward step selects the least key above the consumed prefix, and a reverse step selects the greatest key below the consumed suffix. Initially the remaining interval contains the complete ordered map. Each step removes exactly one endpoint and decrements its cardinality, preserving that invariant; at zero, both directions remain exhausted. Cloning preserves the same interval. Native lookup merges the next candidates from the disjoint durable and temporary maps, so observation order is identical to the prior ordered registry, even when an observer's row identity differs from its structural key. Each endpoint seek follows one balanced-tree path, requires O(log n) comparisons and allocates no cursor or copied row. Supplied row sequences retain their separate original order and repeated identities.

Execution stages native successors before their corresponding index-row writes, builds or deletions. Namespace moves admit the complete selected batch before the first index-row write. Column and table reference changes prepare their successor before the existing schema/data rename effects; SQL owns the unchanged expression rewrite, included-column selection and retained display names. Routine references, composite constants, constraint/index trees and partition ancestry retain their existing semantic and transaction boundaries. Restoration validates the original index graph and admits its durable replacement under the original native allowance before migration writes; it joins the existing temporary root without copying temporary records. Failed admission releases only private candidates, preserving the installed root and original persistent rows. The enclosing definition transaction continues to own rollback for later storage or dependent-object failures. Persistent catalog reloads use one publication boundary for selected table membership, logical roots and observed epochs. The boundary retains the preceding roots before clearing transient bindings and commits only after all selected restoration succeeds. On failure or unwinding it restores those original roots, table handles and observed epochs, and invalidates physical-cache certification until a complete refresh succeeds. Thus cleanup admission failure cannot discard the original index generation or certify partly rebound stores. Existing Execution error translation preserves memory admission as SQLSTATE 53200 across index, table and routine publication.

For any successful ordinary observation f over index rows, the native union presents the same ordered structural keys and complete rows as I; therefore f(D union T) equals its preceding result. For a selected backup observation g, the captured map is exactly I restricted to D at selection, and immutable ownership prevents later DDL, rollback, replacement or closure from changing g. SQL references, physical keys, index definitions and encoded persistent rows retain their prior meanings, so this change introduces no SQL operator or optimizer rewrite and cannot alter document, posting or ranked-result composition through index interpretation. This establishes the native index component only. Historical analyzer ownership, other remaining catalog/resource roots, bounded comment eligibility, joint idle publication, complete Engine images, restore activation and public backup jobs retain their separate requirements.

## Immutable native analyzer generations

Analysis's `AnalyzerImageSource` owns the canonical descriptor and ordered encoded dictionary closure independently of executable pipelines, parsed configuration and decoded dictionary models. Named publication prepares that immutable owner before installing a catalog entry. Weak caches on a compiled revision and each resolved dictionary share already published encoded owners while any native entry retains them; a cache alone cannot retain payloads. Static and ordinary shared dictionary bytes keep their immutable allocation. Imported encoded bytes are copied once into a separate native owner, so the source import allowance and compiled model can retire independently. No resolver, source file or mutable analyzer name participates in this preparation.

Common Storage's `AnalyzerCatalog` publishes two roots together: executable revisions for ordinary lookup and their encoded projection for capture. Both successors, copied keys and changed encoded owners are admitted before either root is installed. Insertion, removal and extension leave both prior roots unchanged on any failure. Engine prepares the successor inside its existing transaction boundary before named catalog persistence and supplies only publication, refresh and rollback adapters. Cloning an ordinary generation retains both roots; capture retains only its encoded root and therefore cannot pin a compiled pipeline or decoded model.

Define a revision's image as A = (D, [(k_i, h_i, b_i)]), where D is the exact canonical descriptor and the resource list retains the existing language/hash order. Every native constructor preserves D, each kind k_i and hash h_i verbatim. Static sharing, immutable Arc sharing and imported-buffer copying each preserve every byte of b_i. Consequently the native projection P satisfies P(A) = A. Weak-cache reuse selects the same immutable revision or exact resolved dictionary owner, never an alias lookup, so it cannot change this equality. Live, copied and native snapshots use the same framing function E; E(P(A)) = E(A), including resource order and lengths. Existing image validation reconstructs the same component identities and complete token observations under the same feature configuration, limits and input. Capture does not execute analysis or introduce a ranking operator or optimizer rewrite.

Let C map names to executable revisions and I map those names to P(C(name)). Initially both maps are empty, so dom(C) = dom(I) and the correspondence holds. An insertion builds private successors replacing the same name with r and P(r); all other entries retain their original identities. Removal deletes the same name from both private successors; clear produces the two empty roots; extension is a finite sequence of these valid private insertions followed by one installation. Thus induction preserves the correspondence for every successful publication. Failure before installation preserves the preceding pair exactly. Ordinary observations still read C, while selected capture reads the matching I; immutable nodes preserve those observations through later replacement, rollback and source closure.

Each encoded image caches its complete descriptor capacity, resource-vector capacity, encoded dictionary closure and shared-owner headers. The catalog adds immutable tree/entry structure and key capacities. Shared payloads referenced by different names or sides are conservatively charged for each logical reference; the bound covers the complete reachable encoded closure and is not a claim that aliases occupy separate physical buffers. Capture reserves this cached bound before sharing the root, performs constant work, allocates no catalog nodes and does not enumerate descriptors or dictionaries. Construction leases remain attached to the shared native owners until their final reference retires; each cut independently admits its retained closure and cancellation scope.

A selected child first admits its wrapper, cache node and copied lookup key. Only after all fallible admission succeeds does it transfer that image's original payload allowance from the captured root's remaining reservation. Repeated selection shares the cached child and cannot transfer the allowance twice. The parent drops its root before remaining leases; a child retains only the selected encoded owner, its original allowance and cancellation, so unrelated names and executable revisions can retire. Original and invoking cancellation remain active through short writes. Denied selection consumes no payload lease and cannot affect a separately funded cut.

For historical field bindings, let B map each resolved (table, field) key to (index revision, search revision, optional side names, owner, last phase), and let p(k) be the actual owning table persistence. Storage's `FieldAnalyzerCatalog` privately maintains disjoint durable and temporary runtime roots, together with an encoded root for exactly the durable domain. Insertions prepare both historical image sides and all changed roots before installation; removals delete matching runtime and image membership; clear-durable retires only the durable pair. Field and table renames retain the same historical revisions and partition, replacing only their structural keys. By induction over these operations, runtime lookup is exactly B, and the image root is P(B restricted to non-temporary keys). Merging ordered runtime partitions preserves the preceding global key order. Temporary entries never prepare an encoded projection, including large names and dictionary closures.

Engine binds each native field entry from an already resolved table generation and passes that generation's persistence. The same publication boundary removes entries with their fields and tables, preserves temporary entries during durable reload, and restores the complete native root on transaction rollback. Preparation precedes analyzer rebuild or binding persistence; schema column removal prepares its native successor before physical/schema effects. The existing definition transaction owns rollback for later failures across related changes. No capture performs a new table-name lookup or reconstructs a membership map. Execution's table-metadata source retains the matching native index and field roots from the same original transaction generation, so each component already has its authoritative durable selection.

A field's encoded payload includes both exact image closures and every name, owner and phase field. Its cached complete footprint adds each structural key and native tree node. Capture independently admits this root in constant work without allocations or walking fields, tables, descriptors or dictionaries. Selecting a child admits its copied names, key, cache entry and both image wrappers before transferring either original image allowance. Repeated lookup retains the same child, while independent index/search image handles can outlive the child and parent with their original cancellation and admission. Encoded metadata never retains a compiled analyzer. Therefore image observations of either historical side, including asymmetric revisions under the same current name, remain identical after replacement, rename, rollback and Engine closure. Existing SQL analysis, indexed postings, retrieval scoring and stored binding formats are unchanged.

These arguments establish native named and historical field-analyzer ownership and matching table-metadata capture. Remaining graph/path resources, bounded comment eligibility and final joint Engine capture retain their requirements under #1351 and #1322.

## Immutable native memory graph generations

Let a memory graph state be $S=(V,E,P,M_V,M_E,L,n_V,n_E)$, where $V$ and $E$ map global identities to exact evaluated entities, $P$ maps graph names to vertex/edge membership and their derived label/adjacency indexes, $M_V$ and $M_E$ map identities to owning graph names, $L$ contains label names, kinds, sequences, tombstones and allocation identity, and $n_V,n_E$ are the next ordinary identities. An observation includes ordered reads, errors, entity properties, graph memberships, label metadata, allocation results and encoded image bytes. Existing valid input, value representation and storage-interface preconditions remain unchanged.

Each primary collection is represented by Core's immutable ordered map. Its representation function enumerates entries in key order and recursively interprets partition and registry roots. Empty roots represent empty collections. Insertion replaces exactly the selected key/value, removal deletes exactly the selected key, and balancing changes only tree shape. Induction on these operations therefore gives equality with the previous ordered-map and set representation. Labels and properties are retained without normalization or evaluation; duplicate label identities, missing historical kinds, tombstones, unused counters, raw unattached entities and lazy Datum bytes keep their previous meanings. The public serialized label registry retains its field types and format. Wire and native registries execute the same identity, kind, sequence, merge and tombstone rules through borrowed storage adapters.

A graph mutation computes a private candidate from the current roots. Changed entities update every original owning graph's selected derived indexes; unrelated entity payloads and index roots remain shared. Membership attachment, detachment and orphan retirement use the same identities and graph names as before. Graph set operations select the same ordered identity sets and apply the existing target-population operation. Consequently the representation of every successful candidate equals the result of the previous state operation, including its counter and label effects. The final replacement publishes every root and scalar together before retiring the old generation. Failed admission or unwinding during preparation exposes no candidate state. Transaction and nested transaction checkpoints retain the original complete root tuple; error and panic restoration therefore recover exactly the entering representation. No caller repairs state before invoking these operations.

For each root, the cached retained footprint is its exact native structural size plus the complete payload footprints supplied by its owner. A leaf insertion adds its key and value payload; replacement subtracts the previous payload before adding the successor; removal subtracts the selected payload. Nested partition, membership and label owners supply their own cached totals. Induction over publication establishes the complete total without a capture-time traversal. Native node and direct-buffer leases follow their allocations through sharing and replacement; independently captured images reserve the entire reachable footprint even when they share native data. Capture preserves the original cancellation control through serialization. A denied reservation returns no image and releases its temporary admission.

Clone and capture visit a fixed number of roots regardless of entity, graph, label or adjacency cardinality. A selected mutation changes the ordered-map paths for its actual affected entities, memberships, labels and adjacency entries; it does not copy unrelated entities or complete registry collections. Operations whose semantics affect an entire graph still visit those selected memberships. The deterministic bounds exercise 1, 64 and 4,096 entities and labels, allocation-free native cloning, one captured-wrapper allocation and a 128-KiB ceiling for the selected entity/label update. Twenty mutation forms exhaust admission at multiple points and verify identical images and counters after rejection, followed by successful reuse. These bounds concern native graph storage. Native resource membership is covered below; joint committed publication retains its separate requirements in #1351.

Image output enumerates the same represented keys and writes the same existing scalar and value encodings, so representation equality implies byte equality with `UQAGRA01`. Import reconstructs native nodes directly from ordered primary records, transfers decoded buffer leases into those nodes, rebuilds the same derived indexes and validates the complete candidate before return. It exposes the native owner itself rather than an external reservation detached from its internally retained data. The admission fixture exhausts every allowance below its construction peak, verifies success at the exact observed peak and checks that a cloned imported generation preserves its data and reservations after the original is destroyed. These ownership changes add no graph algebra operator and leave the carrier, matching, traversal, ranking and relational composition contracts unchanged.

## Native graph-resource membership

Let R map each registered Engine graph name to its immutable graph handle. The native representation is a Core retained ordered map with a cached payload footprint and a count c of physical-provider handles. Empty R has c=0. Inserting a handle at k subtracts the previous handle's provider indicator, adds the new indicator and supplies its complete native image footprint; removal subtracts the removed indicator. Core preserves key order and updates its cached structural and payload sums with the candidate tree. Both the tree and c are published together only after admission succeeds, so induction on registration and removal establishes the exact membership, footprint and capability invariants. Rename applies removal and insertion to one private candidate before replacing the live generation. No unsuccessful intermediate candidate can change R or its capability.

For c=0, capture admits the cached complete root and shares its immutable nodes without enumerating names or graph entities. For c>0, capture rejects the source before traversal because physical records require their provider's snapshot. For each selected name k, the captured owner first admits a copied cache key, cache node and image wrapper under its original control. Only after all fallible preparation succeeds does it transfer that handle's already admitted payload footprint into the child image and cache a clone. The child and cache clone share one reservation, so repeated selection neither copies data nor charges the payload twice. Until the parent retires its cache owns every transferred reservation; after retirement, escaping children own their exact original data and allowance. Unselected membership nodes and handles retire with the parent. Checked cancellation precedes selection and output, and children retain the original control alongside the invoking writer's control. Therefore independent cuts cannot extend each other's allowance or cancel each other's output.

Engine stores this native root in its existing catalog cell and transaction undo. Memory publication installs only complete graph candidates; persistent publication, OID updates and path invalidation remain inside the existing storage/statement transaction. A later membership admission failure propagates through that owner boundary, whose rollback restores the entering catalogs, records and path definitions. Restoring a saved native generation shares its root and requires no new native graph-node admission. Physical-session rebinding constructs its entire replacement under the same catalog allowance before installation and retains only provider handles, preserving transaction affinity without hydrating graph data. Ordinary persisted-membership restoration validates the same label metadata but changes only added or removed names. If membership is unchanged, it retains the original native root and consumes no new graph-node allowance; failed additions or removals never publish a partially restored registry. Ordered name lookup, graph relation resolution, graph OIDs, label observations, Cypher results and encoded images therefore observe the same R and graph state at successful boundaries. This changes ownership, not the graph carrier or algebra, and introduces no optimizer rewrite.

The membership fixtures cover zero-allocation capture of 1, 64 and 4,096 names, less than 16 KiB for selected publication, exact and insufficient root admission, failed replacement/deletion/rename, provider-capability transitions, failed child admission, allocation-free repeated child selection and independent cancellation after retirement. Engine fixtures cover captured membership through savepoint rollback, commit, rename and closure, and denied native publication over memory, native SQLite, SQLite KV and redb with later reuse and persistent reopen. Final-source execution and delivery evidence are recorded in the implementation plan; these obligations do not by themselves establish complete Engine backup or joint idle-cut publication.

## Common provider image ownership

`PersistentStorageProvider::capture_backup_image` selects the installed provider's existing complete image mapping. Native SQLite retains its native namespace and projection format; SQLite Key/Value and redb retain their own provider-qualified Key/Value formats. The default rejects capture without opening a session. A generic record interface or an ordinary session does not establish image support. Engine export authority and job lifecycle remain separate requirements.

Let an admitted concrete image I expose cut c, ordered unit stream U, byte streams B and actual-work counters W through its owning state machine. Define A(I) as `OwnedBackupImage`, which retains exactly I and delegates every stream operation to that same value with the same arguments. Its cut is c. Induction on any finite call sequence establishes equal returned steps, unit identities, kinds, declared lengths, bytes, counters and errors: the base observes the unchanged cut, and each delegated call advances precisely the original state machine once. Thus full inventory construction and incremental equality comparison consume the same inputs and preserve their existing image-digest and independently restorable-root arguments. This introduces no query operator, SQL replay, physical remapping or optimizer rewrite.

Construction first checks the invoking cancellation and reserves the concrete source header before entering the provider's capture closure. Insufficient allowance or prior cancellation therefore cannot register a cut or open another source owner. The closure runs at most once. If it fails, its ordinary error path retires partial capture state and the adapter releases its reservation. Cancellation immediately after capture drops the completed source before releasing its header allowance. Success moves that source into the admitted box; its own cursor, payload and snapshot owners retain their original controls. Every later read and advance still enters the original source, so the adapter cannot replace cancellation, extend retention allowances or hold an additional physical read. Its `Send` bound permits transfer without assuming concurrent access to the mutable stream. Destruction retires the concrete source before releasing its heap charge.

The real Engine drills compose this interface with the existing protected repository, import and exclusive target-publication owners. They compare selected original SQL definitions, identities, role grants, comments, complete durable sequence records, ordinary and unlogged rows, stored materialized contents, text-score bits, vector-score bits and graph properties. Durable reservation endpoints are compared exactly, separately from the original process's current sequence-cache position; fresh restored sessions continue from the preserved endpoint. Private definitions and temporary data remain excluded. An independent session commits between actual source reads, including compressed rollback-journal modes. The incremental source survives Engine/provider retirement, and completed archives survive removal of the source and ancestor root. Fresh imported Engines retain new transaction histories and accept later writes and reopen. These finite checks exercise the ownership composition; complete production Engine logical dependency validation, public jobs and memory image encoding/import remain separate delivery obligations.

## Scheduled image capture

Common Storage's `BackupImageCapture` owns one source, the original read control and one repository operation. Its machine retains the current source position and byte offset, pending ordered inventory entries, parent position, hash state, frame buffer, comparison spool and complete-verification position. `BackupOperation::prepare_image` and standalone copy drive that same machine to preparation; they do not maintain a second image-construction algorithm. The public scheduled interface exposes intermediate progress without granting authority to publish an incomplete image.

Let U be the finite strictly ordered unit stream of a conforming source cut, and P the compatible parent's complete inventory when present. Each unit has a finite byte stream; a supplied length is exact, while an omitted length is established by successful EOF. Assume the source eventually completes each bounded position request, every nonterminal payload read advances, and the underlying I/O operation returns. The existing complete-inventory interpretation maps each address to its kind and exact bytes. At every suspended boundary, the machine's completed entries interpret the consumed prefix of U; its current unit stores precisely the consumed byte prefix and that prefix's hash, and its parent cursor denotes the least not-yet-passed entry of P. Initially these prefixes are empty. A pending source call changes no unit; selection appends no entry; a payload call extends only the current byte prefix and hash; a parent step discards only an earlier address. Equal address, kind, length and complete digest reuse exactly the previously justified range under the existing digest assumption. A changed comparison rewinds the retained exact bytes and writes those bytes without rehashing them. A completed current unit appends exactly one entry in source order. Induction on these transitions establishes the same complete image as unscheduled capture, including empty streams, empty units, deletions and explicit tombstones. Frame grouping and encryption nonces do not participate in the logical image digest, so different positive scheduling windows preserve the represented image.

Each call performs one source request capped at 128 positions, one payload or whole-object hash read capped at 16 KiB, one parent entry, one bounded frame, or one verification transition. Source implementations retain their own advertised physical-position and read-release contracts. Parent prefixes and tails are advanced individually; payload comparison and final object hashing retain their offsets between calls. Metadata and frame decoding are bounded by the configured key/frame limits independently of the requested payload window. Positive windows and the finite-progress assumptions imply eventual preparation because every payload, parent, inventory and verification traversal is finite and each transition either advances one such traversal or moves to its next finite stage. This is a work bound, not a wall-clock bound on filesystem synchronization, lock admission or provider I/O.

The complete job header is admitted before operation creation. Variable frame, key, entry and verification buffers retain their original memory reservations; comparison and inventory spools share the encrypted physical workspace budget with final verification, while new objects and descriptors consume the separate encoded staging allowance. Failed admission exposes no replacement. A step owner retires its machine on any returned error or unwind and marks the operation failed before another entry point can proceed. Subsequent advancement and publication reject that state; explicit cancellation remains available. Normal source completion releases the source before repository verification, so prepared publication requires neither the provider nor its original files.

Preparation consumes the complete inventory/unit/object/range certificate for the exact candidate root and checks that the repository removal generation is unchanged since verification began. Root retirement or object collection invalidates that interval and prevents preparation; unrelated captures and publications preserve the verified references and allow preparation to finish. Consequently a suspended verification prefix cannot produce a prepared descriptor, and a verified reference set cannot be certified across intervening collection. Publication then uses the existing durable owner, which resolves an installed matching root before consulting late cancellation. Original cancellation can prevent a new publication but cannot reverse an already installed one. This preserves the established root identity, recovery and independent-child semantics without adding a SQL operator or changing any query composition. Complete Engine image membership, export authority and destination logical validation remain separate obligations.


## Writable document image ownership

Common Storage's `MemoryDocumentStoreBuilder` consumes admitted `StoredDocument` values and publishes one writable `MemoryDocumentStore`. The input must cover every retained field/value allocation under the builder's original allowance, and each document identity must be unique. Native field layouts share exact sorted names; rows retain original values, tuple metadata and allocation leases. No SQL, default expression, analyzer or index rebuild runs while adopting a document. Whole-table assembly, logical catalog closure and Engine activation remain separate requirements.

Let an accepted corpus be the finite partial function D from unsigned document identities to pairs (F, m), where F is the field-name/value map and m is private tuple metadata. For each row, let N be F's strictly ordered keys and V its values in that order. Adoption moves each original name and value into N and V, selects a layout identity whose complete name sequence equals N, and stores (layout, V, m) under the same document identity. Layout hashing selects candidates only; complete ordered equality decides reuse. Therefore looking up a present field n at its unique position i returns V[i] = F[n], an absent projected field remains NULL, materialization reconstructs exactly F, and metadata remains m independently of user fields named xmin. Unique-key admission makes this a function; ordered successor traversal over the native map enumerates exactly dom D, including zero and the maximum identity. The lower-bound seek follows the same key order as lookup, retains only ancestors whose keys satisfy the bound, and visits each remaining right subtree in order, so every emitted key is the least remaining key above the bound.

Ordinary reads, puts, patches, deletes, clears and snapshots use the same native document owner after publication. The reconstructed state supplies the same F and m to those existing transitions, so induction over any finite admitted mutation/read sequence preserves membership, field values, tuple metadata and query observations. Copy-on-write snapshots keep selected values and field layouts while new row maps evolve independently. This representation introduces no query operator or approximate equality, changes no scoring or ordering rule, and requires no additional optimizer rewrite assumption. Wire bytes remain the existing UQADOC01/UQAVAL01 encoding.

During adoption the original reservation remains live while replacement vectors and native map nodes are admitted before allocation. Moving fields frees their old map entries; original name/value payload reservations transfer into their actual shared native owners. Complete field layouts, row nodes and the final native state retain their own allowances. Consuming builder operations make rejection, cancellation and unwinding retire the entire unpublished corpus; no failed partial store escapes. A completed store carries the allocation leases without retaining the input job's cancellation signal, so later native operations use their own controls. Original allocations remain charged until the last snapshot, shared document row or physical query fragment releases them; independently allocated copies have their normal native owners.

Execution keeps those same shared value owners through projection, prefix selection and joins. Thin cached projection handles preserve the preceding inline physical-row footprint and require no allocation per cached scan row. Selected result materialization discards unselected aliases before deciding whether values can move, copies only required aliases when external readers remain, and keeps unconsumed values admitted through consumer failure and unwinding. Legacy unbudgeted row/result APIs explicitly transfer or copy the completed result at their existing ownership boundary; internal scans preserve the native lease. Thus aliasing changes neither the selected values nor their lifetime guarantees.

## Resumable evaluated-value and document images

Core owns the `UQAVAL01` encoding of an already evaluated value, including its exact scalar carrier, floating-point bits, ordered container positions, array shape and bounds, and opaque SQL descriptors or tuple backing. Common Storage prefixes the `UQADOC01` document metadata and applies that same ordered-field encoding. Let E(v) denote the existing finite value encoding of a valid carrier v with nesting depth at most 128, and D(m, f) its document header for metadata m followed by the ordered field-map encoding E(f). Source values remain immutable and borrowed for the reader's lifetime. Numerical formatting retains the existing controlled formatter, and a fields iterator must report its exact length and strictly increasing names.

A value cursor consists of the already emitted prefix p, pending bytes b and a finite stack of continuations K. Interpret each continuation as the not-yet-emitted encoding of its remaining scalar payload, array metadata, field names, child values or physical descriptor slots, in stack execution order. The invariant is p concatenated with b and the interpretation of K equals E(v), or the same map image for borrowed fields. Initialization places the version header before the root continuation, so the invariant holds for the empty prefix. A scalar step replaces its continuation by the identical tag and little-endian metadata plus the unchanged borrowed payload or admitted decimal text. A container step emits its declared size and replaces its continuation by the next ordered child and the untouched suffix; a record retains positional names, including duplicates, while a map or borrowed field source retains strict key order. Array bounds, row type metadata and expanded Datum fields are separate continuations, so suspension never changes their order or interprets their opaque contents. These are exactly the existing codec equations. A partial byte copy moves only a prefix of b into p, preserving concatenation. Induction over continuation transitions and byte copies therefore yields p = E(v) at EOF, independently of every positive output-window partition. The document cursor first emits its fixed metadata prefix and then advances that same map cursor, yielding D(m, f).

A container retains at most one ancestor continuation while its child is encoded; at most three additional leaf/name/descriptor continuations coexist. The fixed stack has capacity 128 + 4 and each child checks the existing depth limit before expansion. Each nonempty read emits at most the smaller of its output length and 16 KiB. Payload reads advance their borrowed slice or formatting-text offset rather than reconstructing any earlier prefix. The fixed traversal state allocates no heap storage; the concrete movable document cursor is admitted for its complete allocation before boxing. Numerical radix conversion retains its separately admitted temporary and output owners. Numeric formatting and an external iterator's own implementation are not bounded by the requested output-byte length; cancellation remains active within the controlled formatter. The enclosing backup job still owns source-retention, staging and total workspace limits.

The Core read boundary permanently fails and retires all continuations, iterator state and decimal text when encoding fails or unwinds. The Common Storage `ImageReader` transfers its active admitted source into the current read boundary before calling it; only successful nonterminal output returns that ownership to the active state. Error, panic, invalid byte count or cancellation drops the source before its allocation lease. A nonempty EOF releases both and enters Complete; an empty read retains active ownership. Complete remains at EOF, while Failed admits no further reads. Read-only adapters preserve the original cancellation owner in addition to the invoking control. Source generation retention stays with the borrowed snapshot and cannot be detached through this cursor. Failed partial output remains unpublished and must be discarded by the enclosing image owner.

Consequently successful segmented encoding and existing synchronous writers have the same exact logical bytes and opaque metadata. No SQL expression, analyzer, callback, training operation or graph traversal is evaluated, so existing query operators observe the same restored values under the existing decoder contract. The cursor adds scheduling boundaries and resource ownership without adding an algebraic operator or permitting a new optimizer rewrite. Complete Engine membership, authorization, catalog/resource encoding, logical import validation and activation remain separate backup requirements.

## Streaming source lengths

A source unit consists of an address a, kind k, finite encoded byte stream b and optional declaration d. If d is present, the source contract requires d = |b|. An omitted declaration changes only how capture learns that length; it does not change b, the inventory format or the import contract. Persistent record mappings and standalone copies retain their exact declarations. Resumable evaluated-memory codecs may omit d and emit their existing encoding once, without constructing a preliminary encoded copy or scanning it to count bytes.

For a selected unit, capture retains the actual byte count n and hash state H for precisely the consumed prefix p. Initially n = 0 and p is empty. Each nonempty bounded read appends its bytes to p and increments n by the returned count using checked arithmetic; a count exceeding the supplied buffer fails. A present d rejects n > d immediately and rejects n != d at EOF. Successful EOF sets the completed metadata length to n = |b| and pins that length for any later protected-spool replay. Thus every completed entry has exactly the same length and digest as the corresponding declared-length input. Absent EOF, error, cancellation and unwind cannot produce a completed entry or publishable root. The owning codec remains responsible for returning EOF only after its complete unit encoding.

Incremental matching first compares the complete address and kind. A known unequal length rejects reuse immediately. Otherwise the existing encrypted, quota-bound comparison spool records the current stream while its hash and exact length are computed. Reuse requires both the completed length and digest to match the parent. A mismatch replays the retained exact bytes under their now-known length without rereading or rehashing the source. Assuming the existing digest collision bound, a reused span represents b; a newly written span also represents b by exact replay. Induction over ordered units therefore yields the same complete image as full capture, with the same deletion, parent independence and standalone-copy semantics. This includes empty streams, shorter/longer replacements and equal-sized changes.

Each scheduled call preserves the existing source-byte, hash-byte, frame, inventory and verification bounds. Unknown length requires only an optional counter in the selected-unit header; it adds no payload-sized memory or preliminary source pass. Potential incremental matches consume the existing combined encrypted workspace allowance and fail before publication when that allowance is exhausted. Final repository integrity verification independently checks every recorded exact length and hash. No SQL evaluation, new query operator or optimizer rewrite is introduced. The actual-memory document drill establishes this codec/repository connection; complete Engine catalog/index/resource membership and restoration remain separate requirements.

## Preparation and removal generations

Repository coordination retains two opaque identities in one atomically replaced `UQAGEN02` record: every mutation changes the publication identity, while root retirement and object collection also change the removal identity before deleting or unlinking anything. Existing `UQAGEN01` records supply their single identity for both observations and transition atomically on the next mutation. Opening either format rejects truncation, trailing bytes and zero identities. Inventory and backup image formats are unchanged.

Let V be the exact references authenticated during preparation, and let e be the removal identity observed under admission before that verification. A successful comparison of e under admission before descriptor installation proves that no removal transition occurred during that interval. New operations, object installations and root publications only add references and cannot invalidate V, so they need not invalidate its verification. Once the prepared descriptor is installed, ordinary retention protects V. A changed e still fails preparation at its owning boundary; it cannot publish a stale reference certificate. Collection continues to compare the publication identity, so new captures and publications always invalidate a previously discovered deletion set. These dual admission rules preserve both reference safety and independent publication progress without caller-side retries or state repair.

Root retirement acknowledges completion only after both the cancellation marker's operation directory and the completed-root directory are synchronized. This order applies equally when retry observes an existing marker and no published root. Failure before either synchronization remains an error; a successful retry durably fences later publication of the retired ID. Retention and all logical image/query observations are unchanged.

## Resumable evaluated-text images

For a retained text document D, let F be its ordered field descriptors and let T(f) be the selected ordered reverse posting keys for field f. The existing image is the concatenation of its magic and field count, followed for each f by its length-prefixed name, exact 84-byte metadata and term count, and for each term by its length-prefixed canonical raw key and ordered occurrence encodings. Occurrences retain duplicate positions, position lengths and optional original UTF-8/UTF-16 offsets; metadata retains analyzer identity and normalization policy. No analyzer, query callback or resource resolver runs during this encoding.

`TextImageCursor` follows that same sequence of fragments. It retains the field iterator, selected reverse-key range, current occurrence suffix and the unconsumed part of one fixed header or borrowed payload. Initially no bytes are consumed. Each successful read copies a prefix of the current fragment, advances its exact position and changes stages only after consuming that fragment. Induction on reads therefore establishes that the emitted bytes concatenate to precisely the old image, independent of buffer boundaries. Final completion still checks that the selected fields cover every reverse key. Field metadata and occurrence validation retain their original order within the encoding, so malformed internal state cannot produce a completed image. The synchronous writer drains this same cursor; it does not maintain a second serialization algorithm.

A read emits at most 16 KiB. Each field or term contributes a fixed-size count before any possibly empty payload, and each occurrence contributes at least nine bytes; zero-length transitions therefore cannot skip an unbounded corpus in one call. Field-range selection and selected posting lookup use the existing ordered indexes. The iterator and control/header positions have fixed retained storage, while names, raw term keys and occurrences remain borrowed from the selected immutable index. `ImageReader` admits the concrete cursor before boxing and releases it on EOF, error, unwind or drop. Original capture, read-only adapter and invoking cancellation remain attached to their own owners; no later reader substitutes an unrelated allowance or token. The stack-based synchronous writer preserves its existing zero-heap-scratch contract.

Because the exact evaluated image is unchanged, importing it reconstructs the same term/occurrence graph, historical field metadata, document frequencies and normalization lengths. Existing text retrieval, phrase matching and scoring compositions therefore observe the same indexed state, with no optimizer rewrite or new algebraic operator. Independent raw UTF-16/duplicate fixtures, empty field metadata, a raw key above 2 MiB, exact/insufficient cursor admission, each cancellation owner and actual captured index retirement exercise these boundaries. Complete Engine image membership, catalog/resource encoding, logical validation and activation remain separate parent requirements.

## Public persistent export admission

Let A be the effective-role authorization snapshot admitted by the existing direct-query transaction owner, C the provider's independently retained committed cut and R the selected repository protection domain. A public export is constructed only when A grants superuser authority, the provider advertises its implemented export path, limits are positive and an encrypted source has an encrypted R or explicit plaintext authorization. A denied start cannot create a repository operation. Failed or unresolved SQL frames follow the existing transaction admission and recovery boundary; the adapter does not repair them or create another transaction protocol.

After admission, the provider supplies the same complete image I(C) used by its existing mapping proof. Repository construction owns the source or releases it on failure; no Engine reference or SQL statement gate enters the returned job. All scheduled transitions, verification certificates, root publication and cancellation are delegated to the existing capture owner. Therefore the image and its full/incremental reference graph are unchanged, and existing value, catalog, text/vector and graph observations follow the provider and repository preservation arguments. Missing memory encoding or production restore activation cannot be inferred from these persistent export paths and remains absent from public capability reporting.

Native SQLite initial restoration now publishes an independent logical image source at the transition that installs its committed native binding. Before physical conversion succeeds, the provider's publication cell is empty. Rollback or failed conversion leaves it empty. Successful installation copies the existing persistence identity and native namespace into a fresh logical session with independent transaction state and cancellation. Existing native bindings can publish that same independent source immediately. A weak link from the initializing session to the provider's cell prevents a retention cycle; the published source carries no link back to that cell. Capture consumes the published owner without source initialization, migration or another opener. Thus the first initialized Engine and later factories select the same committed carrier, while private changes, original cancellation and Engine closure cannot replace or poison the export source.

Owning fixtures cover rollback and conversion failure, every SQLite file mode, cancellation and source lifetime. Public Engine fixtures cover all nine persistent provider/file-mode cases, active private writes, authority and failed-frame denial, encrypted-source policy, original job cancellation, independent writer progress, full source loss, parent retirement and restored observations with subsequent writes. These establish component behavior and deterministic lifecycle bounds; complete Engine memory images, production logical restore validation/activation and fingerprint acceleration retain their original requirements.


## Resumable evaluated graph images

Graph's `MemoryGraphStore::image_reader` and `GraphImageSnapshot::image_reader` open a borrowed, admitted `ImageReader` over the selected native generation. The graph owner keeps separate validation and encoding continuations. `prepare(maximum_positions)` returns exact visited positions and readiness, visits no more than its positive limit and emits no image bytes. A position is one scalar-counter check, native iterator entry, nested membership/index entry or continuation boundary, including empty derived buckets. Each position performs a fixed number of existing ordered-tree operations; this is a position bound rather than a wall-clock claim. Source validation is identical to the synchronous writer's and includes both directions of primary/derived membership, label and adjacency checks. A nonempty read completes remaining preparation synchronously; scheduled source adapters finish bounded preparation before exposing their unit.

The Common Storage reader owns preparation admission and terminal state. It rejects excessive counts and pending results without progress, checks invoking cancellation before and after each call, and retires the cursor before releasing its allocation on error or unwind. `with_source_control` also checks the captured source's original cancellation during preparation and output. Readiness does not imply image completion. Nonempty EOF retires the cursor; empty reads preserve its state, and completed reads remain complete after later cancellation. The borrowed snapshot retains its own source allowance until that snapshot closes.

`GraphImageSnapshot::into_image_reader(control)` consumes the retained snapshot into an independent `ImageReader<'static>`. Common Storage's `ImageOwner` opens the same borrowed codec inside a compiler-generated pinned continuation that owns the immutable source and invoking control. The public reader remains synchronous: each prepare/read command polls that private continuation once, then obtains its response. No executor, spawned task, worker thread, source replay or complete encoded buffer is involved. A fixed 16 KiB transfer window, concrete continuation and outer cursor header are admitted before their allocations; the existing codec headers are admitted when the reader opens lazily. The source retains its original payload allowance. Empty reads preserve ownership and original cancellation before and after opening. Completion, error, cancellation, unwind and unread drop retire the borrowed codec before its source and allocation leases.

For this owning adapter, let O retain the immutable graph S and let C be the borrowed continuation whose prefix invariant is established below. Safe Rust's [pinned future contract](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/pin/index.html) keeps O and the borrow held by C at fixed addresses after the first poll; moving the external handle moves only the pinned box pointer. The compiler enforces that C cannot outlive O. Initially the exchange is idle. Supplying one command causes exactly one corresponding C operation and stores exactly one response before the continuation suspends; the adapter then consumes that response and restores the idle exchange. Induction on commands gives the same preparation positions and byte sequence as direct calls to C. Copying at most the fixed window from its response to the caller preserves the emitted prefix. EOF returns from the continuation, while any error or unwind makes the outer reader terminal and drops the pinned owner. Thus the retained source and its allowance outlive every borrow, and source retirement does not depend on retaining the Engine or original capture handle. New scratch consists only of admitted fixed headers, the compiler-sized continuation and the fixed window, independent of graph cardinality; Core numeric formatting retains its separately admitted existing bound.

The encoder preserves `UQAGRA01`: original allocation counters, global evaluated entities, ordered graph memberships and all label-registry fields retain their existing bytes and order. Vertex and edge properties use Core's `ValueImageReader`; names, property values and native keys remain borrowed. Every count precedes its possibly empty collection or payload, so output transitions cannot skip an unbounded collection without producing bytes. Reads emit at most 16 KiB; fixed traversal stacks and the concrete cursor header are independent of graph size. Core's existing admitted numeric formatter may perform work independent of the requested byte window; this change does not claim to bound that formatter by output length. The synchronous writer drains the same encoder through Common Storage's bounded I/O and retains its zero-heap-scratch behavior for borrowed property carriers.

For preservation, let S be the source-valid primary graph carrier defined above and let V(S) be the finite ordered sequence of the existing validation predicates. A validation continuation stores a prefix position in V(S), including nested and empty-container boundaries. Initially no predicate is consumed; one transition checks the next predicate or advances one explicit boundary. Induction on transitions proves that readiness is reachable exactly when every original predicate succeeds. Suspension does not change S or the next predicate because the source is immutably borrowed. A failed predicate returns no output and retires the cursor. Partition membership in both directions, endpoint-as-payload semantics and historical label-registry acceptance therefore remain unchanged.

Let E(S) be the existing `UQAGRA01` byte encoding. For every encoder continuation, the emitted prefix followed by its current unconsumed piece and remaining ordered continuations equals E(S). This holds initially for the magic and counter header. A scalar/count transition substitutes its identical fixed-width encoding; a name transition substitutes its unchanged length-delimited UTF-8 bytes; an entity-properties transition uses Core's proven exact map continuation; a map/set transition preserves the original native order. Each read consumes only a prefix of the current piece, preserving the invariant. Induction proves that concatenating reads through EOF equals E(S) for every positive window schedule. The existing graph decode argument then gives read(E(S)) = S with the same derived observations and future allocations; no operator, numeric conversion, callback, graph evaluation or optimizer rewrite is introduced. Failed prefixes remain unusable for publication.

Owner verification covers independent wire fixtures, one-byte windows, large borrowed properties without read-time allocations, exact/insufficient cursor admission, empty-index validation bounds, original and invoking cancellation, failed-source retirement and retained generations after replacement. A graph-owned composition test streams the independently owned reader through the actual protected full/incremental repository, mutates the live successor between payload reads, verifies unchanged-unit reuse and restores the selected graph after source and parent-root loss. An Engine-owned test consumes the selected committed graph into a movable reader, closes both Engine and capture, and reconstructs only that selected graph after a later live mutation. Common Storage tests cover cross-thread movement after opening, exact admission boundaries, callback errors and unwinds, cancellation and unread drop; terminal preparation keeps the source header admitted through its destructor. These qualify the graph component; complete committed memory Engine image assembly and Engine restore activation remain separate requirements.

## Resumable evaluated exact-vector images

The exact-vector carrier is the existing ordered mapping from document identifiers to nonempty finite tensors of the declared dimension. Zero-dimensional vectors and an empty mapping retain their existing representation. `ExactImageCursor` borrows that immutable mapping and its tensor/coordinate slices, with a fixed native map traversal stack and a fixed 4 KiB encoding window. It does not query, normalize, retrain or clone the selected payload. `ImageReader` admits the concrete cursor before boxing and owns invoking cancellation and terminal retirement. Read-only and export-only snapshot adapters preserve the original capture cancellation. A consumed `VectorImageSnapshot` uses the already established `ImageOwner` continuation, so the same borrowed encoder and original retention allowance survive live-index and external-capture retirement. Unsupported concrete codecs reject resumable output without buffering their synchronous writers.

Let E(S) be the previously defined exact `UQAVEC01` encoding of a valid source S. A continuation consists of the remaining ordered document iterator, remaining tensor iterator, remaining coordinate iterator, pending fixed bytes and their consumed offset. Its invariant is that emitted bytes followed by pending unconsumed bytes and the encodings selected by the remaining iterators equal E(S). Initially the pending bytes are the unchanged magic, method, dimension, document count and vector count. A document transition substitutes that document's original identifier and tensor count; a vector transition substitutes its original dimension count; a coordinate transition substitutes the unchanged little-endian `to_bits` representation for the next at most 1,024 coordinates. Native iterator ordering preserves document and tensor order. Each read consumes only a prefix of these bytes, maintaining the invariant. Induction through EOF proves equality with E(S) for every positive read-window schedule. The existing decoder therefore reconstructs the same tensors and their exact bits; its established scoring, reduction, identity, future-mutation and composition observations are unchanged. No new algebraic operator or optimizer rewrite is introduced. Invalid dimensions, empty stored tensors or nonfinite coordinates fail the cursor, and incomplete output cannot be published as a verified image.

Every nonterminal transition emits at least four bytes, including counts for zero-dimensional vectors, so a read cannot skip an unbounded number of empty payloads. Each call emits at most 16 KiB and can encode at most one additional 4 KiB window beyond the emitted prefix; native document traversal retains its fixed maximum-height stack. The opening operation uses cached counts and initializes that stack without traversing the corpus. Heap scratch is exactly the admitted concrete cursor and, for an owned reader, the existing admitted continuation and transfer window; neither grows with source cardinality or vector dimensions. The synchronous writer drains an unboxed instance of this same cursor through bounded `ImageOutput`, preserving zero heap scratch. Source and invoking cancellation are checked across preparation and output; errors, cancellation, unwind, nonempty EOF and drop follow the existing terminal reader state machine.

Owning tests compare independently assembled wire bytes at one-byte and arbitrary field boundaries, read a two-MiB vector under exhausted spare admission with zero additional allocations, deny exact cursor admission, retain original cancellation, transfer a started reader across threads after source loss, preserve empty and zero-dimensional carriers and reject an invalid final coordinate without scanning the full source at opening. Retained membership and unsupported-codec tests verify source retirement and failure cleanup. Complete memory Engine image membership, the remaining resumable vector codecs and Engine restore activation remain unfinished.

## Resumable evaluated DiskANN images

The selected memory DiskANN carrier S consists of the immutable sealed physical generation P, its manifest and read/build options, and canonical generation C. C contains ordered document tensors with exact origin identities and finite coordinate bits, ordered current changes, selected revision, population counts and its covered-generation identity. Existing lifecycle owners publish C and P together; a captured image retains those immutable roots without querying, retraining, normalizing or rebuilding them. The encoded image E(S) is the existing method-3 `UQAVEC01` header, manifest, options, ordered sealed records, ordered pages and ordered canonical tensors/changes. Restoration retains P and all represented origins while using the existing fresh mutation clock for subsequent writes.

The canonical validator retains three ordered document/change iterators, counts, revision presence, a fixed origin-batch cursor and an explicit stage. Its first pass checks the original generation identity, each tensor's dimension/count, actual change version, current/changed population sums and selected revision. Its change pass requires every change key to name a retained document. Its merge pass visits complete sealed origins in order: every canonical document before the next base origin must be uncovered; matching identities must agree exactly when no change exists; every trailing canonical document must also be uncovered. These are the original predicates, with the former inner loop over uncovered gaps suspended after each document. Sealed physical sources are constructed only by complete artifact verification and contain exactly the immutable dense origin batches described by the manifest. Advancing their dense batch address therefore selects precisely the same origin sequence as visiting their ordered origin records; each batch contains at most 64 entries and its checksum/shape validation remains unchanged.

For validation, let V be the ordered predicate sequence obtained from these original passes. Initially no predicate is accepted. Each successful transition checks the next metadata entry, batch or boundary and extends only the accepted prefix; the retained accumulators equal their original folds over that prefix. The document merge retains its current base origin while consuming one preceding canonical entry at a time, so suspension neither skips a missing change nor consumes the base twice. Induction over transitions proves readiness exactly when V succeeds. Each requested preparation step performs at most the positive caller-supplied number of transitions; pending progress is positive and repeated readiness reports zero. A transition performs a fixed number of native map operations or decodes one format-bounded origin batch. Native tree lookup retains its logarithmic bound; positions are logical work units, not elapsed-time or physical-I/O estimates. Invalid canonical state fails before any image output, and image import drains this same validator before publishing its private candidate.

For output, the outer cursor concatenates the unchanged header, fixed-size admitted manifest encoding, options, physical stream and canonical stream. Physical output retains native record/page iterators, a small scalar header and a borrowed payload suffix.
