Canonical output retains document, tensor, coordinate and change positions plus a 4 KiB coordinate window. Initially the emitted prefix is empty. Every successful copy emits the next exact bytes of E(S), moving only the corresponding offset; crossing a scalar, record, page or tensor boundary selects the next original ordered component. Coordinates use their original finite `f32::to_bits()` values, including signed zero; no numerical transformation occurs. Induction on emitted bytes establishes that concatenating any successful sequence of read windows yields E(S), independent of window sizes. Every empty payload has a nonempty encoded header/count, and the unique sealed manifest is the sole skipped physical record, so a bounded output window cannot hide an unbounded scan of empty payloads. Both encoding cursors clear pending lengths at EOF, preventing a later read from replaying their final header.

The admitted cursor, fixed-height native iterator state, manifest and coordinate window have size independent of corpus cardinality, record size and tensor dimensions. Sealed payloads remain borrowed; no complete image, page collection or tensor copy is constructed. Preparation and output allocate no additional heap scratch after opening. Each read emits at most 16 KiB; at most one additional fixed coordinate window is encoded beyond the emitted prefix. The selected physical and canonical owners retain their original leases for the reader's lifetime, and owned continuation admission uses the existing Common Storage mailbox/future boundary. Source, capture and invoking cancellation remain active through preparation, empty and nonempty reads; synchronous output retains the original cancellation wrapper around each partial or interrupted writer attempt. Failure, unwind, EOF and drop retire the cursor and its leases through the common image state machine; a failed image is unusable and cannot become published repository state.

Because successful output is exactly E(S), the existing decoder reconstructs the same physical pages, canonical tensors, changes, origins and options. Consequently existing candidate selection, tensor reduction, scores, ranking ties, metadata and later mutations observe the same carrier; probability conversion and optimizer behavior are unchanged. No new algebraic operator, approximation or numeric law is introduced. The prior full/incremental repository composition argument applies to these identical payload bytes. Owning regressions compare arbitrary preparation/output schedules against the predecessor `bfbbab151` evaluated fixture (SHA-256 `b47336d76e29efd63ce346d0d2e427e5544463d05013e40874a2b8be15400432`), retain the independently specified empty image, reject corrupted canonical successors before output, and exercise source loss/rebuild, exact admission, bounded empty-origin work, large tensors without scratch allocation and every cancellation owner. HNSW provides its own resumable image continuation; native table reconstruction, logical closure and public Engine restore remain separate unfinished requirements.

## Retained HNSW record windows

The native spill-map carrier is a finite ordered mapping M from 128-bit keys to immutable record values. A resident root now contains exactly dom(M), with no removed-key placeholders. Insertion retains its existing copy-on-write publication; removal derives the existing Core tree successor M restricted to keys other than k and publishes it only after successful admission. For any selected key q, lookup in that successor is absent exactly when q = k or q was absent from M; all other values and their ordering are unchanged. Retained roots still denote M because their nodes and values remain immutable. On failed removal the logical mapping and count remain M, including when the existing owner changes its representation to an encrypted spill file. Ordered next/last operations consequently select the same live keys, while their native tree traversal no longer includes an unbounded run of deleted slots. This changes neither query support nor retained payload observations.

For a spilled selected root, `RecordBytes` borrows the existing file owner and the leaf's checked immutable extent [b, b+n). Patricia lookup retains its existing bound of at most 128 key branches. Reading at offset i requires 0 <= i <= n and copies the next min(output length, n-i, 4096) bytes through the existing authenticated block cache. No record decoder or whole-payload buffer is invoked. The borrow keeps the file/cache admission alive; sibling roots append new values and cannot change the selected extent. Thus every returned window equals the corresponding substring of the original encoding, including after replacement through another root. A 4 KiB request spans at most five 1 KiB blocks, and the existing cache has at most 64 entries. Cancellation is checked before and after each window, including empty and EOF reads. These primitives borrow a source and do not themselves create an independently owned or terminal image reader.

The HNSW vector record is the original norm bit word, coordinate count and sequence of little-endian f32 bit words. `VectorRead` either borrows the resident coordinate slice or validates that the selected record length is exactly 12+4n and reads at most 1,024 coordinates per call. `from_bits` followed by `to_bits` is the identity on each 32-bit word; no normalization, arithmetic, rounding or coordinate reordering occurs. Consequently concatenating any complete sequence of successful windows reconstructs the same coordinate representation, including signed zero and nonfinite bit patterns at this private record boundary. Image validation still applies the original norm and finite-coordinate predicates, in raw-before-normalized order, and supplies each window's absolute coordinate offset. The first invalid coordinate therefore retains its original diagnostic and is rejected before synchronous image output.

The existing HNSW writer emits the unchanged metadata, norm/count fields and concatenated coordinate windows. Since every vector window equals its original encoding and node/layer/dirty-entry order is unchanged, its successful output is the same evaluated `UQAVEC01` image. The existing decoder, search, persistence deltas and future mutation observe the same HNSW carrier. No algebraic operator, scoring rule or approximation is introduced. Native windows use fixed stack scratch and the retained source cache; the invoking allowance no longer needs to admit a complete spilled vector. This does not bound the current whole-image validation or topology traversal, which still require a resumable continuation and separately controlled workspace before complete HNSW scheduling can be claimed.

Owner regressions retain 4,096-key generations across structural removal and denied publication, read a replaced one-MiB record without payload decoding or allocation, preserve all coordinate bits with exhausted spare admission, and verify absolute invalid-coordinate diagnostics in resident and spilled raw/normalized records. The existing independent HNSW wire fixture and persistence-delta tests continue to define image observations. A four-MiB vector image is written with a 64-KiB invoking allowance. Complete HNSW preparation/output continuation, complete memory Engine image assembly and production restore remain open requirements.

## Resumable evaluated IVF images

The selected IVF carrier S consists of the declared dimension and index parameters, training state, centroids, ordered canonical identities, raw and normalized coordinate bits, stored norms, centroid assignments, sorted inverted lists and training/deletion counters. Capture retains the existing detached admitted `IVFData`; a frozen `VectorImageSnapshot` cannot query or retrain that value. Its borrowed or owned reader opens a fixed cursor without copying the captured payload. The live `IVFIndex::image_reader` entry point first performs the existing detached capture under state admission and releases that admission before returning; capture can copy the index and is not claimed to be constant work. Subsequent live retraining selects a different owner and cannot change S.

Let V(S) be the original ordered validation predicates: configuration and state consistency; centroid dimensions and finite components; canonical identity, ordinal and norm checks; raw and normalized dimensions and finite components; valid centroid assignment; ordered unique list membership; and complete membership cardinality for trained or stale state. The new validator retains an explicit stage, native ordered iterators, coordinate offsets, previous keys and the membership count. Each transition checks the next original scalar or identity predicate, at most 1,024 consecutive coordinate predicates, one list member, or one collection boundary. Empty coordinates and empty lists each retain their own transition. Shared dimension/component helpers preserve the original diagnostic text and absolute coordinate index. Initially the validated prefix of V is empty. Each successful transition extends exactly that prefix and updates only its continuation; induction proves that readiness holds exactly when every original predicate succeeds. Immutable source ownership means suspension cannot alter either the prefix or its remaining predicates. Failed validation produces no output and the common reader retires the cursor.

Preparation reports the exact number of transitions completed, never more than the positive requested limit; pending results contain positive progress and repeated ready calls report zero positions. A transition contains at most 1,024 coordinate checks or a fixed number of native ordered-map operations. Native search retains its existing logarithmic bound; this is an explicit work-position bound, not a wall-clock claim. No preparation or output heap allocation is needed after cursor admission for a frozen IVF source. The fixed iterator/header state and 4 KiB coordinate window remain independent of corpus cardinality and coordinate dimensions. Reads emit at most 16 KiB and can encode at most one additional coordinate window beyond the emitted prefix. Every payload, including zero-dimensional vectors, has a preceding nonempty count or identity encoding, so output cannot skip an unbounded sequence of empty payloads.

Let E(S) be the existing method-1 `UQAVEC01` encoding. The encoder invariant is that emitted bytes, its pending unconsumed window and its remaining ordered continuations concatenate to E(S). Its initial header encodes the unchanged parameters, state and centroid/training/deletion counts. Centroid and raw/normalized transitions emit their original length and coordinate bit sequence through the same shared fixed-window helper as synchronous vector images. Canonical transitions emit the original document/ordinal order, norm bits and optional centroid index. A read consumes only a prefix of that sequence; induction through EOF proves equality with E(S) for any positive byte-window schedule. Validated sorted unique inverted lists are exactly the projection of the encoded assignments, so the existing decoder reconstructs those same lists. Thus decoding restores S, including stale state, overflowed norm observations, signed zero and future query-triggered training behavior. No training, normalization, query, callback, numeric conversion or new algebraic operator occurs during output; existing vector observations and compositions are preserved.

Synchronous writing drains the same validator and encoder from an unboxed cursor before returning. Import drains the same validator after private decoding and list reconstruction. The common admitted reader and owned continuation preserve original and invoking cancellation, including empty reads, and release cursor/source ownership on failure, unwind, EOF or drop. Owning tests use independent stale-state bytes, arbitrary preparation/output windows, 4,096 empty buckets, four MiB of coordinate output under exhausted spare admission, absolute invalid-coordinate diagnostics, malformed source relationships and exact allocation denial. Actual live query retraining, cross-thread output after source loss and cancellation at every preparation boundary preserve the selected image and release both allowances. Complete memory Engine image membership, HNSW continuation and production restore activation remain separate unfinished requirements.


## Resumable HNSW images

Let S be one immutable HNSW generation, including parameters, allocation metadata, ordered nodes, raw and normalized vector bit strings, layer-ordered adjacency lists, tombstones and dirty-node values. Let E(S) be its existing evaluated `UQAVEC01` encoding. The continuation borrows this generation; an independently owned reader retains the same immutable owner through the common pinned image adapter. No query, normalization, insertion, graph rebuild or SQL expression runs during export.

Preparation preserves the predecessor validation order: parameters and image metadata, each node's identity and active membership, raw before normalized vector predicates, complete live/deleted counts, ordered active tensor ordinals, dirty-node membership, then structural identity counts, node identities, allocation bounds, entry/max level, per-layer edges and layer-zero reachability. Coordinate positions examine at most 1,024 values and carry the original absolute offset. Structural validation first scans every identity before considering edges. For each layer, a set insertion detects exactly a repeated neighbor or self edge before checking the degree bound; ordered neighbor visits then test existence, level and reciprocity. Reverse membership scans at most 512 identities per position. Thus the first rejected semantic predicate and its diagnostic remain unchanged; missing workspace or cancellation still terminate preparation before any output.

The duplicate set and reachability workspace use the existing admitted AVL nodes and authenticated Patricia pages. A capped resident prefix remains in its original tree; overflow identities enter a disjoint disk root, and later updates use the root already owning that identity. No transition copies the prefix or compacts the file. Disjointness and point-update induction give the same finite map as ordinary insertion. The resident prefix uses at most 16 KiB, each Patricia path has at most 128 branch positions, and an update appends one fixed-width value and at most 128 branch headers. Clearing a layer retires only this capped resident tree and fixed caches; the ciphertext file has no per-record destructor. Memory admission covers every allocated node, cache and update buffer before allocation. File admission counts the actual physical encrypted slots, including framing and replacement slots; filesystem allocation metadata remains outside that count.

Reachability stores visited node identities in keys below 2^64 and queue positions in a disjoint namespace starting at 2^64. Initially the entry is both visited and queued. For a dequeued node, each unseen layer-zero neighbor is marked and enqueued exactly once. Induction on queue positions gives the same breadth-first reachable set as the predecessor validator, including deleted nodes. At completion the number of queued identities equals the visited cardinality; equality with the complete node count is therefore equivalent to full layer-zero reachability. A failed insertion cannot publish a partially validated image: the enclosing reader or synchronous call retires the complete private validator. Each scheduled position performs one metadata boundary, one forward identity, one coordinate window, or one bounded reverse window; configured preparation limits bound the number of such positions, independently of corpus cardinality.

After preparation, the encoder's invariant is that emitted bytes followed by the unconsumed fixed window form a prefix of E(S), while the saved node/vector/layer/dirty cursor identifies the next original field. Each transition appends that exact field or its next coordinate/neighbor window, so induction on output transitions preserves the prefix. Every node, vector, layer and dirty entry emits a nonempty fixed-size field before any possibly empty payload. Consequently empty vectors and layers cannot hide an unbounded traversal inside a read. Reads emit at most 16 KiB and encode at most one additional 4 KiB pending window; record views retain fixed metadata and borrow original source pages. Norm and coordinate conversions are bit-preserving, adjacency order is unchanged, and successful EOF emits exactly E(S).

The shared structural validator also serves ordinary invariant checks and private reconstruction. Synchronous image output drains the same preparation and encoding continuation. The common reader admits the concrete cursor before boxing and retires it on failure, unwind, nonempty EOF or drop; original source and invoking cancellation remain attached through borrowed and owned adapters. A failed reader cannot resume after a cancellation token is reset. Existing decoding therefore reconstructs the same evaluated carrier, preserving search scores and ties, tombstone behavior, persistence deltas, future graph mutation and every established vector composition. No new algebraic operator, approximation or optimizer rewrite is introduced.

`StorageReadControl::with_temporary_limit` constructs an independent temporary-file allowance whose clones share physical usage. New HNSW validation scratch uses that allowance; already retained source files keep their original storage owner. Engine creates the job allowance before selecting its committed image, and supplies the same derived control to source capture and repository construction. Repository capture reuses an attached allowance only when it is no weaker than the declared workspace maximum, so source scratch, inventory/comparison spools and final verification cannot each consume a separate copy of the limit. The source's SQL admission scope still ends before repository operation construction; a repository admission failure therefore preserves the source transaction's existing state. Complete memory Engine image assembly and restore activation remain separate requirements.

## Resumable model and analyzer images

ML's `model_image_reader` and captured model readers preserve `UQAMOD01`; Common Storage's captured analyzer readers preserve `UQAANA01`. Both use the existing admitted `ImageReader` lifecycle, expose borrowed and independently owned output, and share their encoder with synchronous writes. Independently owned output transfers the selected immutable resource into the existing pinned continuation, without a background producer, serializer replay or complete image buffer. Engine remains responsible for committed resource membership and complete backup image assembly.

Let a selected model be M = (L, a, g) in the stored-model domain above, and let a selected analyzer be A = (D, R) in the analyzer domain above. Their original binary encodings E_M and E_A concatenate tagged, counted scalar and payload fields. A model's projection contains at most fourteen fixed fields per layer; payload references borrow original strings and weight slices. The analyzer projection borrows D and an exact-size iterator over R, emitting each resource's kind, hash and counted bytes. Projection copies no source payload and performs no inference, normalization, training or resource lookup.

For either source S, define p as the consumed prefix length of E(S). Initially p = 0. Each scalar transition writes the original tag, checked count or IEEE representation; each payload transition copies an original byte interval or encodes at most 512 original binary64 values. Its next piece is therefore exactly the next interval of E(S). A read copies a prefix of that piece and retains the suffix and subsequent field position. Induction over transitions and partial reads gives output = E(S)[0..p] for every successful prefix. Source and field exhaustion give p = |E(S)| and stable EOF. Each positive read emits at most 16 KiB; each internal payload piece contains at most 4 KiB. Empty reads advance no source position. Synchronous output drains these same transitions, establishing identical complete bytes for every read schedule and synchronous output.

Model validation retains the original first-layer and alpha checks, then each layer's fixed shape checks and ordered finite-value fields. Each position checks at most 512 numeric values, retains their absolute offset and moves to the next layer only after its complete checks succeed. Graph convolution retains the sequential IEEE-754 binary64 sum and the presence of negative values while scanning the original order; aggregate failure is reported only after all finite-value checks. Induction over coordinates gives the same finite-value result and sequential rounded sum as the original ordered fold, including signed zero, subnormals and overflow. The numeric premise is the existing sequential round-to-nearest binary64 execution without reassociation. Shape checks precede numeric checks, fields and layers retain their order, and aggregate errors follow finite-value errors. Acceptance and the first semantic diagnostic are therefore unchanged. Ordinary runtime validation, stored-model validation and image preparation drain this same owner. Preparation finishes before model bytes are exposed; image import polls the same bounded validator before returning a private candidate.

Under successful validation and unchanged format support, the existing decoding arguments give decode(E_M(M)) = M bitwise and decode(E_A(A)) = A in exact descriptor/resource representation. The existing model prediction and complete analyzer-observation arguments apply by substitution. Every downstream composition F receives the same original model outputs or complete token observations, preserving its decorated inputs, laws and numeric assumptions. No new query operator, optimizer rewrite or approximation is introduced. These component equalities retain the existing requirement for a complete, jointly selected Engine image.

Cursor state contains fixed metadata, iterator positions and fixed windows, admitted before boxing. Native analyzer sources expose their descriptor and exact resource count without a preparatory traversal. Model opening checks only fixed header admission; complete layer/numeric traversal belongs to resumable preparation. Source payloads retain their original capture admission independently of output scratch. Original and invoking cancellation remain active before and after bounded reads and preparation; model numeric positions also poll both controls. Interrupted synchronous writes retain the original output adapter's checks. The common reader replaces active state before invoking its codec, so errors and unwinds retire the partial cursor and cannot resume it. EOF, error and drop release cursor state; independently owned readers release their resource as well. A still-retained immutable source remains unchanged. These lifetime and admission rules compose with the existing capture and repository owners.

Owning tests exercise literal framing, every stored layer and gating variant, the existing independent decoder and CPU predictions, large descriptors and four-MiB weights under exact cursor admission, absolute diagnostics and conflicting validation errors, zero read-time allocations, output boundaries, both cancellation owners, source replacement/closure and cross-thread transfer. Korean and Japanese artifact cases execute with their owning Cargo features enabled and reconstruct complete token observations through fresh resource owners. These finite checks exercise the argument's boundaries; complete Engine image assembly and restore retain their separate requirements.

## Native security catalog images

SQL owns the version-one role (`UQAROL01`), membership (`UQARMB01`), schema (`UQASCH01`), database authority (`UQADBA01`), built-in routine authority (`UQABRA01`) and system relation authority (`UQASRA01`) codecs in [catalog/image](../../crates/uqa-sql/src/catalog/image.rs). Each image starts with its eight-byte magic and a little-endian u64 entry count. Database authority has exactly one entry. Integer fields retain their fixed-width little-endian representation; text is a u64 byte length followed by UTF-8; booleans and option presence use distinct zero/one tags. Role attributes and privilege sets use explicit, exhaustive bit assignments. Unknown bits, noncanonical tags, invalid text, incomplete fields and trailing bytes are rejected.

Role entries contain the native key, OID, object incarnation, tuple revision, definition name, attribute bits and connection limit. Membership entries contain their ordered identity key, tuple OID, all three original name/OID/incarnation bindings and admin/inherit/set bits. Schema entries retain the native name, optional namespace tuple identity, owner identity and optional ordered ACL. Database entries retain the owner and optional ordered ACL. ACL entries preserve PUBLIC versus an explicit role, the grantor incarnation, privileges and grant options. `None`, `Some([])`, repeated ACL paths and their ordering remain distinct. Temporary schemas never belong to a captured durable root and are rejected on image import.

A role identity is an i64 OID followed by sixteen incarnation bytes. A role binding is length-prefixed text, a u32 OID and sixteen incarnation bytes. Role tuple revisions are u64; namespace tuple identities are an i64 OID, sixteen incarnation bytes and sixteen revision bytes. Role connection limits are i32. Attribute bits 1/2/4/8/16/32/64 denote SUPERUSER/INHERIT/CREATEROLE/CREATEDB/LOGIN/REPLICATION/BYPASSRLS. Membership bits 1/2/4 denote ADMIN/INHERIT/SET. Schema privilege bits 1/2 denote USAGE/CREATE, and database privilege bits 1/2/4 denote CONNECT/CREATE/TEMPORARY. A present ACL has a u64 entry count; each entry contains the optional grantee identity, grantor identity, privilege byte and grant-option byte. All counts and tags are emitted even for empty text or vectors.

Built-in routine entries contain a u32 OID, sixteen nonzero revision bytes and a counted ordered vector of EXECUTE ACL paths. Each path contains an optional role identity, grantor identity and one grant-option boolean. System relation entries contain schema/name text, a u64 column count and an optional table ACL. A present table ACL contains sixteen nonzero revision bytes and a counted ordered vector of table privilege paths. Exactly the declared column count follows, each as column-name text, sixteen revision bytes and a counted ACL vector. Table privilege bits 1/2/4/8/16/32/64/128 denote SELECT/INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE/TRUNCATE/REFERENCES/TRIGGER/MAINTAIN, independently for privileges and grant options. Missing table authority, present empty table authority, missing column authority and present empty column authority remain distinct; each relation/attribute revision is preserved. Import rejects zero routine keys or revisions, unknown system relations or columns and duplicate/reversed relation or column keys. Built-in routine existence and referenced role closure remain the complete restore validator's responsibility.

The borrowed reader retains native ordered iterators and a fixed array of scalar/text descriptors; it neither serializes an entire catalog nor scans its entries during construction. Every entry emits nonempty framing, including entries with empty ACLs. One read emits at most 16 KiB and checks the supplied cancellation token between fields and at most 4 KiB between payload copies. Synchronous writes drain the same cursor. A read or write error retires the cursor, and the enclosing unpublished artifact must discard any partial bytes. The caller retains the captured root and its existing admission for the duration of the borrow; an enclosing retained-source adapter remains responsible for its own cancellation scope.

Core owns the shared binary framing primitives. Reads admit declared byte buffers before allocation and preserve exact partial-read, interrupted-I/O, end-of-stream and cancellation behavior. Storage delegates to these primitives while preserving its public structures, typed memory/cancellation errors, I/O context and partial-write count. SQL interprets catalog records and transfers their admitted text, attribute nodes and ACL vectors into native immutable registries; it also funds native map construction and temporary identity-validation maps. System column nodes and payloads are admitted before construction, and declared built-in column names use the existing controlled SQL producer during validation. The database authority owner moves the decoded ACL into its admitted shared tuple without a second ACL copy. Native clones and captures keep the imported allocation leases until their final owning root is released.

### Security image preservation argument

Let C = (R, M, S, A, B, T) denote a committed security catalog: R is the finite ordered role-definition map; M is the finite ordered membership map; S is the durable schema map; A is database authority; B is the finite built-in routine authority map; and T is the finite system relation authority map with its finite ordered attribute maps. Assume the native catalog has valid role and namespace incarnations, unique definition and tuple identities, matching role/membership keys, nonzero role and authority revisions, known system relation/column identities and legal connection limits. SQL's existing role-resolution and authority rules continue to define cross-catalog validity. Let E encode each component by the specified field sequence, and D decode the corresponding complete component image. This component codec does not establish cross-catalog closure or activate a restored Engine.

Fixed-width integer and identity encodings are injective because their byte widths and byte order are fixed. Length-prefixed UTF-8 is injective because the decoder consumes exactly that byte length and rejects invalid text. Exhaustive bit assignments are injective on the seven role attributes, three membership options, two schema privileges, three database privileges and eight table privileges; decoders reject every unassigned bit. Explicit option tags distinguish absence from every present value, including an empty vector. An ACL is an ordered sequence, so encoding retains both order and multiplicity. These facts establish D(E(v)) = v for every field and ACL in the domain.

A native registry supplies its entries in strict key order. The image writes each key and value exactly once; import rejects duplicate or reversed keys and mismatched role/membership keys before a candidate can escape. Induction over the entry count, using the field identity above, establishes D(E(R)) = R and D(E(M)) = M. The same induction gives D(E(S)) = S for the preselected durable schema root; temporary membership is already excluded by native capture. The single-entry rule and field identity give D(E(A)) = A. Routine authority is an ordered OID map whose revision and ACL fields satisfy the same identity; hence D(E(B)) = B. For each system relation, the explicit table option preserves absent versus present empty authority, and induction over the counted, strictly ordered attribute map preserves every column name, revision and ACL. Applying the native registry induction to those relation records gives D(E(T)) = T. Header, count, complete-field and trailing-byte validation make a successful parse consume exactly one complete image.

Initially the reader's emitted prefix is empty. Its descriptors identify the next original fixed-width field or borrowed text suffix. A successful read copies only the next bytes and advances that descriptor's offset; moving to an ACL entry or native successor installs precisely the next specified descriptors. Induction over these transitions shows that any successful output-window schedule concatenates to E(C), independently of window sizes. Every native entry and optional sequence emits nonempty framing, so no read can conceal an unbounded traversal of empty entries. The native tree iterator retains its existing bounded-depth traversal; no SQL, routine invocation, identity allocation, name rebinding or permission recomputation occurs.

Because decoding preserves each identity, attribute, membership option and ACL path, evaluating any existing role-resolution or authority operation over D(E(C)) yields the same result as over C, including renamed-role bindings, PUBLIC grants, default versus empty ACLs and grantor-specific paths. Compositions with those observations therefore retain their original meaning. No algebraic query operator, scoring rule, numerical approximation or optimizer rewrite is introduced. Full and incremental repository composition preserves these same component bytes by the existing authenticated-image argument. Whole-Engine logical closure, joint image assembly and fresh activation remain required before public memory backup or restore can be declared complete.

All decoded allocations are admitted before construction. Their leases move with owned payloads into the private registry, and cached capture footprints include each key and definition capacity exactly once. Immutable tree publication preserves existing roots; a failed candidate releases its decoded payloads and temporary validation maps without modifying another registry. Capturing the imported root independently admits its complete cached footprint, while shared original allocation leases survive native clones, removal from a live registry and retained-reader lifetimes. Thus resource rejection or cancellation changes neither the captured image nor any published catalog state.

## Native type and catalog metadata images

SQL's [catalog image owner](../../crates/uqa-sql/src/catalog/image.rs) also encodes enum definitions (`UQAENU01`), shell types (`UQASHL01`), foreign servers (`UQAFSR01`) and graph catalog OIDs (`UQAGOI01`). Each image uses the common eight-byte magic, u64 entry count, strict native key order, little-endian integers, length-prefixed UTF-8 and zero/one option tags. OIDs in these four formats are u32; role identities retain the security format's i64 OID and sixteen incarnation bytes. Object incarnations are sixteen-byte values that must not be all zero. These codecs return private native registries; complete logical closure, memory Engine assembly and activation remain separate requirements.

| Image | Record order after the native text key |
| --- | --- |
| Enum | Object incarnation; type OID; array OID; array name; schema and local name; owner identity; label count; optional ordered USAGE ACL; counted labels |
| Shell | Object incarnation; type OID; schema and local name; owner identity; optional ordered USAGE ACL |
| Foreign server | Definition name; wrapper name; server OID; incarnation; owner; optional server type; optional version; optional wrapper OID/incarnation; option count; optional declaration-order count; ordered option name/value pairs; declaration-order names |
| Graph OIDs | Namespace OID; label-sequence OID; label count; ordered label records |

USAGE ACLs use the existing object ACL representation: optional grantee identity, grantor identity and grant-option boolean. Each enum label stores its original OID, length-prefixed immutable key bytes, label text and four raw IEEE 754 sort-position bytes. Decoding never allocates new SQL identities or renumbers enum positions. Type keys must match the controlled canonical rendering of their separate schema/name fields; temporary type namespaces are rejected. Import validates unique type incarnations and all enum/shell OID claims within their respective images, legal names, label lengths, finite sort positions, unique label text and strictly increasing label keys and positions. It preserves the distinction between default and explicitly empty ACLs, repeated grant paths, displaced array names and signed-zero bits. Cross-catalog owner and ACL references remain the full restore validator's responsibility.

A server's option map is encoded in strict native key order; its separately counted declaration-order vector preserves the original order. A present order vector must name every stored option exactly once. Absent order metadata and absent wrapper references remain absent, preserving native legacy records without silently migrating them. Empty strings remain distinct from absent type/version values. Server OID and incarnation claims must be unique within the image; the exact owner and wrapper identities are retained for later cross-catalog validation. No foreign handler, validator or remote source is invoked.

Each graph label record contains the u32 label key, sequence OID, relation OID and optional row/array/rule OIDs, ID and properties default OIDs, TOAST table/index OIDs, optional primary-key index/constraint pair, optional endpoint-index pair, trigger OID and counted ordered NOT NULL column-name/OID pairs. Import validates nonzero OID claims, uniqueness across the graph image, nonzero and strictly ordered label keys and strictly ordered column keys. Optional legacy companion records remain optional. Names are not used to regenerate catalog addresses; correspondence to graph data, schemas and complete database-wide OID occupancy still requires enclosing restore validation.

The shared reader retains borrowed definition, ACL, label, option and column iterators and at most 24 field descriptors. Column traversal is shared between system authority and graph metadata, so nested maps do not require heap continuations. Construction performs no catalog prewalk or allocation; each read emits at most 16 KiB, with cancellation between fields and at most 4 KiB between payload copies. Every child has nonempty framing, including empty labels and options. Input admits text, compact enum keys, vector capacities, exact owned-map nodes and temporary identity/name-validation maps before construction. Conversion of a vector to a compact enum key admits both buffers until the original vector retires. Decoded payload leases move into native immutable registry entries; failure and cancellation release the unpublished candidate. The native allowance excludes shared-pointer reference-count bookkeeping, consistently with the existing map contract.

### Metadata image preservation argument

Let C = (E, H, F, G) be the finite durable enum, shell, foreign-server and graph-OID registries satisfying the local validation rules above and the existing SQL owners' cross-catalog rules. Treat an enum sort position as its finite IEEE 754 bit pattern, an enum key as its immutable byte sequence and every ACL or declaration-order vector as an ordered sequence with its original multiplicity. Let W write the specified complete records and R read them. Fixed-width encodings, byte counts and presence tags are injective; UTF-8 validation preserves text, and direct `to_bits`/`from_bits` conversion preserves every finite sort-position pattern, including signed zero. Consequently R(W(v)) = v for every scalar, text, key, optional field and ACL path.

Induction over each counted vector preserves its element sequence. Induction over strictly ordered nested maps preserves their keys and corresponding values, without overwriting or reconstructing identities. Applying those inductions to enum labels, server options/declaration order and graph labels/columns, then to the enclosing registry entries, proves R(W(C)) = C. Native temporary roots are excluded before W selects its input. Header/count validation and rejection of trailing bytes ensure that successful decoding consumes exactly one image. Cross-catalog validity is a premise of this identity, not a claim that isolated component decoding establishes database closure.

For streaming, maintain the invariant that emitted bytes are a prefix of W(C), and the current scalar/text offset plus native successor iterators identify its exact remaining suffix. Copying a field fragment advances only that offset; advancing an ACL, label, option, column or top-level entry installs the next specified record. Each transition preserves the invariant, so every successful window schedule emits the same complete W(C). Nonempty framing prevents hidden traversal of arbitrarily many empty children within a fixed output window. Errors retire the cursor; native mutations operate on separate generations, leaving the captured root unchanged.

Since decoding retains enum comparison keys and sort observations, shell identity/authority, foreign definition bindings/options and graph catalog addresses, every existing operation that observes these values has the same inputs after decoding. Its result and compositions with existing relational, graph and authority operations are unchanged. The codecs introduce no query operator, scoring change, numerical approximation or optimizer rewrite. Their leases follow the decoded payloads and shared roots, so clone, capture, live removal and source closure preserve both bytes and admission until the last owner retires. Repository full/incremental composition applies the existing authenticated-image argument to these unchanged component bytes; public memory restore still requires complete assembly and logical activation.


## Native composite and range images

SQL encodes standalone composite definitions as `UQACMP01` and declared range definitions as `UQARNG01` through the existing captured-type interface. The eight-byte magic, u64 count, strict text-key order, UTF-8 framing, little-endian scalars and optional object ACL representation are shared with the other native metadata images. These are component images: they preserve existing definitions without executing SQL, resolving types again, migrating legacy fields or invoking range support routines.

| Image | Record order after the native text key |
| --- | --- |
| Composite | Sixteen-byte incarnation; type OID; relation OID; array OID; array name; schema and local name; owner; attribute count; optional ordered USAGE ACL; counted attributes |
| Range | Sixteen-byte incarnation; type OID; array OID; array name; schema and local name; owner; selected subtype operator-class OID; collation OID; optional canonical routine OID; optional difference routine OID; optional ordered USAGE ACL; native subtype; multirange record |
| Composite attribute | Name; optional written collation; optional selected collation OID; signed i16 attribute number; dropped boolean; native type |
| Multirange | Sixteen-byte incarnation; type OID; array OID; array name; independent schema and local name |

Native type tags are explicit format values in [the type-tag definition](../../crates/uqa-sql/src/catalog/image/column_types.rs), independent of Rust enum layout. A node contains one u8 tag and that variant's original fields. Numeric precision and signed scale, bit and character modifiers, temporal precision and interval field masks, tensor/vector dimensions, range family/alignment and user-type schema/name/OID companions are retained exactly. Options use separate zero/one presence bytes, preserving absence, zero and empty values where the native type permits them. Range subtype tags 0–5 denote integer, bigint, numeric, date, timestamp and timestamptz; declared range alignment tags 0 and 1 denote integer and double alignment. The interval mask is the native u16 modifier mask. `Array` is followed by its element node; `Domain` contains schema, name, OID, optional array OID and optional collation OID, followed by its base node. `Named` denotes unresolved declarations and is rejected in stored images.

Each type has at most one recursive child. Output retains only a borrowed pointer to the next node; input installs admitted native boxes iteratively. Neither operation needs a recursive codec or a separate traversal stack. The shared reader retains its original 24-field limit, 16 KiB output ceiling and 4 KiB payload-copy windows. Every type node contributes a tag byte, preventing invisible traversal of an unbounded chain in one bounded output window. Attribute, ACL and type continuations share the existing terminal failure/cancellation lifecycle.

The native `ColumnType` owner copies and retires unary chains iteratively. Ordinary and admitted copies use the same node constructors; each constructed box and name buffer is admitted before allocation, and partial output retires before its accumulated lease. Retirement detaches the next child into the loop and leaves a payload-free leaf in its original box, so dropping the current node frees only its own names and empty child box. It requires constant stack and no scratch allocation on success, rejected input, exhausted admission or cancellation. Array modifier removal uses the same bounded ownership path and preserves the original domain boundary. Borrowed built-in signatures retain static scalar references.

Private reconstruction admits strings, vector capacities, boxed type nodes, registry nodes and temporary uniqueness maps before allocation. Composite import preserves dropped attribute records and their non-reusable numbers; it rejects nonpositive or nonascending numbers and duplicate live names. Original written and selected collations remain distinct, including legacy absence. Range import preserves the multirange's independent names and all four allocated type OIDs, rejects duplicate names/OIDs/incarnations and invalid supplied support identifiers, and reuses the range owner's resolved-subtype predicate. That predicate walks domain nodes and consecutive array runs iteratively, visiting each node once. Array OID projection walks to the first non-array element without recursion; domain OIDs and their recorded or legacy-derived array identities keep their original interpretation. It does not derive an operator class, canonical routine, alignment or array name from current catalog contents. Complete cross-catalog type references, role/ACL ownership, support signatures and database-wide OID closure still belong to production restore validation before activation.

### Structured type image preservation argument

Let T be the finite native stored-type carrier excluding unresolved `Named`, and let C and R be valid durable composite and range registries satisfying their owning catalog rules. Define E by the explicit framing above and D by complete private decoding. Fixed-width scalar bytes, tagged options and length-prefixed text are injective; their decoders preserve every represented field. For a leaf type, D(E(t)) equals t field by field. If the identity holds for t, an array's tag and boxed child reproduce `Array(t)`; a domain's scalar/name fields and the same child identity reproduce its complete original definition. Induction over unary nesting proves the identity for every finite t in T. This is stronger than native reference equality, which may compare only an OID: names, companion OIDs, modifiers and alignment are also preserved.

For ownership, a type chain is a finite sequence of unary node headers followed by one leaf. After copying k headers, the destination contains precisely those original headers and a payload-free placeholder for the suffix; the accumulated lease covers exactly their allocated names and boxes. Copying one admitted header preserves this invariant, and installing the leaf establishes fieldwise equality without traversal storage. During retirement the detached suffix and current node partition the original allocations: replacing the owned child with `Void` transfers the complete suffix without copying, while destruction releases the current header and emptied box exactly once. Induction on the remaining chain length proves complete retirement with constant stack and zero new allocation. Partial prefixes obey the same argument, including when admission cannot fund another byte.

Induction over counted attributes preserves their original order, dropped flags, numbers, written collations and selected types. The same argument preserves ordered ACL multiplicity, each independently named multirange and the strictly ordered enclosing catalog. Thus D(E(C,R)) = (C,R), with no identifier allocation, type rebinding, domain evaluation or support invocation. Local rejection rules agree with the represented native invariants; complete cross-catalog validity remains a premise supplied by the enclosing restore owner. Successful decoding consumes exactly one complete image; invalid or truncated input never publishes a partial catalog.

For streaming, the reader's current field offset, pending unary child and remaining native iterators identify exactly the suffix of E not yet emitted. A fragment copy advances its field offset; a type or attribute transition selects the next explicitly framed node. Each transition preserves this suffix invariant and therefore every successful window schedule emits the same bytes. Captured immutable roots retain the same input after rollback, replacement and Engine closure. Box and payload reservations accompany the unpublished native value into the registry; clones and captures keep their original allocation owners, while failure, cancellation and drop release unpublished work without changing another generation.

Existing type, attribute, authority and range observations receive identical native inputs after decoding. Their results and compositions with existing SQL operations are therefore unchanged; no query operator, approximation or optimizer rewrite is added. Full/incremental repository composition preserves these exact component bytes through the authenticated-image contract. Complete memory Engine assembly and fresh activation remain required before the public backup/restore feature is complete.

## Native foreign-wrapper images

SQL encodes a captured foreign-wrapper registry as `UQAFWR01`, followed by the u64 entry count and strictly ordered catalog entries. Each entry contains its text key and name, u32 OID, sixteen-byte incarnation, original owner identity, u64 option count, handler, optional validator and declaration-order option pairs. Handler tags 0, 1 and 2 denote absence, a native handler and a bound function; native tags 0, 1 and 2 denote memory, DuckDB and Arrow independently of their availability in the current build. A validator has its own zero/one presence byte. Text, counts, scalar bytes and tagged options use the shared native catalog framing.

A bound callback retains its OID, optional incarnation, original function name, builtin flag, optional selected collation OID and counted argument-type names. Its optional invocation contains four independent vector counts, optional return type, variadic mode and optional parameter index, followed by argument positions, targets, optional source types and parameter types. Variadic tags 0, 1 and 2 mean none, expanded and explicit. Value tags 0, 1 and 2 mean absence, composite-field selection and assignment. Composite fields retain their type OID, attribute number, optional collation, dropped flag, changed-type presence, result type and optional changed type through the stored-type encoding above. Assignments encode the u64 path-step count, the u64 value-argument index and then that many ordered steps. Step tag 0 denotes a field, followed by its u32 type OID and i16 attribute number. Step tag 1 denotes subscripts, followed by the u64 first-argument index, u64 dimension count, Boolean slice flag and two original omission-mask bytes in lower-then-upper order, including unused bits. Empty and absent invocation fields remain distinct. Callback dispatch and resolution errors are absent under the native wrapper invariant, which both ordinary validation and image input/output enforce through the same predicate.

The reader retains only borrowed callback/option positions and the shared fixed-size field array. Every vector item emits a fixed header or scalar even when its text is empty, and each transition chain without output has fixed length. Together with the existing 16 KiB read ceiling and 4 KiB copy windows, this bounds each read independently of catalog, option or invocation size. No handler, validator or function resolver is invoked. Input admits native vector capacities, strings, boxed contracts, boxed types, registry nodes and temporary uniqueness maps before allocation. Cancellation, invalid tags, duplicate options, malformed native identities, truncation or trailing input discard the private candidate; output failure enters the shared cursor's terminal failed state. Cross-catalog owner and live callback identity checks remain part of complete restore validation, which must also preserve valid references to callbacks that no longer exist.

Let W be the finite native wrapper registry satisfying these local invariants, with resolved stored types in any value binding. Define E as the explicit encoding above and D as complete private reconstruction. Fixed scalar bytes, length-prefixed text and tagged options are injective. The stored-type identity already established above preserves each composite-field type. Induction over each counted invocation vector and assignment path preserves every element and its order; the independent counts preserve legacy empty vectors without deriving one from another. Exhaustive handler, value and path tags then preserve every callback and wrapper field. Induction over the ordered registry yields D(E(W)) = W, including original OIDs/incarnations, missing callbacks, option order and build-independent native selections. No name rebinding, callback execution or migration changes the decoded state.

Partitioning E(W) across reader calls preserves its concatenation because the cursor advances only after the current field is exhausted. Memory admission remains attached to each native root through clones and captures, and failed input cannot publish a partial root. Existing wrapper lookup, authorization and invocation therefore receive identical native inputs after complete decoding and preserve their previous observations and compositions. Full/incremental repository encoding preserves these component bytes under its existing authentication assumptions. Native table reconstruction, logical closure and fresh Engine activation remain separate unfinished requirements.

## Immutable table metadata generations

SQL owns `TableMetadata<T>` for table field names, foreign keys, key constraints, optional row-array names and dropped-attribute metadata. Its sealed payload set contains only native owned strings, vectors and inline identities, flags and physical properties. Columns, CHECK expressions and partition expressions retain their distinct definition producers; an execution-owned retained value cannot enter this metadata owner through a new downstream trait implementation.

For a generation with native value `v`, define `P(v)` as its root layout plus every owned vector capacity multiplied by its element layout and every owned string capacity. Optional values contribute the payload of their present member; inline identities and flags are included in their containing layout. The exhaustive SQL visitors compute `P` at publication without allocation. Reference-count and allocator bookkeeping are excluded consistently with the surrounding payload contract. Independent capture reserves exactly `P(v)` and clones only the immutable root, so its work and allocation are constant in the metadata population while its allowance remains proportional to the complete retained payload.

A writer holds the owning catalog lock, invalidates the cached footprint before mutable access and detaches a shared root before modifying it. On normal completion or unwinding, the SQL guard recomputes the actual destination capacities before releasing that lock. A forgotten borrowed guard leaves no usable footprint, so it cannot authorize a later capture with a stale allowance. A write therefore preserves all preceding immutable roots, and a transaction restore reinstates both the original value and its original footprint. Source snapshots and session adapters carry that native generation without recomputing it at the backup boundary.

The observable projection is the native value itself: capture, independent admission, source replacement and retirement do not change names, declaration order, constraint identities, enforcement flags or dropped-slot properties. Existing SQL consumers borrow the same native definitions and existing image readers observe those values unchanged. This adds no algebraic operation and preserves every composition that observes this metadata. Complete restore validation and public activation remain separate unfinished requirements.

### Imported metadata ownership

Strict foreign-key, key-constraint and dropped-attribute decoders already return a native value `v` with its complete payload reservation `r`. `TableMetadata::read_image` transfers both into one shared owner, admits only the fixed shared header `h`, and caches `B = r + h`. A `SharedTableMetadata` snapshot retains that complete owner, including when it passes through Execution's catalog or constraint snapshot and outlives its Engine adapter. No bare payload reference can outlive its admitted owner. Imported capture reserves `B` independently and clones only the shared handle; it neither visits nor copies the population. Ordinary native publication continues to use `P(v)` as defined above.

Common Storage reconstructs field names directly into one admitted native `Vec<String>`; each decoded string moves into that buffer together with its original payload lease. The final owner combines the vector-capacity reservation with those payload reservations, so its retained allowance is exactly `capacity(vector) * size_of(String) + sum(capacity(string))`. The optional array-type name similarly retains one admitted `Option<String>`, preserving absent, empty and nonempty values. SQL's `TableMetadata::from_admitted` adopts either native value through the same shared-owner boundary as constraint images. Moving the vector and strings preserves their allocation addresses and encoded order; no conversion allocates or copies a second string population. Buffer and payload leases remain live until their corresponding values retire, including rejected input and unwinding. The existing table-properties wire representation is unchanged, so its inverse argument and every metadata observation remain valid after this ownership transfer.

The existing strict image inverse establishes equality of the decoded value with the encoded native metadata. Adoption moves that value without modification; sharing, independent capture and retirement preserve its identity and fields. The final reference destroys the payload before releasing `r + h`. Input failure, cancellation and denied header admission discard the unpublished value, so none can expose a partial generation. Thus the allocation owner changes no SQL or image observation, and composition with existing metadata consumers remains unchanged.

Mutation of an imported generation first constructs an ordinary native copy while the original owner remains alive. The existing guard then exposes only that detached copy for modification and recomputes `P` on normal completion or unwinding. Every earlier snapshot retains the unchanged imported value and `r + h` until its own final release. If detachment does not complete, the original owner and its imported footprint remain selected. A forgotten guard retains the existing invalid-footprint behavior. This is the same generation substitution as ordinary copy-on-write publication; imported allocation lifetime cannot be shortened by parent replacement, rollback or catalog-snapshot destruction.


## Private record source ownership

A private record owner keeps the selected source map as an immutable root. Its record map already retains the allowance used to construct every source entry, so the source root does not retain a second mutable allowance handle. A source mutation restores that root into a stack-local writable map under the original record allowance and publishes the successor only with its selecting record batch. Restoring the root neither allocates nor changes its entries; immutable nodes retain their original reservations. Forks, command snapshots and savepoints share the same source roots, and rollback restores the selected root directly.

Let R be the evaluated private records and S their retained source associations. The representation change replaces only the duplicate allowance handle: R, S, entry identities, allocation leases and selection order remain equal. A successful mutation publishes the same pair of successor roots; a rejected or cancelled mutation publishes neither. Removing or replacing a selecting record still removes its live source association while prior views retain their selected association. Thus read results, failure atomicity, undo and final source retirement are preserved. Cached record-payload admission remains complete. Only writable and undo roots retain its update counter; read-only snapshots retain the same value root and allowance without that unused counter. Undo roots retain the cached footprint with their immutable value root, sharing the writable owner's allowance instead of retaining another handle; restoration installs the root and its footprint together without allocation. The private owner, undo root and read-only snapshot retain their preceding allocation sizes. Spill thresholds, memory limits and source capture semantics are unchanged.

## Immutable table expression generations

SQL owns `TableDefinition<T>` for native column declarations, table CHECKs and inheritance/partition definitions. Publication uses the existing syntax constructors to build an independent immutable generation. An evaluated retained literal is copied through its original value source without executing SQL, then retained as a resident value with its type, declaration, collation and constructor lease. Engine owns only the catalog lock, source identity, transaction snapshot and restoration adapters. Execution retains DDL ordering and the original transaction boundary for persistence and publication errors.

Let `v` be a definition eligible for a committed table under the existing SQL validators, and let `C(v)` be its independent constructor output. Eligibility excludes subqueries, window expressions and execution row sources in stored column, CHECK and partition expressions. The row-type declaration path still permits provisional syntax before those validators run; its enclosing definition transaction and committed-cut selection prevent that provisional state from becoming a committed backup source. This ownership change neither validates a default early nor evaluates its expression. For an already evaluated literal, the source contract supplies the same value representation on every successful read; construction preserves that representation and its metadata while detaching its query source. Structural induction over the existing exhaustive syntax constructors therefore gives `observe(C(v)) = observe(v)` for each eligible definition.

The native owner retains its root constructor reservation and every independently admitted literal child. Let `B(v)` be the root reservation, including the shared root layout, plus those retained child reservations. Construction calculates this sum with checked arithmetic before publishing the generation. Independent capture reserves `B(v)` and shares the immutable owner without traversing syntax, reading a literal source or allocating a copy. A read-only consumer retains the complete native owner; converting a separate external immutable definition into a shared consumer does not make it a native backup source. Native query retention uses the same cached complete footprint and preserves the original constructor leases.

A publication guard exposes the current value only through a shared borrow. Candidate editing occurs on a separate value; construction must succeed before the guard replaces its generation. Source failure, denied admission, cancellation or unwinding before that replacement leaves the original generation and footprint unchanged. Successful replacement changes only the current root; preceding snapshots retain the exact original root. Existing transaction rollback restores that root and its admission metadata together. Consequently a committed cut selecting the original transaction generation observes the same definitions before private DDL, after rollback and after retirement of the source Engine. Independent cut cancellation and final release retire only their own reservations.

These transformations add no algebraic operator. Every existing consumer of column values, constraint identities, expression bindings, partition bounds or image bytes receives the same eligible native value. Composition with existing query evaluation, constraint checking, partition routing and image encoding is therefore preserved by substituting an observationally equal immutable input. Failure of candidate construction reaches the existing owning transaction boundary, which restores any earlier persisted candidate state. Whole-memory inventory, logical image closure, fresh Engine activation and public restore remain separate requirements.


### Native table image adoption

The SQL table-definition image constructors compose the existing strict column, CHECK and hierarchy decoders with the native immutable owner. Let a successful decoder return `(v, r, l)`, where `v` is its reconstructed definition, `r` owns all root payload capacities and `l` is the sum of independently retained scalar constructor reservations. The hierarchy decoder now exposes the same accumulated child count as the existing column and CHECK decoders; this changes no field or wire interpretation. Adoption moves `v` and `r` into the shared native allocation, reserves only its fixed header `h`, and caches `B = r + h + l` with checked addition. It neither traverses nor copies `v`, and all scalar owners retain their original leases. Thus the existing image inverse gives `encode(adopt(decode(x))) = x` for every valid canonical image `x`; every native definition observer receives the identical reconstructed value. No SQL evaluation, name resolution, constraint check or partition routing is invoked during this move.

After adoption, cloning and shared query consumers retain the complete owner, while independent capture admits exactly `B` without visiting the definition. Cancellation, truncated or malformed input, failed header admission and overflow drop the unpublished value and its children before returning their reservations. Since neither decoding nor adoption changes a live catalog, failure cannot publish a partial definition. Relation-wide identities, foreign references and catalog closure remain the enclosing restore boundary's requirements; these constructors provide owned inputs for that boundary rather than an activation certificate. The same substitution argument above preserves expression, constraint and partition observations after successful validation and installation.

## Writable vector registration ownership

A decoded table registration set is a finite partial map R from exact field-name strings to evaluated native vector owners. A successful import preserves each byte of the name and the existing native index; neither decoding nor registration trains an index or reevaluates stored vectors. The caller supplies each index from its owning image decoder and a fully admitted field string from the same construction allowance. The consuming builder rejects repeated names, foreign or incomplete name reservations, cancellation and allocation failure before returning a successor. These assumptions are additional to later complete table and catalog validation; this owner does not certify whole-Engine activation.

Core's `OwnedMap::insert_admitted_key` reserves its exact native node before allocation and transfers the original key reservation into that node. For an absent key, ordered insertion preserves the old mapping and adds exactly the new pair. For an existing key, ordinary and admitted replacement keep the original key and node, change only its value and discard the unused incoming key. Lookup, exact-size iteration, mutable value traversal, deletion and node extraction use the same ordered map operations. Induction over these operations therefore gives the same finite map observations as the original ordered collection. Mutable traversal borrows disjoint value fields while exposing immutable keys; it cannot change ordering or node ownership. No new algebraic operator or approximate comparison is introduced.

Storage first registers the unchanged vector owner in the existing capture domain, prepares immutable field/source membership, and then adopts the field into the native map. The unpublished builder owns every provisional allocation. On any error it is consumed, so neither a prefix nor mismatched membership is returned. On success, the native map and source membership preserve their own original leases without retaining the input cancellation signal. Later successful mutation uses the existing source-publication boundary; retained cuts keep the preceding source identities. Dropping a key's native node destroys its key before releasing the combined key/node reservation; detached node transfer preserves both. An index returned from replacement owns its vector reservations independently of the removed registration. Thus registration lookup and captured-image observations remain unchanged through subsequent writes, source retirement and final reader release.

Registration uses `into_memory_capture_with_control` before exposing a native membership entry. Exact, HNSW and DiskANN owners reserve their exclusive adapter before allocating it and register the original boxed index through the admitted source transition. IVF registers its original mutable data through that same transition without an exclusive adapter, preserving query-triggered training at its owning boundary. Let P be the original decoded allocation, H the newly registered source and W the optional exclusive adapter. Successful construction admits H + W in addition to P; rejection drops all consumed private owners. Repeating registration in the same domain preserves the original H and identity without another allocation, while a different domain fails. Erasing these reservation carriers yields the unchanged registered vector and therefore the same exact bytes, search results and future mutation behavior.

IVF retains the original decoded reservation inside its mutable data, after all payload fields in destruction order. A promoted source reader owns that data even if the outer index or named collection has retired; consequently P and H remain admitted until that reader retires. Exact, HNSW and DiskANN source cells similarly retain their original boxed payload owners; W can retire independently because its allocation no longer exists. Frozen images use their separately admitted copied values and can outlive every mutable source reader. Thus every surviving payload has its original reservation, and releasing an adapter cannot release a still-readable native allocation. These transitions change neither vector normalization, centroid membership, training state, graph topology nor scoring arithmetic.

## Native table authority ownership

The native authority value A consists of the exact owner incarnation, optional ordered relation ACL, ordered column-to-ACL map, optional relation tuple revision and ordered column-to-revision map. These components are the domain already encoded by `BoundTableSecurity::image_reader` and reconstructed by its existing native decoder. The new adoption boundary does not change the codec or bind role names. It places the admitted native A in SQL's existing immutable `TableMetadata` owner. A shared catalog or privilege reader holds the same `SharedTableMetadata` generation, so all role, grantor, privilege, grant-option and revision observations equal those of A. Absence, empty ACLs and column ordering retain their existing distinctions. Cross-catalog role validation and complete Engine activation remain separate obligations.

The native decoder retains some allocations in the outer `Budgeted` value and ordered-map nodes in their own leases. Therefore the complete captured footprint is at least the native value header, ACL vector capacities, column and revision map nodes, their key capacities, and the shared reservation header. Adoption caches the maximum of that complete physical footprint and the existing outer reserved bytes. Ordinary source capture consults this cached total and shares the original generation without walking or copying its ACL population. Each independently retained source has its own capture admission, while original nested node and payload reservations remain with the shared native value. This accounts for nested ownership without releasing it or counting only the outer lease.

Mutation uses the existing metadata write owner: it detaches the selected generation, changes only the new native A, and publishes the resulting footprint before releasing the enclosing state guard, including unwinding. Retained readers continue to observe the old A and hold all of its original allocations. Engine session snapshots and transaction undo retain the selected metadata generation, so rollback restores the same authority and tuple revisions. Existing grant/revoke algorithms, authorization precedence and SQL outcomes are unchanged. Induction over successful publication, rollback and reader retirement preserves both value observations and allocation lifetimes; a failed or cancelled adoption exposes no partial generation. Once the final reader of an admitted generation retires, its payload is destroyed before its reservations are released.


## Writable evaluated-index input

Let an evaluated index image denote `I = (f, P, M)`, where `f` is its original field name, `P` is the carried/temporal/row/comparison policy and `M` is a finite partial function from full-width document identities to evaluated Core values, including their original physical representations. Equality of images requires the same name, policy and value-image bytes at every identity; a missing identity differs from an identity whose stored value is SQL NULL. Historical policy may be more conservative than the current population because removal does not reverse earlier eligibility observations. This construction introduces no new query operator.

`ValueIndexImageBuilder` consumes the declared metadata and each admitted value in strictly increasing identity order. Strict increase proves uniqueness, and completion compares the number accepted with the declared count; therefore successful completion has exactly the supplied domain. Each value moves into a Core retained entry without reevaluation or payload replacement. Decoded field ownership moves into the existing immutable SQL metadata carrier. For a search key, the policy is copied exactly after checking that its flags cover the surviving values' required comparison domain. These checks permit historical conservatism but reject an image that would falsely select an ineligible accelerator. A carried column may retain unobserved flags because ordinary conversion from a search key clears them; it performs no predicate lookup, and ordinary conversion back to a search key classifies its retained values. Its original carried policy is therefore preserved without imposing search-key requirements. Thus the completed cache has precisely `f`, `P` and `M`, including absent/NULL distinctions, floating-point bits and preserved expression results.

For a search key, Common Storage constructs a physical ordered relation `B`: each non-NULL `M(d)` contributes `d` to the bucket for its Core ordering equivalence class, while the separate ordered set `N` contains exactly the identities whose value is NULL. Key copying preserves the Core value and its ordering relation. Increasing input identities make each bucket and `N` strictly ordered, and distinct input identities prevent duplicate contributions. Consequently `B` and `N` equal the structures built by the existing native column index over `M`, regardless of tree shape. A carried index has no search relation and retains only its evaluated values and original policy.

For any supported predicate, the unchanged policy first chooses the same existing access path. Exact, range and set probes then visit the same Core key classes and identity buckets, and NULL probes use the same `N`; restoring document order at the existing posting boundary yields the same postings and cardinality. A predicate requiring fallible SQL comparison visits `M` in the same increasing identity order, with the same selected catalog and comparison function, so values, error order and cancellation boundaries retain their existing contract. Since every exposed observation is unchanged, any composition of these existing probes, projections and posting consumers has the same observations by substitution; no additional algebraic or optimizer law is assumed.

Completion returns the existing writable cache. Its ordinary replacement and removal operations update the same evaluated map, search relation and NULL identities. Earlier captures retain immutable value entries; replacing a live entry cannot mutate a selected predecessor. Later spill uses the existing retained-index builder and retains the original historical eligibility flags, so changing the physical backing does not select a less conservative execution path. Explicit changes of index use and clearing retain their established native behavior. Input does not replay the defining SQL, rerun stored expressions or train a replacement model.

Every decoded value keeps its original reservation in the retained map entry. Map nodes reserve their layouts before construction, while derived physical keys and identity buffers are admitted by Common Storage before allocation. Finishing private map construction selects only the allowance used for later path allocation: it does not visit or alter a node, payload or cached footprint. Existing entries and shared predecessors therefore retain their original leases until their last owner retires, even when later writes use a different ordinary allowance. Independent capture charges the complete cached node and payload footprint under its own allowance. Imported field, physical-key and NULL owners include their shared headers. Cloned writable physical indexes share admitted key owners, so opaque tuple bytes and row/record descriptors retain their original reservations even when the original index retires. Bucket buffers are independently copied at the existing ordinary mutation boundary; each shared key is immutable, and its final alias retires the complete admitted key payload.

The physical and evaluated builders are distinct unpublished types. Ordinary mutation is available only after completion; a failed consuming addition, failed completion, cancellation or unwinding drops the entire partial candidate. An owning value is destroyed before its reservation is released, so neither rejected input nor a surviving alias can expose unadmitted original payloads. Completed owners retain no input cancellation token. `MemoryValueIndexesBuilder` additionally adopts each original name into the writable membership map and publishes the existing independently identified capture child; duplicate, foreign or failed registration drops the whole unpublished set.
