Native encoding of that result is unchanged, so established value observation and composition laws remain those of the original image correspondence; no SQL evaluation, catalog rebinding or numeric approximation is introduced.

For a call with byte limit B and poll limit S, every source read requests at most the minimum of the remaining B, the pending field fragment and 4,096. The driver subtracts the actual returned count before another read. Thus the sum of source bytes is at most B. Each iteration performs one poll and at most one read attempt; an interrupted read also consumes that iteration, so neither repeated interruptions nor metadata checkpoints bypass S. UTF-8 input retains at most three unfinished bytes between fixed fragments, appending only validated text to its original admitted string. These are byte, read-attempt and continuation-poll bounds, not a wall-clock bound on arbitrary codec computation. Array-shape validation, decimal construction and some descriptor conversions still use their existing owning operations after input; scheduling those operations and integrating catalog/component readers remain separate unfinished requirements.

Construction reserves the complete future layout and shared channel before invoking the codec constructor. Pending fields and container buffers reserve their native allocations before construction and keep those original leases across every yield. An advance takes exclusive ownership of the active state before any fallible operation; error, cancellation, zero limits or unwinding destroys that state instead of restoring it. The pinned future is destroyed before its reservation and the final channel owner. Completed output retains its native payload reservations independently of the retired input and cancellation signal. The synchronous driver uses an inline channel and pinned stack future, so it introduces no heap allocation for fixed scalar values. These ownership properties preserve the original failure and retained-output contract without publishing an incomplete value.


Nested codecs use `AsyncImageRead` and `decode_image_from` to lend their owning transport while retaining a child channel and continuation in the enclosing future. Before each child poll, the owner consumes a checkpoint. A requested child prefix is supplied by the same ordered outer transport; checkpoint-only work and interrupted reads therefore cannot bypass the outer poll ceiling. The outer reader remains the only source reader. Applying the continuation invariant inductively at each nesting level preserves the complete byte sequence and original child result without retaining a whole child image. The child future and fixed buffers are included in the original enclosing future layout and retire with that owner. Framing, catalog grammar and Engine integration remain with their respective consumers.

Core JSON scanning and string decoding retain their original byte ranges, nesting stack, lexical position and partial decoded UTF-8 output. A token transition either advances to the next grammar state or reports the original error; checkpoints leave that state unchanged. String decoding appends the next exact decoded fragment, so induction on the retained input position gives the same string, native capacity and diagnostic order as the synchronous decoder. Relation rendering applies the same argument independently to each quoted component and separator. Native value copying retains its explicit parent stack and original capacities, reuses the same immutable descriptors and copies each owned field exactly once. Erasing checkpoints from any successful trace therefore yields the original native value and byte observations. No scalar evaluation or rebinding occurs.

Retained payload accounting exposes one original native field or parent-stack transition per iterator step, including zero-byte transitions. Summing a completed traversal gives the same checked sum of original capacities as the ordinary visitor; sharing and opaque carriers retain their existing accounting. A suspended owned transformation keeps the input reservation until the input value and partial output retire or transfer to the completed result. Failure, cancellation, invalid windows, unwinding and abandonment cannot return an allowance while its native allocation survives. Existing atomic allocator, container, decimal and scalar-dependency operations are explicit boundaries; the byte/poll protocol does not imply a wall-clock guarantee.


## Native dictionary input continuations

### Native dictionary frame input

Analysis's shared `morphology::frame::FrameInput` retains the section directory, selected encoded offset, native decoded buffers, incremental SHA-256 state and the existing public zlib inflater. An advance supplies at most 4 KiB of encoded input and 4 KiB of output space; plain sections copy and hash at most 4 KiB. The inflater keeps its own fixed scratch and may decode into that scratch before returning output, so these limits describe the supplied and consumed slices rather than an invented count of internal decompression operations. Directory validation still precedes section allocation and preserves the existing format, encoded/decoded limits, semantic identity, order and contiguous-range checks. Native vectors reserve their declared decoded capacity once without first initializing the whole payload.

For a valid encoded section S with decoded bytes D, let p be the consumed encoded prefix and q the produced decoded prefix. Initially p = q = 0, the digest is SHA-256's initial state, and the retained inflater is in its initial zlib state. Each advance preserves that inflater and passes exactly the next encoded interval; the public streaming inflater yields exactly the next interval of D. Plain sections use the corresponding direct byte interval. Appending and hashing only the returned bytes therefore preserves `buffer = D[0..q]` and the digest state for that same prefix. Induction over advances, together with the streaming inflater's contract, gives exactly D at completion. Completion requires zlib end, exact stored and decoded lengths and the recorded section digest; truncated streams, excess output, trailing encoded data and digest mismatches cannot publish a section. The unchanged directory identity and record counts consequently preserve the complete native frame representation used by both language validators.

The continuation consumes itself on every advance and owns all completed and partial sections until the whole frame succeeds. Errors or abandonment release that entire private state, including previously completed sections and inflater scratch; success transfers each original vector without copying it. The ordinary Nori and Kuromoji loaders drain the same continuation, preserving their model inputs and dictionary identities. Descriptor preparation and integration with the enclosing analyzer image remain separate requirements; frame completion alone does not establish bounded resolved-analyzer restoration.

### Native dictionary table input

Nori and Kuromoji share ordinary and retained frame/table construction through Analysis's private `morphology::work` interface. The ordinary caller drains the same native future with non-suspending work; a retained caller uses its original `AsyncImageInput` channel. Cost cells, word and Korean morpheme records, node/arc decoding, suffix-rank derivation, surface ranges, character rows and Unicode intervals retain their native positions and partially built vectors. Fixed record loops checkpoint at least every 128 records. Lexical validation also retains its ordered arc checks, longest accepted lengths, UTF-16 traversal stack and visited masks. Scratch initialization and final reachability scans use at most 4 KiB per checkpoint interval; each traversal position checkpoints before processing its arcs. The original decoded capacities are reserved once, and each selected section remains owned until its declared record count and strict EOF have been checked.

For each native record array, let j be the next record and P the already constructed prefix. The transition reads the same fixed-width fields, applies the same ordered predicates and appends the same native record as ordinary decoding; a checkpoint changes neither j, P nor the reader offset. Induction on j therefore preserves the complete array and the first structural error. Lexical suffix counts and rank outputs use the same checked additions in the same arc order. Length validation and UTF-16 traversal preserve their original node order, stack order, state masks and surrogate transitions; splitting zero initialization and the final zero search changes no visited value or acceptance predicate. Directory counts and EOF remain after each native validator, and dictionary count checks remain after complete assembly. Consequently the resumed fixed tables, lexical lookups, morphology references, costs and pinned Unicode observations equal ordinary reconstruction, with no scoring or numeric approximation.

All partial table and section owners stay inside the single private constructor. Dropping that future, a malformed table or a control error retires the complete candidate before the input continuation's allowance; no partial dictionary enters a live cache. Successful output retains its original native vectors in the returned immutable model. Encoded buffers and input scratch retain their existing owners, while decoded models remain subject to the separate Analysis representation limits. The string continuation below supplies pooled-string validation/copying. The following provenance constructors supply canonical JSON and structural validation; descriptor preparation is integrated into retained resolved-image reconstruction, while enclosing Storage analyzer-image integration remains open.

### Native dictionary string input

Native dictionary string pools and Japanese completion keys use the same retained string constructor as ordinary loading. Each field retains its original encoded slice, validation offset, accumulated UTF-16 count and copy offset; validation/counting and copying visit at most 4 KiB of supplied UTF-8 per fragment. An incomplete scalar at a fragment boundary stays in the original slice until the next fragment. Complete UTF-8 validation precedes the exact UTF-16 length limit, and that limit precedes the original exact-capacity string reservation. Pool population retains progress between groups of 128 records, including empty strings. Analysis owns these decoded capacities under its existing representation limits; the archive channel does not claim to charge them as encoded input scratch.

For a valid field B, the fragment boundaries partition B at scalar boundaries. Concatenation of the validated fragments therefore equals B, and additivity of UTF-16 width over complete scalars makes the retained count equal the ordinary whole-field count. Copying the same partition into the once-reserved output preserves bytes, capacity and native field order. A checkpoint changes none of the encoded slice, offsets, count or partially copied string. For invalid input, the first invalid scalar remains the first rejected scalar; its absolute byte position equals the validated-prefix offset plus the native fragment error's position. Retained `Utf8Offset` errors keep that prefix and the standard fragment error without rescanning earlier bytes. Ordinary callers retain the existing `Utf8` variant and standard whole-field error; only that non-suspending error adapter reconstructs the standard error from the original field. Both paths preserve the complete diagnostic, invalid width and UTF-8-before-length-limit ordering. Failure or abandonment drops partial strings, completed pool entries and the enclosing private dictionary together; no partial native resource is published. Descriptor preparation is integrated into retained resolved-image reconstruction; enclosing Storage image integration remains unfinished.

### Retained lexical construction and Japanese completion

Analysis owns retained UTF-16 ordering, completion-key conversion and minimal lexical graph construction. The ordinary constructor and retained input run the same loops. Stop words, stop tags and all completion keys are validated in their original order before per-mapping alternatives and construction; the native length and UTF-16 graph predicates remain after complete construction. Duplicate alternatives remain valid. Prefix comparisons, key conversion, state hashing, exact signature comparisons and arc publication checkpoint between at most 128 records; each pending suffix and collision candidate also checkpoints. Native allocation, hash-table capacity changes and destruction retain their existing atomic costs.

Let a state signature be its terminal bit and its ordered list of `(label, target)` pairs. The registry maps a randomized signature hash to a chain of previously interned native nodes. Hash equality only selects that chain; reuse requires equality of the entire signature, including every target. Thus collisions can change comparison work but cannot merge unequal states. Equal signatures receive equal hashes within the same builder. Induction over the unchanged suffix-minimization order therefore produces the same first-seen node identities, ordered native arcs and root as exact signature interning. Original checked suffix sums give the same rank output for every accepted UTF-16 word. Hash randomization does not affect native record order or persistent bytes.

For adjacent input words, retained comparison visits equal UTF-16 units until the first unequal pair or end, so its common-prefix length and order equal ordinary lexicographic comparison. Appending the same pending suffix and interning in the same child-before-parent order preserves the builder invariant. Fragmented key conversion counts the same scalar widths, reserves the original exact UTF-16 capacity and appends the same units. Checkpoints change no validated prefix, hash accumulator, candidate position or output array. Removing them yields the original ordered preparation trace and diagnostic precedence; its completed lexicon therefore selects the same completion alternatives and preserves all downstream token observations without approximation.

Insertion and finalization consume the builder. Their futures retain the previous key, pending states, registry and native arrays until success returns the successor or completed lexicon. An error or dropped future cannot leave a partially modified reusable builder; it retires the complete candidate. The enclosing analysis constructor likewise owns all stop sets, completion mappings and lexical state until validation succeeds. Encoded image ownership and the existing separate Analysis representation limits remain intact. Descriptor preparation and enclosing analyzer/binding image integration remain separate requirements before complete public restoration.

### Retained provenance canonicalization and structural validation

Analysis retains canonical output, vocabulary copies and structural provenance predicates through the same work channel as native dictionary construction. Object keys are inspected incrementally: an already ordered object lends its native iterator, while unordered keys enter an ordered borrowed-key map. Container traversal is iterative and keeps one next-child cursor per open container. One retained string-encoding buffer is reused across all values and property names in the output. String output serializes complete scalar-boundary fragments of at most 4 KiB with the public JSON serializer and concatenates their escaped interiors between one pair of quotes. A fragment can expand to at most six times its input length. The workspace enables arbitrary-precision JSON numbers; their public `as_str` representation is copied in at most 4 KiB fragments without numeric conversion. No unknown JSON field is removed or projected into a narrower schema.

Let C be the established canonical encoding of a native JSON value. For a string, the serializer escapes each scalar independently, so concatenating the escaped interiors of a scalar-boundary partition equals the escaped interior of the original string. The enclosing quotes therefore give the same C. Number spelling is unchanged by borrowing its public representation. The adjacent-key predicate admits a native iterator exactly when it already has the original Rust string-key order; otherwise ordered-map iteration yields that order. Both paths therefore equal the original explicit sort, including when the dependency's optional insertion-order map is enabled. Reusing encoding scratch changes no emitted fragment. Arrays retain their original child order. Structural induction over the finite JSON tree proves that retained construction yields exactly C, including every unknown value. A checkpoint changes no next child, borrowed key, output prefix or number/string offset. Comparison with the original input keeps the same length and byte-equality predicate after complete canonical construction.

Vocabulary copies retain each original string's exact capacity and bytes; uniqueness and required-script checks keep their original ordering before byte-class validation. Inventory traversal preserves the first duplicate/invalid-size check, digest check and final complete-membership predicate. Digest-pinned image validation selects the same first marker, including markers crossing fragments, and retains empty-prefix/suffix diagnostics. Runtime comparison retains JSON kinds, array order, complete object membership and exact arbitrary-precision number spelling. Induction over its paired child cursors gives the same native JSON equality, including additional runtime fields. These predicates inspect captured metadata without resolving a URL or replacing an identity. Both language loaders and ordinary dictionary tools share these implementations.

Each provenance constructor owns its complete parsed JSON, copied vocabularies and temporary canonical output until all predicates succeed. The dictionary owns its original encoded section through structural validation and the directory-count/EOF check. Failure, cancellation, invalid windows and abandonment retire all partial and completed candidate allocations together. Native ordered-map comparisons, allocation and destruction keep their existing atomic costs. The native syntax constructor below now supplies these predicates directly. Descriptor preparation is integrated into retained resolved-image reconstruction. Public scalar dependency calls retain their documented atomic boundaries; enclosing Storage analyzer/binding image integration remains unfinished.

### Retained provenance syntax construction

Analysis constructs native provenance values directly from Core's borrowed JSON events. The continuation retains its byte position, open array/object frames, each pending object key and the completed root. Core owns the grammar, UTF-8 validation and string escape decoding; the enclosing Analysis scheduler preserves its original cancellation/error boundary. Ordinary loading and retained dictionary images drain this same constructor. The existing manifest byte limit, 127-open-container ceiling and Analysis model limits remain in force; decoded model allocations remain separate from archive-reader admission.

For every previously accepted canonical manifest, primitive events produce the original null, boolean or arbitrary-precision number, while Core's scalar escape decoder produces the original Unicode string. Each completed child appends to the current array or replaces the same object key in its current map. Induction on the event sequence therefore preserves child order, last-key replacement and every complete native value. Closing a container transfers that value into its original parent; only the final grammar EOF returns the root. Suspending before any event, during a token scan or within a string copy preserves the source offset and unfinished parent state, so the final value is independent of window partitioning. Canonical equality, required vocabularies, runtime comparisons, source hashes and section EOF remain the same subsequent predicates, preserving dictionary observations and the analyzer compositions defined above.

Token scans and decoded string copies checkpoint within 4 KiB fragments. The first one-poll call yields before a whole manifest can be materialized; cancellation or abandonment drops the incomplete value stack with the enclosing candidate. The public arbitrary-precision number constructor, native map comparisons and allocation/destruction retain their existing atomic costs. This constructor does not claim a complete work bound for those dependency operations or enable public Engine restoration. Independent JSON reference cases cover exact values, malformed/truncated input and nesting boundaries, while actual Nori/Kuromoji images exercise canonical fields, later structural diagnostics and complete candidate retirement.


### Retained descriptor syntax and wire construction

Analysis's shared `json::Input` builds native values from Core's retained event grammar and string decoder; dictionary provenance and descriptor construction use that same owner. Descriptor admission keeps its original two-pass order: decoded object-key uniqueness over the complete input precedes typed wire construction. The second pass visits source properties in order, retains pipeline values directly and moves decoded profile strings into their original vector. Both object and sequence forms accepted by the original serde structures remain accepted. Canonical output now uses the shared writer below; configuration preparation and scalar/regex dependency parsing retain their separate boundaries. This component does not establish complete bounded descriptor or analyzer restoration.

Let `W` be the original wire tuple consisting of descriptor data and its fingerprint, with descriptor data containing format/revision fields, length policy, the exact native pipeline value and runtime profiles. The first pass's stack contains exactly the decoded key set for each open object. Starting with empty sets, each key either enters its current object's set once or rejects before any typed construction; closing an object retires only its own set. Induction over grammar events therefore establishes the same global uniqueness predicate, including equal escaped and unescaped names, before schema, fingerprint or revision diagnostics.

For the second pass, each native struct owner contains the original source position and a partial map of its admitted fields. Object fields select the declared field by decoded name; sequence fields select the same declaration-order position. Shared native value construction preserves scalar values, object membership and array order; primitive schema conversions use the existing public serde interfaces, including original numeric diagnostics. Required fields and sequence arity complete before the struct can return. By induction on fields and nested values, accepted construction returns exactly `W`; invalid reached fields retain their original ordering, and no absent metadata is synthesized. Fingerprinting, revision checks, configuration validation and executable profile checks consume that same `W`, so the existing accepted canonical bytes, resource identities and observations are unchanged.

A checkpoint preserves the reader, object-key stack, partial value stack, typed fields and profile vector. Erasing checkpoints yields the same sequence of key and field transitions; changing a positive input window therefore changes only suspension points. Cancellation, error or abandonment drops the entire unpublished owner. The tests compare independent serde wire values, object/sequence forms, ordered schema errors and escaped duplicates, and interrupt both syntax passes. The existing descriptor regression additionally rejects first-poll allocation of the complete wire: the original path allocated 1,012,746 bytes before yielding, while the retained construction satisfies its 64 KiB first-window bound. Native allocator, map comparison/destruction and scalar dependency conversion retain their explicit atomic boundaries. The following sections define retained canonical output and configuration reconstruction.

### Retained canonical descriptor output

Analysis's shared JSON writer serializes native values through retained container cursors and scalar-boundary string fragments. Dictionary provenance retains its byte-vector output; descriptor data and complete wire output use a string sink that checks the existing encoded-byte limit before each append. Descriptor fields and runtime profiles are borrowed in canonical key order, and the pipeline is traversed without copying it into another complete JSON tree. Ordinary creation and retained restoration use this same writer. The existing public `RawValue::from_string` constructor and serializer are preserved; its dependency-owned syntax scan remains atomic, so this component makes no work-bound claim for that public dependency call.

Let `C(v)` be the established canonical JSON bytes of native value `v`. The shared writer visits arrays in their original order and objects in the same explicit borrowed-key order used by `C`; each string emits one pair of quotes around concatenated escaped scalar fragments. Escaping is scalar-local, and public arbitrary-precision number spelling is unchanged. Structural induction therefore yields exactly `C(v)` without a cloned intermediate tree. The descriptor writer enumerates exactly the original seven fields in canonical key order, with the same snake-case length-policy tags, decimal integers, nullable three-byte Unicode versions and ordered profile expressions. Substitution of the shared pipeline/string encodings proves equality with the previous canonical serde descriptor bytes. The wire adds the same descriptor and lowercase fingerprint fields, so the original serializer and hash identity remain unchanged.

Restoration computes canonical data and its digest before fingerprint, revision and configuration/profile checks, preserving their validation order. Those later checks borrow the descriptor without mutating it; successful completion can therefore reuse the already-computed bytes and digest instead of cloning, encoding and hashing the same data again. Each bounded append first checks its resulting encoded length, so a successful complete wire obeys the original maximum. Any limit, cancellation, window or dependency failure discards the same unpublished candidate. A checkpoint preserves the borrowed native position and exact output prefix, giving window-independent bytes and original immutable observations.

Independent serde references cover long escaped strings, arbitrary-precision values, both length policies, Unicode-version fields and exact byte limits. The output allocation regression fails on the original whole-tree construction, which allocated 748,900 bytes in one poll, and checks every completed output poll against its fixture's 256 KiB ceiling. Cancellation, invalid windows and abandonment cover early, middle and final native output states. Native allocation/map comparison/destruction, raw-JSON validation and scalar/regex dependency parsing retain their explicit atomic boundaries. Executable and complete language-resource preparation are implemented by [retained resolved analyzer reconstruction](#retained-resolved-analyzer-reconstruction). Enclosing Storage analyzer/field input remains separate unfinished work.

### Retained descriptor identity and runtime profiles

Analysis retains descriptor digest state and native regular-expression/Unicode profile construction through the shared ordinary/retained work interface. Descriptor SHA-256 updates consume at most 4 KiB per explicit byte fragment and retain the existing domain prefix. Regular-expression identity keeps its established version-one preorder: each node contributes its tag and fixed-width metadata, followed by original literal/name bytes or its ordered children. The continuation keeps one next-child iterator for each open concat/alternation, hashes long payloads in 4 KiB fragments and visits class records through counted checkpoints. Immutable Unicode property ranges use a retained counting pass followed by the same ordered encoding. This does not resolve a dictionary or substitute a runtime profile.

Let E(h) be the established byte encoding of a native regular-expression tree h, and H its SHA-256 identity with the unchanged domain prefix. A leaf emits the same tag, metadata and payload; a capture or repetition emits its original metadata before its single child. A concat/alternation's next-child iterator visits exactly the left-to-right order produced by the preceding reverse-push traversal. Structural induction therefore yields the same E(h). Partitioning any payload b into b1 through bn leaves SHA-256 unchanged because successive updates consume exactly their concatenation b. Immutable property iteration gives the same range count and range bytes in both passes. The Unicode-word flag, conditional word-class suffix, legacy Unicode-16 context rule and final field comparison consequently yield the same profile identities and acceptance predicate. No scoring, tokenization or algebraic operation changes.

Ordinary descriptor restore drains this same constructor without suspension. Retained execution owns the parsed descriptor, reconstructed configuration, native regex trees, digest state and partial profile vector until completion. Suspending preserves their next offsets; cancellation, invalid input windows or abandonment drops that candidate through the image owner's terminal transition. The continuation does not reread source bytes. Tests compare explicit independent long/wide native-tree encodings, an established expression digest, complete canonical descriptor bytes/configuration and allocation retirement at multiple actual suspension points. Descriptor syntax/value construction now uses the shared retained input above. Native configuration materialization and validation follow the retained constructors below. Public dependency parser calls remain atomic; this component does not establish complete analyzer or Engine restoration.

## Native analyzer configuration construction

Analysis owns one configuration constructor for the analyzer, tokenizer, character-filter, token-filter and normalization carriers. Its two input adapters preserve their established domains: original images require the producer's ordered fields and admit decoded allocations under the original archive allowance; descriptor values preserve the public JSON deserializer's object and positional forms, defaults, aliases and feature-selected vocabulary. Both yield the same native `Analyzer` fields without compiling stages or resolving any resource. Descriptor materialization retains its existing separate Analysis limits.

Let a constructor state consist of the selected field schema, completed native fields, the next sequence or map position, the current string offset and its retained output, and open structural cursors. Each field selects exactly the existing native constructor. Scalar conversion uses the public dependency visitors; tagged-body admission visits original non-discriminator values before typed fields, preserving numeric representation errors and property order without copying a complete JSON tree. A positional source visits the same declared field order and applies the same missing-field/default and excess-element rules. The image adapter additionally rejects missing required fields before consuming a later payload.

For a string, the next transition appends the next complete UTF-8 fragment of at most 4 KiB. Induction on the byte offset gives the original complete string. For a sequence, induction on the retained entry index gives its original ordered native prefix; for a map, each completed key/value pair enters the same native ordered map. The fixed field schema then constructs the same analyzer variant and exact options as ordinary input. These arguments apply independently to the enabled Nori and Kuromoji variants and to their explicit normalization profiles. Existing deferred validation and source-path ownership are unchanged.

A checkpoint changes no selected field, native prefix or next source position. Erasing checkpoints therefore gives the ordinary construction trace for every successful positive partition of the work allowance. Pending input cannot publish a partial analyzer. Cancellation, invalid windows, malformed input, failed image admission and abandonment drop the partial fields and retained original input together. Original image reservations follow their allocations; descriptor values remain borrowed until their native fields finish. Native allocator, container and public scalar-conversion calls retain their atomic costs. Retained configuration validation and common executable preparation are defined below. Public regex parsing remains atomic. Complete language-resource preparation is integrated into retained resolved-image reconstruction; enclosing Storage analyzer/field input remains open.


## Retained resolved-configuration validation

Analysis validates a restored pipeline through borrowed native configuration views. Each view exposes the same enabled variant, declaration-order fields, scalar representation, optional field omission and ordered container children as the public serializer. The size pass retains the original compact serializer's write boundaries, including each unescaped string span and individual escape, while scanning at most 4 KiB between checkpoints. A completed view therefore has the same encoded length, and the first crossing of the descriptor-byte allowance reports the same required size. Stage and enabled-feature admission precede that traversal; exact resource identities, forbidden synonym paths and stage validation keep their original precedence.

Let S be the existing resolved snapshot transformation and V its native field representation. Restoration previously accepted exactly those inputs J for which canonical(J) = S(decode(J)). The fixed-point predicate for ordinary stop filters requires an empty language and strictly increasing explicit words: the empty language contributes no builtins, and strict order is equivalent to the existing sorted-set transformation leaving the vector unchanged. The Korean POS predicate requires an explicit strictly increasing ordinal vector, precisely the fixed points of default expansion, sorting and deduplication. Japanese explicit stop sets use the analogous strict string ordering; their resource/default predicates still run first. All other snapshot fields remain unchanged once synonym paths have been rejected. Thus these predicates hold exactly when S(c) = V(c), with the two always-explicit synonym fields included.

The retained comparison checks exact object membership and array lengths, then the corresponding original child values. Strings compare their next equal byte fragments without copying them; fixed scalar fields retain their public JSON representation. Structural induction gives comparison success exactly when canonical(J) = V(c), independently of the JSON dependency's object iteration order. Combining this equality with the fixed-point predicate gives the original restoration acceptance condition. Stage validation completes before a canonicality mismatch is reported, preserving later invalid-stage errors. No complete analyzer clone, canonical JSON tree copy, stop-word set reconstruction or resource lookup is needed. Successful restoration returns the same originally decoded configuration.

A suspended owner retains its original input, native configuration, next field/container cursors, string position, encoded count and validation progress. Checkpoints change none of these observations. Cancellation, invalid windows, rejection or abandonment retires the complete unpublished owner; no cache or database is modified. Tests compare the public serializer at every small byte limit, long escape boundaries, all enabled field vocabularies, unknown/default/sequence shapes and the original snapshot acceptance/error order. The allocation regression originally requested 10,705,203 bytes during one poll; the retained fixture bounds every poll below 64 KiB and interrupts construction, size validation and comparison. Atomic allocator/container operations and existing public scalar dependency calls retain their stated boundaries. Common executable preparation follows below. Language-resource preparation and enclosing field/database restoration remain separate integration work.

## Retained common analyzer preparation

Analysis prepares a compiled revision from one immutable descriptor and its already resolved language handles. The retained constructor first reconstructs the same canonical configuration, then visits character filters, the tokenizer, token filters and normalization in their existing order. Ordinary cache publication drains this constructor without suspension while retaining its existing lock boundary; image continuations own unpublished preparation directly. Neither path resolves a host resource during common preparation.

Mapping preparation traverses the original lexical map and groups borrowed entries by descending key length. Within each length bucket traversal is lexical, so flattening the buckets yields exactly the former ordering by descending length and then lexical key. Each original string is copied in scalar-aligned fragments; induction on the copied byte offset gives the same final strings. Stop preparation inserts the same builtin and custom words into the same ordered set, then owns that ordered sequence. Synonym preparation preserves each original lexical key and its expansion sequence, including repeated alternatives. These constructions therefore preserve the prepared lookup functions and ordered character transformations.

Replacement preparation retains the next dollar position, literal prefix and capture fragments. Each transition uses the same escaped-dollar, braced/unbraced reference and missing-reference rules. Fragmented byte searches return the same first delimiter; named capture comparison visits exactly the original capture order. Induction on the next dollar position gives the same fragment sequence and capture-use flag as ordinary regex expansion. The completed replacement owns its text without changing fragment ranges. Public regex compilation and scalar conversion retain their existing atomic boundaries.

For any successful positive partition of the work window, removing checkpoints leaves the same configuration and stage-construction trace. The prepared stage sequence therefore induces the same token terms, positions, offsets, length policy and downstream scoring inputs. Checkpoints do not publish a partial stage or modify a cache. Cancellation, invalid windows, errors and abandonment retire the original descriptor reference, native configuration and all completed or partial stages with their continuation. Before retained preparation, the mapping/synonym fixture allocated 4,643,931 bytes in one poll; the extended mapping/synonym/stop fixture checks every retained poll against a 64 KiB ceiling. Independent regex output checks cover long Unicode and capture syntax, and lifecycle cases interrupt multiple preparation positions. This fixture bound does not bound atomic allocation, container or dependency operations; complete language-resource preparation is integrated into [retained resolved analyzer reconstruction](#retained-resolved-analyzer-reconstruction), while enclosing Storage analyzer/field restoration remains open.

## Retained native ordering and user-rule preparation

Core's synchronous and retained ordering drivers share one heap-transition owner containing the active heap end, next construction root, current root/child and next comparison or swap. A sift compares the same children, selects the greater child, compares it to the root and swaps only when strictly greater. Descending construction roots establish the max-heap invariant by induction because each child's subtree is already a heap. Each extraction moves the maximum to the next suffix slot and restores the remaining heap. Induction on that suffix proves sorted output, while every mutation is a swap and therefore preserves the original multiset and each element's allocation ownership. Equal keys retain the same unstable behavior as the original algorithm. Neither driver allocates sorting scratch. A failed checkpoint/comparison performs no pending swap; suspension retains the next transition and long-comparison continuation.

Analysis orders user-rule indices by the pair (UTF-16 surface, original source index), using Core's retained heap owner and the existing retained common-prefix comparator. The index is a strict tie breaker, so the resulting permutation is exactly the former stable UTF-16 ordering. Korean preparation therefore selects the same first source definition for duplicate surfaces, while Japanese preparation reaches the same duplicate FST rejection after validating that entry. Original entry strings stay in their source owner; sorting copies no rule payload.

The shared source reader retains CR and LF split boundaries, including the empty CRLF slice. Korean preparation retains its original Java comment-suffix, trim and ASCII field-splitting rules. Japanese preparation retains its distinct comment predicate, CSV quote toggles, first four fields, final-field quote preservation, double-quote collapse and space-splitting behavior. A suspended scan keeps its byte/character offset, quote/separator state and completed fields; each next step consumes the same next scalar or delimiter. Induction on source position establishes the same parsed entry sequence and first syntax error. UTF-16 counting/encoding visits scalar-aligned fragments, whose concatenated encodings equal the original complete encoding. Segmentation lengths, whitespace-insensitive surface equality, reading/POS strings, context identities and entry/word indices therefore remain unchanged. Native lexical construction uses the existing retained builder and preserves its original validation order.

Ordinary user-resource compilation drains these same constructors under its existing cache lock; resolver admission and concurrent content interning are unchanged. An image continuation owns source borrows, parsed entries, lexical ranks, native builder and partially prepared words until completion or terminal retirement. Checkpoints cannot publish a partial user dictionary. Independent pinned Korean and Japanese fixtures verify complete lexical/morphology output and errors; retained cases additionally cover long common prefixes, first-definition rules and cancellation/invalid-window/abandonment during each pass. Core cases verify heap boundaries, duplicate values, bounded comparison progress and original reservation retirement. Atomic allocation and destruction retain their existing boundaries; the [resolved image owner](#retained-resolved-analyzer-reconstruction) connects these language-resource constructors to analyzer image restoration; enclosing Storage framing and field integration remain separate.


## Retained resolved analyzer reconstruction

Let a validated descriptor D select its configuration C and an ordered artifact closure A of pairs (language, SHA-256) with immutable encoded owners. Reconstruction admits the original artifact count, strict identity order and per-language byte ceilings before validating D against the destination descriptor limits. Each language pipeline resolves a requested identity lazily from A; it hashes the exact supplied bytes in successive fragments and uses the shared native decoder. A request cannot consult a host resolver or an existing cache. A per-pipeline snapshot reuses each resolved identity. Unused artifacts remain undecoded until the final exact-closure check rejects them, preserving the original missing-resource, hash, model and closure-error precedence.

Ordinary language resolution and image resolution share the same pipeline algorithm and prepared stage constructors. Ordinary sources retain their existing callback placement, cache locks and single decoded allocation on concurrent cache misses; only the image source uses retained decoding without a live cache. Source resolution changes an input name to the same fixed artifact identity, user preparation selects the same model and exact rule source, and native default expansion uses that selected model. Retained identity ordering preserves the final unique language/hash sequence. The descriptor is immutable and already canonical, so pipeline preparation leaves its reconstructed diagnostic configuration equal to the descriptor observation. That owned configuration transfers directly to executable preparation; repeated decoding or validation is unnecessary inside the private reconstruction boundary.

Japanese word preparation retains UTF-16 counting, allocation, scalar conversion, pinned lowercase, lexical sorting and duplicate removal. Each scalar emits the same units; simple lowercase preserves UTF-16 width or raises the same error. Core's shared heap transitions order those same vectors, and adjacent equality removes exactly the original duplicates. Counter checkpoints preserve the existing native callback positions without requiring thread-local callbacks to be Send; retained image futures still satisfy the native input owner's Send bound. A partial word remains inside its Budgeted transformation, and the aggregate preparation owner drops its word buffers before their reservations. Duplicate buffers are destroyed before splitting their corresponding lease. Cancellation, invalid windows, allocation failure and abandonment therefore retire the original candidates without releasing another owner's admission.

For any successful partition of positive work windows, erasing checkpoints yields the same descriptor configuration, requested-artifact trace, model identities, user dictionaries, stage sequence and normalization selection. The compiled revision consequently preserves token terms, graph positions, offsets, length policy, normalization and downstream scoring observations. Its dictionary views borrow the original admitted encoded allocations; decoded model and executable allocations retain their existing separate Analysis limits. A complete revision is returned only after its selected resource sequence equals A. No intermediate dictionary, user rule or executable stage becomes visible through a live cache, and failed or abandoned construction releases the supplied encoded owners.

The public retained descriptor and image entry points compose through one AsyncImageInput without reading its transport. Their owning tests cover independent token expectations, exact descriptor bytes and original resource addresses, mixed language closures, first-definition user rules, wide case-insensitive stop sets, lazy unused-artifact rejection, unavailable resources and terminal retirement. Native allocation/container operations and public scalar, regex and raw-JSON dependency calls retain their stated atomic boundaries. Storage owns descriptor/artifact framing, historical field selection and catalog publication; connecting those enclosing consumers remains a separate integration requirement.

## Retained analyzer and field-binding input

Common Storage owns `read_analyzer_image_from` and `FieldAnalyzerBinding::read_image_from`, while Analysis owns descriptor and exact dictionary/executable reconstruction. The original synchronous readers drive the same native input functions on their invoking stack. Storage reuses the shared retained frame reader for each field's independent index and search image; it neither materializes a complete nested image nor restarts a decoder after yielding. The frame owner preserves partial length headers, the remaining canonical payload, the short-final-frame condition and terminal state through interrupted transport. Ordinary and retained frame reads use the same transition checks.

Let A = (D, R) be the existing resolved analyzer carrier and F = (N_i, A_i, N_s, A_s, O, P) a field binding with optional names, independent historical revisions, owner O and last assignment phase P. Their established encodings E_A and E_F are unchanged. For either decoder, a suspended state contains exactly the consumed prefix, the incomplete native field and its admitted storage, and all earlier complete fields. Every read extends that prefix by the exact next bytes; every preparation step changes only private reconstruction state. The retained child channel projects precisely one canonical framed stream and leaves the parent's following field untouched. Induction over reads, checkpoints and child completion therefore produces exactly the original D, R, names, revisions, O and P. Existing validation order rejects malformed magic, declared limits, resource ordering, incomplete or noncanonical children, trailing bytes and inconsistent bindings before any result is published.

The descriptor's encoded allocation remains live until its Analysis-owned preparation finishes. Artifact buffers retain their original input reservation through the compiled resource's last owner. Field names and the binding header transfer their original leases into the returned native binding; completed first children remain private while the second child is unfinished. Typed memory, cancellation, I/O and Analysis failures preserve their existing boundaries. Invalid windows, cancellation, errors, unwinding and abandonment drop the retained future and all unpublished children; a failed decoder cannot resume. Source EOF precedes retained executable reconstruction, whose continuation performs no further transport reads. The existing standard-library UTF-8 validation of one admitted descriptor remains an atomic dependency call; native byte input and Analysis-owned preparation retain the caller's separate byte and poll limits.

For any successful schedule, these prefix and ownership invariants yield D(E_A(A)) = A and D(E_F(F)) = F under the existing complete-token, exact-resource, name, phase and failure observations. Composing either decoder with canonical framing preserves the same identity on valid images, so enclosing capture/restore introduces no new query operator, scoring approximation or rewrite. Owning tests exercise independent wire bytes and token terms, split multibyte names, interrupted input, enabled Korean/Japanese artifacts, existing-cache isolation, post-EOF work, first-child retention and terminal retirement. Native catalog publication composes with these decoders as described below; complete Engine restoration remains a separate integration requirement.

## Retained analyzer catalog publication

An analyzer catalog is a pair (R, I) of immutable runtime and encoded-image maps with equal key sets. A field catalog additionally preserves the independent historical index/search revisions, optional names, owner and last phase. For each key k, I(k) is the established image projection of R(k). Native input consumes an unpublished successor, reconstructs private revisions, prepares their encoded projection and publishes both roots only after complete admission. A failure destroys that successor; every previously retained generation still refers to its original roots.

Analysis reconstructs an exclusively owned CompiledAnalyzer using the same exact descriptor/resource constructor as ordinary image input. Its consuming image preparation copies canonical descriptor fragments and imported artifact fragments into independently admitted encoded storage. Static and shared artifact bytes retain their existing immutable owners. At any checkpoint, the copied prefix equals the corresponding source prefix and all earlier fields are complete. Induction over fragments and resource order therefore gives exactly the original image projection, including canonical bytes, resource order, identities, capacities and retained-size accounting. Ordinary cached capture drains the same constructor while retaining its original cache locks; private preparation publishes its completed weak cache only after the encoded owner exists and before sharing the compiled revision. No lock spans a suspension and no live cache exposes an incomplete revision.

Storage retains admitted runtime names and field bindings while their independent image names and images are prepared. Each original Produced or Budgeted owner remains intact across awaits, including a completed first field side while preparing the second. Partial buffers are dropped before their reservations; completed images contain no executable model owner and no imported retained-byte owner. Consequently retiring runtime revisions releases import admission independently of encoded capture, while retiring the last capture releases its own admission. Changing a completed catalog's future mutation allowance preserves every existing node and payload owner; cancelling or exhausting the original input allowance cannot change its tokens, names or encoded observations.

Named and field publication preserve the original validation sequence: complete decoding, native admission and identity checks, image preparation, paired-root construction and final cancellation check. Failed insertion, duplicate identity, foreign admission, cancellation, invalid windows or abandonment cannot return a catalog with only one updated root. For successful input, substituting each decoded R(k) and its proven image I(k) into the existing lookup and capture operations gives the same observations as ordinary native publication. Immutable root sharing and later ordinary mutation preserve this correspondence by induction over catalog operations. This introduces no query algebra operation or numerical approximation. Native allocator and ordered-container operations retain their established atomic boundaries; the archive transport, names, descriptor and dictionary copying retain independent byte and work windows.

Owning regressions exercise wide Unicode names and descriptors, exact dictionary bytes, changing windows, post-EOF preparation without transport reads, exhausted original admission, independent future mutation, prior generations and complete failure retirement. Engine supplies archive selection and retained resource handles; connecting those adapters and completing persistent catalog loading remain separate work.

## Resumable graph and path input

Graph's native `UQAGRA01` and `UQAPTH01` readers share one grammar between synchronous calls and `AsyncImageInput` continuations. Graph input retains vertex/edge counters, evaluated property carriers, exact partition membership, label/kind/sequence registries, aliases and tombstones. It constructs the ordinary native adjacency, label and membership maps without executing a query or rewriting dangling edge endpoints. Path input retains each ordered label sequence and its strictly ordered materialized identity pairs without evaluating reachability again. Native values use Core's retained image decoder through Storage's existing error boundary. No graph operator, path approximation or scoring rule is introduced.

Let a graph image denote `G = (V, E, P, L, c_v, c_e)`, where `V` and `E` are the ordered native entity maps including their original property representations, `P` is partition membership, `L` is the complete label-allocation state, and the two counters determine future ordinary identity allocation. Let a path image denote the finite ordered mapping `Q : sequence(label) -> set((source, target))`. An input state consists of the next source offset, partial native fields, constructed private maps, pending native traversal/validation position and original allocation reservations. Suspension changes none of these semantic components. Fixed field reads consume the same byte concatenation; fragment text copying preserves scalar boundaries and original bytes; chunked lexicographic comparison returns at the first unequal byte or, for equal prefixes, the same length comparison. Native payload traversal visits each field and return transition once and sums the same capacities. Therefore each resumed transition matches the corresponding synchronous transition, and induction over successful decoding yields the same `G` or `Q` for every positive partition of byte/poll allowances.

Partition construction invokes the same native insertion algorithms for primary identities, adjacency, labels and reverse membership. A label-name copy reserves the ordinary selected capacity before appending fixed UTF-8 fragments. For each membership prefix, the constructed maps are exactly those obtained by ordinary insertion of that prefix. This invariant holds initially for empty maps and is preserved by each identical insertion, so complete derived maps and their cached charges agree. The retained validation cursor applies the existing counter, registry and forward/reverse membership predicates before graph publication. Complete input is accepted precisely when the same predicates accept the synchronous candidate; neither path introduces an endpoint foreign-key requirement. Thus graph lookup, traversal, graph-set composition, future identity allocation, path lookup and image output have the same observations as the captured state, including empty graphs, unattached entities and label tombstones.

Every pending allocation retains its original reservation inside the unpublished native owner or continuation. Publication moves the admitted name, graph/path data and header into the ordinary catalog without copying their payloads; completed graph data selects the independent mutation allowance. Clones and captures keep the native reservations until the last retained owner retires. Cancellation, malformed framing, denied allocation, zero windows, changed selection and dropped continuations destroy private owners before releasing their reservations. Post-EOF validation or adoption performs no further source reads, so a terminal failure cannot replay or publish a partial image. The successful transition therefore preserves both native observations and the existing mutation/rollback ownership model. Explicit reads and checkpoints obey byte/poll ceilings; native allocation, deallocation and ordered-container comparisons retain their original atomic costs and are not a wall-clock guarantee. Persistent loading, complete logical dependency closure, public activation and final-source qualification remain separate requirements.
